# internal/core

> **Isolation ([ADR-0154](../adr/0154-the-pipeline-is-the-only-controller-and-each-agent-is-an-isolated-replaceable-worker.md)).** The orchestrator builds the artifact index and checks the typed flow decisions of the agent cell (I-4, I-8). The spec of record: [llm-isolation.md](../llm-isolation.md).

> Filled incrementally by file group. This page so far holds groups 1–2 of 13 (`activating_fields_guard_test.go` through `continuation_snapshot_test.go`); later groups extend each section. Related records: the advisor leaf [internal-core-advisor.md](internal-core-advisor.md) and its extraction [04-advisor.md](../decomposition/04-advisor.md), the other extracted units in [decomposition/](../decomposition/00-program-review-2026-09-14.md) ([ADR-0103](../adr/0103-component-breakdown-program.md)), the state schema in [internal-cyclestate.md](internal-cyclestate.md), the allocation lease in [ADR-0049](../adr/0049-concurrent-multi-cycle-execution.md), the transition kernel in [ADR-0058](../adr/0058-config-drive-transition-kernel.md), the halt floor and the fingerprint ack ledger in [ADR-0072](../adr/0072-system-failure-policy-and-halt.md), the bookkeeping regrade in [ADR-0086](../adr/0086-bookkeeping-regrade-microcycle.md), the audit repair loop in [ADR-0092](../adr/0092-audit-repair-loop.md) and [ADR-0093](../adr/0093-retry-envelope-and-terminal-retro.md), the protected control plane in [ADR-0064](../adr/0064-pipeline-integrity-boundary.md), the docs floor in [ADR-0077](../adr/0077-docs-floor-for-architecture-changes.md), the Build explanation contract in [build-explanation-contract.md](../build-explanation-contract.md), the outcome chokepoint in [ADR-0044](../adr/0044-unified-phase-recovery-protocol.md), the carryover unit in [03-carryover-lifecycle.md](../decomposition/03-carryover-lifecycle.md), the composition-verdict ledger side in [internal-adapters-ledger.md](internal-adapters-ledger.md) and [ADR-0048](../adr/0048-work-conservation-fast-reland-resilient-ship.md), the console lease in [ADR-0080](../adr/0080-runtime-console-plane-separation.md), continuation-on-fail in [ADR-0076](../adr/0076-convergence-architecture.md), its registry release in [ADR-0085](../adr/0085-continuation-registry-release.md) and [ADR-0089](../adr/0089-continuation-retirement-and-live-scope-guard.md) and its ledger gate in [continuation-defect-ledger.md](../continuation-defect-ledger.md), and the operator dials in [runtime-reference.md](../../operations/runtime-reference.md). This page keeps the package-level detail those do not.

## Purpose

`internal/core` is the orchestrator. It allocates each cycle's number, dispatches the phases in order, decides every transition and failure disposition, and runs the deterministic floors that grade a phase's output before the next phase may start. It also owns the batch-level blocker breaker and the boot-time recovery of a dirty main tree. It relocates build output a builder leaked into the main tree, carries audited work forward across fleet rebases and failed cycles, and connects the carryover memory to each cycle.

## Design

- **Each phase dispatch writes the input index of the cell first** (`input_index.go`, X5 of [ADR-0154](../adr/0154-the-pipeline-is-the-only-controller-and-each-agent-is-an-isolated-replaceable-worker.md), spec [llm-isolation.md](../llm-isolation.md) §8.5).
  - `cycleRun.dispatch` calls `writeInputIndex` before the loop of tries.
  - The item ids come from the `fleet_scope` of the cycle context. When the run already has a record in `.evolve/lineage/`, the record wins. The first dispatch writes the record before the index, so a failed index does not lose it.
  - It reads `isolation.lineage_max` from the policy. Then it builds the index, writes the record and writes `<ws>/inputs/index.json`.
  - A failure removes the index of the earlier phase and prints `WARN input index for <phase>`, so the cell gets no index grants.
  - The phase request carries no index entry: the agent opens what it needs (`TestDispatch_TheIndexIsTheOnlyLineageInputAndNoEntryIsPreloadedIntoTheRequest`).
  - The handoff correction of `enforcedHandoffRejection` sends the agent to the `handoff-asks` entry of the index (`TestHandoffAsksTravelInTheIndex`).
- **A resume pends a carrier that a crash left at HEAD** (`resume_carrier_heal.go`, ADR-0105 B1). The unwind and the debugger re-entry each commit a carrier with the trailer `Evolve-Carrier: cycle-<N>/<run>`, rebase it, then pend it. After a crash between the rebase and the pend, a resumed ship re-audits an empty `git diff HEAD`.
  - `loadResumeBootstrap` calls `healCarrierAtHead` before a phase runs. It pends the change on its fork point only when the trailer names this cycle and run. It never acts in an in-place worktree, outside a git checkout or during a rebase. In a git checkout, a git failure fails the resume.
  - The heal does not move `WorktreeBaseSHA`. The resumed cycle sees a pending change on the new base. The tree-binding check of ship then decides the next phase, as for the W0 split of the design review.
  - Pinned by `TestLoadResumeBootstrap_PendsARebasedCarrierBeforeAnyPhaseRuns` and `TestCarrierAtHead_RecognisesOnlyThisCycleAndRunsCarrier`.
- **The routing order is the one successor authority, live and on resume.** The code is in `resume_transition.go`. The live walk takes the successor of an inserted phase from `cfg.Order` (`nextInOrder`). A resume of an inserted phase does the same: `nextInOrderRun` walks `cfg.Order` from the phase and skips each optional phase that `routing-plan.json` marks `run:false`.
  - `routing-plan.json` is the raw plan of the router, before the floor clamp turns mandatory phases back on. Thus `resumeFloor` keeps `build`, `audit`, `ship`, each phase of the ship floor and each phase in `cfg.Mandatory`. A run:false entry never skips a kept phase, on the order walk and on the plan-array fallback. `tdd` is kept unless its conditional rule unpins it, as `router.ClampPlanToFloorWith` does. When the signals cannot be read, `tdd` stays kept.
  - The archetype degrade goes to `build` until build is complete.
  - A last guard, `holdShipFloor`, checks the result of each path. When the result is `ship` and `build` or `audit` is not in `CompletedPhases`, the resume goes to the missing phase. Thus `ship ⇒ build ∧ audit` holds on every resume path, whatever the order or the plan holds.
  - Pinned by `TestResolveResumeNext_ThePlanCannotSkipTheIntegrityFloor`, `TestResolveResumeNext_ThePlanCannotSkipAuditOrShip`, `TestResolveResumeNext_ThePlanCannotSkipAPinnedTDD`, `TestResolveResumeNext_ATrivialCycleUnpinsTDDAsTheFloorClampDoes`, `TestResolveResumeNext_ThePlanCannotSkipAConfiguredMandatoryPhase`, `TestResolveResumeNext_ThePlanFallbackCannotSkipAConfiguredMandatoryPhase` `TestDegradeInsertedSuccessor_GoesToBuildUntilBuildIsComplete`, `TestHoldShipFloor_ShipWaitsForBuildAndAudit`, `TestResolveResumeNext_TheDegradeNeverReachesShipBeforeAudit` and `TestResolveResumeNext_APhaseOutsideTheOrderFallsBackToThePlan`.
  - The resume reproduces only the skips of optional phases that the plan records (`run:false`). The live walk can also skip a phase by a router decision, and a resume does not repeat that decision. The walk reads `cfg.Order` once, so a name that occurs twice cannot loop.
  - `nextFromRoutingPlan` reads the plan's array order. It is a fallback only for a phase that is not in the order. An example is a minted phase that a resumed process did not register again. The archetype degrade (`degradeInsertedSuccessor`) is the last fallback.
  - Before this change, a resume took the plan's array order first, and a no-plan resume degraded by archetype. `bug-reproduction` is an Evaluate phase that runs before build, so a no-plan resume sent it to audit and skipped build. That is the red-test-never-greened shape of the [cycle-1550 incident](../../incidents/2026-08-24-anchor-order-splice-misslot.md).
  - Pinned by `TestResolveResumeNext_ANoPlanResumeOfAPreBuildEvaluatePhaseGoesToBuild`, `TestResolveResumeNext_TheRoutingOrderOutranksThePlanArrayOrder`, `TestResolveResumeNext_TheOrderWalkSkipsWhatThePlanDoesNotRun`, `TestResolveResumeNext_APhaseOutsideTheOrderFallsBackToThePlan`, `TestResolveResumeNext_TheLastPhaseOfTheOrderEndsTheCycle` and `TestResolveResumeNext_APlanThatRunsNoLaterOptionalPhaseEndsTheCycle`.
- **A mint batch is routed once** (`routing_dispatch.go`). `registerMintedPhases` registers each minted spec, then calls `phasespec.ApplyUserRouting` once for the batch. Thus a minted phase anchored to a later batch-mate gets the fixpoint placement, as discovered user phases do. Each mint warning is a `CONFIG_PHASE_ROUTING_DEFECT` signal: a rejected mint, a mint that clashes with a runner, a merge warning or a routing warning. Pinned by `TestRegisterMintedPhases_ABatchMateAnchorResolvesThroughTheFixpoint`, `TestRegisterMintedPhases_AnUnresolvableAnchorIsACodedSignal` and `TestRegisterMintedPhases_ARejectedOrClashingMintIsACodedSignal`.
- **Cycle-number allocation.** `StateUpdater` is an optional storage capability, kept apart from the `Storage` port so every existing fake and adapter stays valid. With it, `allocateCycle` mints `max(LastCycleNumber, LastAllocatedCycleNumber, highest occupied go/acs/cycle<N>) + 1` through the serialized `UpdateState` read-modify-write, before any artifact exists under that number. Without it, the orchestrator takes the legacy `LastCycleNumber + 1` path above the same source floor. Resume never allocates: `RunCycleFromPhase` carries the cycle from the run record.
- **Cycle-end persist is a merge, not a write.** On a `StateUpdater`, `persistCycleEndState` replaces the state with the run's own outcome but max-merges the lease and `LastCycleNumber` with disk, keeps the disk revision (the `UpdateState` increment owns the bump), and unions `FailedAt` and `CarryoverTodos` with what peers wrote, because under `EVOLVE_FLEET` the global lock is skipped.
- **Audit-FAIL disposition at the audit chokepoint** (`decideAfterAuditFail`, [ADR-0093](../adr/0093-retry-envelope-and-terminal-retro.md)). It builds and writes the failure dossier (best-effort forensics), reads the audit's own declared class, computes the retry envelope from the policy table, and only then consults the `RetryAdjudicator` Strategy, and only when more than one action is legal. The proposal is clamped to the envelope. A granted retry re-enters `tdd` or `build` through `reentryPhase`; a decline goes to retro, which is now the terminal learning step once per cycle. A floor halt still routes to retro, so the halted cycle gets its post-mortem, but the returned `SystemFailureSignal` is what stops the loop. `emitAuditRepairDecision` makes the outcome one coded signal: WARN `CodeAuditRepairDeclined` or INFO `CodeAuditRepairGranted` with the attempt number.
- **A decline to a defer-or-quarantine class ends the cycle after retro** (`reintent_decision.go`). `auditDeclineDefers` holds when `CycleState.AuditDeclineReason` is set and the decision is `defer-or-quarantine`. The audit's declared class gives the decision through the policy table. When the class does not, the retro's `failure-decision.json` gives it. `decideAfterRetro` then returns `PhaseEnd` with the `audit-fail-defer: ` reason, after the floor gates and before the bookkeeping regrade.
- **The closeout gets the defer that ended the cycle as a value** (`reintent_decision.go`). When the retro decision holds the `audit-fail-defer: ` reason, `carryDefer` copies the category and the `fix_type` of `auditDeclineDefers` into `CycleResult.DeferCategory` and `DeferFixType`. The live path and the resume path both do this. `DeferOf(result)` gives the values to `cycleoutcome`, so the closeout reads no workspace file (`TestRecordAndBranch_TheDeferThatEndsTheCycleIsCarriedOnTheResult`, `TestResumeExecution_TheDeferThatEndsAResumedCycleIsCarriedOnTheResult`). When the reason is a defer but no defer resolves, `carryDefer` writes a WARN.
  - `deferDecision` has three sources in this order. The first is the declared class of the audit through the policy table. The second is the build's system class, which `failureDossier` computes on each call. It reads the auditor ledger (host evidence) and `build-report.md` (agent-written), never `failure-dossier.json`. The third is a trusted `failure-decision.json`.
  - All three sources are files that agents write. The host makes sure that each file is fresh for this cycle, but it does not prove the content. Before each retro dispatch on the live path and the resume path, `clearRetroDecisionInput` removes any `failure-decision.json`. Thus only this retro writes the file that the orchestrator reads (`TestDispatch_TheRetroNeverSeesADecisionPlantedBeforeIt`, `TestResumeExecution_AResumedRetroNeverSeesADecisionPlantedBeforeIt`). When the removal fails, `CycleState.FailureDecisionUntrusted` is set, and the defer rule and the retro floor ignore the file (`TestClearRetroDecisionInput_ADecisionTheHostCannotRemoveIsNeverRead`).
  - A planted read-only `failure-dossier.json` that claims a system class does not defer the item (`TestAuditDeclineDefers_APlantedReadOnlyDossierClaimingASystemClassDoesNotDefer`). `writeDossier` writes through `atomicwrite.Bytes`, which renames a temporary file over the entry. Thus a planted symlink is replaced and its target stays the same (`TestWriteDossier_APlantedSymlinkIsReplacedAndItsTargetIsUntouched`). A failed dossier write is a WARN.
- **The router cannot overrule a defer** (`decideAfterRetroRouted`). It returns before the router and writes one `routing-decision-<n>.json` with the category, the fix type and the source. One `routing_decision` ledger row binds it. So no router proposal can start a tdd, build or audit round on the same diff. Cycle 1953 is why: the router proposed `retry` after an `intent-malformed` decline, and the cycle ran tdd, a 23-minute build and an identical audit FAIL. Pinned by `TestDecideAfterRetroRouted_Cycle1953ReintentDecisionEndsTheCycle`, `TestRecordAndBranch_Cycle1953ReintentDecisionBreaksTheLoop`, `TestDecideAfterRetro_ADeferDecisionOutranksTheBookkeepingRegrade`, `TestDecideAfterRetro_TheRetroFailureDecisionAloneDefersAfterADecline`, `TestDecideAfterRetroRouted_AFloorHaltOutranksADeferDecision` and `TestDecideAfterRetroRouted_ADeclineWithoutADeferDecisionKeepsTheRetroRoute`.
- **A FAIL that names only the explanation document re-authors it at Build** (`explanation_correction.go`, [logic-first design §5.14](../logic-first-delivery-design.md)). `auditFailEnvelope` reads the audit's failure block once. `explanationCorrectionDocument` locates each defect by its first path-like token (a slash, or a stem of two or more characters with an extension that starts with a letter, so `e.g.` and `2.5` are prose and `probe.c` is a file; a `:line` or `#L` suffix and quotes or brackets trimmed). When every location is the cycle's own `explanationdocs.DocumentPath` and no runner gate diagnosed the FAIL (`CycleState.AuditFailReasons` empty), the FAIL is `explanation-needs-correction`, and `explanationCorrectionEnvelope` narrows a granted envelope to `retry@explanation` or `decline` (`computeRetryEnvelope` is unchanged; a decline or halt passes through). `retry@explanation` re-enters Build through `reentryPhase`, so TDD is skipped, the `code-audit-fail` budget still bounds the round and an adjudicator's `retry@tdd` is clamped. Choosing it writes nothing: the round's brief reads the defects from the failure block (`auditRejectionReasons`, below). The first landing copied them into `audit-fail-reason.json` (`recordExplanationCorrection`); that copy is removed, so a re-author Build that dies before the re-audit is digested from its own failure. `seedAuditRepairContext` then adds `CtxKeyExplanationReauthor` (the document path) to that round's Build dispatch on both dispatch roots, and the build prompt scopes the round to the document. Any defect located elsewhere keeps today's routing. Pinned by `TestDecideAfterAuditFail_AnExplanationOnlyFailReauthorsAtBuildNotTDD`, `TestDecideAfterAuditFail_AMixedFailKeepsTDD` and `TestRunCycle_AnExplanationOnlyAuditFailReDispatchesOnlyTheBuildReauthor`.
- **A passing audit's `NEEDS_CORRECTION` is a document-only rung, not a FAIL** (`explanation_correction_rung.go`, X2, [logic-first design §5.15](../logic-first-delivery-design.md)).
  - Both dispatch roots call `decideExplanationCorrection` after the audit FAIL decision. The trigger is a PASS or WARN audit whose `## Explanation Documentation` reads `NEEDS_CORRECTION` (`pendingExplanationCorrection`).
  - The rung appends the Evidence to `CycleState.ExplanationCorrections`, sets `ExplanationCorrectionActive` and schedules Build.
  - `seedAuditRepairContext` gives that Build `CtxKeyExplanationReauthor` and `CtxKeyExplanationCorrection`. The brief lists the document lines the Evidence cites, then the Evidence (`explanationCorrectionBrief`).
  - The Build that completes clears the flag, and the audit runs again on the corrected document.
  - The budget is the `MaxRetries` of `policy.CategoryExplanationCorrection` (default 1), never `code-audit-fail`. Each round emits INFO `ORCHESTRATOR_EXPLANATION_CORRECTION_RUNG`. A spent budget ships the verified code with WARN `ORCHESTRATOR_EXPLANATION_CORRECTION_EXHAUSTED`.
  - A FAIL verdict never takes the rung. `dossierParams` copies the rounds into the dossier.
  - Pinned by `TestRunCycle_ANeedsCorrectionOnAVerifiedBuildCorrectsTheDocumentOnceAndShipsWithNoAuditFail`, `TestRunCycle_ANeedsCorrectionPastItsBudgetShipsTheVerifiedCodeAndSignalsTheExhaustion`, `TestRunCycle_TheCorrectionBudgetComesFromTheFailurePolicy`, `TestRunCycle_AFailingAuditWithANeedsCorrectionReviewTakesTheFailRouteNotTheRung`, `TestResumePath_ANeedsCorrectionOnAPassingAuditTakesTheSameRung` and `TestRunCycle_TheDossierRecordsEachCorrection`.
- **A post-Build writer that leaves the explanation stale routes back to Build; it does not abort** (`build_explanation_handoff.go`). After a later source writer (tdd in an audit-repair round, test-amplification) both dispatch roots ask `explanationRefreshEligible` (the one eligibility rule) and then call `explanationdocs.RefreshResult`. Such a writer can change the whole-diff digest without changing the material behavior the Builder documented, so the host re-seals its snapshot once the writer's deliverable is final. Each root then hands the outcome to `routeAfterExplanationRefresh`, the one home of every route back to Build, and only schedules what it returns or aborts with what it returns. A material-scope change (`requiresBuild`) re-enters Build, unbudgeted, so that the Builder, not the host, rewrites the rationale. A failure the validator classes as the explanation's own content (`errors.Is(err, explanationdocs.ErrContent)`: a cited path no longer in the diff, Changed Areas drift, a missing, malformed or oversized document or section) re-enters Build for the owner to re-author it, and costs depend on the writer. When every legal successor of the writer already leads to Build (`StateMachine.everyPathReaches`, a walk over the configured legal graph, with no phase-name literal), the round is free: tdd's only successors are build-planner and build, so the Build runs anyway and its own explanation floor re-validates the document. Any other writer spends one `AuditRepairAttempts` from the `code-audit-fail` budget, read straight from `computeRetryEnvelope` (legal only while it still offers `retry@build`); that bound keeps a writer that re-runs after every Build from looping. A spent budget aborts as `ErrCycleLevelFailure` (`wrapCycleLevelError`), so it ends the cycle and the batch continues; the message names the envelope's reason (`… no explanation re-author round left: retry budget spent for code-audit-fail (2/2)`). A binding or unreadable failure (activation, host snapshot, git, the Build report, a document that is not a regular file, a manifest write) is not `ErrContent` and aborts raw, as before. Every taken round emits one WARN `phase.outcome` `ORCHESTRATOR_EXPLANATION_REAUTHOR_ROUTED`: `fields.next` is Build and `fields.charged` says whether the round spent the budget; a charged round adds `fields.attempt` and `fields.envelope` (the envelope's unchanged reason). The event leaves `Event.Attempt` unset, because a routing disposition is not a dispatch attempt; it carries no verdict either, so the dashboard does not count it as a run. The command is named for what it does (`routeAfterExplanationRefresh`): it may spend, signal and print, so it is not a query. The rung never sets `AuditRepairActive`. In cycle 1822's own shape an audit had already spoken, so the flag was already true and the Build carries that audit's brief; a round before any audit leaves it false and the Build carries no repair brief. `archiveRepairPrompts` numbers its archive by `AuditRepairAttempts`: a free tdd round keeps the repair round's number (`build-prompt.round1.txt` holds the prompt round 1 replaced), and a charged round inside an active repair archives the replaced repair-build prompt as `round<N+1>`. The classification has one home, `explanationdocs.checkBuild`, which returns content verdicts and a fault on separate channels; this package only disposes of them. Pinned by `TestApplyPostReviewGuards_AStaleCitationAfterTDDRoutesToBuildWithoutSpendingTheBudget`, `TestApplyPostReviewGuards_AStaleCitationAfterABudgetedWriterSpendsOneRepairAttempt`, `TestApplyPostReviewGuards_ABudgetedWriterPastItsBudgetEndsTheCycleNotTheBatch`, `TestApplyPostReviewGuards_BindingMismatchStillAbortsAsBatchFatal`, `TestRunCycleFromPhase_AStaleCitationAfterAResumedTDDRoutesToBuildWithoutSpending`, `TestRunCycleFromPhase_AResumedRefreshAbortStopsBeforeBuild`, `TestApplyPostReviewGuards_AMaterialScopeChangeAfterAWriterRoutesToBuildWithoutCharge` (the material route for tdd and a budgeted writer: Build, nothing spent, no re-author signal), `TestRunCycleFromPhase_AResumedMaterialScopeChangeRoutesToBuild` and `TestStateMachine_EveryPathReaches`.
- **A FAIL that only deterministic gates forced repairs at Build, led by the gate's remediation** (`audit_gate_remedy.go`, [incident](../../incidents/2026-10-07-cycle-1828-gate-forced-fail-earned-no-repair-and-halted.md)). `auditGateRemedies` is the one table of the audit's deterministic gates: per gate, the stable prefix of its diagnostic, the one-step remediation (skills-drift: the worktree's own generator, `EVOLVE_WORKTREE_ROOT=<worktree> go run ./cmd/evolve skills generate` in `<worktree>/go`, because an installed binary renders its own build's templates) and `isStaticReading`, whether the gate reads the tree without executing it. Every derived block declares `gateDerivedClass` (`code-audit-fail`). `gateFailureBlock` derives a `phasecontract.FailureBlock` from `CycleState.AuditFailReasons` only when only deterministic gates forced the FAIL over a PASS or WARN narrative (a verdict-conflict record exists): every other reason is a table gate's diagnosis, and there is one or more. Each defect reads `<remediation> — <diagnosis>`; any other reason, or no conflict record, means no block. A reason that carries `HarnessRedClauseMarker` (an EGPS red the harness could not run) is no gate's diagnosis. `auditFailEnvelope` uses the derived block only when the audit report declares no block of its own, and then narrows a granted envelope to `retry@build` or `decline` (`gateRemediationEnvelope`, through `narrowRetryEnvelope`, which `explanationCorrectionEnvelope` also uses). `auditRejectionReasons` renders the derived block first under the same condition, so the repair brief and the adjudicator lead with the remediation; a declared block keeps the brief it had. The table lives in `core`, not `phases/audit`, because the retro floor reads it too, and `phases/audit` renders each producer's remedy from it (`AuditGateRemedy`) and the harness-red clause from `HarnessRedClauseMarker`, so the text has one home; `AuditGateOf` is exported only so `phases/audit/audit_gate_remedy_binding_test.go` can feed every real producer's diagnostic through it, as the bookkeeping classifier's binding test does. Pinned by `TestAuditGateFail_SkillsDriftOverPassNarrativeGrantsOneBuildRepairLedByItsRemediation`, `TestAuditGateFail_TheDerivedRouteIsRetryAtBuild`, `TestAuditGateFail_AFailNarrativeBesideAGateDiagnosisDerivesNothingAndKeepsTheFloor`, `TestAuditGateFail_AHarnessRedEGPSEarnsNoRepairAndKeepsTheHalt`, `TestResumePath_GateForcedAuditFailRepairsAtBuildWithinTheBudget`, `TestAuditFailEnvelope_AnAuditorDeclaredClassKeepsItsFullEnvelope`, `TestAuditRejectionReasons_AnAuditorDeclaredBlockKeepsTheDerivedGateBlockOut` and `TestGateFailureBlock_DerivesTheClassOnlyWhenGatesAloneForcedTheFail`.
- **Round-scoped audit artifacts** (`audit_round_artifacts.go`). The auditor persona pre-writes `acs-verdict.json`, and `audit.Classify` honors a pre-staged file. So every audit re-dispatch (repair round, bookkeeping regrade, ship-error recovery, debugger `RERUN_PHASE`) first renames the previous round's `acs-verdict.json` and `audit-report.md` to `*.round<N>.*` archives. The one primitive, `supersedePreviousAuditRound`, sits in the audit pre-dispatch block of both dispatch surfaces, beside `resetFloorFailReason`, never at a branch site: a branch site would cover only some re-audit paths and open a window with no verdict artifact across the re-entered `tdd`/`build` phases. The round index is the greater of the persisted `AuditDispatches` counter and the count of completed audit phases (the backstop for checkpoints written before the counter existed). The full design is in [the incident record](../../incidents/2026-09-02-repair-round-stale-acs-verdict.md).
- **Bookkeeping regrade** (`bookkeeping_regrade.go`, [ADR-0086](../adr/0086-bookkeeping-regrade-microcycle.md)). The classifier owns the reason prefixes (`defect ledger: `, `closure claim without a citation: `, and the anchored `verdict-conflict: auditor narrative=PASS|WARN` record). The producers in `internal/phases/audit` are pinned to these matchers by `phases/audit/bookkeeping_reason_singlesource_test.go`, which feeds real minted diagnostics through them. The target phase resolves through the `bookkeeping-regrade` RecoveryMap key, so catalog config may remap it; the compiled fallback is `audit`.
- **Blocker breaker** (`blocker_breaker.go`). A pure, deterministic function over a batch's failure digests, evaluated by the loop after every iteration. Rules, in evaluation order: `guard-class` (guard aborts are pipeline machinery failing by construction), `unexplained-failures` (digests with no machine-readable reason: a diagnosability breakdown), `identical-fingerprint` (Rule B: identical failure identities cannot be distinct honest defects) and `consecutive-failures` (back-to-back failed cycles, whatever their fingerprints). The first rule that trips names the halt. The breaker is task-agnostic: same-task repeats belong to S5 quarantine (`task_retry_ceiling`), so a batch of many different honest rejections never trips it. A zero ceiling disables a rule only inside `BlockerBreakerConfig`; policy cannot pass one, because a non-positive policy value keeps the default. After the digest rules, `EvaluateLaneDeferrals` runs the `lane-deferrals` rule over the batch's lane-deferred cycles.
- **The breaker counts a failure class as one identity.** The `identical-fingerprint` rule counts a digest by its class fingerprint when it has one (`breakerIdentity`). Else it counts the exact fingerprint. An ack of either fingerprint removes the digest from the count ([failure-identity-lifecycle.md §4](../failure-identity-lifecycle.md#4-failure-class-fingerprint)).
- **A claimed item keeps a readable contract path** (`task_contract.go`). `taskItemRefs` takes each id's path from `fleet_scope_paths`, which is written before the claim. The claim then moves the record into `processing/cycle-N/`, so that path no longer opens. When a pair path is not readable, `taskItemRefs` asks the scope-path resolver again, and the resolver returns the `processing/` path of a claimed item (`inboxmover.ResolveDispatchState`). Before this, cycles 1946 and 1950 told the builder `inbox record unreadable` in every correction prompt, so it never saw the acceptance criteria. Pinned by `TestTaskItemRefs_StalePendingPathReResolvesToTheClaimedRecord`, `TestResolveDispatchState_ProcessingCarriesTheClaimedPath` and `TestScopePathResolver_ClaimedItemResolvesToItsProcessingPath`.
- **A reason's detail never moves its identity.** `AssembleFailureDigest` classifies and fingerprints the fail reasons with their `cyclestate` detail stripped (`withoutDetails`), `normalizeReasonForFingerprint` strips it for every caller, the contract-block identity included, and `carryover.Summary` strips it from the failure-learning record (the P0 todo's text and fingerprint), so a refusal that names its paths (the audit's undeclared inputs) keeps one class and one fingerprint across path sets (`TestAssembleFailureDigest_AReasonsDetailNeverChangesItsIdentity`, `TestNormalizeReasonForFingerprint_ContractBlocksShareAnIdentityAcrossTheirDetail`).
- **Fingerprint ack ledger.** `.evolve/resolved-fingerprints.json` is an append-only JSON array (`fingerprint`, `resolved_at`, `resolved_by`). `LoadResolvedFingerprints` feeds `BlockerBreakerConfig.AckedFingerprints`. It is written by `evolve loop --reset --fingerprint` (`AppendResolvedFingerprint`) and by transactional inbox consumption (`ConsumePipelineDefectFingerprint`), which parses the fingerprint from an item's `consumed_by` text and falls back to its auto-filed `notes`.
- **Boot recovery** (`boot_preflight.go`). A leak into the main tree makes every later cycle's tree-diff guard FAIL against whichever phase runs first, wedging the loop until someone stashes by hand. So `runLoop`'s boot path calls `QuarantineDirtyTree`, which moves leaked tracked source into a named stash (recoverable with `git stash pop`, never discarded), and `ShipSHAMismatch`, which reports the `SELF_SHA_TAMPERED` drift at boot instead of only when ship fails. The wiring lives in `cmd/evolve/cmd_loop_boot_recovery.go`.
- **Build handoff floor** (`build_floor_reviewer.go`). Deterministic checks run as part of Build verification, so a red floor rejects the deliverable and the existing correction ladder turns it into a bounded in-phase fix; audit and adversarial review still follow as the judgment layer. The reviewer is the named list itself, `BuildHandoffFloor.Review` (below), which owns policy only; each check is a `BuildFloorCheckFn` (production: `DefaultBuildFloorChecks` inside the composition root's `production` check). The engine composes:
  - `RemovalClaimFailures`, `PlaceholderTokenFailures` and `personaBudgetFailures`, which run outside `changedPackageFloorChecks` because that engine returns early when the diff yields no Go test packages. Both a build whose only claim is a deletion and a lane whose only change is a persona doc produce exactly zero packages.
  - `docsFloorWarn`, the [ADR-0077](../adr/0077-docs-floor-for-architecture-changes.md) floor. It labels with the blocking-grade classifier `docsfloor.IsArchitectureClass`, which drops test-only diffs (the WARN's main false positive), but its verdict stays WARN.
  - `commentFloorFailures` (`comment_floor.go`), the comment regrowth guard: the comment lines the build adds, counted across the whole diff with `commentaudit.AddedAcrossDiff` (a comment moved into another file, or renamed with its file, is not added; nothing under `docs/` is code, so the predicate packages continuation adoption archives under `docs/private/research/` add nothing, `TestCommentFloorFailures_AnAdoptedAncestorsArchivedPredicatesAddNoComment`; it lists its own paths with `--no-renames` so a rename shows both sides). Its stage is `policy.json` `comment_floor.stage` (`CommentFloorConfig`, compiled default `shadow`), read with `config.GateStage` like every staged dial: shadow WARNs on the phase log, enforce rejects the handoff with every added line named, off is silent, and an unknown word is off with a WARN naming it. An unreadable diff fails open with a WARN, and git's errors are carried in the error, never printed. `changedWorktreePathsSince` lists paths with `-z`, so a non-ASCII name is the raw path every floor reads. Also pinned by `TestCommentFloorFailures_ACommittedBuildIsJudgedAgainstItsBase`, `…_WithoutAPolicyBlockItOnlyShadows`, `…_ANewFilesPackageDocIsNotAdded`, `…_AnUnreadableDiffFailsOpen`, `TestCommentFloorStage_AnUnknownWordIsOff`, `TestWorktreeGit_CarriesGitsOwnErrorInsteadOfPrintingIt` and `TestChangedWorktreePathsSince_NamesANonASCIIPathAsGitStoresIt`. Pinned by `TestCommentFloorResult_EnforceRejectsEveryAddedCommentByName`, `TestCommentFloorResult_ShadowOnlyWarnsAndOffOrACleanDiffIsSilent`, `TestCommentFloorFailures_JudgesTheWorktreeAgainstItsBaseAndSparesAMovedComment` and the wiring proof `TestDefaultBuildFloorChecks_RunsTheCommentFloor`.
  - `sizeFloorFailures` (`size_floor.go`), the size-ratchet floor against the base: `sizeratchet.CheckAgainstBase` reads the offender list and the changed `.go` files at the base (`commentaudit.ReadAtBase`). It measures the same files on disk. It refuses a listed function that grew past its base size, and a new function past 50 lines. So a lane cannot regrow a function before the boundary `evolve ratchet tighten` removes the slack. A module without a list at the base is silent, and an unreadable base, diff or module prefix fails open with a WARN. Pinned by `TestSizeFloorFailures_RefusesAListedFunctionThatRegrowsIntoItsSlack`, `TestSizeFloorFailures_IsSilentWithoutAWorktreeOrAListAndFailsOpenOnAnUnreadableBase`, `TestSizeFloorFailures_JudgesAnUncleanWorktreePathAgainstItsModule` and the wiring proof `TestDefaultBuildFloorChecks_RunsTheSizeFloor`.
    - **The refusal** tells the lane to shrink the function back to its base size and not to edit the list. It does not name the writer, so it sends no lane to run it.
    - **The module prefix** is `filepath.Rel(worktree, codequality.ModuleDir(worktree))` (`moduleRelPrefix`), so an unclean worktree path still maps `go/p/a.go` to the module.
    - **No stage dial, by design.** A clean tree passes by construction: every listed function is at or under its allowance at the base. The refusal is exact and cheap to satisfy. A shadow period lets a lane consume slack before the tighten, which is what this floor exists to stop (R4 of the logic-first design). The comment floor has a dial because it started with an existing debt; this floor starts with none.
  - `changedPackageFloorChecks`, which reuses the post-build selfcheck machinery (`changedGoTestPackages` → `runBuildSelfCheck`). Packages enrolled in `.apicover-enforce` run once, under the coverage-instrumented pass in `apicoverNamingFailures`, which doubles as their selfcheck and applies CI's apicover naming floor at handoff. The rest take the plain selfcheck. The floor writes `build-selfcheck.json` for the ACS toolchain gate, and the advisory binding skips its duplicate run when the floor is enforced, so there is one go-test pass per build.
  - The floor reads `go/.apicover-enforce` and `go/.cover-strict` from the base and from the tree together (`baseAndTreeList`, `floor_lists_base.go`, 2026-10-21). `floorListRegressions` (`floor_lists_regression.go`) refuses a lane that drops a base line or lowers a base floor. It also refuses a `.cover-strict` line that does not parse. An unreadable base WARNs, and the floor judges the tree list alone (gate-input-inventory D3). (TestEnforcedPackages_ABuildCannotDropAPackageFromTheBaseList, TestCoverChangedFailures_TheStrictFloorsOfTheBaseStillBindAfterTheBuildLowersThem, TestFloorListRegressions_RefusesADroppedOrLoweredLineAndAcceptsARaise)
  - `addedTaggedTestFailures`, which runs every added test package under the build tags its files declare, using the same seed (`changedpkgs.ChangedFilesChecked`) and grouping (`addedtests.Groups`) as the ship gate's added-test backstop. Each package runs under `addedtests.PackageTimeout` (20 min), the per-binary deadline its twin, ship's added-test backstop, gives the same package (`repoContractTestTimeout` aliases it), so the floor is never stricter than a later gate running that package (`taggedTestArgs`, 2026-09-30): at 120 s the floor killed cycle 1763's own `-count=50 -race` stability predicate, and the lane edited the floor to survive.

  **One go-test budget for every floor run (2026-10-05).** The untagged self-check of changed packages (`unitTestArgs`) and the coverage-instrumented pass (`coverTestArgs`) run under `addedtests.PackageTimeout` too, so all three floor `go test` argvs and ship's repo contract share one deadline. Before, the self-check had its own `-timeout 120s` and the coverage pass `-timeout 300s`. A green `./cmd/evolve` takes 105–127 s on the loop host, so the floor killed it on every correction round, and Go's timeout panic dumped whichever goroutine was live. Cycles 1787, 1791, 1792 and 1798 failed that way after correct builds; a lane could not fix it and must not raise the floor that grades it (inst-L1763b). ADR-0083 had treated the 120 s as the alarm for a slow ordinary package. A floor deadline is the wrong place for that alarm, because it blames whichever lane touches the package; a package wall-time budget check is the right place (inbox `cmd-evolve-unit-wall-time-and-package-budget-check`). `TestGoTestTimeouts_AreTheSharedPackageBudget` (`internal/guards`) fails any `-timeout` in a go-test argv (one carrying the `"test"` token), or any `-test.timeout`, in a non-test file under `go/internal` or `go/cmd` whose value is not `addedtests.PackageTimeout` (directly, or through a package constant that aliases it); a go-test argv with no `-timeout` gets Go's 10 m default and is out of its reach (the residual is listed in the guards package notes), and `phase_bindings_selfcheck.go` and `addedtests/budget.go` are on the protected surface. **Hold time and wait time are separate budgets.** The coverage pass holds the host-wide verification lock (`verifylock`) while it runs, so a hung test can now hold it for the whole 20 m budget, as ship's repo contract can. That hold time is for correctness: a deadline shorter than a green package's runtime is the false RED this fix removes. The wait is for fleet fairness: a lane queued behind a hung holder waits at most `verifylock.MaxWait` (15 min, `coverLockWait`), then runs unserialized with a WARN that names the bound (`verifylock.AcquireWithin`), the same bound and degrade path the ACS suite has used since ADR-0080. Pinned by `TestScopedCoverFunc_BehindAHeldLockWaitsOnlyItsBoundThenRunsUnserialized` (red first: it waited for the caller's 60 s deadline, then could not run) and `TestCoverLockWait_IsTheSharedVerificationWaitBound`. The plain unit check (`realGoUnitTest`) takes no lock, and a sandboxed lane pane cannot open the lock file at all (inbox `verifylock-unreachable-from-sandboxed-pane`). Also pinned by `TestUnitTestArgs_RunAChangedPackageUnderTheSharedBudget`, `TestCoverTestArgs_RunTheCoveragePassUnderTheSharedBudget` and `TestTaggedTestArgs_RunAnAddedPackageUnderShipsBackstopBudget`.

  The changed-path set is derived once per handoff (`changedFloorPaths`) and passed down, so the path-driven engines judge the same diff with one `git diff`.
- **Protected-surface floor** (`ProtectedSurfaceFloorChecks`). It asks the ship tripwire's question (`ship/integrity.go` `verifyNoControlPlaneEdits`) at the one phase that can still act on the answer. The membership predicate (`guards.IsProtectedSurface`) is injected by the composition root, because `guards` imports `core`.
- **Scope-delta floor** (`ScopeDeltaFloorChecks`, `scope_delta_floor.go`, [ADR-0087](../adr/0087-scope-delta-adjudication.md)). At build exit it classifies every changed path against the lane's declared scope with `scopedelta.Classify`. For each path outside the scope it emits one `ORCHESTRATOR_SCOPE_DELTA` signal and writes one stderr line for the builder. It returns no failure, so the handoff never blocks on it: this is the shadow rollout of ADR-0087.
  - The declared scope is the `files` of the lane's claimed records in `.evolve/inbox/processing/cycle-N/`, filtered by the pin `lane-scope.json` (`LaneDeclaredFiles`). A sibling lane is another `runs/cycle-*` lane whose pin carries the same `goal_hash` (`SiblingLaneFiles`). A path in a sibling's scope is `cross-lane`. `ClassifyLaneChanges` (`lane_scope_assembly.go`, beside `lanescope.go`) builds the `scopedelta.Scope` from the declared files, the protected paths among the changed ones and the sibling files. It then runs `Classify` on every changed path. It is the one scope assembly, shared with `evolve cycle scope-delta`: the floor drops the in-scope entries and emits, and the command maps the entries to rows.
  - An unreadable claim file makes the declared scope an error, never a partial list. Each caller then prints its own WARN and keeps its safe default (`TestLaneDeclaredFiles_AnUnreadableClaimFileRefusesThePartialScope`).
  - The protected member (`guards.IsProtectedSurface`) is injected by the composition root, as for the protected-surface floor. A path it names is `boundary`.
  - The signal is `gate.passed`: WARN for carve and refuse, INFO for a computed closure. The reason is the classifier's own. It goes through `ReviewInput.Signals`, which `reviewInputFor` sets to the orchestrator's center. The pre-handoff probes carry no center and write the stderr line only. While the rollout is shadow, the loop's cycle summary line (`formatSignalReport`, `gates: N passed`) counts one passed gate per path outside the scope.
  - A cycle with no declared file (no claimed record, or records without `files`), no worktree, no project root or an unreadable claims dir reports nothing. The last case WARNs. An unreadable pin loses the wave, not the report: the lane then has no sibling, and the delta is still reported.
  - Pinned by `TestScopeDeltaFloorChecks_ReportsEveryOutOfScopePathWithOneCodedSignal`, `TestScopeDeltaFloorChecks_IsSilentWithoutAScopeToCompareAgainst`, `TestScopeDeltaFloorChecks_AnUnreadablePinLosesTheWaveNotTheReport`, `TestClassifyLaneChanges_JudgesEveryChangedPathAgainstOneAssembledScope`, `TestSiblingLaneFiles_SameWaveOnlyAndWarnsOnACorruptPin`, `TestLaneDeclaredFiles_FollowsThePinAndCountsEveryClaimWithoutOne`, `TestReviewInputFor_TheOrchestratorHandsItsSignalCenterToTheFloor` and the composition-root proof `TestProductionBuildFloorChecks_ReportsAScopeDeltaThroughTheComposedFloor`.
- **Repo-contract floor** (`RepoContractFloorChecks`, `repo_contract_floor.go`). It runs ship's repo-contract fixed scanner pack (the suites `repocontract.Packages()` lists) at build exit, so a red the lane caused goes back to the build correction, with the failing test named, before any audit. Cycle 1745 is why: a raw `git init` fixture passed the build self-check and two audits, and only ship's pack ran the raw-git ratchet. The pack run (`RepoContractPackFn`) is injected by the composition root as `ship.RunRepoContractPack`, because `phases/ship` imports `core`; the runner stays in ship and the suite list in `repocontract.Packages()`. Whether the pack runs is one decision shared with ship's gate, `repocontract.PackRuns(gate, root)` (`internal/repocontract`): only while the gate is on and `<root>/go/go.mod` declares evolve-loop's own module (`github.com/mickeyyaya/evolve-loop/go`, derived from `repocontract`'s own import path so it cannot drift from `go.mod`, and pinned against this repository's tree by `TestPackRuns_RunsInThisRepositorysOwnTree`). A foreign module, or a tree without `go/`, is skipped with a note at both, because the pack is evolve-loop's own guard set. #711's e2e fixtures (`e2e.local/fixture`) are that shape: ship had been refusing every one of them (`REPO_CONTRACT_GATE`) under tests that asserted only that a ship role was reached, and the floor moved that refusal to build exit. In evolve-loop's own tree a pattern that matches nothing (a lane that deleted or moved a guard suite) is still a named `[build failed]` red at both, carrying go's own `pattern …: no such file or directory` text, since the lane can restore it; classing that as infra was declined because ship would then call a deleted guard suite "safe to re-dispatch" on every attempt. It follows ship's dial, `gates.repo_contract_gate` (compiled default `enforce`), with ship's meaning: unset and `off` are off, and a typo runs as enforce with ship's own note. The dial is read from the project's `policy.json`, or the worktree's when the caller gives no project root (`evolve selfcheck build`); a policy it cannot read or parse WARNs and runs the pack. There is no separate stage because the floor moves a verdict ship already enforces to an earlier, cheaper rung; a shadow period would only delay what ship blocks anyway.
- **The project root of the repo-contract floor.** `RepoContractPackFn` takes the project root and the worktree. The floor passes `ReviewInput.ProjectRoot`. Thus the pack of ship writes its greens into the verdict store that the ship reads (plan wave-latency-2026-10 D6, [internal-testverdict.md](internal-testverdict.md)). Pinned by `TestRepoContractFloorChecks_HandsThePackTheProjectRootOfTheSharedVerdictStore`.
- **Build explanation floor.** `MandatoryBuildHandoffFloor()` is run by the mandatory reviewer outside the optional reviewer seam, so a caller cannot disable or replace it. `NewExplanationLifecycleReviewer` is the last reviewer in the production chain and seals a Build output only after every earlier reviewer approved, so a rejected attempt is never frozen. The lifecycle vocabulary that `guards/integrity_surface_explanation_callsites_test.go` derives is parsed from the function declarations of `build_explanation_handoff.go`.
- **One named floor, run by the cycle and by both pre-handoff probes** (`build_handoff_floor.go`, [ADR-0117](../adr/0117-pre-handoff-probes-run-the-build-floor.md)). `BuildHandoffFloor` is an ordered list of `BuildFloorCheck{Name, Run}` and a Composite: `Failures` runs every check on one input and returns every failure line in check order, `Names` lists the checks, and `Review` judges build handoffs only and rejects with `build handoff floor: N deterministic check failure(s) — fix these exactly before handoff:` and the lines (Retry set), so the list is the `DeliverableReviewer` itself. It is the only floor constructor: the opaque `NewBuildFloorReviewer`, `NewBuildExplanationReviewer` and `ChainBuildFloorChecks` are gone. Core owns the mandatory half, `MandatoryBuildHandoffFloor()`: the explanation-documentation check, which skips a cycle whose contract version is 0 itself. The mandatory reviewer holds that half as its `floor` field and runs it. The composition root owns the composed half (production checks, plus the document solution floor on a document registry) and mounts that list itself in the reviewer chain. `WholeBuildHandoffFloor(composed)` is the mandatory half followed by the composed half, the order the cycle runs them, and is what `evolve selfcheck build` and `evolve phase verify build` run. `Orchestrator.BuildHandoffFloorNames()` walks the reviewer chain (the mandatory half and every `BuildHandoffFloor`, nested chains included) so the composition root can prove the cycle and the probes iterate one list. A list wrapped inside another reviewer type is invisible to the walker; that shape is left to review. The file is on the protected surface.
  - When the explanation half rejects, the mandatory reviewer also runs every `BuildHandoffFloor` of its chain (`floorChecks`). It returns one rejection with the union of the failures, in check order.
  - Thus the one correction of a build gets the full list: the explanation gap, the size allowance, coverage, apicover and STE. Before this change, the rejection named only the explanation gap, and 5 of the 7 failed cycles of wave 143 failed on the next floor.
  - The other reviewers of the chain do not run on a floor rejection (`TestMandatoryExplanationReviewer_AnExplanationGapCarriesEveryComposedFloorFailureInOneRejection`). `floorCheckNames` is the names of `floorChecks`, so the cycle runs the same list that it names.
- **The floor's inputs have one projection.** `ReviewInputFor(cs, phase, projectRoot)` turns a cycle's binding (cycle, run id, explanation contract version, base SHA, workspace, active worktree) into a `ReviewInput`. The live review ladder and the resumed review build their input with it, and the ladder's correction directive and evidence digest read the worktree from that input, not from the dispatch-time snapshot. The resume bootstrap sets a legacy checkpoint's missing `cycle_id` to the resumed cycle once, so the projection is total on resume and no caller patches its copy. `ReadRunCycleState(workspace, evolveDir)` is the one reader of the persisted binding: the run workspace's `run.json` mirror when a workspace is given, because under fleet lanes the global state file holds whichever run wrote last, else `ResolveCycleStatePath(evolveDir)`. A cycle worktree's `.evolve/cycle-state.json` is a symlink to its run's `run.json`, so a worktree's own `.evolve` resolves its own cycle. A missing or unparseable file is an error naming the path, never a zero state. `BuildHandoffProbe{Workspace, EvolveDir, ProjectRoot, Worktree}.Input()` reads the state, refuses one that binds a different worktree than the one asked about (`sameDirectory`), and projects it; when it cannot bind, it returns the unbound input (`Phase`, `Workspace`, `Worktree`, `ProjectRoot` only) with the reason, and the probe says so.
- **One fresh session after a session-recoverable end** (`launch_recoverable.go`, [ADR-0044 amendment of 2026-10-11](../adr/0044-unified-phase-recovery-protocol.md)). `LaunchRecoverable(ctx, launch, req, onRecoverable)` is the one retry loop of F31.
  - It launches once. When the response is `SessionRecoverable` and the context is live, it asks `onRecoverable` for the next request, marks it `FreshSession` and launches once more.
  - It clears `SessionRecoverable` on the second response, so a caller around it does not retry again.
  - `LaunchWithFreshSession(ctx, bridge, req)` uses `SameRequest(req)` as the hook. The phase runner passes its postmortem hook.
  - The phase advisor, the plan judge, the failure advisor and the retry adjudicator use `LaunchWithFreshSession`. Retro, the swarm workers, `subagent` and the chain walk of `bridgechain` use it too.
  - Pinned by `TestLaunchRecoverable_*` and one `…SurvivesADeadShellFirstSession` test for each caller.
- **Advisor seams kept in core.** `AgentIdentity` is an alias of `advisor.Identity`, so the failure advisor and the retry adjudicator keep their spelling. `AdvisorDepthExceeded` is a dormant injectable backstop that always returns false: the `EVOLVE_ADVISOR_DEPTH` signal was retired, and the mint denylist is the only active recursion guard ([04-advisor.md](../decomposition/04-advisor.md), operator question 6).
- **ShipError has no code-to-class mapper in core.** Each call site in `internal/phases/ship` chooses the class, and `NewShipError` records it verbatim. The table in `apicover_shiperror_test.go` is transcribed from those call sites, so a drift between the table and production is a protocol regression.
- **Test-side fixtures.** `advisor_fixture_test.go` holds the one rich `RouteInput` the advisor goldens are captured over, rendering every prompt section at once. `apicover_*_test.go` files name and exercise exported symbols for the `apicover` gate ([ADR-0050](../adr/0050-modularization-and-unified-phase-io.md)). Each asserts a real behavior, never `_ = pkg.X` padding.
- **The handoff floor's text checks** (`build_removal_check.go`, `build_placeholder_check.go`, `build_persona_budget_check.go`). A removal claim is machine-read only from a fenced ```` ```json ```` block with a `removedPaths` array, taken from the build report or its promoted copy under `deliverables/`. Prose is never parsed, because a matcher on "removed" would false-block honest reports. Each claimed path is checked against the worktree and then against its Git index (`git ls-files --error-unmatch`), since a filesystem-only deletion comes back on the next checkout. `PlaceholderTokenFailures` refuses any `UPPER_SNAKE_PLACEHOLDER` slot left in the report, one failure per token per line. The persona budget check runs `./internal/prompts` only when the diff touches `agents/evolve-*.md` and that package is not already in the derived set.
- **Build-leak recovery** (`recoverBuildLeak`, tests in the integration-tagged `buildleak_recover_test.go` and `leak_ownership_recovery_test.go`). A non-Claude builder is not bound by the Claude-only role gate, and the OS sandbox is off on nested macOS, so it can write into the main tree. Recovery moves that output into the worktree, stages only the relocated paths so the auditor's `git diff HEAD` sees it without pollution, and restores the main tree. The tests use a real `git worktree add`, because a fixture of two independent repos once masked a directory-rename bug.
  - **The parameter objects.** `leakRecovery` carries the project root, the worktree, the cycle number, the phase baseline, the phase's `writeAuthority`, the lane's `mainTreeOwnership` and its `leakExemptions` (`exempt`). `Orchestrator.recoverPhaseLeak` builds it from a `phaseLeakScope` (project root, cycle state, phase, baseline, `leased`) and is the one seam both the live loop (`cycleRun.recoverBeforeReview`) and crash-resume (`reviewResumedDeliverable`) call. `writeAuthority` has three values: a source writer (`worktreePhase`) authors every path; the scout authors only the evals its lane owns; every other phase authors nothing. A phase that does not author a path relocates it only when it is an untracked non-deliverable; a deliverable or a tracked edit it did not author is left for the tree-diff guard.
  - **The `-uall` listing.** Untracked files come one per line, so `os.Rename` never meets a directory collision, and a bare directory entry (a nested worktree) is skipped through `isLegitimateMainTreePath`.
  - **A worktree copy is never overwritten, and a differing main-tree copy is never lost.** When the worktree already holds a file at a leaked untracked path, the worktree's copy wins. A main-tree copy with the same bytes is dropped. One that differs, even at the same length, is moved to `quarantineDir(projectRoot, cycle)` (`.evolve/quarantine/cycle-<N>/<path>`, the home `ensureCleanWorktree` already uses), under `uniqueQuarantinePath`, so a second copy never overwrites the first. The move logs a WARN that names the quarantine path. A compare that cannot read both copies fails recovery and keeps the main copy. Pinned by `TestRecoverBeforeReview_ADifferingMainTreeCopyIsQuarantinedNotDeleted` and `TestRecoverBeforeReview_AnIdenticalMainTreeCopyIsDroppedWithoutQuarantine`. This is the untracked twin of the tracked-edit rule (a diverged worktree edit is authoritative). Before this rule, a lane's own scout copy surfacing in a later phase replaced the eval TDD had written. Also pinned by `TestRecoverBeforeReview_OwnEvalLeakNeverOverwritesTheWorktreeCopy`.
  - **Fail, never report clean.** Recovery reads the main tree through `withSnapshotRetries` (`readMainTreeStatus`). A read that still fails after the retries returns false and fails the phase, as does a leak that was detected and could not be recovered safely. The final `git add -f` stages only the relocated paths: `-f` because a relocated path may be gitignored in the worktree, and a failed stage returns false because relocated-but-unstaged work would be invisible to the auditor.
- **Main-tree ownership** (`leak_ownership.go`). N fleet lanes share one main tree, so "absent from my baseline" no longer means "mine". `mainTreeOwnership` is the one Specification that answers "may this lane claim this main-tree path?" Five owner rules key a path to its owner:
  - `.evolve/evals/<slug>.md` to the item `<slug>`;
  - `go/acs/cycle<N>/…` (through `acssuite.AncestorCyclePackages`) and `docs/explain/builds/cycle-<N>-<run>.md` (through `explanationdocs.IsCycleChangeRecord`) to cycle N;
  - a verified active mint to the mint registry;
  - the dossier `knowledge-base/cycles/cycle-<N>.json` or `.md` to the dossier of cycle N (2026-10-17).

  A lane holds three things:
  - the items that `committedset.Committed` binds it to (the lane pin, else the top_n of triage, minus deferrals);
  - the slugs that it materialized in its workspace eval home;
  - its own cycle number.

  With no pin, no decision and no materialized eval, and with no cycle number, the lane holds everything, which is the single-writer rule.
  - **Recovery reads `foreignOwner`, whatever the path's git status, and fails closed when nobody live holds the owner.** A path keyed to an owner the lane does not hold is never claimed.
    - When a live sibling or the mint registry holds the owner (`heldBySibling`, with the sibling set loaded only on the first foreign path), the path stays in the main tree with one line naming the owner (`left <path> in the main tree — it belongs to <owner>, not this lane`).
    - The dossier of another real cycle has an owner: the ship of that cycle wrote it, and that cycle can be sealed. A cycle is real when its run directory exists, live or closed. The guard does not charge such a dossier, and recovery leaves it in the main tree. A dossier of a cycle with no run directory, or of the cycle of this lane, stays a leak. A sibling ship in the rolling pool writes its dossier while this lane runs (`TestFilterRealLeaks_AnotherCyclesDossierIsNotThisLanesLeakButItsOwnIs`, `TestRecoverBeforeReview_AShippedCyclesDossierStaysInTheMainTreeAndIsNoLeak`, `TestRecoverBeforeReview_ThisCyclesOwnDossierInTheMainTreeIsStillALeak`).
    - When nobody live holds it, recovery fails the phase with a WARN naming the path and owner. Each path needs its own holder, and an expired sibling lease holds nothing. Recovery runs on both entry points, so this holds on crash-resume too, which has no tree-diff guard. Pinned by `TestRecoverBeforeReview_AKeyedPathNoLiveLaneHoldsFailsRecovery`, `TestRecoveryFailsAPhaseThatLeftAnEvalNoLiveLaneHolds` and `TestRunCycleFromPhase_AnEditToAnUnownedCommittedEvalFailsTheResume`.
    - A sibling's staged deletion, add or edit of its own committed eval is left exactly as staged (`TestRecoverBeforeReview_SiblingsStagedChangesToItsCommittedEvalAreLeft`). The rule covers a new file and an edit to a committed one alike, because a sibling re-materializing a committed eval is the same theft as copying a new one. The check runs before the status switch, and build artifacts are never keyed.
    - The trade-off: when such an edit is this lane's own leak of legitimate work, it is left too. Across 186 cycle commits since 2026-08-01, 6 edited a committed past-cycle predicate package and 5 edited a committed eval, all in their worktrees. Only a leak of such an edit into the main tree reaches the rule. If no live sibling holds the owner, the guard then charges the phase: attribution is impossible, so recovery fails closed.
  - **Recovery and the guard read one exemption set, `leakExemptions`.** It is built by `mainTreeOwnership.exemptions` from the lane's ownership and the cycle-start console lease, and carried to recovery in `phaseLeakScope`.
    - A keyed path the lane does not hold is exempt only when a live sibling run holds its owner (`liveSiblingsOf`: `runlease.LiveRuns` over the run root, minus this lane's own workspace) or it is a registered mint.
    - A console-leased path is exempt. Recovery leaves it with one line, and the guard waives it with its WARN.
    - A key nobody live holds is still charged to the phase, so the guard is never weaker than before.
    - An unkeyed path, such as a source file, can have an owner too. The owner is a live sibling of the same goal that names the exact file in its scope, when this lane does not hold it. This closed the inbox item `treediff-guard-worktree-phase-crosslane-residual` (2026-10-20).
    - Only an exact-file entry counts. A directory entry, such as `go/internal/core`, holds nothing. Real items declare directories often, and a prefix match excused this lane's own leaks under them (independent review of PR #1037).
    - A sibling counts only when its lane pin has the goal hash of this lane's pin, the sibling rule of `SiblingLaneFiles`. A lane pin without a goal hash gives no exemption.
    - This lane holds a path that its own declared scope or a closure rule covers. `liveSiblings.declaresAgainst` asks `scopedelta.Classify` with `DefaultClosureRules`, so the scope rule has one home.
    - The guard does not charge such a path. It WARNs with the path and the holder (`excused: the declared scope of cycle N holds it`). Recovery leaves the path in the main tree with one line. The guard of the sibling charges it.
    - The trade-off: the sibling's file can be a leak of this lane. Then the sibling's guard charges it only inside its own phase window.
    - A lane with an unreadable or empty scope holds every unkeyed path. A sibling with an unreadable scope holds none. A WARN names each unreadable scope, so an unknown scope never makes the guard weaker.
    - A protected surface is never exempt, and with no protected-surface rule (`WithProtectedSurface`) no unkeyed path is exempt. The production orchestrator always has the rule.
    - Pinned by `TestFilterRealLeaks_OnlyAFileALiveSameGoalSiblingNamesIsNotThisLanesLeak`, `TestFilterRealLeaks_ALaneWithoutAKnownScopeOrSurfaceIsChargedForEveryUnkeyedPath`, `TestRecoverPath_LeavesAPathAnotherLaneHoldsAndNamesTheHolder`, `TestRecoverBeforeReview_ASourceALiveSiblingDeclaresStaysInTheMainTree`, `TestRecoverBeforeReview_ASiblingsDirectoryEntryDoesNotHoldThisLanesLeak` and `TestGuard_APathALiveSiblingDeclaresDoesNotAbortThisLanesPhase`.
    - The registry is one of the owner rules, so `filterRealLeaks` has no second copy of the mint check.
    - Crash-resume adopts no console lease, so a leased keyed path no live lane holds fails a resume.
    - Pinned by `TestRecoverBeforeReview_AConsoleLeasedPathIsLeftJustAsTheGuardWaivesIt`.
  - **The eval layout has one home.** `EvalFilePath(root, slug)` builds `<root>/.evolve/evals/<slug>.md`; recovery, ownership and `evalgate` all use it.
- **The recovery baseline is captured per phase, and the dispatch carries it.** `cycleRun.snapshotMainTree` takes one `git status --porcelain -uall` per dispatch, through the guard's `gitDirtyPaths` seam, into `dispatchResult.beforeDirty`. Both the recovery (`cycleRun.recoveryBaselineFor`) and the tree-diff guard read that one set, so they cannot disagree. The snapshot is taken at every phase dispatch, at the remediation fix dispatch, and at the gate's re-run after a fix; crash-resume takes one before every resumed dispatch through the same function.
  - **A failed snapshot is retried, and a phase never runs without one.**
    - Every main-tree read goes through `withSnapshotRetries`: the guard's pre- and post-phase reads (`retryingDirtyPaths`) and recovery's own `git status` (`readMainTreeStatus`).
      - It makes 3 attempts, with a 100 ms then 200 ms backoff through `backoffSleep`, and stops at once when the context is cancelled. The backoff is short because a transient `git status` failure (an index refresh racing a writer) clears in milliseconds, and every phase dispatch waits on it.
      - Recovery that still cannot read fails the phase instead of reporting the tree clean.
      - The guard aborts the phase when its post-phase read, or its re-check after the binary discard, still fails (`abortUncheckedPhase`).
      - The post-phase check runs before the ledger append, so a leak or an unreadable tree aborts the cycle without recording the phase as a success.
    - `defaultGitDirtyPaths` returns git's error instead of an empty set.
    - When every attempt fails in a git checkout, `snapshotMainTree` returns `errPrePhaseSnapshot`, and the phase is not run. A fresh dispatch aborts the cycle with that error. The remediation fix dispatch and the gate's re-run void their round, and the gate's FAIL stands. A resumed dispatch fails the resume (`cycleRun.snapshotThenDispatch`).
    - Only a project root with no `.git` at all (`plane.Classify` reports not-exist) gets no guard and no recovery, because there is no main tree to protect. A `.git` that exists but cannot be read is a checkout whose snapshot failed. The decision is taken once per cycle, at the first snapshot (`cycleRun.mainTreeIsCheckout`, shared across a resume's phases through `resumeExecution.checkoutDecision`), so a phase that deletes `.git` cannot turn the guard off for later phases (`TestSnapshotMainTree_ADotGitRemovedMidCycleDoesNotTurnTheGuardOff`, `TestRunCycleFromPhase_ADotGitRemovedBetweenResumedPhasesDoesNotTurnTheGuardOff`).
    - The earlier rule, which degraded to "guard and recovery skipped", let a leak made during a transient failure ship silently.
    - Pinned by `TestRunCycle_ATransientPrePhaseSnapshotFailureIsRetriedAndTheLeakRecovered`, `TestRunCycle_APrePhaseSnapshotThatKeepsFailingStopsBeforeThePhaseRuns`, `TestRunCycleFromPhase_APrePhaseSnapshotThatKeepsFailingStopsBeforeThePhaseRuns`, `TestRecoverBeforeReview_AFailedSnapshotNeverRecoversAgainstAnEmptyBaseline`, `TestDefaultGitDirtyPaths_ReportsAFailedGitStatus`, `TestCycleRun_RecoveryBaselineNeedsASnapshotOfACheckout` and `TestRecoverPhaseLeak_SkipsAPhaseWithNoPrePhaseSnapshot`.
  - **The remediation round's observer spans the whole run.** `cycleRun.snapshotThenRun` takes the snapshot, then `observedRun` starts the observer, runs the phase and cancels the observer only after the run returns. Pinned by `TestObservedRun_TheObserverWatchesTheWholeRun` and `TestMaybeRemediate_TheGateReRunsOwnLeakIsRecovered`. A path dirty when the phase starts is never that phase's leak. The trade-off: a later phase no longer sweeps an earlier phase's residue. After this change that residue is only state that must not move (`.evolve/` runtime state, mints, console-leased paths) or what a phase leaves when its own guard snapshot degraded.
- **Carry-forward filter** (`carryforward_filter.go`). A deterministic, zero-LLM screen. `CarryforwardCandidateLandable` runs an is-ancestor and `git cherry` patch-id supersession screen, then a real 3-way `git merge-tree --write-tree` dry run, which writes only to the object DB. `ClassifyFleetRebaseCandidate` splits a not-landable answer into already-landed and conflict for ship recovery's fleet-rebase pre-screen (`recoverFromShipError`); `evolve branches audit|prune` uses the same screen. All git goes through the `gitRunner` seam, which `carryforward_filter_test.go` scripts per subcommand (`seamGit`).
- **The fleet rebase with derived regeneration** (`ship_recovery.go`, [ADR-0049](../adr/0049-concurrent-multi-cycle-execution.md)). `rebaseCycleBranchOntoMain` rebases the cycle branch onto `main` after a peer moved `main`.
  - It never rebases the project root, and an empty worktree does not rebase.
  - The result is `ok` for a clean replay. It is also `ok` when each conflict is a derived conflict of the `internal/derived` catalog. Then the rebase regenerates each fired entry once, with the generator of the worktree.
  - The re-audit binds the regenerated tree, so the tree binding of ship still holds.
  - A conflict on any other path returns those paths to the debugger. An infra failure or a failed regeneration returns no paths.
  - The abort runs under a detached context with a 30 s limit. Thus a cancelled cycle context cannot leave the worktree half rebased.
  - A replayed commit that becomes empty after the regeneration is skipped.
  - `rebase --continue` runs with `core.editor=true`, so it keeps the replayed message and does not wait for an editor.
  - A `--continue` that fails means that a later commit conflicts. The loop classifies it again, up to `maxRebaseContinueSteps`.
  - `unmergedRebasePaths` reads `git diff --name-only --diff-filter=U -z`. Thus each path has the same bytes as the paths that the worktree fence reports.
- **The post-build normalizer** (`normalizeDerivedProjections`, `phase_bindings.go`). It runs only in the build iteration of `recordAndBranch`, before the audit binds the tree.
  - It checks each catalog entry whose inputs the cycle changed. It regenerates the entry when the check fails, and stages the outputs of the entry.
  - The audit iteration then binds the staged outputs with `worktreeContentSHA`. Thus the committed tree is the tree that the audit bound.
  - A failure only gives a WARN. The docs and skills gates stay the backstop.
- **Superseded-branch prune** (`prune_superseded_orphans.go`). `PruneSupersededOrphans` keeps a superseded `cycle-*` ref, before any PR check or `git branch -D`, when it is checked out in any worktree (`KeptCheckedOut`, exact `branch refs/heads/<name>` match from `git worktree list --porcelain`) or named by a continuation-registry binding (`KeptBound`); a delete git still refuses is `KeptDeleteFailed` on that ref and the walk continues. The worktree list and the registry are loaded before the first ref, and either failing aborts the walk before any delete, since an undecidable keep must never become a delete. Pinned by the `TestPruneSupersededOrphans_*` tests and the real-git `TestBranchesPrune*` CLI tests.
- **Ship is a pure executor; recovery is the orchestrator's job.** Ship never rejects a cycle itself — it returns a structured `ShipError`, and `recoverFromShipError` (`ship_recovery.go`) records it for forensics, then asks the routing strategy's `Recover()` (Strategy + Chain-of-Responsibility) for the next phase. Recovery is structural — always available via `StaticPreset.Recover` — so it runs regardless of the dynamic-routing stage; it is error handling, not routing. It aborts loud when the recovery depth is exhausted, when the strategy routes to end (an integrity breach or an unmapped error), or when the candidate ship→X edge is illegal.
- **Composition carry-forward** (`composition_carryforward.go`, `composition_scoped_review.go`). Core's half of RUNG 0 (trivial rebase) and RUNG 2 (scoped merge review). `internal/adapters/ledger` imports core, so the snapshot, the composed-tree gate run, the verdict writer and the scoped reviewer are `Option`-injected closures that `cmd/evolve` binds; `CompositionVerdictInput` and `compositionPatchID` mirror the ledger's types. `CompositionFastPathWired` and `ScopedMergeReviewWired` let the composition root prove its wiring in a real test (`cmd/evolve/cmd_cycle_composition_test.go`). `IntersectingHunks` builds the reviewer payload on the same old-side overlap test as the wired `RunScopedMergeReview`. `identityCarryForward` (`identity_carry_forward.go`, ADR-0105 B3) is the carry for a contract cycle: after `routeRebasedExplanation` rebinds a byte-identical rebase it proves the pended tree against the audited tree with `treedelta`, runs the composed gates under the fence, writes the `identical-rebase` record to the root ledger and returns Ship; any doubt returns Audit. Every rung writes through the project ledger, `<projectRoot>/.evolve/ledger.jsonl`, and the ledger writer keeps the diffs in the evidence store beside it; no rung chooses where its evidence lives. Until 2026-10-06 the identity carry kept its diffs in the cycle worktree, which is deleted when the cycle ends, and RUNG 0/2 wrote through `<worktree>/.evolve/ledger.jsonl`, a symlink to the project ledger (`linkGuardDeps`) whose tip, lock and evidence store were the worktree's own ([incident](../../incidents/2026-10-06-carry-refused-ledger-verify-f5.md)). Pinned by the core_test `TestCarryRecord_VerifiesAfterItsCycleWorktreeIsRemoved` (the real ledger writer through the real identity carry) and `TestCompositionCarry_ChainsFromTheProjectLedgersTipThroughALinkedWorktree` (both rungs, through a worktree linked as production links it); `identity_carry_export_test.go` exposes the two harnesses to those external tests (`CarryAPendedIdenticalLaneForTesting`, and `RungsOnALinkedLedgerForTesting`, which returns each rung as a named field rather than taking a flag), since core's own tests cannot import the ledger adapter. `carriedAudit` picks the run's auditor row and requires the base its worktree stood on to be the base the change was authored on (see Invariants). Pinned by `TestRouteRebasedExplanation_AByteIdenticalRebaseShipsOnTheCarriedVerdict`, `TestRouteRebasedExplanation_AMainThatMovedBeforeTheAuditStillCarries`, the nine declines of `TestRouteRebasedExplanation_ACarryThatCannotBeProvenReturnsToAudit` and `TestCarriedAudit_ARowNamingNoBaseNeverCarriesEvenOnAnEmptyBase`. All three `MissingComposedGates` trip sites (RUNG 0, RUNG 2, the identity carry) decline through `declineComposedGates`: one `ORCHESTRATOR_COMPOSED_GATE_DECLINED` event naming the cycle and the failing gates in `Reason`, each failing gate's output tail in its own `tail_<gate>` field, and one stderr line tagged with the cycle and the code. The injected runner returns per-gate `ciparity.GateOutcome` tails, but the protected `compositionGateRunner` field stays status-only, so `runGateSet` collects the tails through a per-call context sink. `boundedTail` trims each tail from its front, so it fits the Signal Center's 512-rune value bound (which keeps the front) and its share of `MaxLineBytes`, measured after JSON escaping. Pinned by `composed_gate_decline_test.go` and `composed_gate_tail_bound_test.go`.
  - Since 2026-10-21, RUNG 0 is retired (see the ship recovery bullets). Thus only RUNG 2 and the identity carry remain as trip sites of `MissingComposedGates`.
  - Ship re-proves only `identical-rebase` records (`carry.go`). Thus a RUNG 2 `scoped-review` record does not carry at ship. RUNG 2 has no production reviewer, and stage 7 of the landing-queue plan retires it.
- **Carryover seam** (`carryover_lifecycle.go`, [03-carryover-lifecycle.md](../decomposition/03-carryover-lifecycle.md)). An `Orchestrator` built as a literal gets its lifecycle lazily. `wiredCarryover` reads the Signal Center live, because tests apply `WithSignalCenter` after construction. The exported package-level facades run on a Null-Object lifecycle for tests and ACS predicates and carry `Deprecated:` notes naming the wired path.
- **Console lease** (`console_lease.go`, [ADR-0080](../adr/0080-runtime-console-plane-separation.md) S4). An exact-path, expiring operator waiver for the tree-diff guard, read from the git common dir and adopted once at cycle start; the cycle log records the digest of the adopted bytes.
- **Continuation-on-fail** ([ADR-0076](../adr/0076-convergence-architecture.md) C). At the preserve decision a FAILed cycle's dirty worktree is snapshot-committed onto its cycle branch, and a manifest is stamped into the workspace, but only when the carry-forward screen reads the snapshot Clean against main. The manifest is also registered under the lane's `lane-scope.json` todo ids, so a wave-planner lane with no inbox claim still leaves a binding; adoption resolves claims first and lane scope second. The adopting cycle provisions from the snapshot commit (work inherits through git history, never dirty state), keeps the original base as its review base so the cumulative work is reviewed whole, and serves the prior findings to Build. `advanceContinuationBase` then merges the pinned main tip while the merge is clean. `prepareAdoptedTree` then archives the ancestor's two kinds of cycle-owned artifacts under one retention rule (design A2): its unpublished explanation records and its own predicate package (`go/acs/cycle<M>`, absent at the base), both into the dated `docs/private/research/archived-*/` archive. Before 2026-09-30 the predicate package stayed in the tree, so the build floor's added-test self-check ran the ancestor's contract: cycle 1764 failed on 1761's allow-list fence, which diffed against 1761's own base and counted every file main had gained since. An invalid continuation falls back to fresh provisioning, loudly. A declined binding is released at the decline site ([ADR-0085](../adr/0085-continuation-registry-release.md)).
- **A retracting phase runs alone** ([ADR-0149](../adr/0149-a-test-writing-phase-that-states-fail-on-its-own-work-is-retracted-to-its-dispatch-tree.md) D6). `batchArchetype` gives a `retract_on_fail` phase no archetype, so `evaluateBatch` never runs it beside a read-only phase. The fence of that phase can restore a retracted test. Pinned by `TestEvaluateBatchAt_ARetractOnFailPhaseNeverRunsBesideAnotherPhase`.
- **The repair directive of a continuation** ([failure-rate review §4.4](../../incidents/2026-08-17-failure-rate-review-1481-1503.md)). After the archive, `continuationRepairFirst` asks `continuation.RepairFirst` for the reds of the prior try that are still in the tree. The directive goes under `CtxKeyContinuationRepairFirst`, and Build renders it first. An unreadable verdict is a WARN, and the descriptive findings still go to Build. Pinned by `TestRunCycle_AContinuationWithAnInheritedRedLeadsWithTheRepairDirective` and `TestRunCycle_AContinuationWithoutARedCarriesNoRepairDirective`.
- **Two signals for the optimistic admission record** ([ADR-0143](../adr/0143-admission-holds-only-shared-edits-and-the-ship-gate-owns-api-drift.md) W1b). `ORCHESTRATOR_COMPOSED_GATE_DECLINED` carries `fields.gates`, the failed composed-tree gates. A fleet rebase conflict in `recoverFromShipError` publishes `ORCHESTRATOR_FLEET_REBASE_CONFLICT` (a `ship.warning` with `fields.paths`). It is not a landing-conflict ship error, so the oversight does not count the conflict twice. Pinned by `TestRecoverFromShipError_AFleetRebaseConflictPublishesItsPaths` and the decline tests.
- **`ChangedWorktreePaths`** is the exported projection of the host reviewer's changed-path derivation, so `evolve phase verify build` cannot drift from it ([ADR-0077](../adr/0077-docs-floor-for-architecture-changes.md)).
- **`WorktreeEvolveInvocation`** gives the one invocation that runs a worktree's own `evolve` from its source: `go run ./cmd/evolve <args>` in `<worktree>/go`, with `EVOLVE_WORKTREE_ROOT=<worktree>` after the inherited environment. `regenerateDerivedArtifact` and the audit skills-drift gate use it, so a lane's generator change is regenerated and graded by the lane's own code. Pinned by `TestWorktreeEvolveInvocation_RunsTheWorktreesOwnEvolveFromItsSource`.
- **`ChangedWorktreePathsSinceBase`** lists the paths changed since the cycle base, committed work included, plus untracked files. Without a base it diffs HEAD. The build-floor reviewer (`changedFloorPaths`) and the audit skills-drift gate use it. Pinned by `TestChangedWorktreePathsSinceBase_SeesCommittedWorkFromTheBase`.
- **The debugger's signal-branch gate is keyed on `successorStrategy`, not the phase name** (ADR-0058). The debugger is a control phase with no registry home, so `builtinControlSpec` supplies its default `branching_strategy: signal`, overlaid by `specFor` with registry precedence. A registry `debugger` entry overriding to `verdict` branching skips the entire signal branch, so no successor is scheduled for any decision signal, including `BLOCK`.
- **`successorStrategy` gates the retro history branch the same way it gates the debugger's signal branch: config-driven, never name-keyed.** `recordAndBranch`/resume take the failure-adapter history branch only when the phase's `branching_strategy` resolves to `history`; an unset catalog, or a `retrospective` entry that omits the field, degrades to the literal default (retro→history, every other phase→verdict/`""`) — byte-identical to the pre-config flow.
