A registry override to verdict branching makes both the live loop and resume skip the branch entirely (`RetroDecision` stays empty, no successor scheduled), proving the gate consults the spec rather than a literal `current == PhaseRetro` check; resume's history-branch entry condition stays lockstep with the fresh loop's. Pinned by `TestSuccessorStrategy`, `TestRecordAndBranch_RetroGateIsStrategyKeyed`, `TestRecordAndBranch_RetroDegradesToHistoryWhenUnconfigured`, `TestResume_RetroDegradesToHistoryWhenUnconfigured` and `TestResume_RetroGateIsStrategyKeyed`.
- **Persisted shapes are additive-only** (`bytestability_test.go`). The legacy JSON key sets of `State`, `LedgerEntry` and `CycleState` never change; a new field is `omitempty` and appended to its additive list, so an older file keeps its bytes.
- **`RealInboxForTest`** holds the real inboxmover functions. `continuation_adopt_inbox_test.go` (package `core_test`) injects them from `init`, because an in-package inboxmover import is a cycle while `core_test → inboxmover → adapters/ledger → core` is not.
- **Contract-block CLI escalation** (`contract_escalation.go`). A correction ladder that keeps re-dispatching the same CLI after a deliverable-contract block can burn every correction and trip the contract-gate breaker, silently demoting it from enforce to advisory for the rest of the run. Escalation retries the re-dispatch — never the phase itself — on a different CLI family: a soft overlay (`llmroute.ApplySoftOverlay`) promotes the target ahead of the profile's own chain for that one re-dispatch and reverts when the ladder ends; the profile on disk is never touched. It triggers at the contract gate's own second consecutive block (`ReviewResult.Blocks`, the breaker's own counter, never a locally re-counted ordinal that could desync from it) or a second identical remediation-carrying rejection (`ReviewResult.Remediation`, set only by evalgate); a remediation-less zero-block rejection (a capacity or task-binding failure) never escalates.
- **Escalation picks from the router's decision** (2026-10-06, L1b of the CLI routing table, [ADR-0119](../adr/0119-one-routing-table-one-resolver.md)). `routingDecision` resolves the phase's (agent, phase) through the CLI router the composition root injects with `WithCLIRouter` (`CLIRouterWired` reports it; pinned by `TestOrchestrator_CLIRouterWiredReportsTheInjectedRouter`), so the dispatched CLI (`contractDispatchCLI`) and the escalation candidates (`contractEscalationCLI`) come from the chain the runner walks, probe, bench and universal tail included. The first candidate of a different family that `Decision.Allows` (the decision's allowed families) wins. The universal claude fallback is appended only to a legacy decision, so a declared table is never left. Without an injected router, a one-profile legacy router over the phase's own profile (`cliroute.SingleProfile`) keeps the old behaviour. A routing refusal is a stderr line and no escalation, never a crash. Pinned by `TestContractEscalation_PicksFromTheTable` and `TestContractEscalation_NeverLeavesTheAllowedSet`. (Before L1b, escalation called `llmroute.Resolve` itself and checked each candidate with `policy.ValidatePin` against the profile's `allowed_clis`.)
- **Only an identical contract block escalates.** Two blocks are the same defect when their `deliverable.Violation.Code` sets intersect — covering subset, superset and equal — computed from the parsed violation codes rather than the whole rendered reason text, because a whole-string compare reads a partially repaired defect set as a different defect and suppresses escalation exactly where an incapable CLI's signature is strongest. When either block yields no codes, identity falls back to `failure_digest.go`'s `normalizeReasonForFingerprint`; the zero-value "no prior block observed" case reports identity=true so a cycle that aborted mid-ladder (leaving the process-global breaker hot) still gets an escalation on the next ladder's first block.
- **A phase whose whole dispatch chain is one CLI family gets a structured re-prompt instead of nothing.** `composeContractSalvageRetry` re-dispatches the same CLI, same round, same budget, changing only the directive: it carries the contract validator's output verbatim under a distinct heading instead of a paraphrasable rejection. It is breaker-neutral (no extra dispatch, no extra correction, no `ModelRoutingCLI` change) and disjoint from escalation, so one block's budget is never spent twice.
- **`contractArtifactDetermined` is an allowlist, fail-closed classifier**, not a denylist: a violation code it has never seen has unknown repair mechanics, and the safe answer is false (skip the hash short-circuit). It has no production caller yet — it exists so a future hash-equality short-circuit has this precondition ready; building that short-circuit alongside it would be scope creep.
- **A contract-gate demotion stages an autofile intent through `dispositionrouter` rather than writing `.evolve/inbox` directly**, because a mid-flight inbox write can race `inboxmover.Claim`'s rename and resurrect a claimed item into double work across fleet lanes; `recurrence.ApplyBoundary` is the only sanctioned inbox writer. `ContractGateDemotion{Phase, CLI, Cycle, Blocks, Weight, Reason}.Intent()` is the pure builder of that intent (a parameter object since the engineering-craft audit, 2026-10-06; it took six positional parameters): an autofile to the console, `contract-gate-demoted-<phase>`, with `priority_class: correctness` (a gate that stopped judging deliverables; plan P2 prerequisite, 2026-10-06, [ADR-0121](../adr/0121-inbox-priority-is-a-computed-rank.md)). It is exported so cmd/evolve's `TestInboxWriters_EveryAutofiledItemCarriesAKnownClass` stages the real stager's intent through `recurrence.ApplyBoundary`. Its `Pattern` (`contract gate demoted enforce->advisory on phase <phase> (cli=<cli>)`) is both the recurrence identity and the filed item's title stem, since `recurrence.applyIntent` renders `recurring failure <pattern> (<n> occurrences)`, so it is phrased as a defect, not a bare label (this was a code comment until P2; `TestContractGateDemotion_IntentIsACorrectnessClassedConsoleAutofile`).
- **Raw tool output is kept only on fail** (`pruneToolOutputOnPass`, `tool_output_retention.go`, called by `completeCycle` after the dossier). At the seal of a PASS cycle it deletes the files that `gcpolicy.ToolOutputFiles` names (`ship-repocontract-scan.log`, `integration-tier.log`) from the run dir. Any other verdict keeps them for forensics. A delete failure is a WARN, and the cycle stays sealed.
- **The closeout writes the shadow retrospectives** (`writeShadowRetrospectives`, `retrospective_shadow.go`, called by `completeCycle` after the dossier). It passes the project root, the workspace, the cycle and the carryover todos of the state to `recoveryretro.WriteShadow`. At stage `shadow` that writes only into `<workspace>/retrospectives/`. An error is one WARN line, and the cycle stays sealed. Spec: [recovery-retrospective.md](../recovery-retrospective.md) §5. Pinned by `TestRunCycle_TheCloseoutWritesTheShadowRetrospectiveIntoTheWorkspace` and `TestWriteShadowRetrospectives_AFailureIsAWarnLineOnly`.
  - `archivePollutedWorkspace` names its archive with `gcpolicy.PollutedArchiveName`, so gc discovery takes the archive as a dead run.
  - Pinned by `TestCompleteCycle_APassSealDeletesTheRawToolOutputAndKeepsTheRest`, `TestCompleteCycle_ASealThatIsNotPassKeepsTheRawToolOutput` and `TestArchivePollutedWorkspace_MintsANameThatGCDiscoveryAccepts`. The plan is [logging-and-process-hygiene-2026-10.md](../../plans/logging-and-process-hygiene-2026-10.md).
- **Chronicle digest seeding** (`seedChronicleDigest`, called once per cycle from `planCycle`; the resolved `policy.ChronicleConfig` is injected at the composition root via `WithChronicleConfig`, and the zero-option default is `shadow`). Three modes: `off` writes and injects nothing; `shadow` assembles `DigestInput` from the committed dossier history and live failure state and writes `recent-outcomes.md` into the run workspace but does not inject `Context["recent_outcomes"]`; `enforce` does the same write and additionally carries the rendered bytes into every phase request so scout/triage render it. Best-effort throughout: a digest failure WARNs and the cycle proceeds.
- **Fleet scope resolution** (`planCycle`). `Context["fleet_scope"]` is authoritative from `<workspace>/lane-scope.json` when a supervisor or a prior attempt of this orchestrator provisioned one, over the env snapshot, so cross-lane env drift can no longer split lane identity; an absent file falls back to the env snapshot (sequential-loop byte-identical), and an env-scoped run pins its own `lane-scope.json` before any phase runs. `fleet_scope_paths` then discloses each scoped id's live inbox record path beside the id list — only PENDING ids resolve, so a consumed or non-inbox id is silently omitted rather than pointing at stale evidence.
- **`finalizeCycle`'s floor stack** runs in a fixed order after `finalizeOutcome`: the cross-artifact invariant stack (four weak deterministic verifiers over the cycle's own artifacts, purely advisory, recorded beside the verdict-coherence floor's decision without ever influencing it), the verdict-coherence floor itself (a negative verdict whose on-disk artifacts are green means the pipeline forged the verdict, a SYSTEM-level failure that halts the batch — unless a fully green, fully-valid audit report shows the mismatch was only a benign clean-exit-late-write race, which self-heals to PASS instead), the lost-landing floor (a PASS verdict attached to a cycle whose ship hit landing-queue contention and correctly routed elsewhere, keyed on the cycle's own ship artifacts rather than a HEAD delta, which in fleet mode belongs to whichever sibling landed last), and the silent-no-ship warning (phases ran but this cycle's own ship never landed and no audit-advisory block was recorded, so any worktree changes were discarded).
- **Graduated remediation** (`maybeRemediate`, between `reviewAndGuard` and `recordAndBranch`). On a FAIL verdict from a remediable, deterministic gate with rounds remaining, the builder is dispatched once per round with the gate's own report as the correction directive, then the same gate re-runs and its fresh verdict replaces the original — remediation re-earns a verdict, it never overrides one, so every downstream phase runs unchanged after it. The round cap is hard (`workflow.remediation_rounds`, zero in core's zero-value config so untouched paths stay byte-identical) and only phases in `workflow.remediable_phases` participate; `remediationDenied` refuses judgment/control phases outright regardless of configuration, because granting a re-roll against an LLM-judgment verdict would be a gamed verdict, not a fix. `CycleResult.Remediations` records every round's outcome, so a remediated cycle is never a silent PASS. The economics: a mechanical, gate-prescribed defect (missing tests, format, naming) costs a bounded in-phase fix instead of discarding and re-running a whole sound cycle.
- **Re-plan rejection telemetry** (`recordPlanRejectionsKind`, `decision_branch.go`). `ValidatePlan` runs on the raw re-plan before the floor clamp, so an unknown phase the clamp would silently drop still leaves an attributable rejection record. Each plan kind (the upfront `plan` and each post-scout `replan-<n>`) writes its own `advisor-rejections[-<kind>].json`, so accumulation across re-plans needs no read-modify-write of a shared file. Pinned by `TestReplan_UnknownPhase_RecordsRejection`, `TestReplan_KnownPhasesOnly_NoSpuriousRejection` and `TestReplan_MultipleReplans_AllRejectionsRecorded`.
- **Post-phase leak classification has two guard-only exemptions plus the console lease** (`cyclerun_postreview.go`, `cycleRun.leakExemptions`). `filterRealLeaks` exempts specific legitimate main-tree writes so the guard stays armed for real escapes:
  - `isLegitimateMainTreePath`: a phase's own `.evolve/` workspace writes and orchestrator gate state;
  - a keyed path another owner holds (`mainTreeOwnership.heldBySibling`): a live sibling lane, or the mint registry for a registered, TTL-fresh, content-verified mint. A registry read error disables the mint rule, fail-safe, and quarantines the registry through `activeVerifiedMints`. The scout has no exemption of its own: its eval home is its workspace, a copy it leaves in the main tree is relocated by recovery, and an eval nobody live holds is charged like any other deliverable. Source files and both sides of a leak-disguising rename still classify as real leaks. Pinned by `TestGuardIgnoresScoutEvalMaterialization`, `TestGuardStillChargesAnEvalNoLiveSiblingHolds`, `TestRunCycle_SiblingEvalWrittenDuringTDDIsNeitherChargedNorClaimed`, `TestMainTreeOwnership_HeldBySiblingNeedsALiveHolder`, `TestIsActiveMintPhasePath`, `TestVerifiedActiveMints`, `TestGuardRecoversCatalogWritesSourcePhaseLeak` and `TestGuardStillAbortsNonSourcePhaseLeak`.
- **`selectNext`'s spine-integrity re-check** (`cyclerun_select.go`). After the dynamic-routing override picks a candidate `next`, an unsatisfied spine re-digests once (the artifact may have landed between the routing digest and this check) before deciding fail-open vs fail-closed. The abort keys on `cfg.SpineFloor`, never the overloaded `PhaseRecovery` dial (which also carries a bidirectional channel and failure-adviser promotion). Only a clean absence (no digest error, nothing in `DigestDegraded`) at `SpineFloor==enforce` fails closed; a degraded read, a digest error, or any stage below enforce fails open with a WARN and a recorded `SpineFailOpen`. The operator escape is the existing `cfg.Mandatory` set — removing an anchor from it, not a new waiver.
- **The linear transition spine is a data table (`spineOrder`), walked by `spineNext`, instead of a per-phase switch in `Next`.** `spineOrder` is a config-independent trust anchor like the legality graph: config selects among legal edges (`on_pass`/`on_fail`), it cannot move the spine itself. `core.SpineFailOpen` is a re-export of the `cyclestate` leaf's own type, never a parallel struct, so core's records and the dossier's projection cannot drift apart. Pinned by `TestSpineNext_WalksCanonicalSpine`, `TestSpineNext_InjectedConfigSpine`, `TestSpineFailOpenAlias_IsTheCyclestateRecord` and the full byte-identity oracle `TestTransitionKernelOracle_Next`.
- **`UnsatisfiedSpineAnchor` is the exact complement of `SpineSatisfiedUpTo` and names the FIRST unsatisfied predecessor anchor**, so a fail-open telemetry record points at the real cause rather than the last anchor in the chain; a reporter that could disagree with the gate, or invent a missing anchor when the spine is satisfied, would make the telemetry lie, which is worse than no telemetry. `recordSpineFailOpen` accumulates one `SpineFailOpen{Phase, MissingArtifact, Reason}` per fail-open event onto `CycleResult.SpineFailOpens` — repeats must never collapse to one record, and an unrecorded, fully-satisfied cycle carries zero. Pinned by `TestUnsatisfiedSpineAnchor_NamesTheMissingPredecessor`, `TestUnsatisfiedSpineAnchor_AgreesWithSpineSatisfiedUpTo`, `TestRecordSpineFailOpen_CarriesPhaseArtifactAndReason` and `TestRecordSpineFailOpen_UnrecordedCycleHasNoFailOpens`.
- **A retro PASS is graded as the cycle's FAIL for floor and dossier purposes** (`decideAfterRetro`). Retro is reached only from an audit FAIL, and the retro persona is read-only outside its own artifacts, so the tree ship would commit is byte-identical to the one the auditor rejected — a retro PASS answers only "is the post-mortem complete", never "did the cycle recover". Substituting a FAIL verdict keeps every genuine FAIL/WARN path unchanged and stops a well-written retrospective from making a failed cycle read coherent to the verdict-incoherence floor. See [the retry architecture review](../retry-architecture-review-2026-08-27.md). Pinned by `TestDecideAfterRetro_RetroPASSIsNotRecovery`, `TestDecideAfterRetro_RetroPASSStillFacesTheFloor`, `TestDecideAfterRetro_RetroPASSReachesTheSameLadderAsFAIL`, `TestDecideAfterRetro_RetroFAILPathUnchanged` and `TestDecideAfterRetroRouted_RetroPASSCannotDropTheFloorSignal`.
- **`floorFailReason` is a forensic-only workspace artifact; the ADR-0072 coherence floor never reads it.** The workspace is agent-writable (the audit agent already writes `audit-report.md` there), so only `CycleState.AuditFailReasons`/`ShipFailReasons` — orchestrator memory, set at the verdict-record chokepoint (`recordFloorVerdictFailure`) — can mark a FAIL as diagnosed; a `<phase>-fail-reason.json` dropped by any workspace writer can never talk the floor out of halting. `persistFloorFailReasons` writes both carriers in lockstep and clobbers on every call (a superseding record with no error-severity diagnostics erases a prior explanation rather than letting it ratchet, since a stale explanation from a superseded attempt must never mark a later, differently-caused FAIL as diagnosed); warning-only diagnostics explain nothing and clear both carriers. `resetFloorFailReason` runs at every dispatch of a floor phase, so a re-dispatch (ship-error recovery re-audit, debugger `RERUN_PHASE`) never inherits a stale explanation — except it defers to whichever phase currently owns the shared audit-fail-reason file when another phase already claimed it. Pinned by `TestDetectVerdictIncoherence_WorkspaceReasonFileAlone_StillHalts`, `TestDetectVerdictIncoherence_WarningOnlyDiags_StillHalts`, `TestRecordFloorVerdictFailure_PersistsAuditFailReason` and `TestPersistFloorFailReasons_ClobberAndReset`.
- **`detectVerdictIncoherence` is the ADR-0072 deterministic floor for the verdict-incoherence category: a recorded FAIL/WARN whose on-disk audit + ACS artifacts are both green is a forged verdict and halts, regardless of orchestrator judgment or `strict_audit`.** Scope is deliberately narrow: a RED artifact is a coherent genuine task failure (never-stop); the "silent no-ship" (`CycleOutcomeSkippedUnknown`) shape is left to the orchestrator's judgment layer, since a benign no-op cycle can also produce it. Two shapes are NOT forgery and never halt: (1) a diagnosed downgrade — `hasSubstantiveFailReasons` (the one spelling of "diagnosed": `AuditFailReasons` or `ShipFailReasons` populated in orchestrator memory) feeds `SubstantiveError`; (2) a clean-exit-late-write race — the bridge can declare a phase's clean exit before its post-turn async writes land, so when the on-disk report passes the injected `ContractVerifier`'s full `Verify` chain (not the cheap sentinel parse `ReadCycleVerdicts` uses), the recorded verdict reconciles to PASS instead of halting; a PASS-sentinel-tagged but malformed report still fails `Verify` and still halts — the anti-laundering boundary. The verifier is injected (`WithContractVerifier`) because `core` cannot import `deliverable`; an unconfigured (nil) verifier leaves `DeliverableValid=false`, the pre-fix conservative halt — the self-heal is purely additive, never a launder. Pinned by `TestDetectVerdictIncoherence_ForgedVerdict_Halts`, `TestDetectVerdictIncoherence_SilentNoShip_DefersToOrchestrator`, `TestDetectVerdictIncoherence_GenuineFail_NoHalt`, `TestDetectVerdictIncoherence_ReconcileUsesFullVerify`, `TestDetectVerdictIncoherence_PassVerdict_NoHalt`, `TestDetectVerdictIncoherence_DiagnosedGateFail_NoHalt`, `TestDetectVerdictIncoherence_ShipPhaseExplainedFail_NoHalt` and `TestDetectVerdictIncoherence_AuditPhaseBehaviorUnchangedByShipField`.
- **`applyFailureDecisionFloor` is two independent gates, either of which halts.** (1) The deterministic failure-dossier candidate (`buildFailureDossier`) — a broken pipeline cannot dodge it. (2) The orchestrator's own `failure-decision.json` judgment, which can halt even when it proposes a retry (the cycle-1001 shape). Gate 2 is narrowed by one corroboration rule: a floor-category claim is dropped only when it is contradicted by BOTH the deterministic dossier (silent) AND the same agent's own `disposition.json` (`legit-rejection`), cross-checked against `failure-digest.json`'s fingerprint/cycle so a fabricated, stale or foreign-cycle disposition cannot buy a repair; an absent digest makes the identity unverifiable, which is not the same as verified-good, so it halts too. A prose floor claim is separately overruled when persisted evidence refutes it (`floorClaimRefutation`, one rule): a `verdict-incoherence` claim against a recorded FAIL that carries persisted substantive fail reasons (an audit- or ship-floor diagnosed downgrade is a justified negative verdict, not a forgery), and any floor claim when only static gate readings forced the FAIL over a PASS or WARN narrative (`staticGateReadingsForcedTheFail`: a conflict record, every other persisted audit fail reason a diagnosis from an `isStaticReading` row of `auditGateRemedies`) and no ship fail reason is recorded (a task-level FAIL with a named remedy, the cycle-1828 `infra-systemic` shape). A gate that executes code (go vet, acs-durable, the integration tier, apicover-enforce, EGPS) never refutes, because its offenders can be the host's own failure. The overrule is still logged so the missing halt is traceable. - **The disposition cross-check reads a harness-held digest record (2026-10-21).** `AssembleFailureDigest` writes the digest to the workspace and to `.evolve/failure-digests/cycle-<N>.json` (`failureDigestRecordPath`, `writeFailureDigestRecord`). No profile grants that directory.
  - For a workspace in the `.evolve/runs/cycle-<N>` layout, `crossCheckAgainstDigest` reads only that record. A retro that rewrites the copy in its workspace thus cannot make its disposition agree.
  - A missing record is an unverified identity. This replaces the read of `failure-digest.json` that the bullet above names ([gate-input-inventory.md](../gate-input-inventory.md) D6).
  - Pinned by `TestVerifyDisposition_ARetroThatRewritesTheWorkspaceDigestStillFails`, `TestVerifyDisposition_AMissingHarnessRecordIsUnverified`, `TestEnsureFailureDigest_WritesTheHarnessRecordOutsideTheWorkspace` and `TestAssembleFailureDigest_AnUnwritableHarnessRecordIsAnError`.
- **The retro completion records the failure class (2026-10-27).** `finalizeRetroCompletion` calls `recordFailureClass` (`failure_class.go`) after `VerifyDisposition` passes. For the layers `pipeline-code`, `harness` and `infra`, it writes the class fields to the harness digest record and to the workspace digest. The fields are `class_fingerprint`, `class_layer`, `class_recurrence`, `class_recorded_at`, `class_route` and `class_route_reason`. The route comes from `dispositionrouter.Decide`. An error is a WARN on the orchestrator stderr; the disposition gate result does not change.
  - A console route stages one autofile intent for the class (`stageClassEscalation`).
  - A later `AssembleFailureDigest` of the same exact fingerprint keeps the class fields (`withRecordedClass`), so the abnormal-exit epilogue does not erase them.
  - `loadResolvedFingerprintTimes` reads the ack ledger once for `LoadResolvedFingerprints` and for the class count.
  - The design is in [failure-identity-lifecycle.md §4](../failure-identity-lifecycle.md#4-failure-class-fingerprint).
  - Pinned by `TestFailureClass_TheFloorShortCircuitCyclesShareOneClassAndTheThirdRoutesToTheConsole` and `TestFinalizeRetroCompletion_AClassRecordErrorWarnsAndKeepsTheGate`.
- **`decideAfterRetroRouted` layers the router strictly above the deterministic floor and the bookkeeping regrade, never below.** The floor (`applyFailureDecisionFloor`) and a granted bookkeeping regrade are both decided before `o.strategy.Decide` runs, so neither can be overridden by a routed retry; `extraEnv` always comes from the deterministic adapter (`SetEnv` is kernel-owned), never the routed decision. A router-proposed branch illegal from `retro` (`CanTransition`) falls back to the deterministic branch, except a failure-scoped insert (fault-localization/bug-reproduction), which clamps to `tdd` instead — the state-machine clamp must never upgrade a proceed-to-end into a retry. Pinned by `TestDecideAfterRetro_EmitsRoutingDecisionArtifact`, `TestDecideAfterRetroRouted_PassArmIsAFailureBranch`, `TestDecideAfterRetro_InsertClampedToLegalRetry` and `TestDecideAfterRetro_ArbitraryIllegalPhaseClampsToBaseline`.
- **`recoveryTarget` resolves a control phase's recovery successor from its `RecoveryMap`** (`spec.Recovery.Targets[key]`), falling back to a literal default when the key, the spec or the catalog is absent. The chosen edge is still gated by `CanTransition` at the call site, so config selects the target and the legality graph constrains it. Pinned by `TestRecoveryTarget_ConfigDriven` and `TestRecoveryTarget_DegradesWithoutCatalog`.
- **Every routing/plan decision artifact is hash-bound into the ledger the same way**: marshal, write, sha256, append a `LedgerEntry` carrying `ArtifactPath`+`ArtifactSHA256`; a marshal/write/append failure WARNs and is swallowed, since forensics must never abort a cycle. `recordPhasePlanKind` additionally binds the advisor's own prompt/response capture files (`advisor-{prompt,response}-<kind>.txt`) when they exist, so a post-hoc mutation of a persisted routing prompt or response is detectable through the same hash chain; an absent capture (best-effort, or a pre-existing cycle) binds nothing. `recordPlanRejectionsKind` keys its artifact by plan kind (`advisor-rejections[-<kind>].json`) for the same reason `recordPhasePlanKind` keys `phase-<kind>.json`: several plans in one cycle (the upfront plan plus each post-scout re-plan) each need their own record instead of the last writer erasing the rest. `recordDebuggerDecision` binds the SHA of `debug-decision.json` rather than duplicating the action/root_cause payload into a field `LedgerEntry` does not have. `recordRoutingDecision` also appends one `phase_skipped` ledger entry per PSMAS-declined optional phase, preserving the resume/audit-binding contract those phases need. It is also where a registry gate becomes visible: for each clamp whose rule is `router.RuleSkipWhenGatesPlan` or `router.RuleInsertWhenGatesPlan`, `emitRegistryGatedPhases` raises one `ORCHESTRATOR_PLAN_PHASE_GATED` (WARN, kind `advisor.warning`, fields `phase`, `rule`, `next_phase`), because a planned phase the registry removed is the advisor's plan being overruled and belongs on the loop log, not only in `routing-decision-<n>.json`. Both routing roots (the walk and the post-retro branch) record through it. Pinned (code, kind, severity, module, cycle, run and fields) by `TestRecordRoutingDecision_ARegistryGatedPlannedPhaseEmitsACodedSignal` and `TestRecordRoutingDecision_AClampThatRemovesNoPlannedPhaseEmitsNoGateSignal`.
- **The advisor plans for the lane's item** (`advisor_lane_items.go`). `advisorPlanInput`, the one builder of the plan and re-plan input, sets `RouteInput.LaneItems` from the lane pin (`LaneScopeIDs`) and each id's live inbox record (`scopePathFor`, `inboxbatch.LoadFile`): id, kind, acceptance and the deliverable kind. The kind is normalized (`router.NormalizeDeliverableKind`, so an unknown word is no declaration), and an item that declares none inherits `.evolve/domain.json` like scout does. `resolveDeliverableKind` is the one owner of that precedence, declared > domain default > `code`, for these lane items and for `dispatchSignals`: each caller passes the domain default it may read (`projectDomainDefault(projectRoot)`, or `noDomainDefault`), and a declared kind never reads it. An id with no resolvable or readable record is carried with the reason in `Unresolved`, so the prompt names it rather than dropping it. A run without a pin carries none, and the prompt is unchanged. Pinned by `TestPlanCycle_TheAdvisorPlanPromptCarriesTheLanesScopedItem` (through `RunCycle` and the planner seam), `TestPlanCycle_AnUnresolvableLaneItemIsNamedInThePlanPrompt`, `TestLaneItem_TheDeclaredKindIsNormalizedAndAnUnknownWordFallsToTheProjectDefault` and `TestResolveDeliverableKind_DeclaredBeatsTheDomainDefaultWhichBeatsCode`.
- **`enforceNext`** (`routing_dispatch.go`) maps the router's proposed next phase back to a `core.Phase` and returns it only if it differs from the static successor and survives both kernel gates: a legal edge (`CanTransition`) and the artifact-backed spine gate (`SpineSatisfiedUpTo`); otherwise the static successor stands. This is the non-bypassable disposal floor for Enforce mode — neither the strategy nor the advisor can reach ship without a real PASS/WARN audit artifact.
- **`writeCycleDossier` takes one params struct, not positional arguments** (`cycleDossierParams`, mirroring `dossier.BuildOpts`), so a field addition touches only the producer and the site that supplies it, not every call site. `Outcome` keeps the producer's own name because it is the raw cycle outcome `dossierVerdict` maps, distinct from `BuildOpts.FinalVerdict`. `Destination` says where the pair goes. `DossierCommitted`, the zero value, writes the corpus pair and commits it, as a sequential production cycle does. `DossierFilesOnly` writes it and never commits: the `--simulate` root's contract, since a no-LLM plumbing walk must never mutate the operator's git history. `DossierPending` writes it to `dossier.PendingDir` with no lock and no commit, for the loop to publish at the wave boundary ([cycle 1704](../../incidents/2026-09-26-a-failed-cycles-closeout-moved-main-under-a-passed-sibling.md)). The composition root chooses committed or files-only (`WithDossierDestination`); `cycleRun.dossierDestination` turns a committed choice into pending for a fleet lane (`fleetMode(req.Env)`), and a files-only choice stays files-only. Pinned by `TestWriteCycleDossier_ParamsStructPreservesFixedInputBytes`, `TestWriteCycleDossier_ParamsAreKeyedAndOptional`, `TestWriteCycleDossier_CommitFalse_WritesFilesOnly`, `TestWithDossierDestination_IsTheRootsKnob`, `TestDossierDestination_AFleetLaneDefersOnlyACommit` and `TestWriteCycleDossier_APendingDossierTakesNoLockAndCommitsNothing`.
- **The dossier closeout commit shares ship's own lock file.** `defaultGitMutationLock` acquires `flock.ShipLockPath(projectRoot)` — the exact file `internal/phases/ship`'s `acquireShipLock` takes — so a lane's dossier commit and a sibling lane's ship commit can never race on the shared `.git/index`; the kernel releases the flock on process death, so a crashed lane cannot wedge the fleet. The acquire runs in `finalizeCycle` after ship has already released its own lease, so it can never self-deadlock. A lock-acquire error fails open (WARNs and proceeds unserialized) rather than orphaning a best-effort dossier; `git commit`'s own busy-index retry is the partial backstop, and a rare lost closeout record is preferred over failing the cycle. Pinned by `TestWriteCycleDossier_AcquiresGitMutationLock`, `TestWriteCycleDossier_ConcurrentLanesEachAcquireAndRelease`, `TestWriteCycleDossier_LockError_FailsOpen`, `TestNewOrchestrator_WiresGitMutationLock`, `TestDefaultGitMutationLock_LocksShipIntegratorFile` and `TestWriteCycleDossier_ConcurrentRealRepo_BothLand`.

Best-effort: absent artifacts leave the block absent, never a blocked write, and a PASS dossier's shape is untouched even when stale failure artifacts linger from an earlier failed attempt. Pinned by `TestDossierFailure_FailCarriesIdentity`, `TestDossierFailure_ReasonsTruncatedAndCapped`, `TestDossierFailure_AbsentArtifactsDegrade` and `TestDossierFailure_PassKeepsShape`.

A ship PASS that the host latched keeps the floor down; else `bindingWitnessesLanding` reads `ship-binding.json` and accepts it only when git confirms it. The cycle matches, the commit is a full object id, and its tree equals the claimed tree. It is an ancestor of the project `HEAD`, and it is not an ancestor of the pre-cycle `HEAD`. Pinned by `TestDetectLostLanding_PlantedShipBindingDoesNotClearTheFloor`, `TestDetectLostLanding_ShippedViaBuildExitIsNotFlagged`, `TestDetectLostLanding_SiblingCycleBindingDoesNotCreditThisCycle` and `TestFinalizeCycle_ShipLatchAndProjectRootReachTheFloor`.
- **A resume takes the ship latch and the pre-cycle `HEAD` from the host resume witness, not from the state file** (`resume_witness.go`, [ADR-0039 D4](../adr/0039-failure-floor-and-failure-signal-contract.md)). The host writes `.evolve/resume-witness/cycle-<N>.json` when it sets the pre-cycle `HEAD` and when a ship PASS sets the latch. A phase agent cannot write that directory. `loadResumeBootstrap` calls `applyResumeWitness` with the host cycle. A missing witness, a corrupt one, or one that names another cycle or `run_id` clears both values.
- **The resume witness also holds the ship error.** `recoverShipErrorRoute` records it in the witness, and the lost-landing floor reads it before the workspace `ship-error.json` ([ADR-0039 D5](../adr/0039-failure-floor-and-failure-signal-contract.md)). Pinned by `TestResumeWitness_AShipLatchPlantedInRunStateIsStillALostLanding`, `TestResumeWitness_APreCycleHeadMovedBackInRunStateDoesNotClearTheFloor`, `TestResumeWitness_AWitnessThatDoesNotNameThisRunIsNotTrusted`, `TestResumeWitness_TheHostRecordsTheWitnessWhereItSetsTheFields` `TestResumeWitness_AResumedShipPassRecordsTheLatch`, `TestResumeWitness_TheResumeTakesTheWitnessOfTheHostCycle`, `TestResumeWitness_AnOverwrittenShipErrorStillFlagsTheLostLanding` and `TestResumeWitness_ALatchAfterAShipErrorKeepsTheShipError`.
- **A landing-lost or other system-level signal reaches the committed dossier verbatim**, under the top-level `system_failure` key (`category` + `evidence`, generic so a future producer of `SystemFailureSignal` is not precluded). Evidence is never truncated the way failure reasons are: the floor formats one bounded line whose tail is the operator's actionable instruction, and a byte cap would cut exactly that part. Both production callers thread it — the normal closeout (`completeCycle`) and the abnormal epilogue — so a cycle that dies mid-phase with a stamped signal is not silently downgraded to an unexplained WARN/FAIL. A landed sibling of the same transient race carries none of it, and an ordinary PASS with no signal writes byte-identical to a producer with no signal support at all. Pinned by `TestDossierSystemFailure_LostLandingReachesTheCommittedDossier`, `TestDossierSystemFailure_LandedSiblingCarriesNone`, `TestDossierSystemFailure_OrdinaryPassStaysByteClean` and `TestDossierSystemFailure_AbnormalEpilogueThreadsTheSignal`.
- **`skipped_phases` and `phases_run_verdict_not_adopted` are distinct dossier records and must never be conflated.** `skipped_phases` means a phase genuinely did not run (the abnormal-epilogue closeout entry, with its skip cause); `phases_run_verdict_not_adopted` means a non-floor phase ran and returned a verdict the floor did not adopt (its own Verdict, not a skip reason) — recording a phase that ran under `skipped_phases` made the dossier contradict its own artifacts, which downstream disposition/recurrence analysis reads to learn which judgment phases executed. Pinned by `TestDossier_RetroThatRanIsNotRecordedAsSkipped` and `TestDossier_AbnormalExitStillRecordsATrueSkip`.
- **`SeedDispositionSkeleton` preseeds a continuation's inherited OPEN defects** (`disposition_seed.go`). At adoption the orchestrator already knows the ancestor's OPEN ids, so it writes `defect-dispositions.json` with one status-OPEN entry per inherited id, sparing the auditor from hand-enumerating the ancestor ledger. Gate semantics stay unweakened: the disposition preflight sees the file as present, but the per-id reconcile still rejects status OPEN, so a seeded entry the auditor never touches still blocks the cycle by name — a seeded DEFERRED would have laundered the defect, so OPEN is the only honest seed. Seeding the file also satisfies the audit phase's secondary-artifact hold immediately (an accepted interaction), so the per-id gate plus the bookkeeping regrade cover an auditor that finishes without upgrading. Every failure mode (unreadable ancestor ledger, no OPEN entries, an existing dispositions file) is a silent no-op by design — the seed is convenience, never enforcement, and an existing file is never clobbered. The seeder decodes the ancestor ledger through its owning leaf, `internal/core/defectledger`, the same Doc and OPEN vocabulary the audit gate itself writes and grades, so the two sides cannot drift; the leaf's `Read` is Signal-Center-free, so a broken ancestor ledger reports through the caller's own module (e.g. carryover's `CARRYOVER_WORKSPACE_READ_FAILED`), never a spurious `AUDIT_*` code. Pinned by `TestSeedDispositionSkeleton_OpenIdsOnly`, `TestSeedDispositionSkeleton_NeverClobbersAndNoOpCases`, `TestApicoverNamed_SeedDispositionSkeleton` and `TestSeedDispositionSkeleton_DecodesThroughTheLeaf_AndReadsAreSignalFree`.
- **`VerifyDisposition` makes retro's `disposition.json` a fail-HARD required deliverable** (`disposition_gate.go`), the fail-HARD counterpart to `readFailureDecision`'s fail-soft boundary: an absent or malformed file, an out-of-vocabulary enum (each field named in the error), a fingerprint/recurrence disagreeing with `failure-digest.json` (`crossCheckAgainstDigest`, stopping an agent from inventing a failure identity), or a salvage-floor violation (`worktree_has_value=true` requires a non-empty `pointer`) all return a loud error. `finalizeRetroCompletion` wires the gate into the composed retro-completion path (`recordFailureLearning`), so a live FAIL cycle surfaces the gate's own error in `RetroDecision` instead of silently recording a clean outcome; the S1 digest assembler runs pre-retro on that composed path and is authoritative over any pre-seeded digest, so a disposition copying a foreign or stale identity still fails the cross-check. Pinned by `TestDispositionGate_RetroFailsLoudWithoutValidDisposition`, `TestDispositionGate_CrossChecksFingerprintAgainstDigest`, `TestDispositionGate_RejectsInvalidEnums`, `TestDispositionGate_SalvagePointerRequiredWhenValue`, `TestDispositionGate_WiredIntoRetroCompletion` and `TestFinalizeRetroCompletion_SeamContract`.
- **The disposition schema example is single-sourced between the retro persona and Go.** `dispositionSchemaExample` is the literal JSON the retro persona's `agents/evolve-retrospective.md` also carries verbatim; a drift between the two, or an example that no longer passes the real `VerifyDisposition` gate, fails CI instead of failing a live retro against a fail-HARD gate. Pinned by `TestDispositionExample_PersonaAndGoAgreeAsJSON` and `TestDispositionExample_PassesProductionGate`.
- **`dispatchSignals` is core's one digester feeding dispatch-time selectors** (`dispatch_signals.go`, [ADR-0099](../adr/0099-deliverable-kinds.md)) — the skill-overlay `when` rule and the deliverable-kind floor read the same digest. The kind is the declared one once a report spoke (triage over scout); before any declaration, only a kind-declaring phase's own dispatch on a clean digest may read the project default (`.evolve/domain.json`), so a document-domain project's first scout dispatch already carries the solution persona. Every other case falls to `code`, matching exactly what the integrity floor (`DocumentCycle`, the tdd pin) reads — a degraded digest never lets the domain default reclassify a torn cycle. The precedence itself is `resolveDeliverableKind`, shared with the advisor's lane items; `dispatchSignals` decides only which domain default it may read.
- **The document deliverable's build-floor check (`solution_floor.go`, ADR-0099 slice 2) judges `<root>/<slug>/` deterministically through the one engine, `internal/solutioncheck`, projected as a `BuildFloorCheckFn` so the correction ladder fixes a malformed deliverable in-phase exactly as the Go floors do for code.** `SolutionViolations` is the single classification + collection every projection (the floor, the audit gate) calls; the kind and the bound slugs come from the kernel's own reads (the triage-authoritative report header and the triage decision), never the builder's report, and the tree resolves to the worktree when the cycle has one, else the project root — the one tree-resolution rule shared by the floor and the audit gate. A document cycle that binds no task is itself a loud violation (`SolutionViolations`'s empty-binding message): "nothing to check" must never read as "checked, clean". `DocumentCycle` is the single classification predicate the floor, the audit gate, the task contract and ship share; `BoundTaskIDs` is also the reader ship uses for its commit-prefix. A document cycle's seeded Task Contract skips the Go predicate inventory (there is no ACS suite for a document deliverable — the floor is `evolve solution check`); a code cycle still renders it. It also states the tdd rule and its result for the cycle (`tddContractLine`: "TDD: not required for this cycle (phase-registry.json conditional_mandatory.tdd = …): routing decides whether tdd runs, not this item's acceptance"), computed by `router.TddPinned` from the registry rule and the cycle's kind signals (the loaded config always carries a tdd rule: `config.defaults` seeds it and the registry and env only replace it, so the line always names one), so the auditor reads the one tdd decision beside the acceptance and never grades tdd's routing as a criterion (ADR-0099 amendment 2026-10-01). Pinned by `TestSeedTaskContract_DocumentCycleStatesTddFromTheRegistryRule` and `TestSeedTaskContract_ARegistryThatPinsTddForDocumentsSaysSo`. Pinned by `TestSolutionFloorChecks`, `TestDocumentCycle`, `TestSolutionViolations`, `TestSolutionViolations_EmptyBindingIsLoud`, `TestChainBuildFloorChecks`, `TestSeedTaskContract_DocumentCycle` and `TestBoundTaskIDs`.
- **`seedDispatchContext` is the one place both dispatch surfaces (the live loop and resume) enrich a phase's context from the cycle's records**: the Task Contract (ADR-0098) via `seedTaskContract`, then the project's default deliverable kind (`.evolve/domain.json`, ADR-0099) via `seedDomainDefault`, then the registry's configured document root via `seedDeliverableRoot`. `seedDomainDefault` reaches only kind-declaring phases (scout/triage); a `domain.json` that exists but cannot be parsed reports loudly and leaves the default absent, never a silent reclassification. `seedDeliverableRoot` carries the configured root only to kind-declaring phases, whose prompts carry no Task Contract block; every other phase reads the root from the rendered Task Contract instead. Pinned by `TestSeedDomainDefault`, `TestSeedDomainDefault_MalformedIsLoudNotSilent` and `TestSeedDeliverableRoot`.
- **The Task Contract (`task_contract.go`, ADR-0098 R4) projects a cycle's acceptance criteria and ACS predicates deterministically into the tdd, build and audit prompts, under one heading, instead of the pre-ADR-0098 path** — two LLM hops of scout/triage prose, and a predicate list the builder only saw if it grepped for it. `taskContractPhase` gates it to exactly the phases that write against the criteria (tdd, build) and the one that grades against them (audit), so the auditor reads the same words the builder was handed. `ContractTaskIDs` is the one id set the contract binds to — delegated to `internal/committedset` (the lane pin when present, else triage's top_n minus deferrals), shared with the cycle dossier and the TDD→Build scope gate; it is never `triage-report.md`'s markdown `## top_n`, which lives in triage's own working-id namespace where decomposition sub-ids are the documented norm. `taskItemRefs` resolves those ids to inbox records: the dispatch context's `fleet_scope_paths` id=path pairs only place a committed member's record, never add or remove one, so a stale request-context scope can never rebind the contract; a member neither source can place renders unresolved, never dropped. `composeTaskContract` projects each record's acceptance verbatim, discloses sanitization explicitly (a sanitized preview still points at the authoritative source), and an unreadable record is a loud line, never a silent omission. For build and audit (never tdd, which hasn't written predicates yet), `listACSPredicates` runs `go test -list -tags acs` over the cycle's `go/acs/cycle<N>` package under `acssuite.DefaultTimeout` — deliberately the constant, not the lane's policy override, since this is one compile of one package and a dispatch must never wait longer than the lane would; a missing package, a compile failure or an empty package is reported in the note, never hidden. A present-but-empty lane pin (`{"todo_ids":[]}`) is not authoritative empty membership — treating it as one would wipe the triage-derived refs and make the block silently disappear — so `LaneScopeIDs` falls back to the decision exactly as an absent pin does. Pinned by `TestComposeTaskContract_VerbatimAcceptanceAndLoudGaps`, `TestComposeTaskContract_SanitizedCriteriaAreNotClaimedVerbatim`, `TestTaskItemRefs_PathsThenScopeThenTriage`, `TestTaskItemRefs_DeferredScopeIsNotMandatory`, `TestTaskItemRefs_ContractTaskIDsParity`, `TestTaskItemRefs_ScopePathsResolvePathsNeverMembership`, `TestListACSPredicates_InventoriesTheCyclePackage`, `TestListACSPredicates_FailureBranchesAreLoud`, `TestDispatch_TaskContractReachesTDDBuildAndAudit`, `TestResume_TaskContractSeededOnTheResumeSurface`, `TestTaskContract_MultiSlugProjectionParity`, `TestDispatch_TaskContractMultiSlugLiveAndResume`, `TestContractTaskIDs`, `TestContractTaskIDs_WhitespacePaddedIDsAreNotPhantomMembers`, `TestLaneScopeIDs_ExplicitEmptyPinIsNoPin` and `TestSeedTaskContract_RendersIdenticallyForPinDecisionAndDeferral`.
  - Since 2026-10-21 the contract marks each criterion that triage defers in `## deferred_acceptance` as `(deferred by triage: <reason>)`. Its preamble says that the eval never narrows the acceptance (ADR-0098 N2). Pinned by `TestSeedTaskContract_TheTriageReportsAcceptanceDeferralReachesTheContract`.
  - `ContractCriteria` counts the filed criteria of the committed items for the `acceptance-rows` gate (N3).
  - `resolvedDeferrals` keeps only the cards that name a filed criterion. It refuses a set that defers every criterion of an item (`TestResolvedDeferrals_OnlyARealPartOfTheAcceptanceIsDeferred`).
  - `closeoutCarryover` carries a deferred criterion only for a shipped cycle (N4, `TestFinalizeCycle_AFailedCycleCarriesNoDeferredCriterion`). `itemPathFor` finds the consumed item of a shipped cycle, so the todo keeps the words of the criterion.
- **The sentinel-error taxonomy is closed and collision-free by construction, and "infra teardown" is a narrow, deliberately incomplete union.** Every sentinel is a distinct `errors.New` value so a branch on one can never accidentally match another; `IsInfraTeardownError` recognizes only `ErrArtifactTimeout` (exit 81) and `ErrTransientBridgeFailure` (exit 80/85/86/124, or exit -1 under a caller-cancelled context) — both end the session without implying the agent failed, so the phase runner reconciles against the on-disk deliverable instead of synthesizing a FAIL. A substantive launch/boot/safety/cost error is deliberately excluded (its output is untrustworthy, so it hard-fails without consulting disk), and so is `ErrAllFamiliesExhausted` (a batch-level quota-terminal condition, not a per-phase reconcile trigger) and a missing agent persona doc (`ErrAgentDocMissing`, a config defect). `IsOptionalSkippableError` widens the union by exactly one case (`ErrAgentDocMissing`) for `optionalInfraSkip`'s gate, single-sourced beside the sentinels so the dispatch site and its tests share one spelling; widening it further needs a paired incident and regression pin, never an ad hoc call-site check. `isArtifactTimeout` stays timeout-only and must never fold into the union — it feeds the failure-diagnostic writer's exit_code/delivery-cause attribution, a narrower question than teardown reconciliation. Pinned by `TestSentinels_ErrorsIs`, `TestSentinels_MessagesArePresent` and `TestIsInfraTeardownError`.
- **The post-build checking phases run concurrently as one batch, then merge serially.** `evaluateBatch` finds the contiguous run of archetype-`evaluate` phases strictly after build and before audit (audit is the sole verdict-brancher and stays serial); it scans from just after build so a pre-build evaluate phase (e.g. bug-reproduction on a bugfix cycle) is never batched, and returns nil below 2 phases so the caller keeps the sequential path. `dispatchEvaluateBatch` builds every phase's `PhaseRequest` serially first (the PhaseIO assemble must not race), then runs each phase's runner and self-heal retry concurrently (bounded by `cfg.ParallelEvaluateConcurrency`) with no shared-state mutation, then folds every outcome in a single-goroutine serial merge: `recordPhaseOutcome`, `CompletedPhases`, the ledger and cycle-state writes all happen there, except admitted-skip records a worker appends through the synchronized ledger. The batch verdict is weakest-link (FAIL > WARN > PASS, `mergeVerdict`). A hard dispatch error is all-or-nothing: every phase's outcome is still recorded before the cycle aborts (no phase's result is silently dropped). `dispatchRunnerWithRetry` delegates to the same retry core the sequential loop uses, diverging only in its own `evaluateBatchRetryOpts` (ship recovery and backfill disabled), never a second hand-maintained loop. `evaluateBatchAt` only returns the batch when the given phase is its first member, so the loop batches the whole run in one iteration and never re-enters mid-run. A known v1 fidelity gap, accepted because evaluate phases are read-only and the feature ships dormant (`StageOff` default) until after a shadow soak: the deliverable-correction ladder and the tree-diff leak guard do not run for batched phases. Pinned by `TestMergeVerdict`, `TestDispatchEvaluateBatch_Concurrent`, `TestDispatchEvaluateBatch_WeakestLink` and `TestDispatchEvaluateBatch_CrashSafety`.
- **`optionalInfraSkip` and `postShipObserverSkip` fail closed on every edge, and the mandatory guard always wins.** A phase mis-marked `Optional: true` in the catalog but also listed in `cfg.Mandatory` never skips (the mandatory guard is generic and config-driven, so it overrides the catalog flag); a phase inside the resolved ship floor never skips even if catalog-optional; a phase absent from the catalog entirely never skips (fail closed on a catalog miss); a non-infra error is never swallowed, even on an optional, off-floor phase. `postShipObserverSkip` additionally requires `shipped=true`, excludes ship itself explicitly, and matches only `RoleControl` optional phases (an evaluate-role optional phase, however shipped, never matches). Pinned by `TestOptionalInfraSkip_WrappedArtifactTimeoutError_StillMatches`, `TestOptionalInfraSkip_NonInfraError_NeverMatches`, `TestOptionalInfraSkip_MandatoryOverridesOptionalFlag`, `TestOptionalInfraSkip_OnFloorPhase_NeverMatches`, `TestOptionalInfraSkip_UnknownPhaseNotInCatalog_NeverMatches`, `TestPostShipObserverSkip_NotYetShipped_NeverMatchesRegardlessOfPhase`, `TestPostShipObserverSkip_ShipItself_NeverMatchesEvenIfShipped`, `TestPostShipObserverSkip_WrongRole_NeverMatches` and `TestPostShipObserverSkip_MandatoryOverridesEvenWhenShippedAndControl`.
- **A tdd predicate that reads a protected path stops the cycle as a FAIL with a route code.** The `PhaseTDD` branch of `selectNext` calls `routeProtectedPredicates`. The scan is in `internal/acspathscan`, and `WithProtectedSurface` injects the membership function. The termination is `tdd-protected-predicate-route`, the verdict is FAIL, and the item routes at the closeout. The design is in [the cycleoutcome note](internal-cycleoutcome.md#protected-predicate-route-tdd).
- **Triage's empty-commitment gate distinguishes legitimate no-work from a claim failure, and it lives where the decision is consumed, not merely proposed.** An explicit empty `top_n` is authoritative no-eligible-work evidence regardless of the phase verdict that carried it (even a FAIL triage with an explicit empty decision is planned no-work, never a task-level failure) — but only when the inbox holds no claimable work either; a still-populated, lane-claimable inbox item turns the same empty commitment into a named, non-PASS termination (`claim-failed`/`no-commitment`, never `CycleTerminationTriageNoWork`), distinct from `IsTriageNoWorkResult`'s accounting. A console-routed item is operator-owned and never counts as claimable. What counts as claimable pending work is the lane menu triage itself offers, on both the lane-scoped and the sequential path: `WithLaneMenu` injects it (the composition root passes `inboxmover.PartitionLaneMenu`'s ready set, with the real protected-surface predicate and the dependency rule), and without the option only console routes and kinds are withheld (`LaneMenuWired` reports the binding). The cycle's own claims (`processing/cycle-N/`) are never filtered by the menu: an item the cycle claimed and left uncommitted is a claim failure whatever its deps, so the failure drain releases it instead of stranding it in processing (`TestHasClaimableInboxWork_AClaimIsWorkTheCycleTookWhateverTheMenuSays`, `TestUnansweredClaimableWork_TheLanePathAsksTheMenuForPendingItemsButNeverForClaims`). Both scans load a directory through `admittedIDs` over `inboxbatch.ScanDir`: a missing directory is empty, and only an unreadable file (one the loader could not read or parse) counts as possible work, because an unreadable queue is never proof that nothing was claimable. The sequential check fails closed on one in the root or in its own claims (`TestHasClaimableInboxWork_AnUnreadableFileIsNeverProofOfNoWork`, `TestHasClaimableInboxWork_AnUnreadableClaimIsNeverProofOfNoWork`). The lane check fails closed when a scoped id is unanswered while any file is unreadable, since that file may be it, and otherwise ignores an unrelated broken file (`TestRunCycle_AnUnrelatedMalformedItemDoesNotFailALane`). A notice about a readable item, a sanitized field or a duplicate id, is not unread work: the menu has already judged that item, and the tracked inbox carries such notices on about two in five items, so counting them turned every empty commitment into a claim failure (`TestHasClaimableInboxWork_ANoticeAboutAReadableItemIsNotUnreadWork`, `TestUnansweredClaimableWork_ANoticeIsNotUnreadWorkOnTheLanePath`, `TestRunCycle_EmptyTriageOverWithheldWorkIsPlannedNoWorkWhenAnItemWasTrimmed`). A lane judges only its own scope: a scoped item is where triage left it, still pending in the root or claimed into `processing/cycle-N/`, and it is unanswered unless the decision answers for it (`committedset.Dispositions`: an escalation, a rejection, a shipped skip or a reasoned drop, never a deferral); another lane's work is never this lane's claim failure. Pinned by `TestRunCycle_EmptyTriageOverWorkTheLaneMenuWithholdsIsPlannedNoWork`, `TestHasClaimableInboxWork_AsksTheLaneMenu` and `cmd/evolve` `TestWireOrchestrator_TheNoWorkCheckAsksTheLaneMenu`. The gate sits in `cyclerun_select.go`'s `PhaseTriage` branch of `selectNext` (via `o.triageTermination`), reached identically from a fresh `RunCycle`, `RunCycleFromPhase` and the resume cursor, because a router-`Route`-only proposal is just advice that `enforceNext`'s own clamps (the ship-planned floor) can silently discard. Committed work always outranks any termination proposal, however it arrived. Triage may be re-dispatched at most once for a claim race, and the gate only reads the inbox — it never consumes the claimable item, so a later cycle can still claim it. Pinned by `TestRunCycle_EmptyTriageClaimableWorkStopsBeforeImplementation`, `TestRunCycleFromPhase_ResumedEmptyTriageWithClaimableInboxStopsBeforeImplementation`, `TestRunCycle_EmptyInboxIsPlannedNoWork`, `TestRunCycle_CommittedWorkWithClaimableInboxStillDispatchesImplementation`, `TestRunCycle_EmptyTriageCommitmentIsPlannedNoWork`, `TestRunCycleFromPhase_ResumedFailedTriageWithEmptyDecisionIsPlannedNoWork`, `TestSkippedUnknownWarningExcludesPlannedNoWork` and `TestIsTriageNoWorkResult`.
- **The empty-triage-commitment termination is additive to `CanTerminateEarly`, never a redefinition of it.** `CanTerminateEarly(from, shipPlanned)` still means exactly what it always meant (false whenever `shipPlanned`); the empty-commitment case is a distinct check inside `enforceNext` that can terminate at triage even when ship is planned, but only when the commitment is genuinely empty (an absent `triage-decision.json` is an *unknown* commitment, not an empty one, and stays blocked) and no committed work exists. The check cannot leak past build — once real work exists downstream it is evaluated, never abandoned. Pinned by `TestSelectNext_StaticRoutingHonorsTriageTermination`, `TestEnforceNext_EmptyTriageCommitmentIsALegalTermination`, `TestCanTerminateEarly_ShipPlannedInvariantIntact` and `TestResumeCursorTriageTerminationOverridesScheduledSuccessor`.
- **`CanTerminateEarly` is config-driven, with the ship-planned guard staying a Go invariant** (ADR-0060). A catalog's `early_exit` field overrides the literal pre-catalog set in either direction; a ship-intended cycle can never early-exit regardless of config. With no catalog it degrades to the literal pre-catalog set, byte-identical to before; only the pre-build decision points (scout, triage) may terminate early, and the guarded scout/triage→end edges are structurally legal (`CanTransition`) independent of whether `CanTerminateEarly` authorizes taking them. Pinned by `TestCanTerminateEarly_ConfigOverridesLiteral`, `TestCanTerminateEarly_ConfigDriven`, `TestCanTerminateEarly_DegradesToLiteral`, `TestCanTerminateEarly`, `TestEarlyExitEdgesAreStructurallyLegal` and `TestEarlyExit_NeverShipsWithoutFloor`.
- **The cycle-start operator-directives hook is called once per cycle.** An injected provider's snapshot is stamped into the ledger by version and threaded to every phase's `OperatorDirectives`; an empty `directives.Set` stamps nothing and threads an empty string, byte-identical to no provider configured. Pinned by `TestOrchestrator_WithDirectivesProvider_SnapshotStampThread` and `TestOrchestrator_WithDirectivesProvider_EmptySetNoStamp`.
- **A repeat failure pattern can never emit a bare `proceed`.** `escalateRetroReason` rewrites a deterministic `proceed:` reason to an escalated `adapt:` reason once the recurrence ledger counts the pattern at 2+, so a recurring defect keeps getting louder instead of being reported identically to a first-time pass; a generic classification pattern (operator-reset/loop-fatal echo) is exempt, since its count is corpus noise, not a recurring defect. Pinned by `TestDecideAfterRetro_NthOccurrenceForcesAdapt` and `TestC662_EscalateRetroReasonIgnoresGenericPatterns`.

- **One classification of a walled dispatch** (`quota_exhaustion.go`, [ADR-0106](../adr/0106-logic-first-delivery.md) Q1). `isQuotaWall` reads the runner's exit 85: the runner walks its whole family chain inside a single `Run` and returns 85 when the walk exhausted every family it may use after meeting a wall. The correction re-dispatch (`cyclerun_correction.go`), the remediation re-run (`cyclerun_remediate.go`) and the resumed review gate (`resume.go`, consumed by `resume_execution.go`) return through `pauseForQuota`, the same seam the first dispatch's retry ladder uses, so a wall met anywhere in a cycle is the DEFERRED checkpoint, the `all_families_exhausted` ledger kind and the `quota.paused` signal — never a FAIL outcome, a failure lesson, a retrospective or a failure digest. The reading depends on llmroute's fallback triggers including exit 85 (the default), so the chain steps past a walled family before the runner returns; on the resumed review gate the pause records the dispatch attempts and the abort reason names the correction that met the wall. Since wave 24 the runner surfaces the wall of any exhausted walk that met one (`bridgechain.WallKeeper`), so one sample means "the walk ended at a wall", not "every family walled"; the sentinel says so ("the dispatch chain ended at a quota wall"), and the resume paths carry the runner's error in `quotaWall` (which unwraps to both the sentinel and the cause), so every pause and its `quota.paused` line name the walk. Pinned by `TestRunCycle_AllFamilies85_CheckpointsAndDefers`, `TestRunCycle_WalledRemediationRerun_DefersLikeAFirstDispatch`, `TestRunCycleFromPhase_WalledResumedCorrection_Defers`, `TestRunCycleFromPhase_AWalledResumedDispatchNamesItsWalk` and `TestErrAllFamiliesExhausted_ClaimsOnlyThatTheDispatchEndedAtAWall`.
- **The quota-wall rule** (`isQuotaWall`, `ErrAllFamiliesExhausted`, since 2026-10-09). One dispatch is walled when the runner returns exit 85. The runner walks its whole chain inside one `Run`, so one sample is enough. `allFamiliesQuotaExhausted` keeps its two-sample rule until its tests model the tiered chain. The runner returns 85 only when the walk has an exit 85, and each other started rung is proven exhausted or neutral ([internal-phases-runner](internal-phases-runner.md)). Thus a lost pane or a stall with `healthy` or `unknown` usage is never a quota pause (cycle 1853, [incident](../../incidents/cycle-1853-secondary-defects.md)).
- **The pause carries its walk.** `pauseForQuota` copies `core.WalkError.CLIs` to `CycleState.QuotaWalkCLIs` (`quota_walk_clis`, additive) before the checkpoint hook runs. The checkpoint reads the reset evidence of those families only. Pinned by `TestRunCycle_AWalledPauseHandsTheWalkedCLIsToTheCheckpoint` and `TestRunCycle_ALedgerFaultDoesNotLoseTheQuotaPause`.
- **The abnormal epilogue** (`abnormalEpilogue`).
  - RunCycle defers it. It acts only when the cycle did not reach the normal closeout.
  - A quota pause and a graceful cancel have their own checkpoints, so it does nothing for them.
  - It is best effort and loud, and each step tolerates a failure of the others.
  - The steps are the failure digest, the `closeout` skip, the FAIL seal (ADR-0101), the dossier, the continuation manifest (ADR-0076) and the `aborted` state.
  - The seal reason is the template plus `epilogueCauseSuffix(cause)`. It is `cause=` for a task error and `teardown=` for an infra teardown (ADR-0072), with the tail of `causeHead`.
  - Since 2026-10-09 a teardown with an artifact-timeout cause names that cause first (`teardown=pane_lost: …`). Thus a lost pane never shares a fingerprint with a `review_pause` timeout.
  - Pinned by `TestEpilogueCauseSuffix_APaneLossNeverMergesWithAReviewPauseTimeout` and `TestAbnormalEpilogue_ADossierOrStateWriteFaultStillLeavesTheDigestAndTheAbortedPhase`.

- **`emitPhaseBindings`** (defined in `phase_bindings.go`) writes the per-agent provenance ledger entries ship's verification requires after a phase completes; both `RunCycle` and `RunCycleFromPhase` call it, because a resume path that skips it binds ship to a stale auditor entry from an earlier cycle and fails `AUDIT_BINDING_HEAD_MOVED`.
- **The fork point** (`forkPoint`, `git merge-base HEAD main`) stays put as a lane's worktree base even when a peer lands on main after the rebase; the lane's carried base is the fork point, never main's later tip. Pinned by `TestRouteRebasedExplanation_BindsTheForkPointWhenMainMovesOn`.
- **`normalizeScoutGoalHash` reconciles a scout↔triage lane-identity mismatch by machine-stamping the pinned `lane-scope.json` goal hash into the scout report, not by aborting.** The scout prompt asks the LLM to echo the pinned goal hash into its Decision Trace, but a deterministic transcription flip can reproduce the same wrong digit on every retry, so a hard-abort gate on mismatch could never self-heal and would false-abort otherwise-healthy cycles; the pin is authoritative regardless of the echo, since the echo verifies nothing per-cycle workspace isolation doesn't already guarantee. The stamp only fires when the mis-echoed value is itself a canonical 64-hex goal hash, so a non-canonical echo (truncation, placeholder, hallucination) can never trigger an unbounded whole-file replace. Every degraded path (unreadable report, non-canonical echo, write failure, absent pin, absent echo) fails open with a WARN, never a silent proceed or a false abort. Pinned by `TestNormalizeScoutGoalHash_StampsMismatchToPin`, `TestNormalizeScoutGoalHash_NonCanonicalEchoRefused`, `TestNormalizeScoutGoalHash_FailOpen` and `TestNormalizeScoutGoalHash_WriteFailureIsLoudNoOp`.
- **Fleet mode skips the whole-cycle global project lock** (`EVOLVE_FLEET=1`): concurrent fleet cycles run in separate processes, each isolated by its own per-run worktree and workspace, and serialized on every shared resource by that resource's own flock (`state.json` via `UpdateState`/`withStateLock`, the ledger chain, the `.evolve/ship.lock` integrator) — so the coarse lock would only make fleet cycles needlessly refuse each other. The sequential loop (no `EVOLVE_FLEET`) keeps the lock, byte-identical to before. Pinned by `TestRunCycle_FleetMode_SkipsGlobalLock` and `TestRunCycle_Default_AcquiresGlobalLock`.
- **The integrity floor activates the advisor's plan, not just Enforce mode** (ADR-0024 §1). At `Stage>=Advisory` the orchestrator computes the upfront whole-cycle plan, clamps it to the floor (`ClampPlanToFloor`), and lets the clamped plan drive phase selection: the advisor drives every non-mandatory phase, while the floor forces the ship-chain (build, audit) before ship regardless of how small `cfg.Mandatory` is or whether the advisor's plan omits them; a planner error degrades to the configurable spine via the trigger path. Under the hybrid cadence (ADR-0024 §2), the per-transition Proposer fires only at branch transitions (post-build, post-audit) when a plan is driving, not at every phase, so the plan and the per-transition proposer never double-spend a routing decision. `Stage:Off` ignores a scripted plan entirely and runs the byte-identical legacy spine with no upfront plan call. Pinned by the `TestFloorActivationCycle` scenario catalog (`floor_activation_scenarios_test.go`).
- **The infra-teardown union predicate is enforced single-sourced by an AST uniqueness scan, not just by convention.** `IsInfraTeardownError` is the one place the timeout-or-transient union (or its De Morgan negation) may be spelled; `findInfraTeardownUnionSpellings` parses every non-test `.go` file in a scanned package, walks binary expressions, and fails if the union appears anywhere but `IsInfraTeardownError`'s own body — a uniqueness assertion that can only be satisfied by removing a duplicate spelling, never by adding text, so it cannot be gamed into a degenerate source-grep. The scan covers `internal/core` plus every consumer package (`internal/bridge`, `internal/phases/runner`), since the predicate's consumers live outside `internal/core` and a package-local scan would miss a re-spelling there. The timeout-only half is pinned the same way in the opposite direction: `adviseOnUnclassifiedFailure`, `isArtifactTimeout`, `wiredFailureDiag` and `DeliveryFailureCause` must keep referencing `ErrArtifactTimeout` (or its injected `isArtifactTimeout` gate) alone and must never grow a reference to the transient sentinel, its transient-only component, or the union helper, because a transient bridge bounce relabeled as an artifact timeout would be recorded as exit 81 and fed to the fatal-signature classifier as if it carried a diagnosable pane. `optionalInfraSkip`'s gate must also agree with `IsOptionalSkippableError` for every error shape, or the call site has grown a private error taxonomy. Pinned by `TestInfraTeardownUnion_SpelledExactlyOnce`, `TestInfraTeardownUnion_ScanCoversConsumerPackages`, `TestInfraTeardownUnion_DetectsPlantedDuplicateOutsideCore`, `TestOptionalInfraSkip_GateAgreesWithIsOptionalSkippableError`, `TestIsTransientBridgeError_StaysTransientOnly`, `TestWritePhaseFailureDiag_TimeoutOnlyNotWidened` and `TestTimeoutOnlySites_NotWidenedToUnion`.
- **The correction ladder's re-dispatch is a typed interaction, recorded to a per-phase ledger and a per-cycle rollup** (ADR-0045 I1). Each contract-correction re-dispatch appends one `interaction.Outcome` (kind `correction_redispatch`, the triggering rung, a decision id correlating every rung of one correction, and a payload digesting the violation) to `<workspace>/<phase>-interactions.ndjson`, resolved by the re-dispatch verdict (`accepted` or `rejected_again`); `RunCycle`'s deferred persistence writes `interaction-summary.json` beside `phase-timing.json` on both the normal and the abort exit path, so an exhausted correction ladder still flushes the rollup. Pinned by `TestCorrectionRedispatch_RecordsInteractionOutcome` and `TestCorrectionRedispatch_ExhaustionRecordsRejectedAgain`.
- **The scout-eval-materialization correction round-trip is proven end-to-end against the real `evalgate.materializationGate`, not a faked reviewer.** `evalgate_escalation_test.go` (package `core`) proves the CLI-escalation ladder with a hand-authored fake reviewer, but never drives the real gate: a real `scout-report.md` naming a missing `.evolve/evals/<slug>.md`, a real gate rejection whose remediation names the exact workspace eval path, a correction re-dispatch, and a real gate re-review that approves only once the eval exists at that exact path. A remediation-carrying correction directive must drop the gate's default "do not change unrelated files" clause, since the whole point of a remediation is to require a file creation; a non-complying re-dispatch must keep failing every round rather than silently passing once a correction round has merely happened. `materialization_correction_delivery_test.go` lives in the external `core_test` package because wiring the real `evalgate.NewReviewer` (which imports `core`) alongside `core` from an internal test file would be an import cycle. Pinned by `TestMaterializationCorrectionDelivery_RemediationReachesReDispatchAndClearsTheRealGate` and `TestMaterializationCorrectionDelivery_NonComplyingReDispatchStaysRejected`.
- **RUNG 2** (`mergerung2.go`) reviews only the hunks that intersect between the audited diff and the composed (rebased) diff, escalating to RUNG 3 (full re-audit) only on genuine entanglement; an empty intersection is compatible without invoking the reviewer at all. The reviewer is an injected closure so this package stays adapter- and LLM-agnostic, and any LLM-assisted resolution it returns is suggestion-grade: it is trusted only after `ResolutionMatchesAudited` re-enters RUNG 0's patch-id verification on the resolution diff itself, never on the reviewer's say-so. Hunk parsing and header parsing fail closed on any malformed diff or `@@` header, never silently reporting compatible.
- **`isActiveMintPhasePath`'s exemption is clamp parity, not provenance.** With the OS sandbox off, any on-disk `.evolve/phases/<name>/phase.json` is forgeable, so the guard never authenticates the registry file; it only exempts exactly the two paths a real mint writes (the bare `.evolve/phases/<name>` directory entry and `.evolve/phases/<name>/phase.json` itself) for a name that is registered, TTL-fresh, and content-verified by `verifiedActiveMints` — a novel name, a companion payload under a registered name, or an unclamped spec all still fire the guard, so a forged entry can never smuggle anything a sanctioned mint could not already carry. `verifiedActiveMints` `Lstat`s before reading (rejecting anything but a small regular file — no FIFO block, no symlink escape, no oversized read) and only trusts a spec that parses as a `PhaseSpec` whose name matches its directory, is optional, and passes `ValidateUserSpec`. `mintNameRE`'s kebab-case floor is enforced before any filesystem access, since a registry name is an attacker-influenceable string used as a path segment. Pinned by `TestIsActiveMintPhasePath` and `TestVerifiedActiveMints`.
- **A judgment phase's FAIL verdict teaches without halting** (`judgment_lesson.go`). A judgment phase (`premise-challenge`, `plan-review`, `adversarial-review`) returns FAIL as a verdict with `err == nil`, a shape neither learning path catches: `recordFloorVerdictFailure` fires only for the authoritative ship floor, and `recordFailureLearning` early-returns without a dispatch error. `recordJudgmentLesson` records the objection as a carryover todo only, deliberately never a `FailedRecord`, because `state.FailedAt` feeds `failureadapter.Decide`'s two halt vectors (`tailInfraTransientStreak`, `sameClassStreak`) — a phase whose whole job is to object must not be able to halt the batch by objecting. Pinned by `TestRecordAndBranch_PremiseChallengeFAILTeaches`, `TestRecordAndBranch_PremiseChallengeFAILDoesNotRecordFailedApproach` and `TestJudgmentLessonFullPath_PremiseChallengeSentinelFAILTeachesWithoutHalting`.
- **The Task Contract reaches any phase that asks for it.** `seedTaskContract` seeds the block for the built-in tdd, build and audit (`taskContractPhase`) and for any catalog phase whose spec lists `task_contract` in `prompt_context` (`requestsContext`). The phase declares what context it needs, so no user phase name lives in core; `code-review` asks for it so the reviewer judges correctness against the bound acceptance. Pinned by `TestSeedTaskContract_AUserPhaseThatAsksForItInPromptContextGetsTheBlock`.
- **The code-review phase's findings are recorded at the completion boundary** (`review_findings.go`, [ADR-0124](../adr/0124-code-review-phase.md)).
  - When a `code-review` dispatch completes with any verdict but FAIL, `recordReviewFindings` hands the workspace, the cycle and the round to `internal/codereview`. The round is `codereview.RoundFrom` over the persisted `CompletedPhases`, so the delta re-review is round 2 on resume too.
  - `internal/codereview` appends the report's findings to `defect-ledger.json` through the ledger's one merge rule. The core emits the returned `REVIEW_FINDINGS` event, stamped with the run id, under `codereview.Settings`: the resolved `WorkflowConfig.CodeReviewRepair` (stage and threshold) and `WorkflowConfig.QualityIndex` (the thresholds the scores qualify against).
  - The call sits in `phaseCompletionRecord.persist`, which the fresh loop and the resume path share, and in the parallel evaluate batch (`recordBatchMember`, the per-member success bookkeeping beside the batch's ledger append), the one completion surface that bypasses `persist`.
  - A FAILed or SKIPPED review is not recorded: its report failed the structural contract, or the phase degraded, and its `phase.outcome` already says so.
- **An optional evaluate phase degrades on an exhausted correction ladder** (`correction_exhaustion.go`, [ADR-0124](../adr/0124-code-review-phase.md) fix round 1, H2).
  - `Orchestrator.skippableOptional` is the one optional-phase predicate: not configured mandatory (`isConfiguredMandatory`), a catalog spec that is `Optional`, and not in the ship floor. `optionalInfraSkip`, `postShipObserverSkip` and the exhaustion degrade all call it, so a phase the operator lists in `config.mandatory_phases` is never skipped by any of them.
  - `degradesOnExhaustion(phase, completed)` names the class: `skippableOptional` with the evaluate role, past the ship floor. `degradesRejection` adds the deliverable test: only a present but malformed deliverable degrades. An absent one (`ReviewResult.DeliverableAbsent`, set by the gate for a missing or empty artifact) keeps the abort: a non-admitted missing deliverable never reaches Ship, the #542 admission rule pinned by `TestOptionalSkip_RealDeliverableGateFreshAndResume`.
  - `reviewInputFor` builds every review input both ladders send and sets `ReviewInput.BreakerExempt` from `degradesOnExhaustion`, so the class's blocks never reach the contract gate's breaker. The breaker-count escalation and its salvage re-prompt, which warns of a breaker about to open, therefore do not fire for it.
  - `rejectAfterCorrections` is the fresh ladder's exhaustion tail and `reviewResumedDeliverable` the resume ladder's. Both degrade through `degradeExhaustedReview`: a WARN, a `contract_exhaustion_skip` ledger entry and the SKIPPED response. The fresh ladder also records the failure learning first; the resume ladder records none on any of its outcomes, its abort included. Every other phase keeps today's abort messages byte-identical.
  - `noteContractExhaustionSkip` is shared with the non-canonical-verdict degrade. It appends the ledger entry, and for `code-review` emits `REVIEW_SKIPPED{reason: malformed}` (`recordReviewSkipped`) with the round the dispatch would have been.
  - Pinned by `TestReviewWithCorrections_AnOptionalEvaluatePhaseThatExhaustsItsCorrectionsIsSkippedNotAborted`, `TestReviewWithCorrections_ABreakerExemptLadderNeverWarnsOfABreakerThatCannotOpen`, `TestReviewWithCorrections_AnOptionalEvaluatePhaseWhoseDeliverableIsAbsentStillAborts`, `TestReviewWithCorrections_AnotherOptionalEvaluatePhaseDegradesWithoutAReviewSignal`, `TestReviewWithCorrections_ADegradedReviewStillRecordsItsFailureLearning`, `TestReviewWithCorrections_ASkippedReviewNamesTheRoundItWouldHaveBeen`, `TestReviewWithCorrections_OneCorrectionIsCountedInTheAbortMessage`, `TestReviewWithCorrections_AnAbortWithNoCorrectionsTeachesTheRetroItsFirstAttempt`, `TestReviewResumedDeliverable_AResumedReviewIsBreakerExemptAndDegradesLikeAFreshOne`, `TestReviewResumedDeliverable_StillAbortsOutsideTheDegrade` (an absent report, an operator-mandatory phase, a floor phase) and `TestReviewWithCorrections_APhaseOutsideTheOptionalEvaluateClassStillAborts` (a floor phase, a pre-floor phase, a required evaluate phase, an optional plan phase, an operator-mandatory optional evaluate phase).
  - Pinned by `TestCodeReviewCompletion_RecordsItsFindingsAsShadowRowsAndSignalsThem`, `TestCodeReviewCompletion_ASecondDispatchIsRoundTwo`, `TestCodeReviewCompletion_AFailedReviewOrAnotherPhaseRecordsNothing` (a failed review, a skipped review, other phases), `TestDispatchEvaluateBatch_RecordsTheCodeReviewsFindingsLikeTheSerialPath` and the lockstep grep `TestJudgmentLesson_WiredInLockstepWithFloorRecorder`.
- **`judgmentTeachingPhases` is an explicit set, never derived.** The obvious derivation (`remediationDenied[p] && !isAuthoritativePhase(p)`) silently sweeps in the control phases `PhaseRetro` and `PhaseDebugger`; retro is the mechanism that writes lessons, so teaching from a retro FAIL would recurse into its own output, and `recordFailureLearning` already excludes `PhaseRetro` by a guard a derived call would route around. Audit is absent because it is authoritative and already recorded by `recordFloorVerdictFailure`. `plan-review` is listed even though it has no catalog `phase.json` yet, so the lesson works the day it becomes one. Pinned by `TestJudgmentLesson_ControlPhasesDoNotTeach` and `TestJudgmentLessonEndToEnd_ControlPhaseFAILReachesNoPlannerPrompt`.
- **Token telemetry S4 projects the terminal attempt's counts into both the timing entry and the usage sidecar.** `recordPhaseOutcome` is the chokepoint that threads `PhaseOutcome.Tokens` into `phase-timing.json` and `<phase>-usage.json`, beside the existing `cost_usd` field; a sidecar written before the tokens field existed still parses, with `Tokens` zero-valued via `omitempty`. Pinned by `TestRecordPhaseOutcome_ProjectsTokensToSidecarAndTiming` and `TestSidecar_LegacyWithoutTokensParses`.
- **`writeCatalog`'s overflow bucket points at `phase-inventory.json` instead of enumerating names.** Once the catalog exceeds `maxEnrichedCatalogCards`, the overflow cards are neither individually named nor listed in a bare enumeration; a one-line pointer to `phase-inventory.json` preserves selectability without bloating the prompt. Pinned by `TestWriteCatalog_OverflowNotEnumerated`, `TestWriteCatalog_PointerLinePresent`, `TestWriteCatalog_PointerLineRequired_Negative` and `TestWriteCatalog_NoOverflow_NoPointer`.
