- **The advisor's Plan path writes its raw plan through the artifact-completion contract, not stdout.** The brain writes `routing-plan.json` and the bridge reads it back, the same contract every phase agent uses, replacing an earlier brittle stdout scrape; the orchestrator's own clamped result is persisted separately as `phase-plan.json`, so both the advisor's raw proposal and the orchestrator's clamped decision survive for forensics. Pinned by `TestPhaseAdvisor_PlanParsesArray`.
- **A stub persona in a prompt test is structurally blind to a real schema conflict.** Every advisor-prompt test that injects `WithPersona("PERSONA BODY")` cannot see that the shipped `agents/evolve-router.md` carries its own existing-phase response-schema example; when that persona example omits a field (e.g. {cli,tier}) and appears before the Go-appended example, the composed prompt shows two competing schemas and an LLM mimics the earliest/most-authoritative one, so the optional field is emitted only intermittently. `realRouterPersona` loads the real, on-disk `agents/evolve-router.md` (resolved via `runtime.Caller`, reading the worktree copy) so these goldens validate the actual shipped persona, not a stub. Pinned by `TestComposePlanPrompt_RealPersonaExistingExampleCarriesTierAndCLI` and `TestRealPersonaFrontmatterOutputFormatEnumeratesTierCLI`.
- **The audit-binding's verdict-cache write is a projection, not a second record, and is write-fail-closed.** `recordAuditBinding` projects the audit verdict into the content-addressed verdict cache keyed by the same worktree tree SHA the binding records, but only when the verdict is `verdictcache.Reusable` (a FAIL or SKIPPED must never enter it) and only when a fresh-base guard passes (`verdictcache.ProbeEligible`, fed the worktree's own base commit — never `projectRoot` HEAD at audit time, which can diverge under fleet concurrency when a sibling ship advances main mid-cycle). The guard is deliberately asymmetric with the read side's fail-open Lookup: a poisoned Put sits in the shared store for every future consumer, so a missing base identity or a write failure skips the Put instead of guessing. `worktreeContentSHA` is the single source for both the audit binding's `WorktreeTreeSHA` and this cache key, so the recorded and looked-up values are computed identically.
- **`buildSelfCheck` is a false-green backstop: a builder's own self-report can lie.** A builder can delete an env read, break a pre-existing unit test in a changed package, satisfy its own ACS check (which does not run that package's unit tests), and hand off a green build-report — the regression would otherwise only surface at audit, two attempts later. Running the changed packages' unit tests deterministically after build records ground-truth so audit and retro see the exact failing tests. UNIT tests only (no `-tags integration`): env-dependent tmux/REPL integration tests are intentionally excluded so a real regression fails the check while a flaky live-launch test does not. Like build-gofmt and build-derived-regen this is deterministic work that must not depend on the LLM builder remembering; best-effort and NEVER aborts — audit stays the verdict authority.
- **The build-entry graduation guard is abort-capable, unlike the WARN-only `buildSelfCheck`.** A package that is genuinely NEW this cycle (no committed files at HEAD) and absent from `go/.apicover-enforce` FAILS the build phase with an explicit `abort_reason`, because enrollment is a hard shipping obligation the builder itself must satisfy — not a diagnostic left for audit to re-discover attempts later. `buildGraduationCheck` reuses `ciparity.NewUngraduatedPackages` over the same changed-path derivation the WARN-only self-check uses, so the two seams cannot disagree on scope; it fails open (empty reason) when the worktree is empty or the enforce list is unreadable.
- **A graduation abort's message is the remediation interface, not just a diagnosis.** `buildGraduationCheck`'s abort reason spells out the exact two edits per ungraduated package — the pattern line to append to `go/.apicover-enforce` and the `apicover_named_test.go` path to create — as copy-pasteable text, and names which of the two apicover gates it is (the repo-wide enforce list, never the per-cycle ACS coverage gate), since the builder never reads the ADR that explains the distinction. Multiple flagged packages each get their own two edits rather than one shared prescription. Pinned by `TestBuildGraduationCheck_MessageEmitsTheExactTwoEdits`, `TestBuildGraduationCheck_MessageIsPerPackage` and `TestBuildGraduationCheck_MessageDistinguishesTheTwoApicoverGates`.
- **No raw `carryover_summary` writer may exist below `EVOLVE_PHASE_IO=enforce`.** A populated carryover backlog in state must not appear in `Context["carryover_summary"]` on any dispatched request at off/shadow — that key, and the typed `PhaseInput`, both stay absent/zero until enforce. A cap on the carryover bytes is a guard on what arrives through the typed envelope; it is not a licence to mint the uncapped bytes into the legacy Context path instead. Pinned by `TestPlanCycle_CarryoverSummary_NoRawWriterBelowEnforce`.
- **`worktreeContentSHA`'s tree identity is scoped to DECLARED content only, never untracked residue.** Declared means content already in the lane's git index (tracked files, plus anything the builder explicitly `git add`ed), tracked modifications on disk, and the paths the cycle's build and TDD reports name, untracked or not. Since F43 part 2 (2026-09-28) it is `shipmanifest.TakeShipTree` after the real-index `git add -u`: the tree the audit seals and Ship stages, read through `gitReadIn`, an adapter over the `gitCapture` seam. It takes the cycle workspace (every caller passes `cs.WorkspacePath`) and returns empty without one, because without the reports the ship tree is unknown; an untracked file nobody staged — a bug-reproduction reproducer, a regenerated coverage artifact, another agent's scratch file — must never enter the tree the audit binding records or the verdict-cache key, or the binding attests content the auditor never reviewed and the cache keys on foreign content. The scoping is deliberately not the degenerate `HEAD^{tree}`: an unstaged tracked modification must still be captured, or every dirty lane would bind a base-identical (and cross-lane shared) identity — exactly the tree-drift and fresh-base cache collision the verdict-cache guard exists to prevent. A lane whose only difference from its base is unrelated residue must keep its base identity exactly, since an empty declared selection manufacturing a "changed" identity is what `ProbeEligible` reads to decide cacheability. Pinned by `TestWorktreeContentSHA_ExcludesUnrelatedUntrackedResidue`, `TestWorktreeContentSHA_StagesDeclaredNewFile`, `TestWorktreeContentSHA_CapturesUnstagedTrackedModification`, `TestWorktreeContentSHA_ResidueOnlyWorktreeKeepsBaseIdentity`, `TestEmitPhaseBindings_AuditBindingTreeExcludesUnrelatedResidue`, `TestWorktreeContentSHA_BindsTheTreeTheAuditSealed`, `TestWorktreeContentSHA_WithoutAWorkspaceBindsNothing` and `TestEmitPhaseBindings_AuditBindingHoldsTheDeclaredUntrackedDeliverable`.
- **`phaseAgentName` is a static phase→agent name table, not a lookup through the phase packages.** `core` cannot import `internal/phases/*` to read a phase's own `AgentPromptName()` (those packages already import `core`, so it would be an import cycle), so this package-private table is the cycle-safe single source for resolving `.evolve/profiles/<agent>.json` inside `core`; each value strips the `evolve-` prefix from the phase package's own `AgentPromptName()`, matching the convention `phases/runner/runner.go` uses. A phase with no LLM agent (e.g. the native ship phase) is intentionally absent — a miss resolves to a nil profile, `ValidatePin`'s documented nil-profile pass-through, rather than fabricating a wrong mapping. Since L1b every profile lookup in `core` goes through `phaseProfile`, which tries this table and then the `<phase>.json` convention, so a minted phase outside the table finds its own profile and a phase with neither still resolves to nil (2026-10-06, ADR-0119). Pinned by `TestPhaseAgentName_ValuesResolveToCheckedInProfiles`, which asserts every value resolves to a real profile file on disk, so a rename desync fails loudly.
- **Threading the cycle goal into the advisor's `RouteInput.GoalText` is the precondition for goal-aware planning** — selecting or minting a design phase. Pinned by `TestOrchestrator_ThreadsGoalTextToPlanner`.
- **Routing `Stage=Shadow` computes and ledgers a routing decision every iteration but never overrides the static state machine's phase transitions.** Pinned by `TestOrchestrator_Shadow_LogsTesterInsert_StaticPathUnchanged`.
- **A trivial-cycle digest makes `tdd` skippable; the kernel-validated override (`CanTransition` + `SpineSatisfiedUpTo`) is what adopts the router's scout→build shortcut.** Pinned by `TestOrchestrator_Enforce_TrivialCycle_SkipsOptionalMiddle`.
- **Phase-timing entries stamp each phase's archetype (plan/build/evaluate/control) from the phasespec taxonomy** (single source) rather than a hand-maintained list, so evidence roll-ups can bucket cycle time into productive/checking/planning/recovery. Pinned by `TestPhaseTiming_ArchetypeClassified`.
- **Every `PhaseRequest` carries a run id** (the `RunCycle` loop, resume, and failure-learning retro) so the bridge can mint run-scoped tmux session names and the session registry can attribute ownership. Pinned by `TestCB5_EveryDispatchedPhaseCarriesRunID`, `TestCB5_ResumePathCarriesRunID` and `TestCB5_FailureLearningRetroCarriesRunID`.
- **The per-cycle challenge token** (8-byte hex, minted in `RunCycle`, threaded via `Context["challengeToken"]`) is consumed by scout to bind its eval and serves as the anti-replay fallback source. Pinned by `TestRunCycle_MintsChallengeToken` and `TestRunCycle_PreservesSuppliedChallengeToken`.
- **Audit/TDD-phase build-artifact churn recovery.** When a guarded phase (tdd/build/audit) leaves only tracked build-artifact paths (`go/evolve`, `go/bin/evolve`) dirty in the main tree, the orchestrator discards that churn there, re-checks, warns, and continues the cycle instead of aborting via the tree-diff guard; any other dirty path still aborts, and recovery never reverts non-artifact (operator) files. A TDD-phase leak of a tracked file is relocated into the active worktree and main is restored to HEAD. Pinned by `TestOrchestrator_AuditLeakRecover` (subtests `binary_churn_recovered`, `non_binary_leak_aborts`, `mixed_leak_still_aborts`) and `TestTDDLeakRecover`.
- **cwd vs write axis.** Every phase's subprocess dispatches with cwd = the cycle worktree (universal), but the write axis (role-gate + tree-diff guard + normalize) still keys off `worktreePhase`/`WorktreePhase` and a minted phase's `writes_source`, restricted to actual source writers — cwd is not a write permission. An advisor-minted phase defaults to write-capable unless it explicitly sets `writes_source:false`. When worktree provisioning fails in a sequential cycle, every phase dispatches with `Worktree=""` and the role-gate is left to block any write attempt loudly; a fleet lane defers instead (the `LaneDeferral` invariant below), because the bridge refuses every fleet launch that has no worktree. Pinned by `TestCB1_EveryDispatchedPhaseCarriesWorktree`, `TestCB1_WriteAxisUnchanged`, `TestCB1_ProvisioningFailureDegradesToEmptyWorktree`, `TestInsertedPhaseWritableInheritsWorktree` and `TestInsertedReadOnlyPhaseDoesNotGetWorktree`.
- **`WorktreePhase` reads the one list of source writers.** The list is `phasecontract.SourceWriterPhases`: build, tdd, `test-amplification` and `bug-reproduction`. The role guard lets each of them write into the active worktree, and `shipmanifest.ReportFiles` reads the report of each one. `TestSourceWriterPhases_AreTheRealTreeCatalogsSourceWriters` fails when the list differs from the phase specs that keep `writes_source`. Pinned also by `TestWorktreePhase` and `TestRole_EverySourceWriterPhaseMayWriteItsLaneWorktreeAndAReaderMayNot`.
- **`discardMainLeak` requires a tracked path**: it shells out to `git checkout HEAD`, so it errors on an untracked file even if gitignored. Pinned by `TestDiscardGitignore`.
- **Phase-outcome single chokepoint.** Every terminal phase dispatch — happy advance or any abort (retry exhaustion, non-canonical verdict, review-gate reject, tree-guard abort, ledger-append failure) — records exactly once: `PhasesRun` membership, a `phase-timing.json` entry and a `<phase>-usage.json`, carrying the phase's own verdict (synthesized FAIL, never invented PASS) and a non-empty `abort_reason` on aborts. Pinned by `TestPhaseOutcome_TreeGuardAbort_RecordsBuildOutcome`, `TestPhaseOutcome_AbortPaths_AlwaysRecordTimingAndUsage`, `TestPhaseOutcome_NeverInventsPass` and `TestPhaseOutcome_SingleChokepoint_OneRecordPerDispatch`.
- **Control-plane recovery routing.** A `CONTROL_PLANE_VIOLATION` ship error recovers via BUILD (owner of the diff) instead of the generic precondition route's re-audit, avoiding an infinite re-audit loop against the same rejected diff. Pinned by `TestRunCycle_ShipControlPlaneViolation_RebuildsThenShips`.
- **Deterministic failure-learning floor.** When the LLM retro degrades (runner error or non-canonical verdict), the orchestrator still writes `retrospective-report.md` plus a failure-lesson YAML under `.evolve/instincts/lessons/`, instead of only a stderr warning. Pinned by `TestRecordFailureLearning_RetroRunnerError_WritesDeterministicArtifacts` and `TestRecordFailureLearning_NonCanonicalVerdict_WritesDeterministicArtifacts`.
- **`optionalInfraSkip` consults the configured mandatory phase set, not a hardcoded ship check**, so any mandatory phase — not just ship — is protected from the infra skip even when its catalog spec marks it Optional. Pinned by `TestMandatoryPhaseNeverInfraSkipped` and `TestShipNeverInfraSkipped`.
- **A phase minted at cycle start becomes both dispatchable and routable with no recompile** — the same path a built-in phase takes. Pinned by `TestRegisterMintedPhases_MakesPhaseDispatchableAndRoutable`.
- **`recordPhaseOutcome` is the one production chokepoint that derives the context-fill projection.** `phaseOutcomeFrom` copies `ResolvedModel` (the tier) and `Tokens` off the `PhaseResponse`; `recordPhaseOutcome` then calls `contextfill.WindowSizeForTier(out.ResolvedModel)` — an unresolvable tier (a concrete model id, empty, or unknown string) yields `contextfill.ErrInvalidWindow` and both `ContextFillRatio`/`ContextWindowHot` stay zero rather than a guessed window. Pinned by `TestRecordPhaseOutcome_ProjectsContextFillWhenTierResolvable`, `TestRecordPhaseOutcome_ColdPhaseIsNotFlaggedHot`, `TestRecordPhaseOutcome_UnresolvableTierLeavesContextFillZero`, `TestRecordPhaseOutcome_ZeroTokensRecordsZeroFillNotHot` and `TestPhaseOutcomeFrom_CarriesTierProvenanceAndTokens`.
- **`RetroDecision` is prefix+suffix composed.** When a cycle has no `disposition.json`, the disposition gate prepends a `disposition-gate:` prefix reason, with the underlying branch decision (e.g. `proceed:`) carried as a suffix. Pinned by `TestOrchestrator_RetroPASS_DoesNotRouteToShip`.
- **Recurring audit-fail disposition has two modes**: strict mode blocks on 2 distinct `code-audit-fail` records; fluent mode (the default) proceeds with awareness and ends the cycle. Pinned by `TestOrchestrator_RetroFAIL_RecurringAudit_FluentEnd`.
- A debugger that served a fleet-rebase recovery hands its resolved tree back to the rebase route (`resumeFleetRebaseAfterDebugger`): the tree is carried as a commit on the fork point, rebased, pended and routed via `routeRebasedExplanation`; the debugger's own target is honored for every other recovery (`ship_recovery_debugger.go`).
- **The fleet-rebase recovery** (`recoverFromShipError`, `ship_recovery.go`, ADR-0049, ADR-0105). It is a chain of steps with no LLM.
  - A contention error (a sibling lane moved main) waits a jittered backoff before the recovery, so lanes in lockstep do not collide again. Other errors recover at once.
  - The pre-screen `ClassifyFleetRebaseCandidate` runs before the replay. A candidate that main already holds ends with no replay and no audit. A pre-screen error does not stop the recovery: the rebase runs and reports its own failure.
  - A predicted conflict gets no unwind. The rebase finds the same conflict, and one code path routes it to the debugger.
  - The rebase replays the lane onto the new main. A conflict only in generated projections (for example `control-flags.md`) is regenerated from the merged source. A conflict on any other path is overlapping work and goes to the debugger.
  - After a real unwind, the recovery pends the change whatever the replay did. An aborted replay leaves the carrier, and the pend restores the audited shape that the debugger and a re-ship expect.
  - A legacy cycle (no explanation contract) tries RUNG 2, and else goes to Audit with a WARN that names the reason. RUNG 2 reviews only the hunks that intersect, and its resolution must match the audited patch-id (`TestRecoverFromShipError_ALegacyCleanRebaseIsAuditedAgainForAStatedReason`, `TestRunCycleFromPhase_ALegacyRebaseWhoseMainMovedIsAuditedAgainForAStatedReason`).
  - RUNG 0 (`compositionCarryForward`, the `trivial-rebase` carry) is retired since 2026-10-21. Its snapshot diffed `main...git_head`, and `git_head` is the tip of main when the audit was bound, not the audited change. Thus it did not carry a real rebase. The live ledger held 45 carry records, and all were `identical-rebase` records of the identity carry (ADR-0105 B3). Every new cycle has an explanation contract, so the identity carry serves the callers of RUNG 0.
  - The recovery calls `router.Recover` directly, so it works when no routing strategy is wired. Every target of the recovery chain is a legal edge from ship in the default graph (`TestRecover_EveryRecoveryRouteIsALegalEdgeFromShip`). The recovery still refuses an illegal edge, because `WithLegalGraph` can change the graph (`TestRecoverFromShipError_ARouteTheStateMachineForbidsAborts`).
- **The rebased explanation** (`routeRebasedExplanation`). It moves the base binding to the fork point. When the host proves the change byte-identical, the approved Build stays: the carry ships it, else Audit runs again. Otherwise the snapshot is invalidated and Build writes the explanation again. Only a change pending on the fork point is rebound, because Audit reads `git diff HEAD`. A committed change goes to Build, whose normalisation pends it.
- **The unwind** (`unwindBeforeFleetRebase`, `ship_recovery_unwind.go`). It takes ship's inbox consumption out of the change that the rebase replays, so the rebased change is the one that Audit reviewed. `unwindShipCommit` puts one carrier commit of the audited tree on the audited base, or says why it declined and changes nothing. `consumptionDecline` holds only when HEAD differs from the audited tree by whole consumption pairs. A pair is an item removed from the inbox root and written to `consumed/` under the same name.
- **A continuation lane reaches the identity proof** (ADR-0128, plan component Q12). The B1 unwind declines for a consumed item that released a continuation, and `unwindBeforeFleetRebase` returns `unwindKeepsConsumption`, because the pointer is only in ship's commit. After a clean rebase, the recovery pends ship's replayed commit on the fork point. The rebind (`explanationdocs.RebindIdenticalRebase`) does not count ship's consumption in the identity, and the released continuation stays in the pending change. The identity carry declines that tree, because ship re-proves the carry on the bytes, so the route is Audit, not Build. When the proof cannot run, `routeRebasedExplanation` writes `identity proof skipped` and emits `ORCHESTRATOR_IDENTITY_PROOF_SKIPPED` with `fields.reason`, not "not proven identical".
- **`ResumeOptions` has seams with production defaults.** `MainAhead` counts the commits of `origin/main` past a base (`git rev-list --count`). `Now` is the clock, `PathExists` is `os.Stat`, and `Log` takes the discovery lines (discarded when nil). `MaxAge` comes from policy `resume.max_checkpoint_age_h` when it is zero.
  - `AllowHeadMoved` waives the age check. `LoadResumeState` sets it when the operator names the state file in `EVOLVE_CYCLE_STATE_FILE`, which is the deliberate override. No env flag sets it (`EVOLVE_RESUME_ALLOW_HEAD_MOVED` was retired in cycle 15).
- **The orchestrator is a registered listener of the Signal Center, and `recordPhaseOutcome` is the first producer** (`signal.go`, ADR-0101 S1). Listeners observe and never decide: `observeSignal`'s summary is reporting evidence only — verdicts, transitions and halts stay with the floors. Lock order: the Center holds no lock while delivering, so `observeSignal` takes only `signalMu`; no orchestrator path holds `signalMu` across an `Emit`, and `observeSignal` never emits, so there is no feedback loop. `signalMu` also makes `SignalSummary` safe to read from a reader goroutine (`cmd_loop`) while the Center's drain concurrently delivers phase outcomes; pinned under `-race` by `TestSignalSummary_IsSafeToReadWhileTheCenterDelivers`. A WARN or FAIL `phase.outcome`/`cycle.sealed` event renders through the one stderr-sink line format, replacing what used to be hand-written at each chokepoint.
- **`signal_cycle.go`'s producers are each an Adapter from a typed value the pipeline already owns to one Event, emitted at the chokepoint that owns the fact**: `cycle.sealed` + `system.failure` at `completeCycle` (the closeout both dispatch roots share, plus the abnormal epilogue for a cycle that died mid-phase), `ship.error` at `recordShipError`, `quota.paused` at the quota pause (`pauseForQuota`, both roots). Producers observe; every decision stays where it was.
- **A phase's WARN/FAIL `phase.outcome` event carries its error-severity diagnostic codes** in `fields.diagnostic_codes`, comma-joined; an uncoded FAIL carries no such field. Pinned by `TestEmitPhaseOutcome_FailCarriesTheDiagnosticCodes` and `TestEmitPhaseOutcome_UncodedFailCarriesNoCodesField`.
- **`emitShipError` projects only the triage whitelist (`shiperr.SignalDebugKeys`) from a `ShipError`'s Debug map into the event's fields, never any other Debug key, and never an empty whitelisted value.** Pinned by `TestEmitShipError_ProjectsSignalDebugKeys`.
- **The orchestrator holds the ship-window lease only for a fleet-rebase recovery** (`shipwindow_hold.go`, since 2026-10-19). An audit PASS takes no lease, because the ship phase takes it over its binding check and its push ([internal-phases-ship](internal-phases-ship.md)). `TestRecordAndBranch_AnAuditPassLeavesTheShipWindowFreeForASibling` (integration tier) pins it.
  - `recoverFromShipError` and `resumeFleetRebaseAfterDebugger` take the lease (`holdShipWindow`) before the rebase onto `main`. The lease then covers the rebase, the composed-tree gates of the identity carry or the composition carry, and the re-ship.
  - `keepShipWindowOnlyForShip` keeps the lease only for a route to Ship. A route to Audit, Build or the end releases it, so no LLM phase holds it.
  - `runPhase` runs the ship in a context marked with `shipwindow.WithHeld`, so the ship does not take the lease again. It releases the lease when the ship returns. `RunCycle` and the resume loop also release it on exit.
  - A canceled wait WARNs and the recovery goes on unleased. A break of a live holder at the TTL emits `ORCHESTRATOR_SHIP_WINDOW_LEASE_BROKEN`.
  - Pinned by `TestRecoverFromShipError_HoldsTheShipWindowFromTheRebaseThroughTheReShip`, `TestRecoverFromShipError_ARebaseRoutedToAuditReleasesTheShipWindow`, `TestRunPhase_OnlyTheShipRunsInsideAHeldWindowAndReleasesIt`, `TestKeepShipWindowOnlyForShip_KeepsTheHoldOnlyForAReShip`, `TestHoldShipWindow_ACanceledWaitRecoversUnleasedWithAWarn`, `TestHoldShipWindow_BreakingAHungHolderSignals` and `TestReleaseShipWindow_AReleaseFailureWarnsAndClearsTheHold`.

## Core file group 3 rationale

The reasons that the comments of the decision, disposition, dossier, failure-learning, lane-scope, leak-recovery, scoped-merge and mint-exemption files carried. The code and the tests state the what.

- **Decision-branch reason strings are a contract.**
  - The reason prefix (`proceed:`, `retry-with-fallback:` and the rest) is grepped by dashboards and scenario pins.
  - When the advisor agrees with the deterministic decision, the deterministic reason string is kept.
  - A rerun target clamps to the upstream phases (audit, build, tdd). Re-shipping is the dedicated RESHIP action. Thus `rerun_phase: ship` must not become a reship that skips the precondition.
  - A nil validation plan writes `[]`, so a validated-clean plan differs from validation that never ran.
- **Disposition vocabulary.**
  - An out-of-vocabulary value is rejected with the offending field named, so a document that parses as JSON still fails the gate.
  - The routing term is `console`, not `escalate`: one vocabulary with the route field of ADR-0074 and the plan-time gate.
  - The seeded skeleton leaves `Reason` empty, so the auditor's upgrade is a value edit and not a shape edit.
  - Seeding never clobbers an existing disposition file.
  - The defect-ledger gate is the loud enforcement.
- **Dossier and digest identity.**
  - The dossier producer takes phase timings as a parameter. `RunCycle` writes `phase-timing.json` in a deferred call that lands after the producer. A re-read then finds no phases.
  - The dossier builder has no `ContractVerifier`, so `DeliverableValid` stays false and the live floor owns reconcile.
  - `backfillFailReasons` reads only orchestrator-authored phase-timing state, never an agent-writable file, so a failure identity cannot be forged from the workspace.
  - Display and identity are two projections of one reason string. The digest, the dossier and `audit-fail-reason.json` keep the reason verbatim. Only the hash input is normalized.
  - The fingerprint carries the phase as a prefix and as a hash input, so two failures that differ only in phase never share an id.
  - The boilerplate router and epilogue templates are single sources shared with their detectors, so the writer and the content-free check cannot drift.
  - The boilerplate patterns are anchored at both ends, so appended text makes a reason content-bearing.
  - Cause chains grow prefix-first, so the cause head is tail-kept.
- **Failure advisor trust boundary.**
  - The model sees the neutralized pane, but the promoted `pane_substr` is matched against RAW panes. A quoted neutralization artifact then promotes a signature that can never fire.
  - `parseFailureAdvice` applies the checks of `recovery.PromoteAdvice` early, so a bad verdict fails at the parse with the raw output in the error.
  - The hook scans the agent-stripped pane, so a novel wedge that quotes a seeded signature in diff content is not read as already classified.
  - It passes an empty prompt on purpose: the echo half is neutered by the protect list.
  - A pane the registry already classifies gets no LLM consultation.
  - The advise timeout is long enough to learn one novel signature. Each promotion saves about 20 minutes of `maxExtends` burn. The abort never hangs on a wedged advisor.
  - A composition-root enforce flip with no adviser wired skips the advise-then-promote path silently. Thus `FailureAdviserWired` exists.
  - The clamp in `router.ClampPlanModelRouting` short-circuits on a nil lookup, hence `ModelCatalogLookupWired`.
- **Failure learning.**
  - `phaseOutcomeFrom` records a canonical agent verdict as-is and synthesizes FAIL for anything else, so a synthesized PASS is structurally impossible.
  - `recordPhaseOutcome` is the one chokepoint, so `PhasesRun`, `phase-timing.json` and `<phase>-usage.json` agree.
  - The learning gate orders incomplete, canceled and quota-deferred.
  - A cancellation is an operator stop, and an all-families quota exhaustion is a resume checkpoint: neither is evidence that the phase failed.
  - The cancel check comes BEFORE the quota sentinel.
  - A ship dispatch explanation is preserved for the coherence floor even when the quota boundary skips learning.
  - A missing persona doc is a known configuration absence and learns deterministically with no LLM dispatch.
  - `completeRetro` is a divergent twin of `phaseCompletionRecord.persist`, kept and named. A PASS retro must still deliver a valid disposition that agrees with the digest. If not, the completion surfaces a gate reason.
  - An out-of-band retro keeps the no-main-tree-cwd invariant.
  - The recorder and the learning engine are built lazily for an orchestrator assembled as a literal. The clock goes through a closure and the Center through an accessor. Tests swap `o.now` and apply `WithSignalCenter` after construction.
- **Final-verdict floor.**
  - Only an authoritative phase (the resolved ship floor plus ship) sets `FinalVerdict`.
  - A later non-floor phase must not clobber it: its non-PASS outcome is appended to `VerdictsNotAdopted`.
  - A non-floor phase degrades to SKIPPED with a WARN only after the floor passed.
  - Before that, an unparseable verdict of scout, triage or intent stays cycle-fatal.
- **Lost-landing floor.**
  - An absent or unreadable `ship-error.json` means ship never ran.
  - Any other reading invents a landing loss on every pre-ship cycle.
- **Lane scope.**
  - `scoutArtifactName` derives from the phasecontract registry. A frozen copy degrades goal-hash normalization into its fail-open branch when the registry moves. That is a lane-identity bug that looks like an absent report.
  - `LaneScope` is an alias of the leaf declaration because exactly one non-test file may declare the wire tags.
  - A broken or empty pin falls back to the legacy env fleet scope with a WARN. It never aborts. It never yields a silently empty contract.
  - The scope is pinned to disk BEFORE any phase output exists.
  - The LAST fenced JSON block with a `goal_hash` wins, since the Decision Trace is the final block of a report.
- **Leak recovery.**
  - The deliverable-path test matches a directory prefix but needs exact equality for a file, so a look-alike name cannot false-positive.
  - `-uall` lists every untracked file, so leaks relocate at file granularity.
  - A tracked-edit relocation assumes the worktree copy is at HEAD (the caller checks), so it never clobbers independent work.
  - `git checkout HEAD -- p` resets index and tree, so a staged-only leak is discarded too.
  - A non-zero git exit comes back as `(c != 0, e == nil)`. The code branches on it. It never wraps a nil error, because that renders `<nil>` and breaks `errors.Is`.
  - `git check-ignore -q` exits 0 when ignored and 1 when not.
  - A launch error on `git diff --quiet` counts as not clean, so the caller takes the conservative discard path.
  - `moveFile` copies and removes when `os.Rename` fails with EXDEV across mounts.
  - Build artifacts a builder may rebuild into the main tree, non-deliverable `.evolve/` state at any depth, and bare directory entries from `-uall` are legitimate.
  - The evolve deliverable prefixes (`evals/`, `phases/`, `commit-prefix-scope.json`) flow only through worktree-based phases and must never escape.
- **Mint exemption.**
  - Registry names are attacker-influenceable path segments, so the kebab-case floor runs BEFORE any filesystem access, and a forged `../x` never leaves `.evolve/phases/`.
  - The spec read is capped at 1 MiB against a planted giant file.
  - A mint writes EXACTLY `phase.json`: a companion payload still fires the guard.
  - With the OS sandbox off, any on-disk `phase.json` is forgeable, so the exemption trusts clamp parity and not provenance.
  - It reads after `Lstat`, so a FIFO, a symlink or an oversized file is never read.
- **Scoped merge review (rung 2).**
  - The reviewer is injected so the core stays pure, and a nil reviewer leaves the rung dark.
  - An empty intersection is compatible and wastes no review.
  - A malformed diff or hunk header fails closed: a non-nil error, no reviewer call, no partial result.
  - A resolution is trusted by recomputed patch-id and never on the reviewer's word.
  - A zero-count old-side range is a unit-width point, so an insertion still overlaps a change that spans it.
  - `scopedReviewMethod` mirrors the ledger constant because core cannot import the ledger adapter, and the two strings must stay identical.
- **Errors.**
  - `ErrArtifactTimeout` covers exit 81.
  - Exit 127 (a missing binary) is NOT transient: an absent CLI is an environment defect and must fail loud.
  - `isArtifactTimeout` must never widen to the teardown union: it feeds only the exit-code and delivery-cause attribution of the failure-diagnostic writer.
  - `ErrUnsafeConfig` marks a flow that can ship without the integrity floor (ADR-0060).
- **Git seam.**
  - `gitRunner` is the one git-execution seam of the package, so tests fake git.
  - A dirty-path error propagates so the guard degrades to snapshot missed and never misreports leaks.
- **Identity carry-forward (ADR-0105 B3).**
  - After the rebind proved the pended change identical, the carry proves the same on the trees and runs the composed-tree gates under the fence.
  - Any doubt returns false and the change is audited again.
  - The gates count only when they ran on the tree that ship commits.
- **Judgment lessons.**
  - `plan-review` is listed although it has no catalog phase today, so the lesson works the day it becomes one.
- **ADR pointers moved here:**
  - ADR-0039 (audit-declared envelope), ADR-0044 (failure advisor and recorder), ADR-0045, ADR-0048 (shadow stage), ADR-0052 (agent identity), ADR-0072 (failure decision and dossier).

## Core file group 4 rationale

The reasons that the comments of the orchestrator, phase advisor, phase binding, self-check and graduation files carried. The code and the tests state the what.

- **Orchestrator wiring.**
  - `PhaseBoundaryCheckpointer` and `QuotaBoundaryCheckpointer` are package-level hooks set by the checkpoint package, because core cannot import it without a cycle.
  - `specFor` canonicalizes the phase name before the lookup, so the core and router spellings of one phase cannot miss each other.
    - The registry is the single source of truth and wins over `builtinControlSpec`.
    - `builtinControlSpec` only supplies the debugger's signal-driven successor for a phase with no registry entry.
  - `successorStrategy` degrades to `literalSuccessorStrategy` on a catalog miss. Config selects among branches the state machine already deems legal and never invents one.
  - A `PhaseMinter` must return a spec with `Optional=true`. A minted phase can never satisfy or displace the build, audit and ship floor.
  - `CycleRequest.Env` and `Context` are copied, so a mutation after `RunCycle` starts never reaches an in-flight or completed run.
  - `legacyExplanationTestStorage` is package-private on purpose. Only core's same-package legacy fake may opt out of the fresh-cycle explanation contract.
    - `explanationContractVersion` has no exported option, so production callers cannot downgrade the mandatory contract.
  - `gitHEAD` is not evidence that this cycle shipped, because a fleet sibling can move HEAD too. The outcome label reads the ship latch, and a HEAD read error counts as "no movement".
  - `gitDirtyPaths` feeds the tree-diff guard, which snapshots it before and after each source-writing phase to catch a leak that escaped the sandbox.
  - `safetyViolations` is computed once at construction over the wired state machine, config and catalog. A non-empty result makes `RunCycle` and `RunCycleFromPhase` fail closed with `ErrUnsafeConfig` before any phase runs, so an unsafe registry edit can never run a phase.
  - `shipFloor` can only relax build and tdd. The router self-seals the non-removable evaluator regardless.
  - `signalMu` is the first production mutex in core. The Signal Center holds no lock while delivering, `observeSignal` never emits, and no orchestrator path holds `signalMu` across an `Emit`.
  - `currentRunID` is read atomically by the construction-time stamping ledger on every append, so goroutine-spawning observers read the run identity race-free. An empty value means no run is in flight and entries are not stamped.
  - `WithWorktreeBase` and `WithWorktreeProvisioner` both set `o.worktree`. Production uses only the base override and tests use only the fake.
  - `CatalogPublisherWired` exists because without a publisher a mid-cycle mint never reaches the live contract resolver.
  - `HasRunner` is the composition root's read seam, because a phase the router nominates without a runner is silently skipped by the missing-runner escape hatch.
- **Nil wiring defaults.** Each optional collaborator has a defined meaning when unset.
  - A nil `planner` gives no advisor plan, so the kernel floor falls back to the never-skip spine. The planner is consulted once at cycle start, only at `Stage>=Advisory`, and its output is always clamped to the integrity floor.
  - A nil `reviewer` is `noopReviewer`: every non-error, non-SKIPPED verdict is a success. When a reviewer returns `Approve=false`, the cycle aborts with its reason.
  - A nil `observer` is `noopObserver`. `Start` is called once before each `runner.Run` and the returned cancel runs once after.
  - A nil `contractVerifier` gives the salvage rung zero budget, so the correction ladder degrades to redispatch only.
  - A nil `failureAdviser` leaves the hook inert. It is consulted only at `cfg.PhaseRecovery == StageEnforce`, and only for unclassified artifact-timeout panes.
  - A nil `directivesProvider` means no directives. The provider is fail-open: it returns a possibly empty set and never an error.
  - The identity carry and RUNG 2 never fire unless all three composition seams are set. A nil `scopedMergeReviewer` leaves RUNG 2 dark.
  - A nil `catalogRefresh` skips the refresh, and a refresh error only WARNs and never blocks the cycle.
  - A nil `modelCatalogLookup` skips the resolvability gate, while guardrail validation still runs.
  - A nil `retryAdjudicator` is a supported production state: policy alone is enough authority to grant a retry.
  - A nil `failureCountFor` disables retry tier escalation, and a nil `continuationFor` means continuations never adopt.
  - The retry tier escalation counts the ids of the cycle claims (`processingClaimIDs`). An unreadable claim file or claim dir prints one WARN line on the stderr of the orchestrator, and the readable ids still count (`TestProcessingClaimIDs_NamesAnUnreadableClaimFile`, `TestProcessingClaimIDs_AnUnreadableClaimDirIsAWarnLine`).
  - An empty `catalog` means only built-in phases exist. A nil `registrar` ignores `MintPhases`.
- **Ordering in `NewOrchestrator` and `RunCycle`.**
  - The outcome recorder reads the clock and the catalog live, because tests swap `o.now` after construction and mints change the catalog mid-cycle.
  - The failure-learning engine is built after `o.carry`, because it mints through the one wired carryover lifecycle.
  - The state machine receives its config-driven verdict-branch resolution only after the options settle the catalog. An empty catalog or spine order degrades to the literal table.
  - The run-id stamping decorator wraps the ledger exactly once, at construction.
  - `newCycleRun` returns a cleanup closure that `RunCycle` defers in its own frame, so the exit actions fire at cycle exit in LIFO order.
  - `cycleRun` is the one addressable home for the dispatch loop's state. Late mutations by sub-methods stay visible to the exit defers and the next iteration.
  - The exit defers keep these rules.
    - The worktree preserve decision reads `cr.preserveWorktree` and `cr.cycleCompletedNormally` at defer-execution time.
    - No exit path leaves a started cycle without its evidence trail.
    - A graceful interrupt resumes the phase that was active. That defer runs before the abnormal epilogue marks the cycle aborted.
  - The fresh-cycle marker is persisted before the first phase. A crash before Build then cannot turn a new cycle into a legacy exemption from the explanation contract.
  - Phase timings are written in a deferred call that also runs when `RunCycle` returns an error, so partial timing data survives for inspection. Interaction outcomes are recorded on abort paths too.
  - The content-addressed audit-reuse probe is shadow only. It runs before the loop, against a preserved or re-dispatched worktree. A fresh worktree skips the lookup to prevent fresh-base collisions.
  - The loop is labeled `OuterLoop`. The extracted sub-methods signal termination with `loopBreak`, and a bare `break` inside the switch ladder exits the switch and not the loop.
  - Triage continuation adoption may replace both worktree and review base, so the final context is sealed once before the first Build dispatch. Adoption is keyed to the actual claim, since triage claims items into `processing/cycle-N` mid-cycle.
  - The post-scout re-plan fires once, after scout's handoff is recorded and before the next `selectNext`. A re-plan thus cannot widen the run set or bypass the spine gate.
  - A deterministic gate that FAILed gets one bounded builder fix and a same-gate re-run. Nothing downstream is bypassed, and the same gate must pass with the audit and ship floors unchanged.
  - Exhausting the iteration budget records an explicit abort naming the stalled phase, so the escape is diagnosable and not `FAILED_UNEXPLAINED`. It runs before `finalizeCycle`, so the recorded FAIL preserves the worktree for salvage.
- **Phase advisor.**
  - All advisor output is advisory.
    - The pure `router.Route()` clamp re-validates it against the kernel floor (mandatory spine, TDD pin, ship needs a real audit).
    - A hallucinated or malformed proposal thus cannot weaken the ship guarantee.
    - Any failure returns an error and the caller degrades to the deterministic static path.
  - `brain` is built once under a `sync.Once`. A bare nil-check is a read-check-write race for a `PhaseAdvisor` built as a literal and first used concurrently.
    - A nil bridge stays nil, so the legacy "nil bridge" fail-safe survives.
  - `wiredAdvisor` is the one construction site of the leaf (`TestPhaseAdvisor_OneConstructionSite`).
    - `bridgeRequestOf` is the one projection of the leaf's request onto the bridge request.
    - `ChainAttempt` marks each launch as one step of the advisor's own chain walk. A chain-walking bridge handle passes it through and does not walk it again.
  - `WithAdvisorSignals` is construction-time DI. The composition root builds the Signal Center before the advisor in the same function.
  - An empty `WithPersona` falls back to the legacy inline framing as a fail-safe.
    - The CLI and model fallback is deep (opus), because composing a cycle and inventing phases is deep-reasoning work.
    - The composition root normally overrides both from the router profile.
  - `writeArtifactAtomically` writes through a temp file and a rename. A capture artifact is thus either absent or complete.
    - The ledger's disk-read SHA equals the span's in-memory SHA.
    - It is single-writer per workspace and kind per cycle, so the fixed `.tmp` suffix cannot collide.
  - `benchedCLIsForRouting` sorts by family for a deterministic, prompt-prefix-cache-friendly prompt. CLI health is advice and never a planning prerequisite, so an unreadable store yields an empty list.
  - The recon is advisory and fails open.
    - `recentChangeCommits` bounds the git-log window.
    - `reconGitTimeout` bounds the git subprocess, so a hung repo cannot stall plan composition.
    - Duplicate paths are kept, because frequency is the churn signal.
    - The git I/O lives in core and is injected into the leaf.
  - The `Deprecated` test facades (`composePlanPrompt`, `sanitizeAdvisorTier`, `writeCatalog`, `mintConfigsFrom` and the rest) stay because ACS-named tests and the protected guard tests call them. Production goes through `Plan` and `RePlan`.
- **Provenance bindings.**
  - FAIL is bound. Without a binding, ship's lookup has nothing for the run and the FAIL verdict is invisible to the gate built to enforce it.
    - SKIPPED stays excluded, since no audit ran and there is no artifact to bind.
  - The phase-agnostic binding for user and inserted phases is dormant until `EVOLVE_PHASE_IO=enforce`. The default loop thus emits exactly the audit and build bindings it always did.
  - `phaseRole` maps audit to `auditor` and build to `builder`, the strings ship's audit binding (`findLatestAudit`) and the `rt-001-ledger-role-completeness` red-team predicate require. It is total over all phases and pinned by `TestPhaseRole`.
  - `bindingInputs` carries the project root, workspace and worktree explicitly. The typed phase output holds the wire result and not the paths the git probes need.
    - `worktreeBase` is the correct operand for the verdict-cache fresh-base guard.
    - The project root HEAD at audit time can diverge under fleet concurrency.
  - `recordPhaseBinding` delegates audit and build to their specialized recorders unchanged, so their ledger bytes stay identical.
    - The bodies are genuinely asymmetric. Only audit computes a worktree-tree SHA, reads its artifact fatally, derives the exit code from the verdict and projects into the verdict cache.
    - The collapse is thus at the dispatch and role-naming layer and not a merged body.
  - `recordAuditBinding` writes the entry that ship's audit binding requires. `tree_state_sha` is the sha256 of `git diff HEAD`, byte-identical to ship's `computeTreeStateSHA`. `git diff HEAD` exits 1 when differences exist, which is not an error, and only an exit above 1 is fatal. The recorder is best-effort: a failure WARNs, and ship then fails loudly on the missing binding and never ships unbound.
  - The binding tree is the tree ship will commit from the builder-declared index, not the auditor's own `HEAD^{tree}`, which can never equal it. Nothing is committed (`write-tree` only).
  - `worktreeContentSHA` is the single source of three values: the audit binding's `WorktreeTreeSHA`, the composition entries' `TreeStateSHA` and the ADR-0048 verdict-cache key. Every recorded, looked-up and verified value is thus computed identically.
    - It returns an empty string when the worktree or workspace is empty or git fails.
    - Callers degrade: ship falls back to the auditor's value and the cache does not record or match.
    - An in-place worktree is the operator's tree, and a cycle never stages its index.
  - `recordBuildBinding` exists because the orchestrator's per-phase entry has role `build`, the phase name, and not `builder`, the agent name.
    - A cycle that goes through formal audit will otherwise false-FAIL provenance with "no role:builder entry".
    - The binding mirrors the audit one and WARNs loudly without blocking the cycle.
  - `recordGenericBinding` is reached only at `EVOLVE_PHASE_IO=enforce`, gated by `emitPhaseBindings`.
- **Build worktree normalization.**
  - `writeBuildDiffSummary` runs after the soft reset, gofmt and the refresh of the derived files, because each of them changes the diff. It writes the diff metrics and the `## Changes` table of `build-report.md` from `WorktreeBaseSHA` (`internal/builddiff`, [posthoc-schema.md](../posthoc-schema.md)). A failed derivation writes `absent` and a WARN line. A remediation round rewrites the report after the first build binding, so `maybeRemediate` binds the fixed build again (`TestRemediation_TheFixedBuildIsBoundAgainAfterTheHostRewritesItsReport`). Pinned by `TestWriteBuildDiffSummary_ANoOpCycleReportsZeroFilesFromItsBase`.
  - `normalizeWorktreeToBase` soft-resets to the cycle base so a committing builder's work becomes pending again. The builder commits for crash safety, but the auditor and the audit binding inspect the pending diff, which a commit empties. It never aborts the cycle and is a no-op when HEAD already equals the base.
  - A persisted base on the resume path can be stale after the operator rebased the worktree onto a moved main.
    - A soft reset to a non-ancestor repoints the branch and stages the whole delta between histories as a spurious diff.
    - It is thus skipped, and the manual recovery's "as-is" state stands.
  - `normalizeBuildWorktree` is shared by `RunCycle` and `RunCycleFromPhase`. The soft reset runs only after Build. The gofmt normalization runs after every worktree phase, because tdd, build and test-amplification all author Go that the audit gofmt gate scans.
  - An in-place worktree is the operator's tree: no soft reset, no `gofmt -w` and no projection regen.
  - The derived-projection regen is build-only and gated on the flag registry (the single source of truth) actually changing, so non-flag cycles pay no `go run` cost.
  - The covering-test corpus for test-amplification is derived after every worktree phase.
    - A resume past build never sees `PhaseBuild` complete in this process.
    - The phase then silently degrades to an unscoped whole-repo search.
    - The derivation is a no-op once the corpus exists.
  - The selfcheck runs after the regen so the tested tree matches what audit binds. When the build-floor reviewer is enforced, the same selfcheck already ran at the review seam, so the duplicate `go test` pass is skipped.
  - `changedWorktreePaths` covers tracked changes against HEAD and untracked new files. A cycle that adds a file under a projection's source prefix is thus not missed.
    - It splits on newlines and not `strings.Fields`, so a path with spaces stays one entry.
    - A path that is not a git repo yields no paths and no error.
  - `ChangedWorktreePaths` is a projection for `evolve phase verify build`, so the gate and the self-check judge the same diff. A second derivation in `internal/cli/phasecmd` can let them drift (ADR-0034).
  - `normalizeBuildGofmt` applies `gofmt -w -s` before the audit gofmt gate. Formatting is deterministic work and must not depend on the LLM builder remembering it.
    - It is scoped to the same module dir the audit gate scans (`codequality.ModuleDir`), so the two cannot disagree.
    - The worktree is cut from CI-clean main, so only this cycle's files can be dirty.
    - A gofmt failure WARNs and never aborts.
- **Build self-check.**
  - The self-check is best-effort and never aborts the cycle. It clears any prior `.evolve/build-selfcheck.json` first.
    - The toolchain gate (`go/acs/regression/buildselfcheck`) reads that artifact at audit and hard-fails on it.
    - Without the clear, a retry that fixes the build can see the previous run's failures and loop forever.
    - The clear sits above the early return for a cycle with no changed Go packages.
  - `realGoUnitTest` runs unit tests only, with no integration tag.
    - It scrubs the subprocess env with `ipcenv.Scrub`, so the campaign's runtime flags do not flip env-sensitive tests. An inherited `EVOLVE_FLEET=1` can flip `internal/bridge`'s fleet-mode worktree guard.
    - A bounded timeout keeps a wedged test from hanging the build phase.
  - A changed path whose package directory no longer exists means the diff deleted the package, so nothing is left to test. This is checked on the filesystem and not by output text, so a present package keeps its real verdict.
  - `goTestExcludedByBuildTags` separates a package whose files are all excluded by build tags from a real failure.
    - Every cycle materializes a `//go:build`-gated acceptance package (`acs/cycleN`). An untagged `go test` of one prints "build constraints exclude all Go files".
    - Flagging it will WARN every cycle and bury real signal.
    - The `imports ` guard keeps the check narrow. When a transitive dependency is the excluded one, `go test` prints an `imports <dep>:` chain and the tested package genuinely failed.
    - That case is still reported, as are compile errors and assertion failures.
  - A tag-gated package is invisible to the default context, so the tagged runner runs an added package under the tags its files declare.
  - `removeBuildSelfCheckArtifact` treats a missing file, the common case, as no error. A write error on the artifact is non-fatal because the WARN already surfaced it.
- **Graduation check.**
  - `buildGraduationCheck` flags a changed `go/internal/<pkg>` package that is new this cycle and absent from `go/.apicover-enforce`.
    - A modified pre-existing package is the enforce ratchet's concern.
    - A deleted or renamed-away package is not new. Flagging it makes graduation hygiene un-shippable.
    - It fails open when the worktree is empty or the enforce file is unreadable, and for a package it cannot prove new. Audit stays the backstop.
  - A test-only package has no exported production symbol, so the repo-wide apicover gate cannot fire on it. `packageHasProductionGoFiles` delegates to the shared predicate `ciparity.PackageDirHasProductionGoFiles`, so the two seams cannot disagree. The vacuous-obligation skip is announced, so a test-only mint that lands unenrolled shows in the cycle log and not at audit.
  - The abort reason lands on the recorded phase outcome (`AbortReason`), unlike the WARN-only unit-test self-check.
- **Test-suite constraints.**
  - `TestMain` zeroes the retry-backoff sleep for the whole suite, saving about 250 seconds of wall clock across the full-cycle retry tests. Only two sequential backoff tests mutate it afterward, and the Go runner drains sequential tests before launching parallel ones, so it stays race-safe.
  - The orchestrator test fakes stay local and do not move to `go/test/fixtures`. These white-box tests exercise unexported internals, and `fixtures` imports core, which creates an import cycle.
  - `insertedLeakRunner` lives in the untagged `orchestrator_testfakes_test.go`. The untagged spine-gate test references it, and a definition behind `//go:build integration` does not compile there.
  - `captureStderr` redirects the process-global `os.Stderr`, so callers must not be parallel.
  - Real-repo fixtures follow production.
    - The repo's `.gitignore` ignores `.evolve/*`. The orchestrator's own workspace writes never show in `git status`, so the clean-tree assertion trips only on the leak under test.
    - The git identity lives in the repo and not in the helper's env. `RunCycle`'s own git children (the dossier-closeout commit) do not inherit it, and CI runners have no ambient identity.
  - `initInsertedLeakRepo` commits one source file first, so the leaked path is a new untracked file, the shape the guard's untracked-only baseline must catch. The leak write uses `Fatalf` and not `Errorf`, because a half-executed write must abort at once or the test reports a spurious guard RED.
  - The real-worktree provisioner's cleanup is a no-op on purpose. A shipped cycle's worktree must survive for the post-run `git diff HEAD`, and `t.TempDir` handles teardown. The runner executes synchronously on the test goroutine inside `RunCycle`, so a plain bool is race-free there.
  - Binding-cache pins stage a tracked modification and not an untracked file.
    - `worktreeContentSHA` stages with `git add -u`, so an untracked file keeps the base identity.
    - That lets the FAIL-verdict pin pass vacuously through the fresh-base guard.
    - The WARN-verdict positive control exists so that a red control marks the FAIL pin's no-cache assertion as untrustworthy.
  - The staging-scope pins require that residue (an unrelated untracked file) never changes a worktree's identity. `ProbeEligible` reads that identity to decide if a verdict can be cached or reused.
    - A declared new file must be retained.
    - A tracked edit that was never staged must be captured.
    - Otherwise the cheapest fixes (bind the base tree, stage nothing) look correct.
  - The phase-input tests rely on `reflect.DeepEqual` against a zero literal, because `PhaseInput` seals channels behind unexported fields. That is valid because `assemblePhaseIO` returns before any `NewPhaseInput` call below enforce.
  - The tier-elicitation tests read the shipped `agents/evolve-router.md` persona through `runtime.Caller`, so the path is cwd-independent and points at the worktree copy. The floor of two existing-phase examples is the anti-delete guard: deleting the persona's example, leaving only the Go-appended one, must not green the golden.
  - A failing Planner under `RunCycle` emits no orchestrator code naming the plan, because the advisor reports its own fault. The verbatim `[orchestrator] WARN phase advisor Plan failed` line still prints.
  - An unregistered signal code is rewritten to `SIGNALCENTER_UNREGISTERED_CODE` with the raw spelling under `fields.raw_code`.

## Invariants

- **The flow seats read their declared files (I-8, X8).** The plan judge, the retry adjudicator and the failure advisor resolve `routing-judge.json`, `failure-adjudication.json` and `failure-advice.json` through `phasecontract.FlowDecision.Resolve`. Each seat takes a Signal Center option (`WithPlanJudgeSignals`, `WithRetryAdjudicatorSignals`, `WithFailureAdvisorSignals`), and `cycleRun.reportProseFallbacks` emits each router-digest prose fallback once for each cycle. The guard `TestFlowCode_OnlyTheLegacySpanFileNamesLastBalancedSpan` keeps the stdout span in `advisor/span.go`. Matrix row N12 is green at `enforce`. Spec: [llm-isolation.md](../llm-isolation.md) §12.1.
- **A cycle number is never reused.** The lease is minted before any run artifact exists, and a crashed run burns its number, because a burned number may have orphaned artifacts and reuse would mix two runs' state. Pinned by `TestAllocateCycleNumber_ConcurrentAllocatorsDistinct` and `TestAllocateCycleNumber_CrashBurnsNumber`.
- **The cycle-end persist never rolls back the lease.** A run's in-memory `LastAllocatedCycleNumber` is stale once a concurrent run allocates, so a blind write would let the next allocator re-mint a number another run holds. Pinned by `TestPersistCycleEndState_NeverRollsBackLease`. The outcome-record union is pinned by `TestMergeFailedRecords_UnionsDiskAndIncoming` and `TestMergeCarryoverTodos_DedupByID`.
- **Storage without `StateUpdater` behaves exactly as before the lease existed.** Pinned by `TestOrchestratorAllocateCycle_LegacyStorageFallsBack` and `TestPersistCycleEndState_LegacyStorageFallsBack`.
- **Deterministic evidence, then policy, then judgment.** The adjudicator chooses only among actions the envelope already allowed. It may be more conservative, never less, and it cannot overturn a floor halt. It is skipped when only one action is legal, so the deep tier is paid only where a real choice exists. A nil adjudicator (Null Object) still yields the policy default. Pinned by `TestDecideAfterAuditFail`, `TestDecideAfterAuditFail_AdjudicatorCannotOverturnTheFloor` and `TestDecideAfterAuditFail_NilAdjudicatorIsSafe`.
- **`audit→tdd` and `audit→build` are decision-only edges.** They sit in the one legality graph, so `decideAfterAuditFail` can schedule them and the graph stays single-sourced with `phase-registry.json` and the trust anchor. `decisionOnlyEdge` refuses them to the routing advisor, which validates its proposals through the same graph. An advisor-routed re-entry would never call `consumeAuditRepairGrant` (it keys on a reason prefix only `decideAfterAuditFail` emits), so the retry would go uncounted, bounded only by `defaultMaxPhaseIterations` (32) instead of `MaxRetries` (2). It could also route backwards after a halt, or override `audit→ship` on a passing audit. Pinned by `TestRouterCannotProposeTheAuditReentryEdges` and `TestAuditFailReentryEdgesAreLegal`.
- **The adjudicator's reasoning reaches the operator.** The clamp rejects an unjustified proposal because the justification is the adjudicator's deliverable, so the reason carries it, and a clamped proposal says it was clamped. Pinned by `TestDecideAfterAuditFail_SurfacesTheAdjudicatorsReasoning` and `TestDecideAfterAuditFail_RecordsAClamp`.
- **`bridgeRetryAdjudicator` returns nil on every failure path, unlike `FailureAdvisor` which returns an error to escalate.** The two are dispatched identically (bridge-dispatched, persona-injected, strict-JSON-parsed), but nil is a working answer here: the deterministic policy has already decided what is legal, and `clampAdjudication` turns a nil proposal into the policy default — the agent can only ever narrow a choice among already-permitted options, never grant a retry policy forbids or overturn the floor. `adjudicationWire` is kept as a separate strict-JSON wire type from the internal `adjudication` type so a wire change cannot silently alter the decision vocabulary. `parseAdjudication` reuses `phase_advisor.go`'s `lastBalancedSpan`, not a naive first-`{`/last-`}` slice, since a span from the first brace to the last one swallows any earlier object (reasoning prose, an example, a stray trailing `}`) and fails to parse, discarding a valid final answer.
- **The repair reason is built from `auditRepairReasonPrefix`, never a literal.** `TestAuditRepairReasonPrefix_IsSingleSourced` reads `audit_fail_decision.go` as text, so even a comment in that file must not spell the quoted literal `"audit-repair: "`.
- **Audit-round retirement ordering.** `supersedePreviousAuditRound` runs before the pre-phase cycle-state write, so a round that crashes or quota-pauses has already persisted its dispatch, and its dead attempt's pre-written verdict is retired on resume instead of honored. Round 0 retires nothing, so an operator or CI pre-staged verdict keeps the honor `audit.Classify` gives it. An existing archive is never overwritten, because the first retirement holds that round's evidence; the stale file is removed with a WARN instead. If a rename fails, the stale file is removed, because a surviving stale verdict silently recreates the cycle-1603 class. Every evidence-losing outcome is reported. Pinned by `TestSupersedePreviousAuditRound_AdvancesThePersistedDispatchCounter`, `TestResumedCrashedAudit_RetiresTheDeadAttemptsVerdict`, `TestRetireSupersededAuditArtifacts_RoundZeroHonorsPreStagedVerdict`, `TestRetireSupersededAuditArtifacts_NeverClobbersAnExistingArchive`, `TestAuditRedispatch_RetiresPreviousRoundVerdicts` and `TestResumedAuditRedispatch_RetiresPreviousRoundVerdicts`.
- **The repair brief derives from persisted cycle state.** The live loop and crash-resume therefore seed the same brief. Both surfaces pass the dispatched phase (`next`), and the seeder never mutates the caller's map, because the dispatch loop reuses `ctxSnap` across iterations and an in-place write would leak a stale brief into every later phase. `AuditRepairAttempts` is a monotonic counter, not a "currently repairing" flag, so gating on it would re-inject a finished repair into unrelated later dispatches. Pinned by `TestSeedAuditRepairContext`, `TestSeedAuditRepairContext_DoesNotMutateCallerMap` and `TestAuditRepairBrief_SeededOnBothDispatchSurfaces`.
- **A gate-derived failure class is read from orchestrator memory and never outranks the auditor or the regrade.** `gateFailureBlock` reads only `CycleState.AuditFailReasons`, so no workspace writer can mint a class or talk the floor out of a halt. The auditor's declared block wins over it. A regrade-eligible reason set (bookkeeping reasons and a conflict record only) can never be gate-only, because no table prefix is a bookkeeping reason, so one FAIL never takes both routes, and a gate beside a bookkeeping reason takes neither. Identical gate FAILs are bounded by the `code-audit-fail` budget (2 by default; the third declines with `retry budget spent for code-audit-fail (2/2)`); ADR-0126 §10 will stop a cycle earlier once one fingerprint is behind two backward edges, when the convergence policy is live. Pinned by `TestAuditGateRemedies_EveryGateDeclaresARepairableClassAndARemediation`, `TestAuditGateFail_IdenticalGateFailsAreBoundedByTheEnvelopeBudget`, `TestAuditGateFail_BookkeepingConflictStillTakesTheRegrade` and `TestAuditGateFail_GateBesideBookkeepingEarnsNeitherRoute`.
- **The bookkeeping regrade cannot be triggered or unbounded by an agent.** Eligibility reads `CycleState.AuditFailReasons`, orchestrator memory set at the `recordFloorVerdictFailure` chokepoint, never a workspace file. The once-per-cycle bound is `CycleState.BookkeepingRegradeAttempted`, consumed by the one primitive `consumeBookkeepingRegradeGrant` that both branch surfaces call. The regrade sits below the ADR-0072 floor and above the router. Pinned by `TestDecideAfterRetro_RegradeOncePerCycle`, `TestDecideAfterRetroRouted_RegradeNotRouterOverridable`, `TestDecideAfterRetro_FloorOutranksRegrade` and `TestRecordAndBranch_RegradeGrantConsumesSlotAndSchedulesAudit`.
- **Unexplained digests never count as identical.** Distinct failures collapse into the empty-evidence bucket (`|unknown|`) or the self-marked `Unexplained` bucket by construction, so the unexplained rule owns them. They do count toward the consecutive streak, which is fingerprint-agnostic by design; filtering them there would reintroduce the masking the rule exists to close. Pinned by `TestConsecutiveFailures_UnexplainedDigestsCountTowardTheStreak`.
- **The streak is keyed by cycle number.** Several digests from one cycle collapse to one entry, so a cycle that repaired twice cannot trip the ceiling-3 streak by itself. The audit-repair loop depends on this. Pinned by `TestBlockerBreaker_RepairAttemptsWithinOneCycleCountOnce` and `TestConsecutiveFailures_DuplicateDigestsForOneCycleCountOnce`.
- **The more specific rule names the halt.** `consecutive-failures` is evaluated last, so a guard-class or identical-fingerprint halt, whose reason carries an actionable repro hint, wins on overlap. Pinned by `TestConsecutiveFailures_GuardClassRuleWinsOnOverlap` and `TestConsecutiveFailures_IdenticalFingerprintRuleWinsOnOverlap`.
- **An ack is scoped to one fingerprint.** It excludes exactly that fingerprint from Rule B and breaks the streak, so a batch resumed to verify a fix does not re-halt on the history it is verifying. A different, unacked fingerprint still halts at the ceiling. Pinned by `TestEvaluateBlockerBreaker_UnackedIdenticalFingerprintStillHalts` and `TestConsecutiveFailures_AckCannotMaskAnIndependentlyTrippingHalf`.
- **Digest collection tolerates damage.** A missing or malformed `failure-digest.json` is skipped, because a PASS cycle writes none and one corrupt artifact must not disable the breaker for the rest of the batch. Pinned by `TestCollectBatchFailureDigests_ScopesAndTolerates`.
- **The consumption parser fails closed.** It anchors on the literal `fingerprint` token, so a `docs/a|b|c.md` path or any other triplet-shaped text never matches, and `ConsumePipelineDefectFingerprint` writes nothing when neither field names a fingerprint. Pinned by `TestParseConsumptionFingerprint_IgnoresUnrelatedPipeDelimitedText` and `TestConsumePipelineDefectFingerprint_ErrorsWhenNoFingerprintFound`.
- **Boot recovery never touches the loop's own state.** `.evolve/` and `knowledge-base/` churn is normal cycle activity and is never quarantined. `go/bin/evolve` is excluded twice: at classification, and again in the stash pathspec. Git may report a collapsed untracked parent directory (`go/`) that an exact-path exclusion cannot see. Because the same pass re-pins the ship binary, stashing it reverts to the old committed binary and reopens the SHA mismatch the re-pin heals. Pinned by `TestClassifyDirtyPaths_IgnoresLoopManaged`, `TestClassifyDirtyPaths_ExcludesShipBinary` and `TestQuarantineDirtyTree_LeavesStatusCleanAndPreservesContent`.
- **The build floor fails open on its own plumbing.** A nil engine, a missing worktree, a `go list` error, a coverage-generation error or an apicover measurement error approves with a loud WARN, because the downstream gates (ACS toolchain, apicover, CI) stay armed, and the floor must never false-block a build over its own plumbing. A test failure inside the coverage run is a real finding, never dropped. Pinned by `TestBuildFloorReviewer_ChecksErrorFailsOpen` and `TestBuildTagVisiblePackagesFailsOpen`.
- **The floor diffs against the cycle base.** The builder's protocol commits its work, so at review time (before the post-record soft reset) a `HEAD` diff is empty and the floor would approve vacuously. With no recorded base, it falls back to the `HEAD` diff plus untracked files. Pinned by `TestDefaultBuildFloorChecks_SeesCommittedBuilderWork`.
- **The protected-surface floor runs with rename detection off.** With it on, `git diff --name-only` prints only a rename's new path, so a protected file moved to an unprotected name, and weakened while it stays over 50% similar, would pass. A nil predicate fails open, since ship's tripwire stays armed. A note in `build-report.md` never clears the check, because it reads only paths. Pinned by `TestProtectedSurfaceFloorChecks_SeesARenameOutOfTheSurface` and `TestProtectedSurfaceFloorChecks_RefusesTheCycle1689Shape`.
- **The repo-contract floor rejects only a named red.** Ship names reds only for its own real red (`packOutcome.realRed`: a failed run with a named test or build failure), so the floor branches on the names alone. A red is one floor line naming every red, how to reproduce it, and the tail (`floorFailureDiagnostic`) of the failing tests' own output, which the classifier keeps apart from the pack's 97KB stream; for the ratchet that is the offending file and its remedy. A nonzero exit that names no test WARNs, with its error, the floor's deadline and the tail of the pack's output, and passes the handoff, because ship's gate retries it once and classes it `REPO_CONTRACT_INFRA`; the floor must never turn a toolchain fault into a builder task. The pack runs under the floor's own wall-clock deadline (`repoContractFloorDeadline`, 120s, a context deadline on the whole pack rather than a go-test `-timeout`), not ship's 20m; a kill names nothing, so it WARN-passes and ship stays the authority. Because it fails open, this deadline can cost the floor an early finding but never rejects a green build, so it stays separate from the single go-test budget above. The pack's own `-timeout 20m` stays, because a test-binary timeout would name the running tests as reds. A kill reaches the `go` command, not its test binary, which is orphaned until its own timeout (ship's cancel does the same; inbox `repo-contract-cancel-orphans-test-binary`). A green run, a missing worktree and the `off` dial are silent. It runs even when an earlier check already failed, so one correction carries every finding. Pinned by `TestRepoContractFloorChecks_ARawGitFixtureIsCorrectedAtBuildExitByTheRatchetsName` (the red line carries the ratchet's message), `TestRepoContractFloorChecks_GreenOrAnUnnamedExitPassesTheHandoff` (the WARN carries the error and the output), `TestRepoContractFloorChecks_AnyNamedRedCorrectsTheBuild`, `TestRepoContractFloorChecks_RunsThePackUnderItsOwnDeadline`, `TestRepoContractFloorChecks_ADeadlineKillWarnsAndPassesTheHandoff`, `TestRepoContractFloorChecks_FollowsTheShipGatesDial` (off, enforce, absent, a typo and an unparseable policy), `TestRepoContractFloorChecks_WithoutAProjectRootTheWorktreesPolicyDecides`, `TestRepoContractPack_ShipAndTheBuildFloorMakeOneDecision` (ship's gate and the floor agree for every gate word and repo shape), the `internal/repocontract` tests and `TestRepoContractFloor_ARedPackCorrectsTheBuildAndNeverReachesTheAudit` (a `RunCycle` whose pack stays red re-dispatches the build with the test named and never dispatches the audit).
- **The apicover floor is diff-scoped.** Only violations in files the change touched hard-fail; a touched package's older debt WARNs. The check needs a coverage profile, because apicover's enforce contract is named and executed, and without a profile every named export reads as false-green. The coverage run takes the host-wide verification single-flight (`verifylock`), the same lock as the EGPS suite, and a lock failure degrades to an unserialized run, never a skipped one.
  - The coverage pass (`scopedCoverProfile`) takes the host verification lock. When it cannot, `ReviewInput.recordUnserializedFloor` writes a `verify-single-flight-unavailable` abnormal event to the cycle workspace, beside the stderr WARN (since 2026-10-21). The event names the lock path and the error. Pinned by `TestScopedCoverFunc_ALostSingleFlightIsAnAbnormalEventNamingTheLockPath`. The unit runs of `realGoUnitTest` take no lock ([internal-verifylock](internal-verifylock.md)).
- **Packages with no Go files under the default tags are dropped before the selfcheck.** `go test` on one is a setup failure ("build constraints exclude all Go files"), not a test failure. This is the second line of defense; the first is that modern `acs` packages are not enrolled in `.apicover-enforce` (see its rationale block). Pinned by `TestBuildTagVisiblePackagesDropsACSPackages` and `TestAPICoverEnforceDoesNotEnrollModernACSPackages`.
- **A failing package's diagnostic keeps the last 400 bytes.** `go test` writes its `--- FAIL` lines, panics and stack traces at the end, and this text is the whole story the next builder is handed. Complete output carries no elision marker, so the marker stays a trustworthy signal. Pinned by `TestBuildFloorSelfCheckFailures_KeepsTailDiagnostic`.
- **`docsFloorWarn` is read as text by an ACS predicate.** `go/acs/cycle1151` counts the lines of that function's body (`acsassert.CountInGoFunc`, comments included) that contain `docsfloor.IsArchitectureClass(` or `docsfloor.LabelArchitecture(`, and requires exactly one labeler. A comment inside `docsFloorWarn` must never spell either call with its opening parenthesis.
- **A menu phase must resolve a persona from `agents/<agent>.md`.** That is the only persona source the dispatch path reads for a disk-loaded spec (`prompts.Loader.Agent` is single-rooted; specrunner's inline `PromptBody` is mint-only). A phase-local `agent.md` is a scaffold for humans and does not count. Control-role detection uses the production inference (`RoleOrDefault`), never a private allowlist. Pinned by `TestRepoPhaseCatalog_MenuPhasesResolveAPersona`.
- **A missing persona skips only a genuinely optional phase.** Mandatory phases, ship-floor phases (even one mis-marked Optional) and configured-mandatory phases still fail loudly, so the skip can never weaken ship ⇒ build ∧ audit ∧ tdd. The skip is ledgered under its own kind, `optional_missing_persona_skip`, never the infra key, and is learned deterministically without dispatching a retrospective agent. Pinned by the tests in `agent_doc_missing_test.go`.
- **`catalog: "on-demand"` hides a phase from the SELECT menu without removing it.** The phase stays installed, dispatchable by explicit plan and mintable, and the declined set is still named in one index line that lists only the declined phases. Only the exact word declines; an unknown word would silently leave the phase on the menu, so the repo catalog is checked for known words. Every render layer is pinned, because a test at one layer never proves the next: `TestAdvisorPrompt_OnDemandPhaseLeavesTheMenuButStaysNamed`, `TestComposePlanPrompt_CarriesTheOnDemandIndex` (persona path), `TestBuildPlanPrompt_CarriesTheOnDemandIndex` (persona-less path), `TestAdvisorPlanInput_PopulatesOnDemandFromTheOrchestratorCatalog` (orchestrator input), `TestRepoPhaseCatalog_CatalogWordIsKnown` and `TestKnownCatalogWord_RejectsTypos`.
- **The shipped registry's activating fields agree with the literal kernel.** ADR-0058 made the kernel config-driven with a byte-identical literal backstop, so dropping an activating field would silently revert that phase to the literal with no observable behavior change. The guard derives its expectations from the literal rather than a fresh golden, so it catches drops and divergence alike. The debugger has no registry home, so its seam value (`builtinControlSpec`) must equal the literal too. Pinned by `TestRegistryActivatingFields_AgreeWithLiteralKernel` and `TestControlSeamActivatingFields_AgreeWithLiteralKernel`.
- **`PhaseBoundaryCheckpointer` is package-global.** The checkpoint package sets it from `init()`, and core cannot import that package, so tests assign their own closure and must not run in parallel with other `RunCycle` tests.
- **A removal claim is judged against the repository, and only claims can fail.** A path absent from disk but still tracked fails; an untracked absent path is an honest removal; a non-repo worktree, a missing root, an escaping or absolute path and unparseable JSON all fail open; a claim false on both axes reports once, with the worktree message. The check runs in the production engine even when the diff yields zero packages. Pinned by `TestRemovalClaimFailures`, `TestRemovalClaimFailures_TrackedButAbsentFromDisk`, `TestRemovalClaimFailures_UntrackedAbsent_StaysHonest`, `TestRemovalClaimFailures_TrackedAndPresent_ReportsExactlyOnce`, `TestRemovalClaimFailures_MissingRootsFailOpen`, `TestRemovalClaimFailures_DeliverablesFallback`, `TestDefaultBuildFloorChecks_IncludesRemovalClaimCheck` and `TestBuildFloorReviewer_RemovalClaimNotActuallyRemoved`.
- **Only an `UPPER_SNAKE` token ending in `_PLACEHOLDER` is a slot**; prose, lowercase names and a missing report pass. Pinned by `TestPlaceholderTokenFailures_RefusesAnUnsubstitutedTemplateToken`, `TestPlaceholderTokenFailures_CleanReportAndProseAreNotTokens` and `TestDefaultBuildFloorChecks_IncludesThePlaceholderTokenCheck`.
- **A token quoted inside a `## Correction N` section is exempt from the placeholder check; a bare token is not.** The correction prompt (`correctionRecord` in `retry_backoff.go`) makes the builder quote the finding it fixed, so the check used to reject the repaired report. A heading alone never hides a slot, and only a backtick span or a fenced block is skipped. The section opens at `## Correction <digits>`, titled forms included, and ends at the next `# ` or `## ` heading, while a `### ` subheading keeps it open. `## Corrections` and every later section are checked in full. Pinned by `TestPlaceholderTokenFailures_BareTokenUnderCorrectionHeadingFails`, `TestPlaceholderTokenFailures_QuotedTokenUnderCorrectionHeadingPasses`, `TestPlaceholderTokenFailures_SubheadingKeepsCorrectionSectionOpen`, `TestPlaceholderTokenFailures_H1EndsCorrectionSection` and `TestPlaceholderTokenFailures_TitledCorrectionHeadingsOpenASection`.
- **A `# ` or `## ` heading closes a fence left open inside a correction section.** An unclosed fence therefore cannot exempt the sections after it. Pinned by `TestPlaceholderTokenFailures_UnclosedFenceInCorrectionEndsAtTheNextHeading` and `TestPlaceholderTokenFailures_FenceClosesAndBareTokenAfterItFails`.
- **The persona budget check is a conjunction.** A touched persona doc alone never rejects, and a lane that touches no persona doc never runs the package. Pinned by `TestPersonaBudgetFailures` and `TestDefaultBuildFloorChecks_IncludesPersonaBudgetCheck`.
- **Build-leak recovery preserves work and never widens the audit view.** Staging is scoped to relocated paths, never `git add -A`, which would sweep a worktree's pre-existing leftovers into the audit diff. A leaked edit to a tracked file relocates when the worktree copy is still at HEAD, including the staged-only `M ` case that `git checkout --` would miss; when the worktree changed the same file, the main-tree copy is discarded and the worktree wins. Gitignored build output never reaches the loop, so `.gitignore` is the discard mechanism and no path list is hardcoded. The tracked release binary `go/evolve` is always discarded, because only the release pipeline commits it.
