Orchestrator runtime state under `.evolve/`, at the top level or nested under a subdirectory, is skipped, and so is a nested worktree that `git status -uall` reports as a bare directory; `.evolve/` deliverables (evals, phase configs, profiles, tracked configs) relocate like any other path unless another owner holds them (main-tree ownership, above). Operator dirt already present when the phase starts is untouched. A correction re-dispatch is a fresh builder dispatch with the same escape risk, so its leak is recovered before review. Pinned by the `TestRecoverBuildLeak_*` tests and `TestCorrectionRedispatch_RecoversLeakBeforeReview`.
- **The carry-forward screen separates conflict from supersession.** A real conflict is `(false, nil)`, never an error; only a git failure errors. Supersession runs before the merge dry run, because a dry run of an already-absorbed change reads clean. A not-landable candidate is `FleetRebaseAlreadyLanded` only when the supersession screen says so, since calling a conflict landed drops overlapping work. Pinned by `TestCarryforwardCandidateLandable_ConflictIsFalseNotError`, `TestCarryforwardCandidateLandable_SupersededShortCircuitsBeforeMerge`, `TestCarryforwardCandidateLandable_PatchIdDuplicateSuperseded`, `TestClassifyFleetRebaseCandidate_ThreeWayVerdicts` and `TestClassifyFleetRebaseCandidate_GitInfraErrorIsError`.
- **The composition fast paths can only narrow what ships.** A missing seam, patch-id drift, a red composed-tree gate, a reviewer error or a writer error falls back to the full re-audit. A RUNG 2 `compatible` verdict composes only when its resolution re-verifies by patch-id against the audited change, never on the reviewer's word. `Composes` is true only for `compatible`, so an unknown verdict fails closed. "Wired" means all three RUNG 0 closures are bound. Pinned by `TestOrchestrator_CompositionFastPathWired`, `TestScopedReviewVerdict_Composes`, `TestReverifyResolution_MatchAndReject`, the `TestIntersectingHunks_*` tests and `mergerung2_consolidation_test.go`.
- **Carryover retirement and minting are conservative.** `RetireCarryoverTodos` removes committed ids and their fingerprint twins, keeps survivors in order, never matches a blank id, and never mutates its input, because the caller re-reads state under a lock and a mutated slice would corrupt a concurrent peer's merge. A defect todo inherits the record's `ExpiresAt`; a record without one leaves the todo unstamped, because the prune keeps unstamped entries and a fabricated stamp would age out data of unknown age. A cross-cycle re-mint of the same defect collapses into one entry and refreshes its expiry. The closeout order is memo merge, prescription merge, then triage retirement. Pinned by the `TestRetireCarryoverTodos_*` tests, `TestApplyDefectsAsCarryoverTodos_StampsExpiryFromRecord`, `TestApplyDefectsAsCarryoverTodos_NoRecordExpiryLeavesTodoUnstamped`, `TestAdoptDefects_CrossCycleFingerprintDedupe`, `TestAdoptDefects_SuppressedRemintRefreshesExpiry`, `TestFinalizeCycle_CloseoutKeepsTheThreeStepOrder` and `TestFinalizeCycle_RetiresTriageDroppedCarryover`.
- **Every carryover construction lives in `carryover_lifecycle.go`.** `TestCarryoverLifecycle_OneConstructionSite` scans the text of every non-test Go file in the module for `carryover.New(`, `MergeWorkspaceCarryover(`, `MergeWorkspacePrescriptionCarryover(` and `ApplyDefectsAsCarryoverTodos(`, so no other non-test file may spell them, even in a comment.
- **A console lease waives nothing it should not.** An absent, malformed, expiry-less or expired lease yields an empty set, and a lease written inside a checkout is ignored, so the guard stays armed; a waiver is exact-path and WARNs. Pinned by the `TestReadConsoleLease_*` tests and `TestFilterRealLeaks_LeaseWaiverIsExactAndLoud`; the location's why stays in code.
- **A continuation binds only resumable, immutable work.** The snapshot is a commit reachable from the recorded branch, so it survives worktree pruning. Only a Clean snapshot is stamped or registered, so unresumable work leaves no binding that later attempts would adopt and re-reject. A cycle without lane scope creates no registry, a blank id never becomes a key, every lane id is registered, and a cycle scoped to a different id never adopts. Registry failures WARN and never fail finalization. Pinned by `TestSnapshotPreservedWorktree_CommitsDirtAndUntracked`, `TestStampContinuationManifest_WritesGatedManifest`, `TestStampContinuationManifest_ConflictingWorkIsNotStamped`, `TestStampContinuationManifest_UnstampableWorkRegistersNothing`, `TestStampContinuationManifest_NoLaneScopeRegistersNothing`, `TestStampContinuationManifest_RegistersEveryLaneScopeID` and `TestRunCycle_UnrelatedLaneScopeDoesNotAdopt`.
- **The abnormal epilogue stamps a continuation too.** It runs after the failure digest, so the manifest's `FindingsPath` names the `audit-fail-reason.json` reason artifact rather than the digest. Pinned by `TestAbnormalEpilogue_StampsContinuation` and `TestStampContinuationManifest_WritesGatedManifest`.
- **A failed base advance stops before Build.** A conflicting merge is aborted under `context.WithoutCancel`, so cancellation never strands a half-merge in preserved work; no phase dispatches against the rejected snapshot and neither the old nor the seeded work is lost. Pinned by `TestAdvanceContinuationBase_ConflictReturnsErrorAndAborts`, `TestAdvanceContinuationBase_NoOpWhenCurrent` and the integration-tagged `TestRunCycle_ContinuationAdvanceFailureStopsBeforeBuild`.
- **Releasing a declined binding removes only the declined scopes.** A scope bound to a different ancestor survives. Pinned by `TestReleaseDeclinedBinding_RemovesMatchingScopesOnly`.
- **An exhausted dispatch loop is an explained, cycle-level failure.** When the iteration bound ends the loop before `PhaseEnd`, the cycle records a terminal abort naming the transition-table cycle, so `cyclehealth.ClassifyOutcome` reads `FAILED_EXPLAINED`, and `RunCycle` returns no batch-fatal error. Pinned by `TestRunCycle_TransitionCycleGuard_RecordsChokepointEscape`.
- **A pre-handoff probe iterates the cycle's floor list with the cycle's input.** The production orchestrator's `BuildHandoffFloorNames()` equals the probes' floor names, a floor mounted anywhere in the chain is named, and both probes run the floor once on `ReviewInputFor` of the bound cycle. A resumed legacy checkpoint is reviewed under the resumed cycle, pinned by `TestResumeBootstrap_LegacyCheckpointReviewsUnderTheResumedCycle`. Pinned by `TestOrchestrator_BuildHandoffFloorNames_ListsEveryFloorCheckTheReviewerRuns` here, and by `TestBuildHandoffProbes_IterateTheCycleFloorsCheckList` and `TestBuildHandoffProbes_BothRunTheSeamFloorOnTheBoundCyclesInput` at the composition root.
- **`ChangedWorktreePaths` reports tracked and untracked changes and is empty for a clean repo or a non-repo**, which makes the docs floor skip instead of inventing a violation. Pinned by `TestChangedWorktreePaths_ExportedForCLIConsumers` and `TestChangedWorktreePaths_EmptyWorktreeIsEmpty`.
- **A trailing `WithCatalogPublisher(nil)` never unwires an earlier publisher.** Pinned by `TestWithCatalogPublisher_TrailingNilDoesNotUnwireAnEarlierValidPublisher`. A nil catalog refresher writes no `catalog_refresh` ledger entry, because a refresher that never ran has no outcome to stamp. Pinned by `TestOrchestrator_CatalogRefresh_NilRefresherNoLedgerEntry`.
- **Source readers of these files.** `go/acs/cycle1153` counts quoted `"build-report.md"` and `"audit-report.md"` literals in `readBuildReport`'s body, comments included. `go/acs/cycle1152` requires `build_removal_check.go` to call `phasecontract.ArtifactName(` or `phasecontract.ArtifactFilename(`. `go/acs/cycle968` requires `ClassifyFleetRebaseCandidate`'s body to mention `CarryforwardCandidateLandable`. `go/acs/cycle1159` counts every non-test file outside `carryforward_filter.go` that mentions `ClassifyFleetRebaseCandidate` as a production caller, so a comment elsewhere would fake one. `go/acs/cycle1181` requires `carryforward_filter.go` to carry no `QUARANTINED-DEAD` marker.
- **A bridge or phase error is a cycle-level failure; only kernel-integrity invariants stay batch-fatal.** `RunCycle` wraps a bridge-exhaustion abort in `ErrCycleLevelFailure` (Phase + Cause, with `Unwrap` so `errors.Is`/`errors.As` reach the root), but a phase-gate denial, a broken ledger chain, or lock contention are never wrapped, because the batch must keep dying on those. The audit↔ship recovery loop is itself bounded (1 initial attempt plus `maxRecoveryDepth` (2) re-audits, the same bound for ship attempts), and exhausting that budget is cycle-level too. Pinned by `TestErrCycleLevelFailure_WrapsCauseForErrorsIs`, `TestOrchestrator_BridgeExhaustion_CycleLevelFailure`, `TestOrchestrator_IntegrityBreach_StillBatchFatal` and `TestOrchestrator_RecoveryDepthBudget`.
- **A salvage snapshot never commits the project root.** When the active worktree resolves to the project root itself — including a symlink alias or a relative spelling of the root — the stamp refuses out loud and stamps no continuation, so an in-place run's own tree is never swept into a snapshot. Pinned by `TestStampContinuation_NeverSnapshotsTheProjectRoot`.
- **A teardown-shaped abort's fingerprint stays in the identical-fingerprint population.** `IsInfraTeardownError` causes are marked `teardown=`, never `cause=`, but the digest is not excluded from Rule B: one systemic infra condition mowing down several lanes is exactly the recurring shape ADR-0072's halt doctrine wants stopped at the ceiling, and the marker exists to make that shape legible in the halt message rather than to exempt it. Pinned by `TestAbnormalEpilogue_TeardownMarkedAndTailKept`.
- **`clearActiveWorktree` only unnames the worktree it just pruned.** It is a read-modify-write against storage, not a rewrite of the `newCycleRun`-era snapshot, because by teardown the cycle run has persisted phase progress through its own `CycleState` copy and writing the stale init snapshot back would discard it; the path guard makes it a no-op once the record has moved on.
- **The cross-artifact invariant stack is bound to the LANE worktree, never `projectRoot`.** In fleet mode a project-root snapshot can name a tree this lane never wrote, so `finalizeCycle` evaluates `coherence.CheckCrossArtifactInvariants` over `cs.ActiveWorktree`. A finding never blocks: it changes no verdict, raises no system failure, and is recorded on every cycle, all-ok included, because an ungathered false-positive rate can never be evidenced, and that evidence is the only door to graduating any of these invariants to blocking. Pinned by `TestFinalizeCycle_EmitsAdvisoryCrossArtifactInvariantsArtifact`, `TestFinalizeCycle_CrossArtifactViolationsNeverBlockTheCycle`, `TestFinalizeCycle_CrossArtifactBindsTheLaneWorktreeNotTheProjectRoot` and `TestFinalizeCycle_VerdictIncoherenceFloorSurvivesTheAdvisory`.
- **The deliverable-kind digest degrades loud, defaults quiet.** A read failure or a `DigestDegraded` reason WARNs on stderr and keeps the conservative `code` kind; a clean absence (no report yet) is silent. A `.evolve/domain.json` that exists but fails to parse is reported and counts as absent, never a silent reclassification. Pinned by `TestDispatchSignals`, `TestDispatchSignals_DegradedDigestIsLoud` and `TestResolveDeliverableKind_DeclaredBeatsTheDomainDefaultWhichBeatsCode`.
- **The worktree fence is one value from one projection** (ADR-0097; one value since 2026-10-21, inbox `worktree-fence-one-value`).
  - `PhaseRequest.Fence` is a `*WorktreeFence`. A nil fence means that the phase writes source. A fence means read-only, except the paths in `Fence.Writable`. Thus a request cannot carry writable paths without a fence.
  - `FenceFor(writesSource, writable)` is the one projection. `PhaseWritesSource(phase, catalog)` is the one rule: the builtin source writers, or a catalog spec with `writes_source`.
  - The orchestrator calls `o.fenceFor(phase, cs)` in the live, resume and evaluate-batch requests, the out-of-band retro and a remediation fix. `ReauditCycle`, `evolve campaign`, `evolve phase --cycle` and `evolve compose --cycle` call `FenceFor`.
  - The structure test `TestEveryPhaseRequestWithAWorktreeTakesTheFenceFromTheOneProjection` enumerates every `PhaseRequest` literal that sets a worktree. Each one must take `Fence` from `FenceFor` or `fenceFor`.
  - The guard reads literals only. A later assignment to `Fence` is not scanned; today the only one is the remediation fix, which calls `fenceFor`. `evolve compose --cycle` runs several phases on one request, so the request is unfenced when any of them writes source.
  - The wire carries both spellings of the fence: `worktree_fence` and the legacy keys `worktree_read_only` and `worktree_writable_paths`. A request decoded with only the legacy keys gets its fence back, so a phase binary on either side of the rename stays fenced.
  - `ReauditCycle` runs its audit behind the read-only fence (`TestReauditCycle_TheAuditRunsBehindTheReadOnlyFence`).
  - `PhaseRequest.WritesSource()` is true for a nil fence or for writable paths. The phase runner hands it to the skill-overlay resolver. `BridgeCompletion` asks only if a fence exists, so a conflict-resolving debugger gets no worktree-evidence contract.
  - Pinned by `TestFenceFor_IsTheOneProjection`, `TestOutOfBandRetro_IsFencedLikeEveryReadOnlyPhase`, `TestOutOfBandRetro_AWritingRetroOfAFailedBuildIsHeldByTheFence`, `TestPhaseRequest_WritesSource_TheWorktreeFenceDecidesWhoWrites` and `TestDispatch_ReadOnlyPhasesAreFencedAndSourceWritersAreNot`.
- **The spine gate blocks only a clean absence at enforce.** A degraded digest or a re-digest error is never treated as a clean absence, so it always fails open, even at enforce. A transient read fault must never false-block a real cycle. A config-waived anchor (removed from `cfg.Mandatory`) cannot block either. Pinned by `TestSpineGate_EnforceBlocksOnCleanAbsence`, `TestSpineGate_ShadowKeepsFailOpen`, `TestSpineGate_EnforceFailsOpenOnDegradedDigest`, `TestSpineGate_ConfigWaiverSkipsAnchor` and `TestDigest_DegradedVsAbsent`. The gate keys on its own `SpineFloor` dial, not `PhaseRecovery`, because `PhaseRecovery` is overloaded (bidirectional channel plus failure-adviser promotion) and stays at shadow.
- **A preserved worktree keeps its `ActiveWorktree` path.** `evolve loop --resume` and `evolve cycle reset` reclaim a preserved lane by that path, so clearing it unconditionally at teardown would trade a stale-path bug for permanently orphaned audited work. Pinned by `TestCycleRunTeardown_PreservedWorktreeKeepsActiveWorktree` and `TestCycleRunTeardown_ClearsActiveWorktreeAfterPrune`.
- **The debugger gate consults the catalog, not a literal `current == PhaseDebugger` check.** A registry override to verdict branching disables the signal branch entirely (no successor scheduled, no loopBreak on BLOCK); with no registry entry the control seam still supplies signal and the gate behaves as before. `sm.Next` stays on the literal debugger→end sentinel for every verdict, since the seam declares no verdict branches. Pinned by `TestSuccessorStrategy_Debugger`, `TestBuiltinControlSpec`, `TestRecordAndBranch_DebuggerGateIsStrategyKeyed`, `TestRecordAndBranch_DebuggerDegradesToSignalViaSeam` and `TestNext_DebuggerSeamDoesNotLeakIntoVerdictBranch`.

- **A wall on a correction, a remediation re-run or a resumed review is a deferral; any other dispatch failure there is still a failure.** Pinned by `TestRunCycle_WalledCorrectionRedispatch_DefersLikeAFirstDispatch`, `TestRunCycle_WalledRemediationRerun_DefersLikeAFirstDispatch`, `TestReviewResumedDeliverable_WalledCorrection_ReturnsTheExhaustionSentinel`, `TestRunCycleFromPhase_WalledResumedCorrection_Defers` and `TestRunCycle_FailedCorrectionRedispatch_IsNotADeferral`.

- **An explanation-review advisory raised on a PASS verdict rides the C1 phase-outcome record** ([ADR-0102](../adr/0102-explanation-review-reasoning-is-the-gate.md)), which is the persistence the decision's feedback loop rests on; the audit classification's half (emitting the prefixed advisory on a PASS) is pinned in `internal/phases/audit`. Pinned by `TestRecordPhaseOutcome_AnAdvisoryOnAPassRidesTheRecord`.
- **A missing `## Explanation Documentation` section in an audit report is a correction, not a terminal FAIL.** The deliverable reviewer's rejection re-dispatches the audit runner once with the rejection reason as its correction directive, the same ladder every other phase's contract rejection uses, and the cycle proceeds on the approved second report. Pinned by `TestAuditContractRejection_RedispatchesTheAuditorWithTheReason`.
- **`enforceNext`'s skip-advance loop must never rewrite a skipped `staticNext` to `PhaseEnd` when `cfg.Order` is empty** (no `phase-registry.json`, as in the e2e fixtures or a bare repo): `nextInOrder` returns `PhaseEnd` for any phase absent from an empty order, which would silently terminate cycles after `tdd`. The original `staticNext` is kept and no advance is reported. Pinned by `TestEnforceNext_EmptyOrderSkipAdvance`.
- **`decideAfterRetro` calls `failureadapter.Decide` with `Strict=false`**, and every non-Proceed `Decision` in `failureadapter` is gated behind `if opts.Strict`, so from this call site `Decide` can only return `ActionProceed`; the RETRY/BLOCK arms become reachable, and need their own tests, only if a future change passes `Strict=true` here. Pinned by `TestDecideAfterRetro`.
- **`NewPhaseAdvisor`'s default identity is `claude-tmux`/`opus`, and empty override options never blank it out.** The model default is `opus` (deep) because composing the cycle and minting phases is deep-reasoning work, not lightweight routing; the composition root normally overrides both from the router profile plus `EVOLVE_ROUTER_CLI`/`EVOLVE_ROUTER_MODEL`. Pinned by `TestPhaseAdvisorOptions`.
- **A sealed FAIL always carries at least one reason.** `backfillFailReasons` fills `CycleResult.FailReasons` when a FAIL sealed with none would otherwise fingerprint as a content-free identity, invisible to the identical-fingerprint breaker. Priority: recorded abort reasons (the real infra error, with its originating phase); else each failing phase's own error-severity diagnostics (a reasoned FAIL, such as a protected-surface rejection, that carried no abort reason); else the failing phase by name with an explicit "phase-infra class" marker; else one explicit unexplained marker — never null. An already-explained FAIL (an audit/ship gate reason already present) and a non-FAIL verdict are untouched. Pinned by `TestBackfillFailReasons_FromAbortReasons`, `TestBackfillFailReasons_UnexplainedMarkerNeverNull`, `TestBackfillFailReasons_NoOpWhenExplainedOrNotFAIL` and `TestBackfillFailReasons_PhaseOwnDiagnosticsNameTheReason`.
- **`FailureAdvisor` is the deterministic-first, LLM-last escalation tail for a `CauseUnknown` terminal state** ([ADR-0044](../adr/0044-unified-phase-recovery-protocol.md)). It is built exactly like `PhaseAdvisor` (bridge-dispatched, persona-injected, strict-JSON-parsed) and fails safe the same way: every failure mode (nil bridge, launch error, malformed output, a cause outside the typed vocabulary, or the persona's own "not fatal" judgment) returns an error, so the caller always escalates to the operator instead of acting on garbage. It never sits on the hot loop for a known failure — its verdicts get promoted into the deterministic registry via `recovery.PromoteAdvice`, so each novel state is paid for once. Its prompt carries a `# Usage evidence` section when the failing phase's workspace holds `usage-evidence.ndjson` (`UsageEvidenceFile`, written by the usage evidence decorator, see [internal-usageevidence](internal-usageevidence.md)). The section is the last five records, framed as untrusted, so quota is verified or ruled out before the advisor reads the pane (`TestFailureAdvisor_PromptCarriesTheUsageEvidenceRecordedForTheFailure`). Pinned by `TestFailureAdvisor_ParsesCauseAndSignature`, `TestFailureAdvisor_EmptyCause_NonFatalSignal`, `TestFailureAdvisor_MalformedJSON_ReturnsError`, `TestFailureAdvisor_UnknownCause_ReturnsError`, `TestFailureAdvisor_NilBridge_FailSafe` and `TestFailureAdvisor_BridgeError_Propagates`.
- **`readFailureDecision` is the fail-soft boundary for the retrospective agent's `failure-decision.json`** ([ADR-0072](../adr/0072-system-failure-policy-and-halt.md)). An absent, malformed, or schema-invalid artifact (an `action`/`level` outside the policy vocabulary) all return `(nil, nil)` — the signal to fall back to the deterministic `failureadapter` — never a cycle-aborting error, so a garbled artifact can never drive an unrecognized branch. It is deliberately unexported: no new apicover-gated public surface. The exact schema the retrospective persona's instructions document round-trips cleanly through it, proving the emitter and consumer share one schema; the instruction-file wiring proof (the emit directive plus write-allowlist widening) is a separate ACS predicate (`C1002_005`) against `agents/evolve-retrospective.md`. Pinned by `TestReadFailureDecision` and `TestFailureDecisionWiring`.
- **The `FailureAdvisor` prompt is the one shipped LLM consumption of raw pane text, and it must traverse `panetrust.Frame`** ([ADR-0045](../adr/0045-corrective-interaction-protocol.md) I5): an untrusted preamble, a neutralized fenced digest, secrets redacted. Pinned by `TestFailureAdvisor_PromptCarriesUntrustedFraming`.
- **`FailureDigest` is the deterministic post-FAIL/pre-retro step that converts a failed cycle's forensic artifacts into a stable failure identity** the disposition gate cross-checks against, so retro can no longer invent it. `AssembleFailureDigest` reads the single workspace SSOT artifact `audit-fail-reason.json` (mirroring `readFailureDecision`'s workspace-file boundary); reading it is fail-soft — an absent or malformed artifact degrades to the "unknown" bucket and still writes a digest, and only a write failure returns an error. Pre-class buckets are classified from the real, lowercased reason text against an ordered keyword table (infra teardown is checked before the gate/verdict buckets, since an infra-severed phase can still mention a floor). Pinned by `TestAssembler_PreClassBucketsFromRealArtifacts`, `TestAssembler_FingerprintComposition`, `TestAssembler_RecurrenceFromLedger`, `TestAssembler_MissingArtifactsDegradeToUnknown` and `TestAssembler_WritesDigestArtifact`.
- **The fingerprint's normalization strips exactly the tokens that vary per-cycle for the SAME defect, never content that distinguishes two different defects**: an audit verdict-conflict's `narrative=<verdict>` token, go-test decimal-seconds durations (an integer-second config token like `-timeout 300s` stays identity-bearing), cycle-numbered artifact paths and bare "cycle N" prose, and a retry loop's attempt/retry denominator. Each rule is anchored narrowly so two different defects never collapse into one fingerprint. Pinned by `TestNormalizeReasonForFingerprint_CycleNumberedPathsFold`, `TestNormalizeReasonForFingerprint_AttemptDenominatorFolds`, `TestNormalizeReasonForFingerprint_DistinctDefectsStayDistinct`, `TestNormalizeReasonForFingerprint_ExistingPinsStayGreen` and `TestFailureDigest_DurationTokensNormalizeOut`.
- **`verdictFailDistinguisher` layers by strength, defect identity first, task identity last.** A sentinel's defects list is authoritative only when the sentinel also carries a `failure.class` — a class-less sentinel falls through to the audit report's bullet-list defects instead. Task identity is the weakest signal (same-task retries with different defects are the common case, and same-task repeats are S5 quarantine's job, not the breaker's), so it is tried only when no defect-shaped content exists anywhere. Pinned by `TestVerdictFailDistinguisher_SentinelDefectsOutrankTaskIDs`, `TestVerdictFailDistinguisher_FallsBackTasksWhenNoDefects`, `TestVerdictFailDistinguisher_ClasslessSentinelFallsSoftToBullets` and `TestFailureDigest_SameTaskDistinctDefectsGetDistinctFingerprints`.
- **`defectHead` truncates from the head, `causeHead` from the tail.** An error chain grows prefix-first ("phase build: attempt N: bridge: \<long path\>: \<root cause\>"), so the distinguishing content lives at the end; a head-kept cut would collapse two different roots sharing one long prefix into one fingerprint. A defect line's identity lives at its head instead. Pinned by `TestAbnormalEpilogue_TeardownMarkedAndTailKept`.
- **`ensureFailureDigest` is the single-source wiring both retro dispatch paths share** (the phase-error path via `recordFailureLearning` and the verdict-FAIL path via `cyclerun` dispatch) — wiring only one path left verdict-path FAILs with no digest and no disposition. It is best-effort throughout: a nil recurrence ledger degrades to recurrence 0, and a digest-write failure only WARNs, because retro must never be blocked by forensics plumbing. Its fallback reason (the caller's own failed-phase/error text) is written into `audit-fail-reason.json` only when no floor already wrote one, and a floor-written artifact always wins. So a phase that dies inside an agent-graded FAIL's repair round is digested from its own failure, while a death inside a runner-downgraded FAIL's round is digested from the gate record the round was repairing ([logic-first design §5.14](../logic-first-delivery-design.md) states why). Pinned by `TestEnsureFailureDigest_WritesDigestWithoutLedger`, `TestEnsureFailureDigest_Idempotent`, `TestEnsureFailureDigest_MissingWorkspaceNeverWritesToWorkingDirectory` and `TestRunCycle_AReauthorBuildThatDiesIsDigestedFromItsOwnFailure`.
- **`buildFailureDossier` composes independent evidence and never trusts the recorded verdict alone**: the deterministic verdict-coherence signal (from the phases' own on-disk artifacts), the audit's self-declared failure envelope (surfaced always, even for a task-level class, so the judgment layer sees the defects prose), and the non-progress counters (policy thresholds plus cycle history). `FloorCandidate` is set only for the two deterministically-detectable floor categories, since a broken pipeline cannot dodge them even with no orchestrator running; a structurally system-level audit class yields it directly, while a system-level claim buried only in defects prose (the cycle-1001 shape) cannot be floored deterministically and must reach the judgment layer instead. Its `SubstantiveError` computation must stay in lockstep with `system_failure.go`'s twin: both fold a diagnosed post-audit ship-gate rejection (`cs.ShipFailReasons`) into "coherent," or fixing one call site and leaving the other stale reproduces the exact false-floor-candidate bug on the ship-phase-explained shape. Pinned by `TestFailureDossier`.
- **The build's system class defers the item; it never halts the loop** (`builderSystemClassUnderGates`, `Orchestrator.failureDossier`, the cycle-1841 shape). The auditor wrote PASS and declared no failure block, so only deterministic gates forced the FAIL (a `verdict-conflict` record). The build writes `build-report.md`, so its word alone must not stop the batch ([ADR-0072](../adr/0072-system-failure-policy-and-halt.md), "a builder's system class defers, never halts").
  - The claim counts only with orchestrator corroboration. At least one repair round ran. The last two audits of the run bound the same worktree tree (`auditedTreeUnchanged`, the auditor ledger rows). An unchanged tree means that the remedy round moved nothing.
  - The build must hand off WARN or FAIL with a system-level class. The class is normalized to its policy category, for example `infrastructure-systemic` to `infra-systemic`, and it is recorded as `build_declared`. It never sets the floor candidate.
  - `decideAfterAuditFail` then declines a further `code-audit-fail` round. Retro ends the cycle through the defer rule. `deferDecision` takes `build_declared` from orchestrator memory after the audit block and before `failure-decision.json`. The fix type is `pipeline-repair`. The closeout routes the item to wait on a console `pipeline-repair-cycle-<N>` item. A false claim thus costs one lane, not the batch.
  - These shapes keep today's dossier and today's repair decision:
    - a builder WARN with no class or with a task-level class;
    - a FAIL narrative of the auditor;
    - a claim before the remedy round, a PASS build or a changed tree.
  - Pinned by `TestDecideAfterAuditFail_ABuildersSystemClassNeverHaltsTheLoop` (an unchanged skills-drift or gofmt reading), `TestDecideAfterAuditFail_AChangedAuditedTreeKeepsTheRepairRound`, `TestAuditedTreeUnchanged_ComparesTheLastTwoAuditsOfTheRun`, `TestFailureDossier_TodaysDossierHoldsWithoutTheShape`, `TestFailureDossier_ANonFloorSystemClassIsRecordedAndIsNoFloor` and `TestAuditDeclineDefers_APlantedReadOnlyDossierClaimingASystemClassDoesNotDefer`.
- **A cycle's `FinalVerdict` is floor-gated: only an authoritative phase may set it directly, once one exists.** `isAuthoritativePhase` is the resolved ship floor (tdd/build/audit, or a configured override) plus ship itself; `floorAlreadyCompleted` derives its answer from `cs.CompletedPhases` (the persisted phase log) rather than a mutable flag, so the resume path gets the identical guard for free. Once an authoritative verdict exists, a non-floor phase's non-PASS outcome is preserved into `CycleResult.VerdictsNotAdopted` (surfaced in the dossier as `phases_run_verdict_not_adopted`) instead of overwriting the floor verdict; before any authoritative verdict exists, the phase's verdict stands unchanged, so a scout-only investigation cycle still gets an outcome. `nonFloorExhaustionDegrade` extends the same split to contract exhaustion: a post-verdict non-floor phase degrades to SKIPPED+WARN and advances, while a floor phase or any pre-floor phase (scout/triage/intent) stays cycle-fatal, since a cycle cannot proceed on an unparseable scout verdict. Pinned by `TestNonFloorPhaseFailure_DoesNotOverrideFloorVerdict`, `TestNonFloorPhaseFailure_FailAudit_StaysFail`, `TestFloorPhaseFailure_RemainsCycleFatal`, `TestResumeNonFloorPhaseFailure_DoesNotOverrideFloorVerdict` and `TestContractExhaustion_NonFloorPhase_DegradesToSkippedWarn`.
- **A floor phase's success-path FAIL (verdict FAIL, no dispatch error) is fed to failure-learning through the same record-only core as an error-path failure.** `floorVerdictError` synthesizes a reason joining only error-severity diagnostics, so a fail-open WARN never pollutes it; `recordFailedApproachState` appends the `FailedRecord`, dedupes a P0 carryover todo by id, and stamps `LastCycleNumber` without running retro. `recordAndBranch`'s guard fires only for the router's floor default (`{tdd,build,audit}`, audit authoritative with no catalog) and only on FAIL, so a PASS or a non-floor phase's FAIL is skipped; the record must reach storage, not only in-memory state, since the loop's abort branches return before `finalizeCycle` persists. Pinned by `TestFloorVerdictError_JoinsErrorSeverityDiagnostics`, `TestRecordFailedApproachState_RecordsFloorFailToStateAndCarryover`, `TestRecordFailedApproachState_DedupesCarryover`, `TestRecordAndBranch_AuditFAILRecordsFloorFailure`, `TestRecordAndBranch_AuditPASSDoesNotRecord` and `TestRecordAndBranch_NonAuthoritativePhaseFAILDoesNotRecord`.
- **`adviseOnUnclassifiedFailure` is the ADR-0044 C3 escalate→advise→promote hook, gated to run only when it can safely learn something.** It runs only at `cfg.PhaseRecovery==enforce` (shadow, the default, never dispatches an advisor) and only for an `ErrArtifactTimeout` abort whose escalation report carries a pane; the deterministic registry is always checked first, so a known pane never pays for an LLM call. It is strictly best-effort — every failure path only WARNs — and runs after the phase outcome is recorded, so it never alters the abort control flow; its only durable side effect is a validated `recovery.PromoteAdvice` promotion that makes the next occurrence deterministic. Pinned by `TestPhaseRecovery_ShadowDefault_NoCorrectiveAction`, `TestPhaseRecovery_Enforce_AdvisesAndPromotes`, `TestPhaseRecovery_Enforce_KnownPaneSkipsAdvisor` and `TestPhaseRecovery_Enforce_AdvisorErrorIsBestEffort`.
- **The hook's deterministic-first short-circuit strips agent-authored content from the pane before matching the registry.** Matching the raw pane would misread a genuinely novel wedge as "already classified" whenever its final pane happens to quote a seeded signature inside agent-authored diff content (an agent editing the detector, a builder writing a fatal-pane fixture) — killing the C3 learning loop exactly where an agent's own text caused it. It reuses the bridge's C2 fast-fail stripper with an empty injected prompt: the echo half is neutered by the protect list regardless (a line carrying a seeded signature is never echo-stripped), so plumbing the phase prompt in here would add I/O for no behavioral difference. Pinned by `TestC1123_AgentDiffQuotedSignatureStillReachesAdvisor`, `TestC1123_BareDiffPrefixedSignatureStillReachesAdvisor`, `TestC1123_RealChromeStillSkipsAdvisor` and `TestC1123_AnchoredSeedUnderDiffLineStillSkipsAdvisor`.
- **The router's and the abnormal epilogue's fallback fail-reason templates are the single source their own boilerplate detector matches against**, so a wording drift between the writer and `isBoilerplateRouterReason` fails a dedicated test rather than silently misclassifying real content as content-free or vice versa. Pinned by `TestAgentGradedRouterReason_MatchesBoilerplateDetector` and `TestAssembleFailureDigest_BoilerplateOnlyReasonIsUnexplained`.
- **An `AbortReason` is not proof a cycle died there.** The ship-recovery path records the transient it recovered from and continues, so `backfillFailReasons` emits both the abort entries and the failing-phase markers unconditionally; an abort-only backfill would fingerprint a recovered transient while silently dropping the phase that actually failed. Pinned by `TestBackfillFailReasons_RecoveredAbortDoesNotMaskRealFailure`.
- **`state.json`'s `failedApproaches` has two appenders with different Go types**: the orchestrator's typed `FailedRecord` (in-memory `State`, persisted via `writeFailureLearningState`) and `failurelog.Recorded` (a raw read-modify-write, used by reset and loop-fatal paths). Unifying them is out of scope (`State` lifecycle vs. an on-disk merge), so a shape-parity test pins the JSON contract instead: every key `failurelog.Recorded` emits must also be a key of `FailedRecord`, with identical spelling, or downstream readers (failure-adapter, pruner) see a forked schema. Pinned by `TestFailedRecord_SupersetOfFailurelogRecordedShape`.
- **A git rename/copy dirties both the old and new path in `git status --porcelain` output.** `porcelainDirtySet` records both, since a deliverable renamed to a look-alike name must not vanish from the tree-diff guard's view because the new path alone classifies as legitimate runtime state. Pinned by `TestDefaultGitDirtyPaths_RenameEmitsBothSides`.
- **`gitCapture` returns stdout untrimmed** (callers parse it themselves) **and reports a non-zero exit via the returned exit code rather than an error**, which is load-bearing for callers that branch on the code (e.g. `git diff --quiet` rc=1, `merge-base --is-ancestor` rc=1). Pinned by `TestGitCapture_RoutesThroughInjectedSeam` and `TestGitCapture_NonzeroExitReportedViaCode`.
- **`recoverBuildLeak`'s per-status disposition for a source-writer phase (tdd/build)**: an untracked leak relocates into the worktree and is staged; the rebuilt release binary (`go/evolve`, `go/bin/evolve`) is always discarded via `git checkout HEAD --`, never relocated, since committing it would ship binary drift; a modified tracked path relocates only when the worktree's own copy is still at HEAD (a divergent worktree edit is authoritative, so the main-tree leak is discarded instead); added/deleted tracked paths and rename/copy leaks are conservatively discarded or (for rename/copy) abort the recovery. It returns true only when every new leak was handled and the main tree is clean of them; an undetermined case (e.g. `git status` itself failed) degrades to true so the tree-diff guard remains the backstop. Pinned by `TestRecoverBuildLeak_RelocatesIntoRealWorktree`, `TestRecoverBuildLeak_StagesOnlyRelocatedPaths`, `TestRecoverBuildLeak_RelocatesTrackedEditWhenWorktreeClean`, `TestRecoverBuildLeak_DiscardsTrackedEditWhenWorktreeDiverged`, `TestRecoverBuildLeak_DiscardsRebuiltArtifactEvenWhenWorktreeClean`, `TestRecoverBuildLeak_RelocatesUntrackedEvalDeliverable` and `TestRecoverBuildLeak_RelocatesTrackedEvolveConfigEdit`.
- **A lane's recovery never claims a main-tree path keyed to an owner it does not hold, whether the path is new or an edit to a committed file; the guard exempts such a path only when a live sibling or the mint registry holds it.** The regression twin `TestRecoverBeforeReview_SiblingLaneEvalWrittenMidPhaseStaysInTheMainTree` puts two lanes on one main tree: a sibling writes `.evolve/evals/<sibling-slug>.md` after this lane's phase starts, and this lane's source-writer recovery leaves it in place. The preservation side: this lane's own leaked eval and a genuine source leak are still relocated and staged (`TestRecoverBeforeReview_OwnLaneEvalLeakStillRelocates`, `TestRecoverBeforeReview_SourceLeakStillRelocatesBesideASibling`), a sibling's eval that appears during this lane's scout stays put (`TestRecoverBeforeReview_SiblingEvalDuringScoutStaysForItsOwner`), a registered mint is never moved (`TestRecoverBeforeReview_RegisteredMintStaysInTheMainTree`), and a sibling's edit to a committed eval is not stolen (`TestRecoverBeforeReview_SiblingEditToACommittedEvalIsNotStolen`). The guard still charges a lane's own keyed leak while the lane's own lease is live (`TestGuardChargesThisLanesOwnEvalWhileItsOwnLeaseIsLive`), and a read-only phase still discards a rebuilt release binary (`TestRecoverBuildLeak_DiscardsRebuiltArtifactEvenWhenWorktreeClean`, one subtest per write authority). The Specification is table-tested by `TestMainTreeOwnership_ForeignOwnerNamesEveryOwnerKeyedClass`, `TestMainTreeOwnership_UnknownIdentityClaimsLikeASingleWriter`, `TestMainTreeOwnership_OwnsEvalOnlyForTheLanesOwnSlugs`, `TestMainTreeOwnership_HeldBySiblingNeedsALiveHolder`, the `TestLaneOwnership_*` tests (including `TestLaneOwnership_MaterializedEvalsAloneMakeTheIdentityKnown`: a lane that has materialized any eval has a known identity, so a slug it never materialized is not claimable) and `TestLiveSiblingsOf_ReadsOnlyLiveRunsOtherThanThisLane`.
- **The scout's eval home is its workspace, and a main-tree copy of its own eval does not stay in the main tree.** Its phase relocates the copy into the lane's worktree before review, so no later phase sweeps it over a TDD-authored eval and no sibling can take it. Pinned by `TestRecoverBeforeReview_ScoutsOwnMainTreeEvalCopyLeavesTheMainTree` and `TestGuardIgnoresScoutEvalMaterialization`.
- **A phase's recovery never claims what was dirty when the phase started**, on the fresh path and on crash-resume. Pinned by `TestRunCycle_PhaseBaselineExcludesASiblingWriteBeforeThePhase` and `TestRunCycleFromPhase_PhaseBaselineExcludesASiblingWriteBeforeThePhase`, where a sibling writes an unowned file between two phases, and by `TestMaybeRemediate_FixDispatchBaselineExcludesASiblingWriteDuringTheGate`, which covers the fix dispatch and the gate's re-run.
- **`evolveDeliverablePrefixes` marks the `.evolve/` subpaths that are genuine repo content** (an agent's legitimate cycle deliverables: `evals/`, `phases/`, `commit-prefix-scope.json`) and relocates them into the worktree like any other leak; everything else under `.evolve/` — runtime state (`runs/`, `worktrees/`, `state.json`, `ledger.jsonl`, `instincts/`, nested `guards.log`) and the trust-sensitive operator-privilege documents (`.evolve/profiles/`, `.evolve/policy.json`) — is never relocated, so an agent write there stays unrecoverable and forces human review rather than letting an auditor review the file that redefines its own constraints. Pinned by `TestRecoverBuildLeak_SkipsEvolveRuntimeStateAndNestedWorktreeDir` and `TestRecoverBuildLeak_SkipsNestedEvolveRuntimeState`.
- **`preserveOnVerdict` keeps a normally-completed cycle's worktree when its final verdict is FAIL**, because a FAIL leaves the builder's work uncommitted and the default exit-cleanup prune would otherwise discard it; a PASS/SHIPPED_VIA_BUILD cycle's work is already in main and a SKIPPED_UNKNOWN produced nothing, so those still clean up normally. Pinned by `TestPreserveOnVerdict`.
- **`LeakRecoverablePhase` and `WorktreePhase` are deliberately separate predicates.** `WorktreePhase` gates write permission for the role gate. It holds for the source writers of `phasecontract.SourceWriterPhases`. `LeakRecoverablePhase` holds for every phase that runs with an active cycle worktree: triage, audit, scout, tdd, build and `bug-reproduction`. Thus leak recovery reaches all of them without a wider write permission, and `bug-reproduction` is in both sets since 2026-10-20. Pinned by `TestLeakRecoverablePhase_CoversAllWorktreePhases`, `TestLeakRecoverablePhase_ExcludesPhasesWithoutAnActiveWorktree` and `TestWorktreePhase_UnchangedByRecoveryDecoupling`.
- **Every correction-ladder rung emits one `gate.corrected` INFO signal** (`CodeGateCorrection`, module orchestrator), on both the fresh-cycle and resume dispatch roots, naming the correction ordinal, the budget, the rung and whether the CLI escalated — so the signal stream reads `rejected → corrected (1/2, redispatch) → passed` for a phase boundary. Pinned by `TestReviewWithCorrections_EmitsGateCorrectedPerRedispatch`, `TestReviewWithCorrections_EscalatedRedispatchSaysSo` and `TestResumeReviewGate_EmitsGateCorrectedOnTheResumeRoot`.
- **`Next`'s verdict branch resolves through a phase's catalog descriptor (`OnPass`/`OnFail`) before falling to the literal per-phase switch** (ADR-0058): when a catalog is wired and declares both fields, the target denormalizes through `phaseFromRouter`; an unresolvable target (an operator typo) fails loudly with `ErrTransitionInvalid` rather than returning an empty phase with a nil error, since that silent shape would let a ship-intended cycle skip its successor unnoticed. Absent a catalog, or a phase declaring neither field, `Next` degrades to the exact literal table — byte-identical, proven by the frozen oracle `TestTransitionKernelOracle_Next`. Pinned by `TestNext_AuditVerdictBranchFromSpec`, `TestNext_AuditDegradesToLiteralWhenCatalogUnset`, `TestWiredStateMachine_ReproducesAuditOracle` and `TestNext_UnresolvableTargetErrorsLoudly`.
- **`gateSatisfied`'s artifact-floor thresholds are config-driven** (ADR-0060): a catalog gate's `verdict_in` set decides which verdicts (PASS, WARN) satisfy a phase's evaluator handoff against the trusted Go signal digest, and an absent handoff never satisfies (anti-fabrication). With no catalog it degrades to the literal artifact map, byte-identical to before. Pinned by `TestGateSatisfied_ConfigThresholds` and `TestGateSatisfied_DegradesToLiteral`.
- **Every `agent_subprocess` ledger writer must stamp `run_id`, enforced by a closed-set source scan, not a behavioral assumption.** Since ship's run-scoped binding lookup and the composition snapshot both refuse an auditor entry with no `run_id` for this run, an unstamped writer is invisible to ship and hard-stops `AUDIT_BINDING_NO_AUDITOR`. `agentSubprocessWriters` is the closed, deliberately-maintained set of files that construct an `agent_subprocess` entry; a scan over the whole module fails both when a new writer isn't listed and when a listed writer stops writing. Each writer must either call `core.RunIDFromWorkspace` directly or be listed in `centrallyStamped` naming the mechanism that stamps it for it (e.g. appending through the orchestrator's `stampingLedger`, which stamps every entry); `centrallyStamped` is self-pruning, since a listed file that starts referencing `run_id` directly fails until delisted, so the exemption list cannot rot into a blanket excuse. The scan is line-level (a line that assigns `Kind:`/`"kind":` writes; a line that compares with `!=`/`==` only reads) and requires the resolver call itself, not merely the string `run_id`, because a source scan can prove the resolver is called but never that its value reaches the emitted bytes — a first stamping attempt once called the resolver while `jsonCompact` silently dropped the key outside its own allowlist, and the source-level test stayed green throughout; only a behavioral test over the real writer catches that class, and each writer therefore also carries its own behavioral pin (`subagent/runid_stamp_test.go`, `cyclesimulator/runid_stamp_test.go`, `core/phase_bindings_fail_verdict_test.go`). Pinned by `TestAgentSubprocessWriters_AllStampRunID` and `TestAgentSubprocessWriters_SetIsClosed`.
- **`LedgerEntry.Source`** (`json:"source,omitempty"`) attributes which mechanism skipped a phase (`psmas`/`router`/`content`); `recordRoutingDecision` stamps `Source:"router"` on every `phase_skipped` entry it appends, and `omitempty` keeps every pre-existing entry's hash-chain bytes unchanged since the key never appears on an entry with no source. Pinned by `TestLedgerEntrySource_FieldPresent`, `TestLedgerEntrySource_RouterStamp`, `TestLedgerEntrySource_RoundTrip` and the amplification suite (`TestLedgerEntrySource_LargeSkipList_Amp`, `TestLedgerEntrySource_NonRouterSourcePreserved_Amp`, `TestLedgerEntrySource_DirectMarshal_Amp`).
- **The debugger of a fleet-rebase conflict may write exactly the conflicted paths.** `rebaseWithDerivedRegen` returns the non-derived paths it met in conflict. Derived projections are regenerated, never handed on.
  - `rebaseRecordingConflicts` is the one writer of `CycleState.ShipRecoveryConflicts`. The ship latch clears it with `ShipRecoveryCode`.
  - `worktreeWritablePaths` hands the paths to the debugger alone, and only while the recovery is a fleet rebase. `fenceFor` puts them in `Fence.Writable`.
  - `unmergedRebasePaths` reads with `-z`, so a conflicted path is the raw path that the fence compares, never git's quoted form. Pinned by `TestRebaseCycleBranchOntoMain_NamesAConflictedFileAsTheFenceSeesIt` (integration tier).
  - Pinned also by `TestRebaseWithDerivedRegen_NamesEveryNonDerivedConflictAndNoDerivedOne`, `TestRebaseRecordingConflicts_TheCycleRemembersWhatItsDebuggerMayResolve` (integration tier), `TestLatchShippedState_ForgetsTheConflictsOfTheRecoveryThatLanded`, `TestWorktreeWritablePaths_OnlyTheDebuggerOfAFleetRebaseMayWriteItsConflicts`, `TestResumePath_TheDebuggerOfAFleetRebaseMayWriteItsConflicts` and `TestRecoverFromShipError_TheFenceKeepsTheDebuggersResolutionOfTheConflictedFile`.
- **The carry compares the base the audited worktree stood on, never main's HEAD at audit time.** The auditor row's `git_head` is the project root's HEAD when the audit was bound, and a sibling's landing moves it while a lane's worktree keeps its base; `worktree_base_sha` is the cycle's worktree base, which `recordAuditBinding` writes from `CycleState.WorktreeBaseSHA`. `carriedAudit` reads `auditledger.Entry.AuditedBase` (the worktree base, else `git_head` on a row written before the field), declines a row that names no base before any comparison, and otherwise requires it to equal the base the change was authored on. Cycles 1724 and 1727 each paid a second audit before this. Pinned by `TestRouteRebasedExplanation_AMainThatMovedBeforeTheAuditStillCarries`, the carry's declines, `TestCarriedAudit_ARowNamingNoBaseNeverCarriesEvenOnAnEmptyBase`, `TestEmitPhaseBindings_TheAuditRowNamesTheBaseTheAuditedWorktreeStoodOn` and `auditledger`'s `TestEntry_AuditedBasePrefersTheWorktreeBaseAndFallsBackToTheHead`.
- **Every `LedgerEntry` field survives the JSON round trip.** `UnmarshalJSON` copies the hand-kept `ledgerEntryWire` field by field, so a field added to one and not the other reads back empty with every fake-ledger test green. Pinned by `TestLedgerEntry_EveryFieldSurvivesTheLedgerRoundTrip`, which fills every field by reflection.
- **A null `cycle` reads as absent, and a numeric `cycle` is a whole number within int32 bounds.** Append-only history carries `"cycle":null` (inbox-lifecycle rows of 2026-07-22), so rejecting it would fail every full-ledger read forever; int32 bounds keep 32-bit and 64-bit builds identical. `LedgerEntry.setCycle`; pinned by `TestLedgerEntry_NullCycleAbsorbed`, `TestLedgerEntry_Unmarshal_CycleOutOfRange`, `TestLedgerEntry_Unmarshal_FloatCycle` and `TestLedgerEntry_Unmarshal_MalformedCycle`.
- **The auditor row's exit code is the host's disposition, never the report's.** `auditBindingExitCode` maps PASS to 0, WARN to 1 and FAIL to 2, so a FAIL stays unshippable whatever the report says and cannot validate stale candidate evidence. Pinned by `TestEmitPhaseBindings_AuditFAIL_RecordsBinding_NoCachePut` and `TestEmitPhaseBindings_AuditBuild_CorrectMapping` (integration tier).
- **The legality graph is config-driven and validated at load as a hard gate.** `legalGraphFrom(config.LegalSuccessors)` builds the same graph the kernel's literal `allowed` map encodes (an empty config degrades to the literal graph, byte-identical); `ValidateSafetyInvariants` rejects a config graph that strands the ship terminal (no edge reaches it) as unreachable. An orchestrator constructed with an unsafe legality graph refuses to run any cycle at all, returning `ErrUnsafeConfig` before any phase executes — the trust-anchor relocation made enforceable, not merely validated. Pinned by `TestLegalGraph_ConfigMatchesLiteral`, `TestWithLegalGraph_EmptyDegradesToLiteral`, `TestValidateSafetyInvariants_ConfigGraphStrandsShip` and `TestOrchestrator_UnsafeLegalGraphFailsClosed`.
- **The lost-landing floor's verdict is WARN, chosen deliberately over PASS or FAIL.** The audit passed and the artifacts are real, so a downgrade to FAIL would misreport a healthy build as broken, while WARN is a value the dossier's own validation accepts and one `IsShippingVerdict` rejects, so the cycle stops counting as shipped throughput without lying about the work's quality. Detection is deliberately narrow: only a claimed shipping verdict with a recorded `ship-error.json` and no `ship-binding.json` counts — a cycle that never reached ship, or one whose `ship-binding.json` is present despite a transient ship-error, is not flagged, since the recovery machinery may legitimately survive a transient error and still land. Evidence is read from the cycle's own artifacts, deliberately never git HEAD movement, since in fleet mode a sibling lane moves HEAD constantly and a HEAD-delta detector would credit one lane's landing to another. The signal never halts the batch, since a landing race the recovery machinery already handled correctly does not warrant stopping healthy siblings; correcting the verdict is the whole remedy. An empty workspace path is guarded explicitly so detection can never fall back to reading the process's current working directory. Pinned by `TestDetectLostLanding_RealCycle1535IsNotAPass`, `TestDetectLostLanding_RealCycle1536Landed`, `TestDetectLostLanding_CycleThatNeverShippedIsNotFlagged`, `TestDetectLostLanding_OnlyShippingVerdictsAreFlagged`, `TestDetectLostLanding_NoWorkspaceDoesNotReadTheProcessCWD`, `TestDetectLostLanding_DoesNotHaltTheBatch`, `TestLostLandingVerdict_IsLegalAndNotShipping`, `TestFinalizeCycle_LostLandingDowngradesTheVerdictAndRecordsTheSignal` and `TestFinalizeCycle_LandedCycleIsUnchanged`.
- **`mandatoryAnchorsFor` derives the spine-anchor order entirely from config** (`cfg.Order` ∩ `cfg.Mandatory`), never a Go literal; a config with no `Order` degrades to the canonical anchor order, byte-identical to before. The artifact map (which phases gate, and on what) stays hardcoded. A non-anchor target (a user/optional insertion, or a post-spine phase) is unconstrained by `SpineSatisfiedUpTo` — the router's plan and legality gate guard those, and `CanTerminateEarly` gates end. Adding a never-skip phase like triage to the mandatory set cannot weaken the ship floor: `SpineSatisfiedUpTo` walks anchor indices rather than counting anchors, so a no-op anchor with no artifact gate cannot shift the index a naive count-based bound would use and silently drop the real ship-floor check (a shippable audit). Pinned by `TestMandatoryAnchorsFor_PureConfigOrderIntersectMandatory`, `TestMandatoryAnchorsFor_FollowsConfigOrder`, `TestMandatoryAnchorsFor_DegradesWhenOrderUnset`, `TestSpineSatisfiedUpTo_NonAnchorIsUnconstrained` and `TestSpineSatisfiedUpTo_TriageMandatoryDoesNotWeakenFloor`.
- **`recordJudgmentLesson` yields when its phase is authoritative** (an operator can put a judgment phase in `policy.ship_floor`), because both recorders would otherwise fire on the same FAIL and the fingerprint dedupe collapses their identical summaries, letting a P1 advice todo shadow a halt-capable `FailedRecord`. The lesson files at `carryoverPriorityLesson` (P1), never P0, since P0 would outrank the cycle's actual blocking work in the next planner pass. Its TTL is classified `IntentRejected` rather than the failure path's default string, which falls to `UnknownClassification`'s 1-day fallback bucket and would expire a lesson across a multi-day loop pause; a falsified premise does not become true again with time. The record persists immediately (mirroring `recordFloorVerdictFailure`) because several abort branches on both call sites return before `finalizeCycle` persists. Pinned by `TestJudgmentLesson_AuthoritativePhaseYieldsToFloorRecorder`, `TestJudgmentLesson_PriorityIsNotP0`, `TestJudgmentLesson_ExpiresAtSurvivesALoopPause` and `TestJudgmentLesson_PersistedToStorage`.
- **A judgment lesson dedupes by todo id across re-dispatch within a cycle**, and both dispatch paths (fresh and resumed) must call `recordJudgmentLesson` alongside `recordFloorVerdictFailure` from the same shared phase-completion boundary, since a path-specific wiring gap is silent until a mid-batch recovery hits it. Pinned by `TestJudgmentLesson_DedupedByID` and `TestJudgmentLesson_WiredInLockstepWithFloorRecorder`.
- **Every catalog phase that declares `classify.verdict_from_sentinel` must be in `judgmentTeachingPhases`.** Letting a phase's stated verdict become the routed verdict without also teaching from its FAIL stops a cycle on an objection that leaves no trace, so the next cycle re-derives the same falsified premise. The check binds at declaration (not at the stage word), so promoting a tracked catalog phase from shadow to enforce can never open the gap. It covers git-tracked catalog phases only; a runtime-minted, untracked phase that declares the key gets neither this guard nor phasespec's stage-word guard (author docs carry that requirement in prose). Pinned by `TestJudgmentTeachingPhases_CoverEveryPhaseThatCanStateItsOwnVerdict`.
- **Every dispatched phase runs with cwd=worktree; `worktreePhase` gates the WRITE axis only, not that.** Audit's read-only verification commands must inspect the builder's pending work in the worktree, not an empty main tree, so cwd isolation is universal since CB.1; `worktreePhase` instead gates role-gate write permission to exactly the source writers (tdd, build), with audit and the read-only spine as non-writers. Pinned by `TestCB1_EveryDispatchedPhaseCarriesWorktree` and `TestWorktreePhase_WriteAxisIsSourceWritersOnly`.
- **The advisor sees CLI health before it plans.** `benchedCLIsForRouting` projects the cli-health store's active benches into the advisor's prompt context; without it the advisor could keep proposing inserts routed to a CLI family that is currently quota-walled, since `RouteInput` otherwise carries no CLI state. Pinned by `TestBenchedCLIsForRouting_ProjectsActiveSorted`.
- **The recursion-guard denylist covers exactly `router`, `evolve-router`, `advisor` and `failure-advisor` (case-insensitive).** A proposed mint whose name matches one of these reserved control-plane identities is dropped from the registered set with an observable reason; every other mint name passes through untouched. Pinned by `TestMintConfigsFrom_RejectsAdvisorRoleMint`.
- **The advisor's bridge launch must carry the cycle's git worktree.** Under `EVOLVE_FLEET=1` the tmux driver refuses an empty `BridgeRequest.Worktree` (no process-cwd fallback), so `RouteInput.ActiveWorktree` flows into it exactly like a normal phase dispatch's `PhaseRequest.Worktree`; the orchestrator copies `cs.ActiveWorktree` into the `RouteInput` it feeds the advisor for the same reason. Pinned by `TestAdvisorLaunch_ThreadsActiveWorktree` and `TestAdvisorPlanInput_ThreadsActiveWorktree`.
- **The Plan prompt must instruct the agent to write the absolute workspace artifact path, not a relative `routing-plan.json`.** Under claude-tmux the REPL cwd is not the workspace (it varies per cycle — repo root or worktree), so a relative write lands where the bridge never polls, which times out and degrades the cycle to the static spine; the absolute path in the prompt must equal `advisorLaunch`'s own watched `ArtifactPath`. Pinned by `TestPhaseAdvisor_PlanPromptUsesAbsoluteArtifactPath`.
- **`sanitizeAdvisorTier` is the sole gate for advisor-emitted tiers.** It accepts exactly the canonical tier vocabulary (`fast`/`balanced`/`deep`/`top`) and rejects everything else, including a legacy model-name alias (e.g. `opus`) — the advisor emits tiers, never model names, and loosening the gate to accept extra strings would violate the driver-agnostic model-routing invariant. Pinned by `TestSanitizeAdvisorTier` and `TestSanitizeAdvisorTier_RejectsHighAndRawModel`.
- **A regular file at the workspace path is treated as nothing to archive.** `archivePollutedWorkspace` degrades to a no-op rather than trying to fix or remove a non-directory at the workspace path; downstream code is left to fail loudly or overwrite it. Pinned by `TestArchivePollutedWorkspace_NotADirReturnsNoOp`.
- **An unrecoverable ship failure preserves the worktree; a recovered ship cleans it up as normal.** When ship aborts the cycle on an integrity-class error, the exit cleanup must not prune the worktree — the audited work needs to survive for operator/recovery triage; when ship recovers and eventually succeeds, the normal cleanup still applies so retries never leak worktrees. Pinned by `TestOrchestrator_ShipFailureAborts_PreservesWorktree` and `TestOrchestrator_ShipRecoversThenSucceeds_CleansWorktree`.
- **`model_routing=auto` is the only mode that projects the advisor's `{cli,tier}` plan onto phase dispatch**; `advisory` and `static` compute and persist the same clamped plan to `phase-plan.json` but never write it into the `PhaseRequest`. Pinned by `TestModelRouting_AutoApplies`, `TestModelRouting_AdvisoryLogsNotApplies` and `TestModelRouting_StaticIsNoop`.
- **An advisor outage (`Plan` returns an error) degrades model routing to the static per-phase profile rather than aborting the cycle** — dispatch continues with empty CLI/tier overlays. Pinned by `TestModelRouting_AutoDegradesToProfileStatic`.
- **The two `model_routing_*` wire fields on `PhaseRequest` are `omitempty`** so a zero-value request marshals with neither key present — an unaware consumer sees no new shape.
- **Missing mandatory-phase handoffs make the router record a fail-open `spine-unsatisfied-warn` per transition at the Enforce stage but never block the cycle.** Pinned by `TestOrchestrator_Enforce_SpineUnsatisfied_WarnsButProceeds`.
- **Phase-timing entries capture `started_at`/`ended_at` by bracketing the actual dispatch, not stamping one instant twice.** Pinned by `TestPhaseTiming_EndStrictlyAfterStart_AdvancingClock`.
- **The `--resume` path (`RunCycleFromPhase`) stamps `started_at` too** — a post-crash resume is exactly when latency evidence matters most. Pinned by `TestPhaseTiming_ResumeStampsStart`.
- **The upfront-plan `RouteInput` must carry the cycle's `IntentRequired` bit through to `ClampPlanToFloorWith`**; without it the floor clamp can't force intent into the plan and the advisory override silently drops the operator's `EVOLVE_REQUIRE_INTENT=1` gate. Pinned by `TestOrchestrator_ThreadsIntentRequiredToPlannerInput`.
- **The tree-diff guard snapshots every dispatched phase, not only worktree-gated ones.** A non-worktree phase (scout, triage, retro, an inserted/minted phase) that writes an untracked file into the main tree fires the guard, filtered only by `isLegitimateMainTreePath`'s allowlist. Pinned by `TestGuardCatchesInsertedPhaseLeak`, `TestGuardIgnoresLegitimateWorkspaceWrite` and `TestIsLegitimateMainTreePath`.
- **The guard and `recoverBuildLeak` share one legitimacy vocabulary for worktree-phase writes**, so the two mechanisms never disagree on what a main-tree write is allowed to be. Pinned by `TestGuardIgnoresOrchestratorSelfWrite_WorktreePhase`.
- **A worktree phase cannot smuggle a deliverable out via rename.** Even when the rename's new path classifies as legitimate runtime state, the old (deliverable) path keeps the guard armed. Pinned by `TestGuardCatchesDeliverableRenameSmuggle_WorktreePhase`.
- **Inserted/minted-phase leak coverage exercises the genuine end-to-end seam** (`parsePhasePlan` → `ClampPlanToFloorWith` → `registerMintedPhases` → dispatch → tree-diff guard): a minted phase's main-tree leak auto-recovers into its real worktree, while the same phase writing inside its own worktree ships clean. Pinned by `TestInsertedPhaseMainTreeLeakRecovers` and `TestInsertedPhaseWorktreeWriteIsClean`.
- **A selected-but-unregistered phase skips loudly only when genuinely Optional** — WARN, the order walk continues, the phase never appears in `PhasesRun`; a missing runner for a mandatory phase still aborts as a wiring bug. Pinned by `TestRunCycle_UnregisteredOptionalPhase_SkippedLoudly` and `TestRunCycle_UnregisteredMandatoryPhase_StillFatal`.
- **No phase dispatch path may run cwd=main-tree, and an abnormal abort must never prune the worktree.** Three independent call sites (`RunCycle`, `RunCycleFromPhase`, and the out-of-band failure-learning retro) each build their own `PhaseRequest` and must honor this. Pinned by `TestCB1_ResumePathCarriesWorktree`, `TestCB1_FailureLearningRetroCarriesWorktree` and `TestAbortCleanupPreservesWorktreeDiff`.
- **The orchestrator additively splices a checkpoint block** (`reason: "phase-complete"`, `completedPhases`) into `<projectRoot>/.evolve/cycle-state.json` after each phase, so `evolve loop --resume` can reconstruct progress after a kill at any boundary; a failed phase never appears in `completedPhases`. Pinned by `TestOrchestrator_PhaseBoundaryCheckpoint` and `TestOrchestrator_FailedPhase_NoSuccessCheckpoint`.
- **A review-gate rejection is recorded as a cycle-level `abort_reason`; the phase's own agent verdict is never rewritten.** A non-canonical verdict is never recorded raw or upgraded — exhausted retries always synthesize FAIL. Pinned by `TestPhaseOutcome_AbortPaths_AlwaysRecordTimingAndUsage` and `TestPhaseOutcome_NeverInventsPass`.
- **A cycle's outcome label comes only from its own ship latch, never external signals** (e.g. another lane's landing moving HEAD); when it did ship, the own-ship result wins even over a retro would-have-blocked advisory. Pinned by `TestFinalizeOutcome_SkippedWithoutOwnShip_NeverShippedViaBuild` and `TestFinalizeOutcome_OwnShipTrumpsAdvisory`.
- **The ship latch (`CycleState.Shipped`) has one home, written by both dispatch roots the moment ship PASSes, and survives a pause/resume.** `completeCycle` forwards the persisted latch to `finalizeCycle` as the outcome label's sole source; HEAD movement (a sibling lane landing mid-cycle) never substitutes for it. Pinned by `TestRunCycle_ShipPassPersistsTheShipLatch`, `TestResumeLifecycle_ShipPassPersistsTheShipLatch`, `TestCompleteCycle_ForwardsTheShipLatchToTheOutcomeLabel`, `TestResumeLifecycle_PostShipSkippedVerdictReadsTheCheckpointLatch` and `TestRunCycle_EmptyTriageCommitmentSurvivesSiblingLanding`.
- **A failed phase's self-reported structured failure block (class/defects/evidence) flows verbatim into the deterministic retrospective and lesson YAML**; supervisor-synthesized summaries are used only when the phase died without self-reporting. Pinned by `TestRecordFailureLearning_StructuredBlockFlowsIntoArtifacts`.
- **An OPTIONAL phase exhausting retries on an infra-shaped error degrades to WARN and the cycle advances** (audit/ship still run); a MANDATORY phase with the same exhaustion, or an optional phase failing non-infra, both stay cycle-fatal. Pinned by `TestOptionalPhaseInfraTimeoutSkipsAndCycleShips`, `TestMandatoryPhaseInfraTimeoutStaysCycleFatal` and `TestOptionalPhaseNonInfraErrorStaysCycleFatal`.
- **The static `nextInOrder` fallback can never run a phase the advisory plan explicitly vetoed (`Run: false`)**, even when `enforceNext` declines the router's proposed jump because `SpineSatisfiedUpTo` rejects it for a missing anchor artifact. Pinned by `TestOrchestrator_AdvisoryPlanVetoSurvivesSpineDecline`.
- **The router runs under an observer like every phase.** The advisor's `Plan` (`planCycle`) and post-scout `RePlan` dispatch the router through the bridge, never through `runner.Run`, so they never reached `observer.Start`. `observedPlan` and `observedRePlan` (`router_observer.go`) start the observer under the router's agent label (`routerAgentLabel`, the one spelling `phase_advisor.go` uses) with the cycle workspace and run id, and stop it when the router returns. The observer then finds the router's pane through `router-pane-watch.json`. Pinned by `TestRouterObserver_InitialPlanRunsUnderARouterObserver` and `TestRouterObserver_PostScoutRePlanRunsUnderARouterObserver`.
- **`WithObserver(nil)` leaves the `noopObserver` default rather than assigning a nil interface**, because the orchestrator calls `observer.Start` unconditionally and a nil `Observer` would panic on that dispatch. Pinned by `TestOrchestrator_WithNilObserver_FallsBackToNoop`.
- **Build explanation sealing waits for host-side normalization**: the first Audit run must see gofmt-normalized Build output, never the raw source Build wrote. Pinned by `TestOrchestrator_NormalizesBuildBeforeSealingExplanationForFirstAudit`.
- **`CycleRequest.Env` propagates to every `PhaseRequest.Env`**: phases read `EVOLVE_CLI` and `EVOLVE_*_MODEL` from it for CLI/model selection; without the passthrough every cycle silently hardcodes to claude-p and the default model. Pinned by `TestOrchestrator_CycleEnv_PropagatesToEveryPhase`.
- **`CycleState.IntentRequired` is persisted** so resume and downstream consumers (the classifier) can tell whether intent ran this cycle. Pinned by `TestOrchestrator_IntentGate_DefaultRunsScoutFirst` and `TestOrchestrator_IntentGate_PhaseEnableRunsIntentFirst`.
- **Intent-required resolution precedence is Context over Env**: `Context["intent_required"]` overrides the environment knob.
- **`PlanJudge` is deliberately not a routing brain, and fails open by signature.** It implements neither `router.Planner` nor `router.Proposer`, so `router.Select` can never wire it as a routing brain; dispatching under the non-router `judge` agent label closes the recursion path without needing the mint denylist. It runs on the fast/cheap tier (`claude-tmux`/`haiku`) by design (D2): deep reasoning is reserved for the confidence-critical Plan/RePlan. `GradePlan` returns only a `JudgeVerdict` value (no error), so nil bridge, empty workspace, nil plan, a bridge error, unparseable output, or an out-of-range score all funnel to the sentinel `Score=-1` — this inverts the advisor convention, where `PhaseAdvisor` returns an error for the kernel clamp to catch; the judge has no kernel clamp behind it. Pinned by `TestRouteQualityJudge_RecursionGuarded`, `TestRouteQualityJudge_ScoresPlanAgainstGoal` and `TestRouteQualityJudge_FailOpenToMinusOne`.
- **`phaseRole`'s audit/build mappings are load-bearing exact strings.** `audit`→`auditor` and `build`→`builder` are the exact role strings ship's audit-binding (`findLatestAudit`) and the `rt-001-ledger-role-completeness` red-team predicate depend on; every other phase binds under its own name (identity fallback), so a user-defined phase still gets a stable, predictable role, and the identity fallback must never rename a known agent role. Pinned by `TestPhaseRole`.
- **`PhaseRequest`/`PhaseResponse` must round-trip through `encoding/json` exactly.** They are the wire format for the subprocess phase override (`pkg/phaseproto`) as well as the in-memory contract between the orchestrator and phases. Pinned by `TestPhaseRequest_JSONRoundTrip`.
- **`VerdictPASS`/`VerdictFAIL`/`VerdictWARN`/`VerdictSKIPPED` are the only allowed verdict strings**, matching EGPS gate semantics. Pinned by `TestVerdict_Recognised`.
- **The phase-timing dossier and the durable log always project the same composed record.** On resume, `flushPhaseTimings` appends the in-memory (resumed) segment to whatever is already on disk rather than substituting for it, so the pre-crash prefix is never dropped; the abort path flushes before the dossier is built and the normal path after, but either way a flush is exactly-once — re-invoking `flushPhaseTimings` must never re-append the same entries. A phase that dispatches twice (a failed attempt plus a repair) appears twice in the log; composition never de-duplicates repeated dispatches, since the log records dispatches, not distinct phases. Pinned by `TestFlushPhaseTimings_ResumePreservesThePreCrashPrefix`, `TestFlushPhaseTimings_IsExactlyOnce` and `TestFlushPhaseTimings_DuplicatePhasesAreReality`.
- **The phase-timing recorder is a lazy Null Object until a Signal Center is wired.** An `Orchestrator` assembled as a bare literal, and a `cycleRun` with no orchestrator at all, both get the Null-Object recorder lazily on first use, once, so every code path still has exactly one writer; `NewOrchestrator` instead builds the wired recorder eagerly. The recorder reads the orchestrator's Signal Center through an accessor rather than a constructor-time snapshot, so `WithSignalCenter` applied after `NewOrchestrator` still routes the recorder's own warnings instead of leaving them silent for the orchestrator's whole life. Pinned by `TestRecorder_LiteralOrchestratorGetsTheNullObjectRecorderOnce` and `TestRecorder_SeesASignalCenterAppliedAfterConstruction`.
- **`phase-timing.json` has exactly one writer.** `cycleRun.flushPhaseTimings` (which caches so composition happens once per cycle) is the one path, calling the writer defined in `internal/core/failure_learning.go`; a second raw caller of `(*outcome.Recorder).WritePhaseTimings` would re-append entries the log already holds, so the durable log and the dossier built from it would silently disagree, and only on the resume path. Because the writer method is exported, Go visibility alone cannot make "one writer" structurally true, so `TestPhaseTimings_SingleWriter` source-scans every non-test `.go` file in the module (outside `internal/core/outcome/`, which defines the method) for a second caller. A legitimate new writer must route through `flushPhaseTimings` on the cycle's own `cycleRun`.
- **`comparePhaseIOShadow` is an independent re-derivation, not a tautology.** It re-derives the expected values directly from the legacy `router.RoutingSignals`, never through `HandoffsFromSignals`, so a projection bug in `HandoffsFromSignals` surfaces as a mismatch instead of being masked by comparing a value against itself. `TestComparePhaseIOShadow_CoversAllProjectedFields` pins that every field `HandoffsFromSignals` projects is compared, including `DefectsBySeverity`'s ordinal-to-word key conversion.
- **Each phase's shadow-equivalence test pins the exact legacy `Context` key it reads.** Scout reads `strategy`, `goal` and the camelCase `challengeToken` (not snake_case); triage reads `fleet_scope` and `carryover_summary` (not `carryover`); intent reads `goal`; ship reads `commit_message`; the debugger reads the four `ship_error_*` keys. These tests in `phaseio_shadow_test.go` run unconditionally, exercising `assembleCycleInputs`/`assembleErrorContext` directly rather than through the `EVOLVE_PHASE_IO`-gated production shadow hook, proving the typed `CycleInputs`/`ErrorContext` envelope is a faithful shadow of every reader ahead of the eventual cutover to reading the typed envelope directly.
- **`compareCycleInputsShadow` is cap-aware, not cap-blind.** It runs the legacy `want` value for the `previous_verdict`/`carryover` fields through the same `phaseio.CapField` bound the typed assembler applies, so an over-cap dispatch is never flagged as a false mismatch and the ledger's mismatch message never leaks the uncapped free text; a genuine drift inside the retained prefix is still reported. Pinned by `TestCompareCycleInputsShadow_CapAware_NoFalseMismatch` and `TestCompareCycleInputsShadow_DetectsRealDrift`.
- **The per-cycle phase-output survey lives in `internal/core`, not the loop CLI.** Fleet-dispatched lanes never traverse the CLI's single-loop post-cycle block; the finalize defers in `RunCycle` and `RunCycleFromPhase` run for every cycle on every dispatch topology, abort paths included, so the emission cannot be skipped by how the cycle was launched. Reading goes through `internal/phaseoutputs`' shared loaders — every decision (gap, chain state, abnormality) is made in that pure layer, and `internal/core` is a thin adapter over it. A cycle that aborts and later resumes emits once per finalize, so two events land on one stream; consumers take the LAST event per cycle (`EventPhaseOutputsSurveyed`). Pinned on both topologies by `TestRunCycle_EmitsPhaseOutputsSurveyToUnifiedStream` and `TestRunCycleFromPhase_EmitsPhaseOutputsSurvey`.
- **`IsShippingVerdict` is the one definition of the shipping-outcome vocabulary; `shippedOutcome` (the throughput hook) and the loop's non-progress breaker both consume it — never a second copy — so a newly added outcome label can't reach one consumer and not the other.** `shippedOutcome` additionally requires HEAD movement between the cycle's pre/post probes, since even a shipping-shaped verdict with an empty or unmoved HEAD (git unavailable, or nothing landed) is not honest throughput evidence. The recorder seam (`ThroughputRecorder`, injected via `WithThroughputRecorder`) fires only after a cycle that actually shipped, and hands the in-memory `State` to the injected recorder to append the cycle's passed coverage-floor count to the rolling window (`state.json`'s `triageThroughput`); nil is a no-op, byte-identical to before the seam existed. The window logic itself lives in `internal/triagecap`, which imports `core` for the entry type, so `core` cannot import it back — the seam is the one-directional import boundary. `hasThroughputCycle` prevents a replayed closeout from double-recording the same cycle. Pinned by `TestShippedOutcome`, `TestThroughputRecorderWired_Probe`, `TestIsShippingVerdict_WholeOutcomeVocabulary` and `TestIsShippingVerdict_IsTheOneDefinitionShippedOutcomeUses`.
- **`State`, `CycleState` and the other state.json sub-records are aliases of the `cyclestate` leaf's DTOs.** `State`, `CycleState`, `BatchAccrual`, `FailedRecord`, `CarryoverTodo` and `TriageThroughputEntry` (`ports.go`) are aliases of the pure on-disk DTOs defined in the zero-dependency leaf `internal/cyclestate`, hoisted there to keep the most-serialized value types out of this package; the aliases keep every existing `core.State`/`core.CycleState`/... call site and the `Storage`/`Ledger` port signatures unchanged, while the byte-identity-critical JSON shape is owned by the leaf.
- **`BridgeRequest.Skills` and `SystemPrompt` share one producer→adapter split.** The runner resolves policy (which skill/system-prompt for which phase/cli/model/tier) and the bridge adapter is the injector that materializes the resolved content into the launch prompt — config decides, the adapter only applies it.
- **A legacy string-`cycle` `LedgerEntry` normalizes to the canonical form on re-marshal.** Re-marshaling a `LedgerEntry` decoded from a legacy string-`cycle` line produces `cycle:0` plus an explicit `cycle_label`, never the original string form, so downstream writers and consumers only ever see normalized data. Pinned by `TestLedgerEntry_RoundTrip_StringCycle_NormalizesToLabel`.
- **`repinShipSHAAfterBuild` closes the within-version-rebuild frozen-pin class.** A within-version rebuild of `go/bin/evolve` between boots can leave `state.json`'s `expected_ship_sha` pin frozen and stale, denying the ship gate with `SELF_SHA_TAMPERED` even though the rebuild is legitimate. `repinShipSHAAfterBuild` re-pins `expected_ship_sha` immediately after a successful build phase, reusing the exact same provenance-gated primitive (`phaseintegrity.RepinIfDrifted`) the boot healer uses, so the two paths can never diverge; a provenance-unverified binary change is never re-pinned, preserving tamper detection; a project with no built binary yet is a fail-open no-op, never a panic. Pinned by `TestBootRecovery_RepinsAfterBuildNotJustBoot`, `TestBootRecovery_AfterBuildRepin_ShipGateSeesNoMismatch`, `TestBootRecovery_PostBuildRepin_UnverifiedProvenance_KeepsPin` and `TestBootRecovery_PostBuildRepin_NoBinaryIsNoOp`.
- **`MergeWorkspacePrescriptionCarryover` carries a WARN prescription forward regardless of continuation binding.** Audit's defect ledger already tags a WARN-carried prescription as an OPEN `"PRESCRIPTION: <text>"` row, and `reconcileAgainstAncestor` already blocks PASS on any unaccounted OPEN row, but only when the current cycle is formally bound as a continuation of the ledger-holding cycle; an ordinary next-lane ship (triage picks lanes by content, not by ledger lineage) never reconciles, so the prescription would otherwise be silently dropped at ship. `MergeWorkspacePrescriptionCarryover`/`Lifecycle.MergePrescriptions` closes that gap: at the cycle-terminal hook (`finalizeCycle`), any OPEN `"PRESCRIPTION: "` row is merged into `state.CarryoverTodos` (dedup by id, idempotent on re-entry) regardless of continuation binding, while FIXED/DEFERRED rows and ordinary (non-prescription) defects are left alone. `TestRunCycle_MergesPrescriptionCarryoverIntoState` is the wiring proof that the real terminal path persists the todo, not just a correctly-behaving but unwired helper.
- **The all-families-quota-exhausted short-circuit lands before `recordFailedApproachState`, not merely before the retro dispatch.** On this arm `CycleState.Phase`/`ActiveAgent` are never mutated to `retro` (so `evolve loop --resume` re-enters the exhausted phase, not retro), `state.FailedAt` and P0 carryover-todo bookkeeping are not recorded, and the retro runner is never called — matching the ADR-0106 Q1 rule that a quota wall is a DEFERRED checkpoint, never a diagnosed failure. The guard matches the typed `ErrAllFamiliesExhausted` sentinel via `errors.Is` through multiple wraps, never "any transient/85 exit": a single-family exit=85 attempt with a differently-shaped sibling is not the all-85 signature and must still learn normally (one retro dispatch, `FailedAt` recorded, the P0 carryover todo queued). A deferred ship quota-exhaustion checkpoint is still kept out of failure learning, but its dispatch error is threaded into `CycleState.ShipFailReasons` since the ADR-0072 verdict-coherence floor reads that carrier regardless. Pinned by `TestRunCycle_AllFamiliesExhausted_DoesNotDispatchRetro`, `TestRunCycle_AllFamiliesExhausted_NeverWritesRetroCycleState`, `TestRunCycle_NonQuotaDispatchFailure_StillDispatchesRetroOnce`, `TestRecordFailureLearning_MultiplyWrappedExhausted_SkipsRetro`, `TestRecordFailureLearning_ShipExhausted_PreservesCoherenceCarrier` and `TestDispatch_SingleFamily85WithSibling_FailureLearningUnchanged`.
