- **`recurrence.Ledger.RecordClosure` is wired at the deterministic retro-closeout seam, not merely exported.** A FAIL cycle's lesson pattern is persisted to `<root>/.evolve/recurrence-ledger.json` (via `Load`/`Save` under flock) keyed by the failing cycle number, with escalation apply staying boundary-only there (nil Escalator/Autofiler) to avoid racing `inboxmover.Claim`. Pinned end-to-end through the real `RunCycle` entry point by `TestC662_RetroCloseoutRecordsClosureInLedger`.
- **A remediated gate's re-run deliverable is reviewed like the original dispatch, not recorded on its verdict alone.** `maybeRemediate`'s re-dispatch response still reaches the reviewer before `recordAndBranch`, so a re-run that omits a declared deliverable or fails any contract check cannot be recorded on the strength of its verdict alone. Pinned by `TestRemediation_GateReRunIsReviewed`.
- **`composeRepairBrief` carries the auditor's own findings, not just the coherence floor's gate strings.** Gate reasons come first (deterministic evidence outranks prose). `auditRejectionReasons` reads them from `CycleState.AuditFailReasons` (`runnerDiagnosedAudit`, the one predicate the X2a classifier, the gate slot and the header lead share), the carrier the coherence floor also asks, never from the forensic `audit-fail-reason.json`, so a leftover record of another phase or a failed best-effort write cannot decide the slot, and the brief reads no file there to WARN about. An agent-graded FAIL has no runner reasons, so the slot carries the verdict sentinel's failure-block defects instead (`phasecontract.ReadFailureBlock`), less any defect that restates a finding the brief lists (`briefedFindings`, the first eight; title, id and title, or id, severity and title, compared on letters and digits). The findings header calls the section above it "gate reasons … their symptoms" only when runner reasons lead. The retry adjudicator's prompt reads the same function. The brief continues with the rejecting round's actual auditor findings (CRITICAL/HIGH first, MEDIUM after, LOW omitted — parsed by the same grammar the dashboard renders, `reportdoc.Findings`, so the operator and the agent read one list), then which findings PERSISTED from the previous round (matched by `reportdoc.FindingKey` against the archived `audit-report.round<N-1>.md`) — the "you were told this already" signal that turns a blind retry into a targeted one. `archiveRepairPrompts` archives the previous round's prompt beside the audit archives (`<phase>-prompt.round<N>.txt`) so what each round was actually told stays forensically recoverable; an existing archive is never clobbered.
- **`repairRoundTier` raises a repair-round re-dispatch to the profile's declared override tier.** It raises a tdd/build re-dispatch's model tier to the phase profile's declared `model_tier_overrides[audit_retry_2plus]` while `CycleState.AuditRepairActive` is set (the same persisted flag the repair brief derives from), because repair rounds re-dispatched at an unchanged tier do not converge. No declaration means the rule is inert (config decides, not Go); the raise is raise-only and envelope-clamped exactly as the ADR-0076 D floor. It is wired through both the live dispatch loop and `resume.go`'s request builder, and the resume path also archives the previous attempt's prompt like the live loop does. Pinned by `TestRepairRoundTier_RaisesToDeclaredOverrideForSeededPhases`, `TestRepairRoundTier_RaiseOnlyAndDeclarationRequired`, `TestRepairRoundTier_EnvelopeMaxClampsThroughRealGuardrail`, `TestRepairRoundDispatch_RaisesBuildTierThroughLiveLoop` and `TestRepairRoundResume_RaisesBuildTierAndArchivesPromptThroughResumePath`.
- **A colliding remediation slug must never suppress the retrospective, the lesson, or either defect's inbox item.** Two defect lines whose text shares a rune prefix longer than `remediationSlugMaxRunes` must not collide into one inbox id and silently suppress the retrospective, the failure lesson, or either defect's own addressable inbox item — even though `remediationSlug` alone would derive the identical id for both titles. The disambiguating suffix must be derived from each defect's full text. Pinned by the executable reproduction `TestRepro1285_F1_CollidingRemediationSlugSuppressesRetrospectiveAndLesson`, which drives the real `writeDeterministicLearning` seam (not the lower-level `faillearn.WriteArtifacts`) so the collision is minted by the real failure-learning engine (`internal/core/failurelearning`).
- **`SealCycle` preserves a sealed cycle's full history; it never simply discards it.** `SealCycle` (`reset.go`) is the complement of `resume.go`: where resume continues a checkpointed cycle, `SealCycle` abandons a stuck/unfinished one while preserving its full history — the workspace moves into a sibling `<workspace>.reset-<UTCnano>/` archive (never deleted) alongside a verbatim cycle-state snapshot and a `reset-manifest.json`, `lastCycleNumber` advances past the sealed cycle so the number is never reused, an auditable hash-chained ledger entry is appended (`cycle:0` + `cycle_label`), and `cycle-state.json` is removed as the abandon commit point that disarms the phase-gate precondition. `state.json` is mutated through a full-fidelity map, never the typed `core.State`, because the typed struct would drop unmodelled fields (like `expected_ship_sha`) on round-trip — the same trap `ship/statefile.go` documents.
- **`AutosealStaleMarker` auto-seals a stranded `cycle-state.json` marker at loop boot when its owner PID is dead, reusing `SealCycle(Force)` rather than a bespoke seal path.** A crashed cycle strands a role-gated marker (e.g. `phase=retro`) whose role-gate then blocks operator/inbox writes, gating even the recovery actions behind a manual `evolve cycle reset --force`; auto-sealing clears it. `markerShouldAutoseal` fails safe toward sealing when the marker's owner pid is unusable (liveness cannot even be asserted) and leaves a live owner's in-progress cycle untouched; liveness is an injected `kill -0`-style probe so tests never depend on the real OS process table. Without a `runlease` naming the owner, the boot path defers to the operator's unfinished-cycle guard (`resume`/`reset`) rather than tearing the cycle down. The automated path stamps `SealOptions.AutomatedRecovery` alongside `Force` (which overrides the fresh-but-dead-owner lease fence), and forces the seal even when the lease heartbeat still reads fresh, since liveness here is pid-based, not heartbeat-age based. Pinned by `TestMarkerShouldAutoseal_DeadOwner`, `TestMarkerShouldAutoseal_LiveOwnerUntouched`, `TestMarkerShouldAutoseal_MissingPidFailsSafe` and `TestAutosealStaleMarker_DeadOwnerSealsViaSealCycleAndClearsBlock`.
- **`SealOptions.AutomatedRecovery` closes a self-declared-role trust gap.** An automated seal (unattended boot self-heal, triggered merely by a dead owner PID) writing `Role:"operator"` identically to a human-initiated seal would let anything that can kill the owning process mint a trust-anchor-eligible ledger line with no human involved. `AutomatedRecovery` routes the automated path to the distinct `operator-autoseal` role instead, which the ledger verify epoch-anchor resolver does not recognize as an operator sign-off.
- **`SealCycle`'s `state.json` mutation shares `storage.UpdateState`'s lock, and ordering is load-bearing.** It runs its `failurelog.Record` call and its own lastCycleNumber/batch read-modify-write under the SAME `<state.json>.lock` sidecar `storage.UpdateState` holds, on the resolved path (a worktree's `state.json` is a link to canonical, so the sidecar lives beside the link where no canonical-path writer contends). `Record` runs before the seal's own RMW, so the seal's write — which re-reads the file and picks up the new entry — stays the final authority on `lastCycleNumber`/`currentBatch`/`lastUpdated`. Without this shared lock a concurrent `storage.UpdateState` caller can read state before `SealCycle` writes and then clobber `SealCycle`'s write with its own stale-based write (a classic lost update); the shared `flock.PathLock(statePath)` serializes the two so both writers' changes survive, under both a single concurrent writer and many, and the lock is released promptly on `SealCycle`'s success path rather than leaked. Pinned by `TestSealCycle_ConcurrentUpdateStateNoLostUpdate`, `TestSealCycle_ManyConcurrentUpdateStateWritersNoLostUpdate` and `TestSealCycle_LockReleasedPromptlyAfterCompletion`.
- **`reset_concurrency_test.go` lives in `core_test`, not `core`, to avoid an import cycle.** It needs to import `adapters/storage`; `storage` imports `core`, so an internal (`package core`) test file importing `storage` would form an import cycle.
- A resolved fleet-rebase conflict never re-audits or reships on the base the ship diverged from; a conflict that survives the debugger ends the cycle instead of a reship that cannot fast-forward (`TestRecoverFromShipError_ADebuggerResolvedConflictReentersTheRebaseNotAStaleReship`, `TestRecoverFromShipError_AConflictTheDebuggerLeftEndsTheCycleWithoutAReship`).
- A resolved fleet-rebase conflict never re-audits or reships on the base the ship diverged from; a conflict that survives the debugger ends the cycle instead of a reship that cannot fast-forward (`TestRecoverFromShipError_ADebuggerResolvedConflictReentersTheRebaseNotAStaleReship`, `TestRecoverFromShipError_AConflictTheDebuggerLeftEndsTheCycleWithoutAReship`). A cycle the debugger ends ends FAIL: a BLOCK, an empty or unknown decision, and every re-entry abort (the conflict survives, the host rebase fails, the carry, pend or route step fails, or the budget is spent). Its ship never landed, so the closeout's failure walk releases its claim for a retry. The FAIL carries a ship fail reason, so the ADR-0072 verdict-coherence floor reads it as diagnosed, not as a forged verdict over green audit artifacts (`TestRecoverFromShipError_AnUnlandedShipOverAGreenAuditIsADiagnosedFAILNotAForgedVerdict`). Before this, the last floor verdict stood (cycle 1725 ended WARN with its item stranded in `processing/`) and the consecutive-fail breaker never saw the failure (`TestRecoverFromShipError_ACycleWhoseRebaseReentryAbortsEndsFAIL`, `TestRecoverFromShipError_ADebuggerThatBlocksAShipRecoveryEndsFAIL`).
- **`SealCycle` resolves a worktree's `state.json` symlink for both the sidecar lock and the read-modify-write, and never severs the link.** A worktree's `state.json` is a link to the canonical file; `SealCycle` takes the `<canonical>.lock` sidecar every canonical-path writer contends on, never a sidecar beside the link, and its `failurelog.Record` plus its own read-modify-write always write through the link, leaving it a symlink rather than replacing it with a regular file. Pinned by `TestSealCycle_SymlinkedStateLocksCanonicalTarget`.
- **`SealCycle` fences sealing on the per-run lease (`runlease`), never the coarse `.evolve/.lock`.** The cycle lock is not held in the gap between cycles, so a sibling `evolve cycle reset` can race a live loop exactly there; the per-run lease heartbeat is the liveness source of truth regardless of whether any flock is held. A fresh heartbeat refuses the seal with `ErrCycleOwnedLive` unless `--force`; a stale, missing or unparsable lease means the owner is gone and the cycle auto-reclaims with no `--force`. `.evolve/.lock` is deliberately not made batch-scoped — that would break shipped fleet concurrency, and the heartbeat already spans the whole run. Pinned by `TestSealCycle_RegressionCycle395` and `TestSealCycle_LeaseFencing`.
- **`LoadResumeState` falls back to a fleet per-run scan only on `ErrNoCheckpoint` with no lane override active.** A fleet lane writes its quota/escalation checkpoint through the same resolver but scoped to its own `.evolve/runs/<id>/cycle-state.json` (`ipcenv.CycleStateFileKey`); a host-global `--resume` (fresh process, no override) resolves only the singleton and would otherwise report no checkpoint while a live per-run one exists. The fallback triggers only on `ErrNoCheckpoint` (a stale PRIMARY checkpoint is a real, specific answer — never scanned past) and only when no lane override governs this evolve dir (inside a lane the override IS the authority, and scanning siblings would let one lane resume another's cycle). Pinned by `TestLoadResumeState_DiscoversFleetPerRunCheckpoint`, `TestLoadResumeState_HostGlobalWinsOverPerRun`, `TestLoadResumeState_EnvOverrideDisablesDiscovery`, `TestLoadResumeState_StalePerRunCheckpointReportsStaleNotMissing` and `TestLoadResumeState_NoCheckpointsAnywhereStillErrNoCheckpoint`.
- **Only an escalation-pause checkpoint (`quota-likely`, `batch-cap-near`, `operator-requested`, `stall-inactivity`) is resumable by another process; `phase-complete` never is.** `PhaseBoundaryCheckpointer` writes an enabled `phase-complete` block after every phase of a healthy run. It is a crash-recovery breadcrumb for the same process. A live lane's worktree exists, so the stale checks do not refuse it. Admitted, it lets a host-global `--resume` drive a running lane twice, or bring back a cycle that already ended in FAIL. Pinned by `TestLoadResumeState_PhaseCompleteBreadcrumbIsNotDiscoverable`.
- **`discoverPerRunResumeState` excludes any candidate with a fresh `runlease` and resumes the newest valid sibling, never the newest unconditionally.** A fresh lease means the lane is alive right now, so resuming it would double-drive the cycle; a quota-paused lane's process has exited, so its heartbeat is stale and it stays discoverable. Candidates load newest-first (`savedAt`, an RFC3339 string so lexicographic order is time order, with `cycle_id` breaking same-second ties); lanes are independent (no supersession), so when the newest is stale an older valid sibling is resumed and a breadcrumb on `opts.Log` names the skipped one. Only when every candidate fails does the newest's error surface, since "stale" and "nothing to resume" send an operator down different paths. Pinned by `TestLoadResumeState_FreshLeaseExcludesCandidate`, `TestLoadResumeState_StaleLeaseDoesNotExclude` and `TestLoadResumeState_NewestStaleFallsBackToOlderValidAndSaysSo`.
- **Discovery refuses a checkpoint that a later launch shipped past, and names the stopped lanes (2026-10-21, inbox item `resume-after-a-fleet-signal-stop`).**
  - In wave 27 a SIGINT stopped lanes 1739 and 1740, which wrote no checkpoint. `--resume` then picked the quota checkpoint of cycle 1709 from the day before. Its `gitHead` was `unknown`, so the HEAD check did not apply.
  - A run counts as finished when its run state is `end` or `aborted`, or when the ledger holds a landed ship for it. `landedShips` reads that row: `role: ship`, `kind: phase`, `exit_code: 0`. A shipped run state stays at phase `ship`, so the phase string alone does not say it shipped.
  - A checkpoint is stale when a later cycle of another launch (another `goal_hash`) shipped after its `savedAt` (`shippedAfter`). It stays resumable after a pool refill of the same launch, a ship before the pause, or a later launch that failed. The refusal names the discard command `EVOLVE_CYCLE_STATE_FILE=<path> evolve cycle reset`.
  - A checkpoint is also stale when a later launch of another goal started after its `savedAt` (`startedAfter`, 2026-10-21). The stopped lanes of that launch are the evidence. A run without `started_at` cannot be judged this way.
  - A checkpoint older than policy `resume.max_checkpoint_age_h` (compiled default 192 h, one weekly quota reset plus a day) is stale too.
  - When `origin/main` has only moved past the checkpoint's base, the checkpoint stays resumable, and the resume logs a WARN with the commit count (`warnMainMoved`). Main moves several times an hour through console merges. Ship's stale-base rebase (`staleBaseAsRebase`) lands the resumed cycle. The claim judge (`CheckpointResumable`) keeps the paused lane's claim.
  - The checkpoint writers (`internal/checkpoint`) record the base as `gitHead`. The base falls back to the run's `worktree_base_sha` or `pre_cycle_head`. When `origin/main` cannot be read, the log says why. The check reads the local `origin/main` ref and does not fetch.
  - Each stale refusal names `EVOLVE_CYCLE_STATE_FILE=<path> evolve cycle reset`. It names the deliberate override `EVOLVE_CYCLE_STATE_FILE=<path> evolve loop --resume` only for use while `evolve inbox claims` still shows the item held. An explicit state file logs a WARN that it waives the age check.
  - Pinned by `TestLoadResumeState_RefusesACheckpointThatALaterLaunchStartedAfter`, `TestLoadResumeState_AMainThatOnlyMovedResumesWithAWarning`, `TestLoadResumeState_RefusesACheckpointOlderThanTheConfiguredAge`, `TestCheckpointResumable_AMovedMainKeepsTheCheckpointAndAllowHeadMovedWaivesTheAge`, `TestLoadResumeState_TheStateFileOverrideWaivesTheAgeBoundVisibly` and `TestBoundaryCheckpointers_RecordTheCycleBaseAsGitHead`.
  - `perRunVerdict` judges each unfinished run: a live lease, no enabled checkpoint, a reason that is not resumable, or stale. `refusalList` names the stopped lanes and the stale checkpoints, up to 20, then a count. It sums the checkpoints that are not resumable by reason, such as `phase-complete 173`.
  - `evolve cycle reset` renames the run dir to `<dir>.reset-<stamp>` (`resetArchiveMarker`), and discovery skips such an archive. A `savedAt` that is not RFC3339 leaves the checkpoint resumable, and the log line says that the stale rule did not apply.
  - Pinned by the tests in `resume_signal_stop_test.go`. They write the shapes of real runs: a shipped run at phase `ship` with its ledger row, and a stopped lane at phase `build`.
- **A resumed cycle must write its next checkpoint back to the file its resume point came from, not the host-global singleton.** `ResumePoint.StatePath` records the origin file (the singleton for a host-global resume, a lane's per-run file when discovery found it there); `ActivateResumeStatePath` points this process's cycle-state resolution at that file via the same `ipcenv.CycleStateFileKey` mechanism a fleet lane uses at spawn, returning a restore func (a no-op when nothing needed to change). Without it, a resumed cycle would checkpoint to the singleton on its next pause while later discovery keeps looking in the per-run file. Pinned by `TestActivateResumeStatePath_RoutesResolutionToTheDiscoveredFile` and `TestActivateResumeStatePath_NoOpWhenAlreadyResolved`.
- **`RunCycleFromPhase`'s worktree teardown must run before its storage-lock release, and must read the closeout state live at defer time.** `clearActiveWorktree` is a read-modify-write of the persisted cycle state and must run under the storage lock this function still holds, so the teardown defer is registered before the lock-release defer (LIFO: teardown fires first). The closure reads `execution.closeout` at defer time rather than capturing it early, since a run that returns before `completeCycle` decides `preserveWorktree`/`cycleCompletedNormally` must see zero values and preserve the worktree, never prune it. Pinned by `TestRunCycleFromPhase_PrunesItsWorktreeOnNormalCompletion`, `TestRunCycleFromPhase_PreservesAFailedCycleWorktree` and `TestRunCycleFromPhase_PreservesAnAbnormallyExitedWorktree`.
- **A resumed deliverable is reviewed with the fresh loop's own skip set — no reviewer, or a SKIPPED verdict — and a legacy (version-0, pre-explanation-contract) checkpoint is never an extra reason to skip.** `mandatoryExplanationReviewer` already delegates on version 0 itself, so exempting version-0 checkpoints a second time at the resume call site silently exempted every other reviewer (the contract gate, the declared-deliverables gate) for any resumed legacy cycle ([ADR-0100](../adr/0100-declared-deliverables-gate.md) §4). Pinned by `TestBuildFloorReviewer_LegacyCheckpointVersionZeroIsExempt`.
- **`RunCycleFromPhase` must append the same ledger provenance entries `RunCycle` does — a `role=auditor kind=agent_subprocess` binding after a shippable audit, and a `role=builder kind=agent_subprocess` entry after build — bound to the resumed cycle's current git HEAD.** Ship's audit-binding verification reads the latest such entry; a resume path that emits neither leaves ship binding to a stale entry from an earlier cycle. Pinned by `TestRunCycleFromPhase_EmitsAuditBinding` and `TestRunCycleFromPhase_EmitsBuildBinding`.
- **`RunCycleFromPhase`'s resume-target guard accepts any phase registered in `o.runners`, not only the 13 spine phases `Phase.IsValid()` recognizes.** An advisor-inserted phase (e.g. a minted `mutation-gate`) is registered in `o.runners` at runtime via `MintPhase` and is a legitimate resume target once a checkpoint records it as `resumeFromPhase`, even though it is not spine-valid; `PhaseEnd` and `PhaseStart` stay rejected regardless, since neither is ever a resumable target. Pinned by `TestRunCycleFromPhase_InsertedPhaseInRunnersAccepted`.
- **A resumed advisor-inserted phase (not spine-valid, e.g. `bug-reproduction`) rehydrates the transition kernel from the run's `routing-plan.json` rather than consulting the static state machine.** `RunCycleFromPhase`'s dispatch loop reads the plan's `{"phase","run"}` sequence to find the next planned phase after the inserted one; when the plan is absent (deleted, or a pre-plan checkpoint) the cursor degrades the inserted phase to its archetype and routes to the next static phase instead of failing. Either way, an inserted-phase resume never surfaces a bare `core: invalid phase` error. Pinned by `TestRunCycleFromPhase_InsertedPhaseTransitionsViaPlan` and `TestRunCycleFromPhase_MissingPlanDegradesNotInvalidPhase`.
- **A resume-loop transition failure (e.g. the resolved successor has no registered runner) must funnel through `recordPhaseOutcome` before returning, never escape bare.** The stalled phase gets a `<phase>-usage.json` sidecar with a non-empty `abort_reason`, so the outcome is `FAILED_EXPLAINED` rather than the [ADR-0044](../adr/0044-unified-phase-recovery-protocol.md) C1 `FAILED_UNEXPLAINED` alarm a bare transition-error return would produce. Pinned by `TestRunCycleFromPhase_TransitionFailureRecordsAbortReason`.
- **The resume dispatch loop (`resumeExecution.run`) mirrors the fresh loop's per-phase behaviors one by one, since it is a first-class dispatch surface, not a lesser copy.** It stamps `PhaseStartedAt` before dispatch; threads the persisted `ActiveWorktree` and `RunID` through `PhaseRequest` so a resumed phase never falls back to cwd=main-tree or a fresh run identity; on `PhaseAudit` resets the floor fail-reason and supersedes the previous audit round's verdict artifacts exactly as the fresh dispatch does; after Build succeeds waits for host normalization before the explanation review, matching `reviewAndGuard`'s ordering; and latches ship-checkpoint state the same way `applyPostReviewGuards` does, so closeout reads this cycle's own ship result regardless of which path produced it. It also calls `decideAfterAuditFail` on an audit FAIL — without it the resume surface would fall through to the static `audit→retro` edge and, since retro is now terminal, the repair a granted retry needs would be permanently unreachable on exactly the surface built for recovery. Its history-branch entry condition stays lockstep with the fresh loop's (`successorStrategy == history`), though the branch body differs by design: resume calls the deterministic `decideAfterRetro` directly, where the fresh loop additionally routes through `decideAfterRetroRouted` at the advisory stage. `seedAuditRepairContext`/`consumeAuditRepairGrant`/`consumeBookkeepingRegradeGrant` are all keyed on `next` (the phase about to run), never `current` (the phase that just finished) — a `current`-keyed seed once missed the first TDD dispatch after a resumed repair grant and rebuilt blind. The debugger's signal-branch override (ADR-0058 S3) is deliberately NOT mirrored: resume only replays the retro/history override, so a cycle resumed at the debugger terminates via the static `debugger→end` edge rather than re-running `decideAfterDebugger`. Pinned by `TestResumePostDispatchExits_RecordTheirOutcome` and the package's broader resume-parity test suite (`resume_parity_*_test.go`).
- **Resume's post-dispatch error exits record through the same phase-outcome chokepoint as the fresh loop, but deliberately do not feed failure-learning the way `cyclerun_postreview.go` does.** Feeding failure-learning (which dispatches a retrospective agent) from one arbitrary resume abort exit would spend a retro dispatch the other exits do not, so all of resume's post-dispatch error returns are recording-only. Pinned by `TestResumePostDispatchExits_RecordTheirOutcome`.
- **`timingOwner` is constructed with `context.Background()` so its deferred timing flushes always complete, but the resume loop's iteration-limit chokepoint escape reassigns `timingOwner.ctx` to the loop's live `ctx` right before calling `recordChokepointEscape`.** `recordChokepointEscape` feeds failure-learning, which dispatches a retrospective agent and skips that dispatch only when its own ctx is already cancelled; leaving `timingOwner.ctx` at `Background()` would defeat that guard and spend a retrospective dispatch after an operator interrupt. The abnormal-epilogue defer is unaffected either way, since it checks the local `ctx` directly rather than reading the owner. All four of the owner's mutated fields (`cs`, `state`, `result`, `phaseTimings`) — not only the two the escape itself appends to — are read back afterward, because `recordFailureLearning` also stamps the retro phase into `cs.CompletedPhases`; skipping the read-back would let the abnormal-epilogue defer's own write of `cs` clobber that retro completion. Pinned (call-site) by `TestResumePostDispatchExits_RecordTheirOutcome`.
- **Resume's terminal `WriteCycleState` write (after `completeCycle`) is resume-only — the fresh path has nothing after it.** If it fails, the closeout has already latched `cycleCompletedNormally`, so the exit teardown still prunes a non-FAIL tree while the checkpoint block may still be live on disk; that is the intended direction, since ship has already merged the work and the tree is spent, so a later `--resume` fails loudly ("worktree no longer exists") instead of re-driving phases on a spent tree, which a preserved tree would invite.
- **`normalizeWorktreeToBase` verifies ancestry before resetting, because the persisted base can stop being an ancestor of the worktree HEAD after a rebase recovery.** A `reset --soft` to a non-ancestor base would repoint the branch and stage a huge spurious diff, so the helper skips (best-effort WARN) and leaves HEAD untouched when the base is not an ancestor. Both `RunCycle` and `RunCycleFromPhase` share the same normalize path, so a resumed build gets the identical post-build re-exposure of pending changes the fresh loop does. Pinned by `TestNormalizeWorktreeToBase_SkipsNonAncestorBase`, `TestNormalizeWorktreeToBase_Idempotent`, `TestRunCycleFromPhase_NormalizesBuildWorktree` and `TestRunCycleFromPhase_NoNormalizeWhenResumingPastBuild`.
- **Resume's history (retro) branch calls `finalizeRetroCompletion` exactly where the fresh history branch does, so a resumed cycle's disposition gate outcome is never silently recorded clean.** An absent or invalid `disposition.json` is surfaced loudly through `RetroDecision` (the `disposition-gate:` prefix), never recorded as a verified-clean retro, on both paths. Pinned by `TestRunCycleFromPhase_HistoryBranchSurfacesDispositionGate`.
- **The fresh-loop/`RunCycleFromPhase` compatibility table in [2026-09-11-large-component-decomposition-plan.md](../../reports/2026-09-11-large-component-decomposition-plan.md) is authoritative for which differences between the two paths are intentional** (parallel evaluation, remediation, the debugger override, legacy contract versions); any other divergence is a parity gap to close, not a design choice.
- **`applyDispatchPolicy`'s model-tier escalation runs after the mode-gated routing projection and independent of it: a retried scoped item still raises the Build dispatch tier to `deep` even when the registry runs `model_routing=static`.** The raise is clamped through the same envelope guardrail as the routing clamp (ADR-0076 D). Pinned by `TestEscalatedBuildTier_RaisesAtThresholdEnvelopeless`, `TestEscalatedBuildTier_RaiseOnlyNeverLowers` and `TestEscalatedBuildTier_EnvelopeMaxClampsThroughRealGuardrail`.
- **A Build dispatch's `BudgetScale` carries the cycle's difficulty multiplier so the engine can stretch the artifact-wait deadline; a scale of 1.0 is left unset, keeping legacy dispatches byte-identical** (ADR-0076 slice A). The same `cycle_size_estimate` signal (the existing scout/triage vocabulary, parsed by `router.Digest` off `triage-report.md` — no new agent contract) also scales the BUILD phase's contract-correction limit only (`correctionLimitFor`): audit and adversarial-review keep their configured ladder untouched, since judgment budgets are not difficulty-conditioned. `scaledCorrectionLimit` rounds half-up, clamps to `policy.MaxContractCorrectionRetries`, never shrinks the base, and treats a multiplier `<= 0` as identity, so a missing signal can never zero the ladder. Pinned by `TestBuildBudgetScale_FromTriageReport`, `TestBuildBudgetScale_AbsentReportIsUnscaled`, `TestSizeBudgetMultiplier_MapsAndDefaults`, `TestScaledCorrectionLimit_RoundsAndClamps`, `TestScaledCorrectionLimit_BuildPhaseOnly` and the composed proof `TestSizeBudget_ComposedLargeCycleExtendsCorrectionLadder`.
- **`degradeInsertedSuccessor`'s archetype-to-phase mapping never skips the audit gate.** A plan-role archetype (an advisor-inserted planning phase) resolves to Build; a build/evaluate archetype (e.g. `bug-reproduction`, `fault-localization`) resolves to Audit, mirroring the real bugfix flow; a control archetype resolves to Ship; any unknown archetype defaults to Audit, the safe integrity funnel.
- **`gitWorktree.Cleanup` refuses to operate when its worktree path resolves to the project root, since `git worktree remove --force` fails harmlessly there but the unconditional `os.RemoveAll` that follows would delete the repository.** The refusal (`sameDirectory`, matching by inode when both paths exist and falling back to text when one does not, so an unstattable-but-textually-identical path still refuses) lives in the provisioner itself, beside `deleteCycleBranch`'s `cycle-` gate, so every caller of `Cleanup` is covered, not only the orchestrator's exit path. This matters on the resume path specifically because `ActiveWorktree` is read from a persisted checkpoint rather than always coming from `worktree.Create`, and can name the project root itself (the same shape `ship`'s `shipDirect` branches on). Pinned by `TestGitWorktreeCleanup_RefusesTheProjectRoot`, `TestRunCycleFromPhase_ProjectRootCheckpointSurvivesTeardown` and `TestSameDirectory`.
- **`writeDeterministicLearning` files an inbox item only for a genuinely self-reported defect, never a synthesized summary echo.** A classed-but-defectless `FailureBlock` (a class set, zero `Defects`) leaves the synthesized summary as the sole entry and must file nothing; a `Defects` list whose one entry merely repeats the summary is the same degenerate shape and must also file nothing — both are a restatement of the failure, not an actionable item. The retrospective itself is still written either way; only inbox filing is suppressed. The call site reuses `faillearn.structuredDefects`, the one named rule for this degenerate case, rather than re-deriving the check. Pinned by `TestWriteDeterministicLearning_ClassedButDefectlessBlockFilesNothing`, `TestWriteDeterministicLearning_EchoDefectListFilesNothing` and `TestWriteDeterministicLearning_StructuredDefectsAreFiled`.
- **Each correction directive asks for a numbered record. A correction of a source writer also asks the bridge for the worktree-evidence contract** ([ADR-0113](../adr/0113-correction-completes-on-worktree-evidence.md)).
  - `composeCorrection(round, reason, remediation)` ends both branches with `correctionRecord(round)`. The agent appends a `## Correction N` section that names what it fixed and where, also when the fix is in another file. Thus the next review and every later reader see what each correction changed. A deliverable that is not Markdown is written again in full.
  - The request states only what the agent must write and why. How the bridge decides completion stays out of the prompt.
  - Both ladders thread their own ordinal: `corr` in `reviewWithCorrections` and `correction` in `reviewResumedDeliverable`. The salvage retry passes the same round through `composeContractSalvageRetry`.
  - `PhaseRequest.BridgeCompletion` is the one rule for who may complete on worktree evidence. That is a request with a correction directive, a worktree and no `Fence`. The fence comes from the orchestrator's projection, so audit, adversarial-review and every unregistered phase are excluded. Every other request keeps the artifact contract, and core still judges the carried deliverable through the ordinary review.
  - Pinned by `TestComposeCorrection_AsksForAnAppendedSectionNumberedByTheRound`, `TestCorrectionRecord_StatesTheAgentsDutyNeverTheHostsCompletionRule`, `TestCorrectionLadder_EachReDispatchAsksForItsOwnNumberedSection`, `TestCorrectionCallSites_PassTheRemediation` (it also scans `resume.go`) and the three `TestPhaseRequest_BridgeCompletion_*` tests. The rule's file, `bridge_completion.go`, is on the protected-surface manifest of the guards.
- **The remediation fix directive asks for the same numbered record.** `maybeRemediate` in `cyclerun_remediate.go` ends the builder fix directive with `correctionRecord(round)`, numbered by the remediation round. The builder appends a `## Correction N` section to its report. The placeholder check of the build floor already parses that heading (`correctionHeadingRE`). The read-only gate re-verify directive is unchanged. Pinned by `TestRemediation_FixDirectiveAsksForTheRoundsNumberedCorrectionRecord` and `TestRemediation_RequestedRecordRoundTripsThroughThePlaceholderCheck`.
- **The completion contracts are one typed vocabulary** (`ports.go`). `CompletionContract` declares `CompletionArtifact`, `CompletionStdout`, `CompletionGit` and `CompletionWorktreeEvidence` once, beside `BridgeRequest.Completion`, which carries that type. The zero value `""` is the artifact contract, and only the bridge's tmux drivers honour a contract (the headless drivers ignore it). The phase judge, the failure advisor and the retry adjudicator name `CompletionArtifact`; the advisor package cannot import core, so it keeps its own string and `bridgeRequestOf` converts it at the seam. The values are wire names: the bridge reads them from `--completion` and `BRIDGE_COMPLETION`, and they travel in the request's JSON. Pinned by `TestBridgeRequest_EachCompletionContractTravelsAsTheNameTheBridgeParses`, and from the bridge side by `TestCompletionContractVocabulary_SpelledOnce` and `TestCompletionContractVocabulary_EveryRequestNamesItsContractByTheTypedConstant`.
- **`composeCorrection`'s two branches differ in whether they forbid collateral changes.** A gate-authored remediation is relayed verbatim (the rejecting gate is the only component that knows what "fixed" looks like for its own violation) and deliberately omits "do not change unrelated files," because some remediations require creating an artifact the deliverable itself is not, and that clause would forbid the very fix requested; the no-collateral instruction survives in the default branch, where the artifact already exists and merely needs correcting. The rejection `reason` string must survive byte-for-byte, appearing exactly once: it carries `deliverable.summarize()`'s `[code] message` tokens, `contractViolationCodeRE` (`contract_escalation.go`) parses those same tokens back out of the directive downstream, and the re-dispatched agent reads the literal violation text — any lossy reformat, truncation or duplication breaks both consumers at once. Pinned by `TestComposeCorrection_CarriesReasonVerbatim`, `TestComposeCorrection_FramingSurroundsTheReason` and `TestComposeCorrection_EmptyReasonStillProducesADirective`.
- **`backoffSleep` is a package var, not an `Orchestrator` field, so `TestMain` can zero it once for the whole test suite instead of forcing every retry test to inject a no-op sleep at construction.** `executeRetryBackoff` is a free function; a per-instance field would force per-test churn the package var removes. It is set once before any test runs, so concurrent reads by parallel tests are safe.
- **`computeRetryEnvelope` is ADR-0072's category table's single retry authority; nothing else may decide legality.** It replaces two things that used to disagree: `fp.Categories[...]` was read for `Level` alone (Action/MaxRetries/FixType were unused), and ADR-0092 built a parallel `max_audit_repair_attempts` knob plus a disposition-prose eligibility rule beside it that arrived at the same cap of 2 independently. It is a pure predicate (no I/O, the policy is passed in) so the live, routed and resume paths get the same answer from one table. A declared class normalizes through `failurelog.NormalizeLegacy` before lookup, since the repo runs two vocabularies (policy's `infra-systemic`/`transport-hang` vs failurelog's `infrastructure-systemic`/`exit-transport-hang`, plus legacy `audit-fail`/`FAIL` both meaning `code-audit-fail`) and skipping normalization would silently decline a retryable class. A system-level declared class does not halt at this chokepoint: both floor gates require `IsFloor`, and minting a halt from an unauthenticated, uncorroborated agent-written class here would let non-floor system categories (transport-hang, non-progress) stop the batch on prose alone — declining instead routes to retro, where the full two-gate floor decides; only the deterministic floor candidate (gate 1) halts here. Pinned by `TestComputeRetryEnvelope`, `TestComputeRetryEnvelope_CapComesFromThePolicyTable`, `TestComputeRetryEnvelope_ZeroCapDisablesRetry` and `TestComputeRetryEnvelope_DeclineReasonsNameWhichVocabularyFailed`.
- **`policyCategoryFor` is the one join between the failurelog vocabulary (13 classes, what the audit contract renders and the deliverables gate enforces) and the policy vocabulary (7 categories, what the retry table is keyed by).** Before this join the envelope looked a failurelog spelling up directly in the policy table and declined "unrecognised class" for words the audit prompt itself recommended. Every known classification either joins to a category the default table knows or to `""` (a failure no rebuild repairs — human abort, operator reset, an integrity breach, a ship-gate config, a transient outage, a rejected intent, a fatal loop), which the envelope declines by name rather than as unrecognised. Pinned by `TestPolicyCategoryFor_JoinsEveryKnownClassificationOrSaysNone`.
- **`SealCycle`'s liveness fence is PID-aware, not lease-freshness-only.** `SealOptions.PidAlive` threads a liveness probe through the fence via `runlease.OwnerLive`: an owner whose process is dead but whose lease heartbeat is still fresh now seals without `--force`, while a genuinely live owner (fresh lease, alive pid) still refuses. A nil `PidAlive` preserves the old freshness-only fence for a caller that has not migrated. Pinned by `TestSealCycle_DeadOwnerFreshLease_SealsWithoutForce`, `TestSealCycle_LiveOwnerFreshLease_StillRefuses` and `TestSealCycle_NilPidAlive_PreservesFreshnessOnlyBehavior`.
- **`shiperror.go` is a back-compat shim over the zero-dependency leaf `internal/shiperr`**: type aliases, const re-declarations and thin func wrappers keep existing `core.ShipError`/`core.Code*`/`core.NewShipError`/`core.AsShipError` call sites unchanged while ship (constructor) and core (matcher) both import `shiperr` one-directionally. Every ship error code must stay reachable through `core`, not only `shiperr`, since consumers (the ship phase, the debugger-routing layer) import `core`. Pinned by `TestCodeManifestGate_DualRegistered` and `TestCodeRepoContractGate_DualRegistered`.

- **`seedTaskRecall` always replaces inherited recall context, including a no-match result, on both dispatch paths.** A scoped lane can never accidentally reuse an unrelated task's memory on resume; it reads the existing durable lesson corpus only and deliberately persists no second mutable memory summary. Pinned by `TestTaskRecall_ReplacesStaleMemoryWithoutMutatingCaller`, `TestAdvisorPlanInput_RecallsGoalWithoutHistory` and `TestDispatch_TaskRecallWithoutFailureHistory` (integration tier).

- **`ValidateSafetyInvariants` is the load-time trust anchor for the ship floor** (ADR-0060). The legality graph and gates are config, so the floor's non-gameability is checked over graph and config *roles* (mandatory anchors, verdict branches), never phase names, so any phase can be renamed without weakening it. Each check and its pinning test:
  - Branch targets (`on_pass`/`on_fail`) must name a known phase that is a legal successor; config selects among legal edges and never invents one (ADR-0058): `TestValidateSafetyInvariants_IllegalBranchTarget`, `…_UnknownBranchTarget`.
  - A config spine (`SpineOrder`) must join known phases by legal edges, because `Next` walks the spine without re-checking `CanTransition`, so an illegal jump such as scout→ship would route around an anchor: `…_IllegalSpineEdge`, `…_UnknownSpinePhase`.
  - `legal_successors` must name only known phases on both sides: `legalGraphFrom` silently drops an unresolvable name, so a typo would degrade the graph with no load error: `…_UnknownLegalSuccessor`, `…_UnknownLegalSuccessorTarget`.
  - A mandatory phase whose artifact gate constrains the verdict may accept only shippable verdicts (PASS/WARN), so a config cannot ship a FAILed evaluation: `…_FloorGateAcceptsOnlyShippableVerdict`.
  - Some mandatory phase must gate on a shippable verdict (F⊆M); without one, `SpineSatisfiedUpTo` admits ship with no verdict gate. A presence-only gate with an empty `verdict_in` does not count. It is judged only when the catalog describes a mandatory phase, because a synthetic catalog in an orchestration unit test cannot describe the evaluator's gate: `…_NoMandatoryEvaluatorRejected`, `…_PresenceOnlyEvaluatorRejected`, `TestSpineSatisfiedUpTo_ConfigurableMandatoryWeakensGate`.
  - Every mandatory anchor must be reachable from the start node, and a graph with no start node is itself a violation: `…_StrandedMandatoryAnchor`, `…_NoStartNodeRejected`.
  - Every start→ship path must traverse a floor evaluator: ship (the last mandatory anchor, by registry convention) must be unreachable once the whole evaluator set is deleted from the graph. Only the evaluator carries the verdict floor, so non-evaluator anchors stay runtime-backstopped by `SpineSatisfiedUpTo`: `…_EvaluatorBypassRejected`, `…_TwoEvaluatorsCollectiveDominance`; the reference flow passes (`…_ReferenceFlowPasses`).
- **Per-cycle worktree provisioning isolates source-writing phases (tdd, build) from the live tree**, so a failed or buggy cycle cannot mutate it; the role gate's only write allowance is `phase==build && ActiveWorktree!=""`. See ADR-0027.
- **`gitWorktree.Create` namespaces the branch and directory name on the stable per-root lane, not the per-invocation run ID**, so concurrent sibling worktrees of one repo never collide on a global `cycle-<N>` branch, and the worktree name survives resume. Pinned by `TestGitWorktree_ConcurrentSiblingsNoBranchCollision` and `TestGitWorktree_GuardStateIsRunScoped`.
- **A reused worktree is revalidated (`git rev-parse --git-dir`) and quarantined clean before its cycle resumes.** A pruned ref can leave a stale directory `os.Stat` sees but git rejects, and a prior failed attempt's uncommitted dirt would otherwise ride into the reused cycle's audited tree. Pinned by `TestEnsureCleanWorktree_QuarantinesInheritedOrphanRedTest` and `TestEnsureCleanWorktree_RepeatQuarantineNeverOverwritesSalvage`.
- **`gitWorktree.Create` always cuts a named branch, never `--detach`**, because worktree-aware ship resolves the cycle branch via `git symbolic-ref --short HEAD` and ff-merges it to main; a detached HEAD yields an empty branch and ship fails. Pinned by `TestGitWorktree_CreateUsesNamedBranch`.
- **A fresh lane is based on the current upstream tip, resolved only on the create path.** The reuse path never re-bases a live lane, since that would discard in-progress work. Pinned by `TestGitWorktree_Create_ReuseSkipsFetch` and `TestGitWorktree_Create_FetchesOriginBeforeBasingLane`.
- **`git worktree add` retries on lock contention.** Every fleet lane shares one `.git`, so concurrent `git worktree add` calls can collide on the repo-level lock and return rc=255 with no stderr beyond "Preparing worktree"; the provisioner treats that as retryable and still fails loudly with the same error after the last attempt. The retry announcement says "retryable", not "transient" — it fires for every failure the classifier does not rule out, which is not the same as an established contention case. Pinned by `TestWorktreeAddRetry_LockCollisionStillRetriesToBound`, `TestWorktreeAddRetry_AnnouncementDoesNotClaimUnclassifiedTransience`, `TestGitWorktreeCreate_RetriesTransientAddFailure` and `TestGitWorktreeCreate_PersistentFailureStillFailsLoudly`.
- **`laneStartRef` never bases a fresh lane on the local checkout's HEAD.** In a multi-lane fleet the shared checkout drifts behind the remote as sibling lanes land, so it fetches origin and cuts from the resolved integration head; a fetch failure fails loudly rather than silently basing on the stale local tip, and a remoteless repo falls back to `"HEAD"`. Pinned by `TestLaneStartRef_IntegrationHeadAuthority`, `TestGitWorktree_Create_NoOriginFallsBackToLocalHEAD` and `TestGitWorktree_Create_FetchFailureIsFatal`.
- **A local `main` ahead of origin is a lane base only when every ahead commit is proven (2026-10-21).** `unprovenAhead` (`landed_main.go`) reads `origin/<branch>..<head>` and accepts two commit kinds.
  - A boundary commit changes only `knowledge-base/cycles/` or `.evolve/inbox/`: a dossier closeout or the loop's inbox stamps.
  - A merge is accepted when origin holds each of its other parents, as for the `evolve sync-main` merge.
  - Any other commit, such as a strand from an older binary or a manual commit, makes `laneStartRef` refuse the base and name the commit. The lane then defers.
  - The check trusts a merge by its parents and does not read the merge's own conflict resolution. The boundary prefixes come from a survey of 200 closeout and stamp commits on origin/main, which touched no other path.
  - `rebaseCycleBranchOntoMain` runs the same check before the fleet rebase. An unproven commit refuses the rebase, so the identity carry and ship never treat it as a landed peer (cycle 1830). A fault while reading the range is a refusal too.
  - Pinned by `TestLaneStartRef_IntegrationHeadAuthority`, `TestRebaseCycleBranchOntoMain_AnUnpushedCodeCommitOnLocalMainIsNotALandedPeer`, `TestRebaseCycleBranchOntoMain_BoundaryCommitsAndASyncMergeStillCarry` and `TestUnprovenAhead_AFaultIsNotAProof`.
- **`laneStartRef` fetches only the base branch, and retries ref-lock contention.** It resolves the default branch first (`originDefaultBranch`) and fetches it alone, through `gitexec.FetchOriginBranch` with the explicit refspec `+refs/heads/<branch>:refs/remotes/origin/<branch>`. One bare store backs the runtime plane, every fleet lane and every console dev worktree, and any of them may fetch at the same moment; a bare `git fetch origin` updates every remote-tracking ref, so contention on any unrelated ref (cycle 1806: a train branch that `gh pr update-branch` had just moved) used to fail the lane's base. Contention on the base ref itself (`cannot lock ref`, `unable to update local ref`) is retried up to `gitexec.DefaultFetchAttempts` with the shared 2 s/4 s backoff; the backoff clock is the same `worktreeAddRetrySleep` seam the add retry uses, and each retry is announced as `[worktree] retry N/M: git fetch origin <branch> after ref-lock contention rc=…`. The branch is the remote's default, never a hard-coded `main`. Pinned by `TestLaneStartRef_FetchesOnlyTheDefaultBranch`, `TestLaneStartRef_FollowsTheRemotesDefaultBranch` (origin/HEAD → `origin/trunk`), `TestLaneStartRef_RetriesRefLockContention`, `TestLaneStartRef_PersistentContentionFailsAfterTheBound` and, against real git with a held `.lock` file, `TestLaneStartRef_RealLockOnAnUnrelatedRefDoesNotFailTheBase` and `TestLaneStartRef_RealLockOnTheBaseRefIsRetried`.
- **A fleet lane's provisioning is attempted twice before it defers.** In fleet mode, when `WorktreeProvisioner.Create` fails (after its own fetch and add retries), `newCycleRun` calls `reprovisionWorktree`, which logs a `re-provisioning once` WARN and calls `Create` again. `Create` is idempotent, so the second call reuses a valid half-built worktree or replaces an invalid one. A sequential cycle calls `Create` once, as before: it runs on best effort without a worktree, so a second attempt buys it nothing. Pinned by `TestRunCycle_AReprovisionedFleetLaneRunsNormally` and, for the single sequential call, `TestRunCycle_ASequentialCycleWithoutAWorktreeKeepsItsBestEffortRun`.
- **A provisioned worktree is checked before the cycle uses it.** `verifyFreshCycleSource` refuses a worktree that already holds an occupied copy of this fresh cycle's identity (the check runs after the upstream fetch), and the refusal ends the cycle before its state is persisted or any phase dispatches. The refused worktree is preserved, because `Create` may have reused it and does not say whether it did. The comment that said this in `newCycleRun` was removed on 2026-10-06 with its stale "creation remains best-effort" sentence; it is archived in [the comment history](../../history/code-comments/internal-core.md).
- **A fleet lane that still has no worktree is DEFERRED before any phase dispatches; it is never run and never FAILed.** In fleet mode (`EVOLVE_FLEET=1`) the bridge refuses every launch without an explicit worktree (`fleet mode: explicit worktree required`, exit 10), so dispatching anyway sealed the cycle FAIL, wrote a failure digest, ran failure learning and queued a lesson about the task, and counted toward the consecutive-failure rules, all for an infrastructure fault (cycle 1806). `newCycleRun` now returns a `*LaneDeferral{Cycle, Cause}` before the cycle state is persisted; `deferLaneWithoutWorktree` records one host ending (phase `start`, verdict `SKIPPED`, abort reason `lane-worktree-deferred: <cause>`, written through `cycleRun.flushPhaseTimings`, the one timing writer) so `cyclehealth.ClassifyOutcome` reads the cycle as `DEFERRED`, the existing deferral outcome the quota wall also uses, and seals the event stream with `cycle.sealed` WARN `ORCHESTRATOR_LANE_DEFERRED` (`fields.step=worktree`, `fields.cause`). No failure digest, `audit-fail-reason.json`, failure-learning carryover or retrospective is written, so neither the digest rules of the pipeline-blocker breaker nor the operator's consecutive-FAIL count sees a task failure. `evolve cycle run` releases any claim the cycle holds without a failure bump and exits `fleet.ExitDeferred` (5), which the wave counts as deferred, not failed. A sequential cycle keeps its best-effort run unchanged: there the bridge has the project-root fallback, and the provisioning cause is recorded in the failure digest as before. Pinned by `TestRunCycle_AFleetLaneWithoutAWorktreeDefersBeforeAnyDispatch` and `TestRunCycle_ASequentialCycleWithoutAWorktreeKeepsItsBestEffortRun`.
- **A deferral that does not clear halts the batch.** Because a deferral writes no failure digest, the digest rules of the pipeline-blocker breaker never see it, and a persistent provisioning fault (a stale `refs/remotes/origin/main.lock` in the shared store, a network or credential outage, any deterministic `Create` error) would otherwise defer every lane of every wave until `--max-cycles`. `CollectBatchLaneDeferrals` reads the batch's run dirs through `cyclehealth.ClassifyOutcome` and keeps only the `DEFERRED` cycles whose abort reason carries the `lane-worktree-deferred` prefix, so a quota DEFERRED never counts; `EvaluateLaneDeferrals` halts on `failure_policy.thresholds.lane_deferral_halt_ceiling` (default 3; a non-positive value keeps the default; the rule cannot be disabled from policy) consecutive deferred cycle numbers, with rule `lane-deferrals` and the last git cause in the reason. A cycle between them that did not defer (a ship, a FAIL) breaks the run. The other way round, a deferred cycle is transparent to the `consecutive-failures` run: `LaneDeferredCycleSet` passes the batch's deferred cycles in `BlockerBreakerConfig.LaneDeferredCycles`, and `consecutiveRun` steps over them (`adjacentOver`), so they neither count nor break it, and a lane that defers between failing lanes cannot hide the failures (F D F D F halts as `consecutive-failures`); a ship still breaks it. The consecutive-run counter is the one both rules use (`consecutiveRun`), and the run-dir walk is shared with `CollectBatchFailureDigests` (`batchCycleNumber`). A deferral whose record cannot be written (the workspace cannot be created) is not returned as a deferral: `deferLaneWithoutWorktree` returns a plain error that names the unrecorded deferral and the provisioning cause, so the cycle fails visibly instead of deferring where the breaker cannot see it (`TestRunCycle_ADeferralThatCannotBeRecordedFailsLoudly`). Pinned by `TestCollectBatchLaneDeferrals_CountsOnlyWorktreeDeferralsInTheBatch`, `TestEvaluateLaneDeferrals_HaltsOnlyOnAnUnbrokenRun` and `TestConsecutiveFailures_LaneDeferredCyclesAreTransparent`; the halts themselves by `cmd/evolve`'s `TestBlockerBreakerHalt_ConsecutiveLaneDeferralsHaltWithTheGitCause` and `TestBlockerBreakerHalt_AlternatingDeferralsAndFailuresHalt`.
- **Console hand-offs past a ceiling in a rolling window halt the loop** (`no_work_handoff_breaker.go`, inbox `no-work-hand-off-rate-alarm`). Since F30 a triage that empties `top_n` by drops ends its lane as planned no-work (exit 0). No FAIL rule counts the item that it hands to the console. `CollectNoWorkHandoffs` reads the last `window` cycle dirs above the acknowledged mark. It keeps those whose `committedset.HandedOff` record names a routed item, so an empty-backlog no-work cycle does not count.
  - `EvaluateNoWorkHandoffs` halts at the ceiling with the fingerprint `NoWorkHandoffFingerprint(last)`. `NoWorkHandoffsResolvedThrough` reads the highest acked cycle from the resolved-fingerprints ledger.
  - Pinned by `TestCollectNoWorkHandoffs_ReadsTheLastWindowOfCyclesAboveTheAckMark`, `TestEvaluateNoWorkHandoffs_HaltsAtTheCeilingWithAnAckableFingerprint` and `TestNoWorkHandoffsResolvedThrough_IsTheHighestAckedCycle`.
- **`integrationHead` resolves a fresh lane's base to the branch the landing actually targets, not always `origin/<default>`.** The remote tip is authoritative off the default branch or when the local main is current or behind (the wave boundary fast-forwards it); the local main is authoritative when it sits strictly ahead (its unpublished landings ride the next push); a diverged history is refused loudly rather than picking either. Pinned by `TestLaneStartRef_IntegrationHeadAuthority`.
- **`linkGuardDeps` symlinks the live dispatcher binary and the guard-read state files into every fresh worktree.** The trust-kernel PreToolUse hooks run `$CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR/go/bin/evolve guard ...`, and the harness pins `CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR` to the cwd (the worktree), never a pre-set value, so the binary and `.evolve/` state — both absent from a fresh checkout — must be linked in or the hooks fail and source phases stall. `cycle-state.json` links to the run's own `run.json` mirror, not the host-global file, because concurrent runs hold whichever run wrote it last; the link may briefly dangle until the first `WriteCycleState` resolves it. A failed link is logged, not swallowed, because a missing state link could let a guard read empty state and wrongly allow a denied tool. Pinned by `TestGitWorktree_GuardStateIsRunScoped`.
  - The `state.json` link is also the cycle-worktree mark that `inboxmover` reads. A mutating inbox verb refuses when its inbox directory sits beside that link, and names the plane root. Thus no `inboxmover` verb moves the tracked inbox copy of the worktree ([internal-inboxmover.md](internal-inboxmover.md)). Pinned by `TestProvisionedWorktree_ItsTrackedInboxCopyRefusesTheMover`.
- **The cross-lane mint exemption verifies content, not just registry membership.** The registrar records a minted name in the shared `mintregistry` before persisting its files, so a concurrent fleet lane's mint landing mid-phase is exempted from the tree-diff guard by name; but a registered name alone buys nothing — the guard also re-parses the on-disk `phase.json` against the same spec-side clamp the registrar enforces (parses, user-floor valid, optional, name matches the directory), so a forged registry entry with an unclamped spec still aborts loudly. A legitimate mint writes exactly `phase.json`; any companion file under a registered name is still a leak. A corrupt registry file is quarantined on the spot (renamed aside) rather than left to degrade the exemption fleet-wide, and the next read sees a clean empty registry. A mint older than `mintregistry.TTL` no longer exempts its path, so the registry cannot decay into a standing allowlist. Pinned by `TestGuardExemptsConcurrentLaneMintedPhaseConfig`, `TestGuardStillAbortsUnregisteredPhaseConfigLeak`, `TestGuardAbortsForgedRegistryEntryWithUnclampedSpec`, `TestGuardAbortsCompanionPayloadUnderRegisteredMint`, `TestGuardQuarantinesCorruptRegistryAndStaysArmed` and `TestGuardStillAbortsExpiredMintPhaseConfigLeak`.
- **`truncateGoal` and `maxGoalTextChars` project the advisor's own goal-text cap; they are consumers, not a second definition.** The judge's goal section and the task-recall digest both render through this projection, at `textcap.TruncateRunes` applied at `advisor.MaxGoalTextRunes` (4000), so core never silently re-implements the advisor's bound. Pinned by `TestTruncateGoal_ProjectsTheAdvisorCap`.
- **The advisor plan never offers a phase whose persona doc is deterministically known to be missing.** Before making the plan, the orchestrator probes every catalog-Optional, non-floor runner that implements `PersonaProber` and hands the absent ones to the router as `RouteInput.UnavailablePhases`; mandatory and floor phases are never probed, so their absence stays a loud dispatch failure, never a silent exclusion. `unavailablePhaseReason` treats a wrapped `ErrAgentDocMissing` as the one deterministic reason to exclude a phase — an unprobeable runner (no `PersonaProber`), a phase with no runner, or a probe failure that is not a known absence all leave the phase selectable, so dispatch surfaces the real cause instead of a plan-time guess. Pinned by `TestAdvisorPlanInput_ListsPhasesWithMissingPersona` and `TestWriteRoutingContext_UnavailablePhasesAreNamedNotOffered`.
- **`VerdictReason` does not replace the wire types.** `PhaseResponse.Verdict` and `FailedRecord.Verdict` keep their bare string for ledger, hash-chain and JSON stability; `VerdictReason` is derived from a phase's `Diagnostics` and carries the extra signal the orchestrator needs to react (classify → PROCEED/RETRY/BLOCK) and the advisor needs for recall. `ReasonFromDiagnostics` projects the Summary through `cyclestate.ErrorMessages`, the one rule every reason surface uses, so the ledger's verdict identity can never name a FAIL differently from the `FailedRecord`, the chokepoint log line or the seal. `Taxonomy.Consequence` is typed as `failureadapter.Classification` rather than a free string, so a mismatch with the failure-adapter's vocabulary is a compile error, not a runtime surprise; `Source`/`FailureMode` stay free strings because they are descriptive, not control-flow.
- **The source-write axis is declared twice on purpose: `writes_source` in the phase registry, and the `WorktreePhase` literal as the role gate's catalog-less floor.** The role gate (`internal/guards`) has only the `CycleState`, no catalog, so it relies on the fixed literal for the built-in source writers — defense in depth, identical to the config value. `TestRegistry_DeclaresWriteAxisForSourceWriters` iterates whatever the loaded registry contains (no phase name hardcoded) so the two can never silently drift.
- **`gitWorktree.Cleanup`'s `os.RemoveAll` never runs against a path this provisioner did not create.** `deleteCycleBranch` runs non-force `git branch -d` only, relying on git's own merged-check as its safety net rather than escalating to `-D`, and is gated on the `cycle-` leaf prefix so Cleanup never branch-deletes an unrelated path. Pinned by `TestCleanup_DeletesMergedCycleBranch` and `TestCleanup_UnmergedBranchSurvives_WarnsOnly`.
- **`resolveWorktreeBaseSHA` records a reused worktree's HEAD verbatim as `WorktreeBaseSHA`, unless HEAD is a salvage snapshot, in which case it walks back to the first non-snapshot ancestor.** Recording a snapshot's own SHA as the base would make `normalizeWorktreeToBase` soft-reset onto the very commit salvage exists to protect, reading the preserved diff as empty; the walk applies only to a snapshot HEAD, since an unconditional walk-back would re-base every ordinary lane instead. When HEAD is readable but its ancestry is not, the captured HEAD is still returned verbatim with a WARN rather than an error, because an empty base disables normalization entirely, the worse outcome — `TestVerdictCacheCollisionRegression`'s missing-base scenario depends on this fail-open path, since it stubs `rev-parse HEAD` and needs capture to survive a failed ancestry walk. A chain that is snapshots all the way down has no honest base and fails loudly instead of falling back to either the snapshot or an empty string. Pinned by `TestWorktreeReuseBase_SalvageSnapshotHEADResolvesToFirstNonSalvageAncestor`, `TestWorktreeReuseBase_SalvagedWorkIsPendingInTheReviewDiffAfterNormalize`, `TestWorktreeReuseBase_StackedSalvageSnapshotsResolveToTheCommitBeneathAll`, `TestWorktreeReuseBase_OrdinaryHEADIsRecordedVerbatim`, `TestWorktreeReuseBase_CommitMentioningSalvageInBodyIsNotTreatedAsASnapshot`, `TestWorktreeReuseBase_UnresolvableSnapshotAncestorFailsLoudly`, `TestWorktreeReuseBase_UnreadableAncestryRecordsHEADVerbatim`, `TestNewCycleRun_UnresolvableSnapshotBaseFailsProvisioning` and `TestWorktreeReuseBase_ProvisioningPathRecordsGuardedBaseInCycleState`.
- **`inPlaceWorktree` is the one predicate every worktree mutator checks before touching a worktree** (`normalizeBuildWorktree`, `recoverBuildLeak`, `worktreeContentSHA`, `snapshotPreservedWorktree`, `rebaseCycleBranchOntoMain`, the provisioner's `Cleanup`, the teardown message), so the operator's project root — including a symlink alias or a relative spelling of it — is never normalized, reverted, staged, committed, rebased or removed by a cycle. An empty `projectRoot` is treated as unknown, never as the root. Pinned by `TestWorktreeMutators_RefuseTheProjectRoot`.
- **Failure routing and breaker rules.**
  - A guard abort is pipeline machinery that failed. It is never a task defect, so its pre-class is `guard-class`.
  - A digest whose fingerprint asserts no defect identity is "unexplained". Distinct failures collapse into such buckets by construction, so the identical-fingerprint rule never counts them. The unexplained rule owns them.
  - The consecutive-failures rule is evaluated last. When two rules trip together, the more specific rule names the halt.
  - An acknowledged fingerprint is excluded from the identical-fingerprint count and from the consecutive streak. A resumed batch that counts it will halt again before the fix is verified.
  - Cycle numbers are batch-global. A fully failed wave of width 2 adds 2 to the streak. A missing digest is a PASS and splits a streak.
  - `ParseConsumptionFingerprint` needs the literal `fingerprint` token before the triplet, so a pipe-delimited path never matches. It fails closed.
  - The ack ledger `.evolve/resolved-fingerprints.json` is written by a tmp-and-rename, so no reader sees a partial ledger. A missing ledger is an empty set.
- **Audit round bookkeeping.**
  - The previous round's verdict artifacts are retired before the pre-phase cycle-state write on both dispatch surfaces. A crashed round's verdict is therefore retired on resume.
  - If archiving fails, the stale file is removed. A surviving verdict is replayed.
  - A counter rollback can leave an archive in place. The first retirement's evidence is never overwritten.
  - `AuditRepairAttempts` is a monotonic counter, not a flag for "repairing now". Audit re-reads its own artifacts, so re-injecting its own rejection is circular.
  - A bookkeeping regrade matches only at the start of a record, so a quoted occurrence inside another message cannot match. Producers never emit a `narrative=FAIL` conflict line. The matcher rejects one that is forged.
  - A FAIL with green artifacts and no `AuditFailReasons` is the signature of a forged verdict.
- **Audited tree.** The tree is the one on the auditor row that ship binds. A run without such a row gives an empty entry, not an error.
- **Boot quarantine.** Boot recovery re-pins the ship binary in the same pass. Quarantining it reopens the SHA mismatch that the re-pin heals. Git can report a collapsed untracked parent directory that `classifyDirtyPaths` cannot exclude, so the pathspec excludes the ship binary too.
- **Console lease.**
  - The lease lives in the git common dir, outside every worktree, so no lane phase can write or shadow it.
  - An absent, malformed, expiry-less or expired lease reads as an empty set, and an unresolved hub gives no path. The guard stays armed in every degraded shape.
  - `expires_at` is required. No expiry means no lease.
  - The lease is adopted once at cycle start, so a write in mid-cycle cannot waive its own cycle. The logged digest comes from the bytes that were adopted, not from a second read.
- **Build floor.**
  - The floor diffs against the cycle base. The builder commits its work, so a HEAD diff is empty at review time and the floor approves with nothing checked.
  - The protected-surface check turns rename detection off. A file moved out of the surface is judged by its old path too. A nil predicate fails open, and the ship tripwire stays armed.
  - Changed packages are tested after `reviewAndGuard` normalizes gofmt and derived projections, so the tested tree is the tree that Audit receives.
  - An added package that is gated by build tags is invisible to the default run. It runs under its own tags. An enforced package runs once, in the coverage pass of the apicover naming check.
  - That coverage pass needs a coverage profile. Without one, every named export reads as covered.
  - Only violations in files that the change touched fail hard. Older debt in a touched package only warns.
  - A failure to run is green, and downstream gates stay armed. Every fail-open branch warns.
  - A package output keeps its tail, because `go test` writes its FAIL lines, panics and stack traces last. Complete output carries no elision marker, so the marker stays trustworthy.
  - The documentation floor classifier drops test-only diffs, and the verdict stays WARN.
- **Build report checks.**
  - The removal check parses only the claim block. Prose is never parsed, because a matcher on the word "removed" blocks honest reports.
  - A claim with an absolute or escaping path is not adjudicated. A claim that is absent and untracked, or a Git failure, fails open.
  - A placeholder token is `UPPER_SNAKE_PLACEHOLDER`. No persona emits one, so this grammar is the only home of the rule.
  - The persona budget check fails only when a persona document was touched and the prompts package is red. It skips when `changedPackageFloorChecks` already runs the package.
- **Carry-forward screen.**
  - Supersession is checked first. A dry-run merge of a change that is already absorbed reads clean.
  - The check uses `git merge-tree --write-tree`, which is a real three-way merge that writes only to the object database. Exit code 1 is a conflict. The bare one-argument form reports clean on conflicts.
  - A candidate that is not landable is either superseded or a conflict. Calling a conflict "landed" drops overlapping work in silence, so supersession is screened again.
  - A conflict routes to the debugger and never short-circuits.
- **Composition ladder.**
  - A composed-tree gate decline falls back to a full re-audit at all three trip sites (rung 0, rung 2 and the identity carry-forward).
  - The rung-2 reviewer is trusted only when its resolution keeps the audited patch-id. A language-model merge is verified, never trusted. An unknown verdict value fails closed.
  - `compositionPatchID` and `CompositionVerdictInput` mirror the ledger adapter, which core cannot import.
  - `WithCompositionGateRunner` takes a function that returns statuses and output tails. The orchestrator field keeps its status-only map, and the option projects statuses and hands the full outcomes to the per-call sink.
  - The snapshot and the gate runner and the verdict writer must all be bound for the fast path. A partial binding is not wired.
- **Continuation (salvage and resume).**
  - Salvage snapshots use a fixed committer identity, so preservation never depends on the git identity of the host.
  - A snapshot stages everything that the run produced, with `add -A`. The staging discipline of a declared manifest applies to ship binding, not to salvage.
  - A clean worktree is idempotent and returns HEAD. A failed merge of the base is aborted and returned. Stale code never reaches Build. A cancelled context must not leave an unresolved merge in preserved work.
  - Only a snapshot that the screen classifies as Clean is stamped. AlreadyLanded has nothing to resume. Conflict belongs to the debugger path.
  - Findings come from `audit-fail-reason.json`, which carries the actual reasons. `failure-digest.json` is only an identity shell. Reasons render as lines, not as escaped JSON.
  - Findings are cut at the last line break inside the budget. A byte cut can land inside a rune or leave half a finding. An unreadable findings file warns loudly, so "no findings existed" differs from "the manifest points at the wrong path". The continuation still adopts, because the work matters more than the notes.
  - A declined continuation releases its registry binding. If the binding stays, it outlives its lineage, and the out-of-band check of the defect-ledger gate fails every later lane on that scope. Only the orchestrator releases. A binding for another ancestor cycle is untouched. A failed release leaves the binding, so the next lane declines again.
  - A lane whose scope came from the wave planner has no inbox item. Its manifest is also registered under each pinned todo id. A blank id is skipped, because `materializeLaneScope` splits an empty scope into one empty string. Every failure is a WARN, because salvage bookkeeping never fails cycle finalization.
  - Adoption runs right after triage, when this cycle's claims exist on disk. A failure before seeding keeps fresh provisioning. A failure after seeding stops the cycle and preserves the worktree.
- **Contract escalation.**
  - Escalation triggers on the gate's own consecutive-block count (`contractEscalateAtBlock`), not on a local ordinal. The breaker is one global file, so an aborted cycle leaves it hot.
  - Block identity is the set of violation codes in the reason. Two blocks are one defect when their code sets intersect. When a block yields no codes, identity falls back to the fingerprint-normalized reason of the blocker breaker.
  - The rule is "prior reason known and different suppresses". It is never "equal escalates", because a hot breaker can start a ladder at block 2 with nothing to compare. The zero value reports true.
  - The code pattern is narrow (no spaces), so bracketed prose in a message cannot fake a code.
  - `contractArtifactDetermined` is an allowlist and fails closed. A repair that deletes a stray copy leaves the watched artifact byte-identical, so an unchanged hash is not proof of inaction. It has no production caller yet. `TestContractArtifactDetermined_CodesMatchDeliverableVocabulary` keeps its codes equal to the deliverable vocabulary, because `internal/deliverable` imports core.
  - The salvage retry runs only when no other CLI family exists. It adds no dispatch and consumes no extra correction, so the breaker count is untouched. A remediation from the gate replaces the clause that forbids edits to other files, because a remediation exists for violations whose fix creates something.
  - A demotion is staged as an intent, never written to `.evolve/inbox`. A write in mid-flight races `inboxmover.Claim` and can revive a claimed item. `recurrence.ApplyBoundary` is the only sanctioned inbox writer. The ledger entry keeps `salvage_attempted` beside `escalated`. A ledger or staging failure never changes the outcome. The intent weight comes from policy, with a compiled default when the load fails.
  - A demotion is reported even when a later gate in the chain rejects the same deliverable.
  - The universal fallback CLI equals the llmroute default. `TestUniversalContractFallbackMatchesLLMRouteDefault` pins it.
- **Correction ladder (salvage rung).**
  - Salvage relocates only into the workspace or `<ProjectRoot>/.evolve`. The basename check bounds the file name and this bounds the directory, so a faulty verifier cannot make salvage write elsewhere.
  - `withinRoot` refuses an empty root. Without that rule, a missing `ProjectRoot` can admit every path in the tree as a destination.
  - The search order is worktree, workspace, then cwd. A phase that writes source most likely misplaced its report in the tree that it edits. A file is relocated first, by an atomic rename, and the destination is verified after.
  - A candidate must be a regular file (lstat, no symlink), non-empty, no larger than 1 MiB and fresh. An unknown phase or a reject that is not shaped like an artifact does nothing.
  - The evidence digest has kernel-verified facts only: names from `git status` and the original path of a stray. `gitStatusNames` keeps stdout untrimmed, because the first porcelain line starts with a status column.

### Cycle driver rationale (dispatch, correction, closeout, covering tests)

- The covering-tests corpus (`covering-tests.md`) removes the whole-repo Grep of test-amplification. The corpus must be on disk or announced. `ensureCoveringTests` closes the resume-past-build hole. `writeCoveringTests` runs only on a fresh `PhaseBuild` completion.
- Derivation of the corpus is fail-open. A corpus already on disk is a silent no-op. A truncated corpus warns on stderr, so a trimmed corpus is never taken for a complete one. `renderCoveringTests` is the single source of the omitted count.
- `sanitizeCorpusPath` replaces backticks and newlines. It does not escape them. The path set comes from the worktree diff, so it is attacker-influenced. The phase agent treats the file as AUTHORITATIVE. Replacement keeps the byte-cap arithmetic exact (one rune in, one rune out).
- `recordCrossArtifactInvariants` is advisory, best-effort and loud. A write failure warns and is ignored. It runs before the verdict-coherence floor. A finding can then be read beside the floor decision and never changes it.
- `finalizeOutcome` credits `SHIPPED_VIA_BUILD` only from the ship latch of the cycle (`CycleState.Shipped`). `latchShippedState` sets it after the deliverable review approves. Movement of main HEAD is never evidence, because a sibling fleet lane moves HEAD all the time. The latch lives in the checkpoint, so a pause and resume after ship cannot lose it.
- `teardownCycleWorktree` is the one disposal rule for fresh and resumed cycles. It prunes only spent trees. A preserved tree holds audited work that can be uncommitted. A cycle that did not complete normally also keeps its tree.
- `evolve loop --resume` and `evolve cycle reset` reclaim the tree by its path. A missed prune costs disk. A wrong prune destroys audited work. `gitWorktree.Cleanup` refuses the project root.
- `ClearCompletedCycleMarker` removes only a COMPLETED marker (`cycle_id <= lastCycleNumber`) that has no live lease owner. It never changes `state.json`, never archives and never writes a lesson. Those duties belong to `SealCycle` on abandon. `readLastCycleNumber` reads the raw field. A missing `state.json` reads 0, so an in-progress marker never counts as completed.
- Dispatch treats a missing built-in or mandatory runner as fatal, because it is a wiring bug. It skips a non-dispatchable optional user phase loudly. Every phase runs with cwd set to the cycle worktree. The cwd is not write permission. The role gate, the tree-diff guard and `worktreePhase` own the write axis. A re-dispatch clears the diagnosed-FAIL explanation of the prior try.
- The failure arms of dispatch run in a fixed order. All-families quota exhaustion (exit 85) comes first. It checkpoints a resumable pause and spends no more tries. Artifact backfill (exit 81 only) comes next.
- Then the optional-phase infra skip, which gives a WARN and never applies to a floor or mandatory phase. Then ship-error recovery, with bounded depth. An integrity breach falls through to the abort. The post-ship observer skip comes last. It accepts any error shape, but only after ship has landed.
- Every degrade stays in failure-learning and the ledger. An unparseable verdict is fatal to the cycle only for a floor or ship phase. Dispatch records the outcome before the failure-learning retro, so the timing record stays in order.
- `countDispatch` (`dispatch_attempt.go`) sets `PhaseRequest.Attempt` before each launch of a phase in the cycle. The launches are the first dispatch, an infra relaunch, a contract correction, a remediation round and a retried evaluate member. So two correction rounds of build write `attempt` 1, 2 and 3 in `llm-calls.ndjson` (ADR-0142 L0, [retry-ladder §6](../retry-ladder.md#6-the-try-record)). The members of a parallel evaluate batch are different phases that dispatch at the same time, so a mutex guards the count. A resumed run keeps one count for all its iterations and its review gate corrections, until a new process restarts it at 1. Pinned by `TestDispatchAttempt_AResumedCorrectionContinuesTheCount`, `TestDispatchAttempt_AResumedRunCountsItsCorrectionsAfterTheResumedDispatch`, `TestDispatchAttempt_EveryPhaseOfAResumedRunSharesOneCount`, `TestDispatchAttempt_ARemediationRoundIsTheNextDispatchOfTheGateAndTheBuilder`, `TestDispatchAttempt_AnEvaluateMembersInfraRelaunchIsItsNextAttempt`, `TestDispatchAttempt_TwoCorrectionRoundsAreAttemptsOneTwoAndThree`, `TestDispatchAttempt_AnInfraRelaunchIsTheNextAttempt` and `TestDispatchAttempt_ParallelEvaluateMembersCountWithoutARace`.
- A build that the review gate rejects after its corrections is recorded as a FAIL with the `BUILD_DELIVERABLE_REJECTED` diagnostic (`rejectedOutcome`, 2026-10-21). The message is the abort reason. Before, the entry kept the PASS of the builder and carried no code. Then the retro's PASS was the last entry, and the cycle classified as system-level, so the item was released without a charge. A rejection of another phase keeps its uncoded outcome. Pinned by `TestReviewWithCorrections_ABuildRejectedAfterItsCorrectionsRecordsACodedFail` and `TestReviewWithCorrections_ARejectedPhaseOtherThanTheBuildKeepsItsUncodedOutcome`.
- `reviewWithCorrections` has a third exit, the proven blocker ([blocker-exit.md](../blocker-exit.md)).
