Before each correction, `provenBlocker` gives the `BLOCKER` defects of the verdict and the floor failures to `BlockerProver`. A proof ends the cycle as FAIL with the reason `blocked-needs-console` and the `BLOCKER_ROUTE` diagnostic, and the item goes to the console.
- `reviewWithCorrections` has a fourth exit, the predicate reroute (`predicate_reroute.go`, the cycle-1761 shape). In cycle 1761 a predicate that TDD wrote in `go/acs/cycle<N>/` was unsatisfiable: its allow-list excluded the explanation document and the eval that the pipeline requires. Build may not edit that directory, so two Build corrections met the same failure and the cycle sealed FAIL.
  - The loop keeps the `./acs/cycle<N>` failures of this cycle (`acsPredicateFailures`) at each real Build re-dispatch. The salvage and live-fix rungs do not count, because they re-review the same result. After the re-dispatch, `predicateDefectRepeats` compares the new failures with the kept ones. An identical list routes the cycle to TDD, once for each cycle (`maxPredicateReroutes`).
  - `samePredicateFailures` compares the identity of each failure (`predicateFailureIdentity`). It drops the test durations, the `FAIL<tab><pkg><tab><time>` and `ok` summary lines and the `=== RUN` lines. It also drops the cut first line of a floor tail that starts with `…`. Two `go test` runs of one failing predicate thus compare as the same failure, and a changed assertion line does not (`TestSamePredicateFailures_RealGoTestOutputOfTwoRunsIsTheSameFailure`).
  - A remediation builder fix never reroutes (`TestMaybeRemediate_ARepeatedPredicateFailureOfABuilderFixNeverReroutesToTDD`). A resumed Build keeps the corrections of today: `reviewResumedDeliverable` has no reroute.
  - `routePredicateDefectToTDD` records the Build outcome with the reason, stores the failures in `CycleState.PredicateRepairBrief`, schedules TDD and returns `loopContinue`. `reviewAndGuard` still runs the post-review guards (the main-tree diff guard) on the rejected Build, and then the loop dispatches TDD next (`TestBuildFloor_TheRejectedBuildOfAReroutePassesThePostReviewGuards`). `seedAuditRepairContext` gives the brief to the TDD dispatch as `predicate_repair_brief`. Each TDD dispatch, also on resume, clears it (`consumeDispatchFlags`).
  - A predicate failure that changes between rounds stays at Build. The second reroute of a cycle falls back to the Build corrections of today.
  - Pinned by `TestBuildFloor_Cycle1761ARepeatedTDDPredicateFailureRoutesToTDDNotBuild`, `TestBuildFloor_APredicateFailureThatBuildMovesStillCorrectsAtBuild`, `TestPredicateDefectRepeats_OnlyAnIdenticalPredicateFailureOfTheMainBuildDispatchOncePerCycle` and `TestSeedAuditRepairContext_OnlyTDDGetsThePredicateRepairBrief`.
- `BlockerProver` (`blocker_proof.go`) is phase-agnostic. It takes a `BlockerCase`, and the caller gives the `Explainable` function. The build caller (`build_blocker_exit.go`) accepts only the cover-strict and ACS predicate failures. One refused claim or one unexplained failure keeps the rounds, and `ORCHESTRATOR_BLOCKER_REFUSED` records it.
- `HandoffReviewer` (`handoff_reviewer.go`) checks the handoff ledger of the pair that ends at the `tdd` and `build` boundaries ([phase-handoff-contract.md §5](../phase-handoff-contract.md#where-the-check-runs-h8)). The console injects the check function, and the reviewer adds a `check` record to `handoff-<pair>.json`. At stage `shadow` it always approves. At stage `enforce` it rejects with the unmet ask ids and the failed evidence, or with `ReviewResult.ProvenBlocker`. `provenBlocker` and the resume review route `ProvenBlocker` through the same exit (`recordProvenBlocker`). `HandoffReviewerWired` reports the reviewer in the chain.
- The floor notes (`floor_blocker_notes.go`) list the uncovered blocks of a cover-strict failure. A note marks a protected file and a dead `json.Marshal` error return. The prover reads these lines as evidence, so the note file is on the protected-surface manifest.
- `reviewWithCorrections` spends rung budget or ends on every iteration, so the ladder is finite. Salvage needs a breaker-neutral verifier. The `live_fix` rung is decided but dormant, because `NamedREPL` is false until the session-request plumbing lands. Its `continue` is safe, because the rung budget is already spent.
- CLI-family escalation fires on the second consecutive block. The block must have the same defect identity as the previous block (`contractBlocksShareIdentity`). A remediation rejection from evalgate uses the correction ordinal in the cycle, because evalgate keeps no breaker.
- Escalation and the structured re-prompt are disjoint. The ladder restores the temporary routing and directive when it ends. Corrections skip the bridge-timeout retry ladder. A non-canonical correction result aborts.
- The self-SHA re-pin after a successful build uses the provenance-gated primitive and fails open. The ship gate stays the backstop. The new-package graduation check aborts the build phase on purpose, unlike the WARN-only self-check. It runs after the pre-review normalize.
- Retro and debugger successors are decision-driven. `scheduledNext` carries them, and dynamic routing must not override it. The audit-FAIL disposition is decided at the audit chokepoint from the failure class that the audit declared.
- `maybeRemediate` never remediates a judgment or control phase (`remediationDenied`). A builder re-roll against an LLM verdict is a gamed verdict. The fix dispatch keeps write access, because a fenced builder loses its fix.
- It runs under its own routing profile and is recorded under its own label. The re-run gets a fresh timing window and the same reviewer. The directive names the artifact file from the phase-contract registry. It does not use the `<phase>-report.md` convention.
- `postScoutReplan` fires once per cycle, after the scout handoff is recorded. It cannot widen the run-set or bypass the spine. It re-plans only on a material divergence from a measured trigger. It caps the depth and escalates at the cap. A failed re-plan does not use a depth slot.
- It validates the RAW plan before the floor clamp. A clamped plan has no unknown phase left to record. It always clamps to the integrity floor, so a re-plan cannot weaken ship. The shadow stage records `phase-replan.json`. The advisory stage swaps the plan.
- `selectNext` never second-guesses an authoritative `scheduledNext`. A spine-gate fail-open is recorded with the anchor from the signals that the gate blocked on.
- `cycleRun` fields that change inside the loop are reference types or reached through `&cr.*`. The exit defers of `RunCycle` then see late values. The phase timings are composed once, at a single flush point. The abnormal path flushes before the dossier is built. The normal path builds the dossier first.
- `defaultMaxPhaseIterations` guards against a transition cycle. `recordChokepointEscape` records the escape through the outcome chokepoint, so the cycle classifies as explained.
- `recordFloorVerdictFailure` feeds failure-learning on the success path for a floor-phase FAIL. It persists to `state.json` at once. Several abort branches of the live loop and of resume return before the final write. An in-memory append is lost and the invisible-retry storm returns. It does not run retro, because the state machine already routes FAIL to retro. Both call sites share one implementation.
- `finalizeCycle` runs in a fixed order. First the verdict-coherence floor, which reports a system failure and self-heals a clean-exit late write. Then the lost-landing floor, which keys on the own ship artifacts of the cycle. Then the silent no-ship warning and the throughput record. Worktree preservation comes last. It must follow `finalizeOutcome`, because it reads the final verdict.
- A preserved worktree is snapshot-committed, and its continuation manifest is stamped, while the tree is live. The carryover order is memo merge, prescription merge, then triage-dropped retirement. It lives in `carryover.Closeout`. `TestFinalizeCycle_RetiresTriageDroppedCarryover` pins it.

## Core file group 6 rationale

The reasons that the comments of the routing-dispatch, state-machine, safety-invariant, ship-recovery, worktree, run-workspace, signal, task-contract and verdict files carried. The code and the tests state the what.

- **Routing and dispatch.**
  - A nil plan (static spine, no advisor) counts as ship-intended, so early exit is never taken without an explicit advisor decision.
  - A router-proposed phase string that is neither a built-in nor in the catalog resolves to the empty phase, and `enforceNext` declines it.
  - A user phase is legal only if it is optional and makes forward progress in `cfg.Order`. The router proposes only the next runnable optional. `SpineSatisfiedUpTo` independently guards the mandatory anchors, so an optional insertion can neither skip the spine nor reach ship illegitimately.
  - Minted phases are registered into three live-read structures: the runners map (dispatch), the catalog (recognition) and the routing config (order, triggers, enable). The runner-collision check gates all three splices, so a name already in the runners map is left wholly untouched and never half-registered. `Catalog.Merge` keeps the built-in on a name clash. The trust-kernel clamp runs inside the injected registrar, so a rejected config is a loud skip and never a registered dead phase. `ApplyUserRouting` extends `cfg.Order` in place, and the mid-cycle `RouteInput` copies inherit that order intentionally. A nil plan, registrar or mint list is a no-op.
  - `nextInOrder` assumes `cfg.Order` is the complete registry order (`applyRegistry` appends every registry phase), so a built-in successor is present whenever a registry is loaded.
  - The core and router vocabularies differ on purpose: core says `retro`, the registry says `retrospective`. `retro` is the serialized identity in `state.json` and the ledger, pinned by `cyclestate.TestPhaseConstants`. So the converter stays and the rename does not (ADR-0060, ADR-0058).
  - The registry key rule is owned by `phasecontract`. A second copy here is how an alias added on one side silently ungated a phase on the other (ADR-0100).
  - Recall for the advisor plan is best-effort: a knowledge-base read error never blocks planning. No knowledge base or no failure history yields the legacy no-recall result. A failure with no matching lesson is the novel-failure signal.
  - `resolvedShipFloor` takes the configured floor, else the router default `{tdd, build, audit}`. The router self-seals the non-removable evaluator either way, so a floor that reaches ship without audit is never returned.
  - A phase that declines its SELECT slot (`phasespec.CatalogOnDemand`) stays installed and dispatchable and is named in the on-demand index line. Declining costs discoverability of metadata, never of existence. Control phases (ship, retro, memo, debugger) are kernel-managed and never advisor-composed. Card order follows the registry order, which keeps the prompt cache-friendly.
  - `entriesFromRecords` is inlined here because exposing it from `failureadapter` is an import cycle. The backfill artifact filename comes from `phasecontract.ArtifactFilename`. The agent's own deliverable contract is projected from it.
- **Run identity and lease.**
  - `stampingLedger` is installed once at construction. The per-run id flows through an atomic value and never through a write to the orchestrator's ledger field.
  - An interface store is a two-word write, so an observer that reads it concurrently can race. An empty id stamps nothing.
  - The run id is a ULID, so lexicographic order follows time order. `crypto/rand` does not fail on supported platforms. A zeroed entropy tail still yields a valid time-ordered id and not a panic.
  - `RunIDFromWorkspace` is the single resolver for out-of-process ledger writers. It fails soft: an unresolvable id returns the empty string and the caller omits the field. The fail-closed half belongs to ship, which refuses to bind an unstamped entry. A missing `run.json` is legitimate (standalone dispatch outside a cycle); any other read error is a real fault.
  - The run-state mirror is symlinked into the cycle worktree, so guard hooks inside it read the run's own state. Under concurrent runs the global `cycle-state.json` holds whichever run wrote last.
  - Every writer that reads then changes the global file serializes on the `cycle-state.json.lock` sidecar (ADR-0049). The workspace path is a projection of `paths.RunWorkspace` (ADR-0103).
  - The lease heartbeat cadence is half the TTL, so a reader always sees a heartbeat younger than the TTL while the writer lives. `leaseRefreshInterval` returns `runlease.DefaultTTL / 2`. `cyclerun.go` and `resume.go` pass it to `startRunLease`.
  - The first write completes before `startRunLease` returns. Thus a gc pass that snapshots liveness after `RunCycle` began never sees the run unleased.
  - A failed write warns and falls back to run-state liveness. It does not abort the cycle.
  - The run directory is created here because `runlease.Write` needs it for its temp file. Stop is wrapped in `sync.Once`, so a second call cannot close a closed channel.
- **Safety invariants (ADR-0060, ADR-0058).**
  - The legality graph lives in config, so the floor's non-gameability cannot rest on a hardcoded graph.
  - The validator quantifies over graph roles (mandatory anchors, verdict branches) and never over phase names. An operator can rename any phase without weakening the floor.
  - Branch targets must be known legal successors: config selects among legal edges and never invents one. A config spine must join known phases by legal edges, because `Next` walks it without re-checking `CanTransition`. An unresolvable `legal_successors` entry is reported here because `legalGraphFrom` drops unknown names.
  - A mandatory phase's verdict gate may accept only shippable verdicts. The gate check applies only when the catalog describes a mandatory phase, so synthetic test catalogs are not falsely flagged. A stranded mandatory anchor is a silent floor hole.
  - Every start-to-ship path must cross a floor evaluator. The check deletes the whole evaluator set and verifies that ship becomes unreachable.
  - If `start` itself is an evaluator, the avoid-walk is empty. That is correct: `start` is on every path and dominates the sink.
  - Seeding `start` unconditionally falsely flags a direct start-to-sink edge.
  - The start node is found structurally (in-degree zero), so a renamed start phase is still found. Ties go to the lowest-sorted name for determinism.
- **Transition kernel.**
  - The linear spine is `start`, optional `intent`, `scout`, optional `triage`, `tdd`, `build`, `audit`. Audit PASS or WARN goes to `ship` and the other verdicts go to `retro`. `retro` goes to `tdd` (retry), `ship` (recovered) or `end` (block), as decided by the orchestrator through the failure adapter. `ship` goes to `end`.
  - The canonical spine literal is not `cfg.Order`. `cfg.Order` interleaves optional insertions that the static spine skips.
  - `audit` is on the spine so `build` to `audit` resolves. The spine walk never takes its own successor, because `Next` intercepts it in the explicit switch.
  - Config drives transitions where it declares them. The literal tables are the byte-identical fallback: no catalog, no `on_pass`/`on_fail`, an empty spine or an empty legality graph all degrade to the literals.
  - `legalGraphFrom` spans the sentinels (`start`, `end`, `debugger`) that the registry phase list does not.
  - `CanTransition` is structural legality; `CanTerminateEarly` is the semantic permission for the guarded scout and triage edges to `end`.
  - `effectiveOrder` here falls back to `cfg.Mandatory`, unlike `router.effectiveOrder`, whose fallback is the router's canonical order.
  - A gate with a config entry but no Go digest reader fails closed, so it forces the reader to be implemented. An anchor with no digest slot counts as present.
- **Ship recovery.**
  - Contention-class ship codes (every `AUDIT_BINDING_*` code and `GIT_FLEET_REBASE_NEEDED`) mean a sibling lane moved main between audit and ship, not a defect in this lane. Their budget is `max(maxRecoveryDepth, fleetWidth+1)`: each sibling can bump HEAD once, plus one clean pass. Every other code keeps the constant. A non-positive width means solo.
  - The fleet width comes from the cycle request's env overlay and deliberately not from `os.Getenv`. `CycleRequest.Env` is the per-lane contract, so siblings cannot leak width into each other.
  - The backoff is fully jittered over a linearly growing window, capped well under a lane's phase budget. A fixed backoff re-collides siblings that hit the same HEAD-moved error in lockstep.
  - Nanosecond-granularity draws make identical durations astronomically unlikely.
  - After the debugger resolves a fleet-rebase conflict, the worktree still sits on the base the ship diverged from. The debugger's target then only rediscovers the divergence one phase later.
  - Inside the budget and after the backoff, the resolved tree is carried as a commit, rebased onto main and pended again.
  - Then it is routed like any rebased change: carry, rebind, or a Build that re-authors.
  - An upstream re-run that the debugger asked for still happens on the rebased tree.
  - A tests-first request yields to the route. The explanation contract refreshes after TDD against a binding that the route has just moved.
  - A surviving conflict, an unfinishable rebase or a spent budget ends the cycle, on stderr and in the Signal Center.
  - The re-entry has no checkpoint of its own. An interrupted re-entry leaves a carrier commit that keeps the change intact, and the resume heal finishes it.
  - The carry commits tracked state only (a resolution edits tracked paths and ship stages by manifest) on the fork point. The branch's earlier commits stay reachable from the reflog only, and every downstream check recomputes the fork point and tree instead of naming them.
  - Pending the rebased change is a soft reset of the carrier onto the commit it sits on: the shape Audit binds and ship commits. After a clean replay that commit is the new base, after an aborted one the audited base.
  - `gitFn` is the humble-object seam for `rebaseWithDerivedRegen`: production passes `gitCapture`, tests inject a fake. The replay loop is bounded by `maxRebaseContinueSteps` so a pathological rebase cannot spin forever.
  - `Code*` and `Stage*` constants re-export the `shiperr` vocabulary; the ship stages and the failure identities are grouped by stage.
- **Signals and ledger.**
  - A nil Signal Center is the null object for tests and the two pinned secondary roots; the production composition root always passes one. The listener is O(1) and never emits or decides.
  - `emitCycleClose` ends the stream with `system.failure` first when the closeout attached one, then `cycle.sealed` with the final verdict. The caller passes its own name so the callee never asserts the caller's identity. A quota pause is a resource event: resumable, never terminal evidence.
  - `gateCorrection` is a parameter object because adjacent bools at a call site can swap silently. The correction ordinal has one home, the `correction` field (ADR-0101).
  - The throughput recorder may mutate state in place because the orchestrator writes state immediately after. A nil recorder keeps the seam inert, and a replayed closeout must not observe the same cycle twice.
- **Verdict and failure floor.**
  - `VerdictReason.Taxonomy` is the source, failure-mode, consequence triple, parallel to `ShipError.{Stage,Code,Class}` so the codebase converges on one error vocabulary. Summaries are clamped by runes and never by bytes, so a non-ASCII message cannot be cut into invalid UTF-8 in the ledger.
  - The floor-fail reason file is forensic only: it is deliberately not read by `detectVerdictIncoherence`, so the file cannot forge or suppress a floor decision. The authoritative carrier is orchestrator memory. A reason is reset at every dispatch of the phase so a re-dispatch never inherits a superseded attempt's explanation.
  - `detectVerdictIncoherence` is the ADR-0072 Go floor. It reads the cycle's own on-disk phase artifacts.
  - It fires regardless of orchestrator judgment or `strict_audit` when a recorded FAIL or WARN sits beside green evidence.
  - The resumable quota-pause path enforces the infra-systemic category, not this function.
- **Task contract.**
  - The contract preamble lives in one place so it cannot drift between the tdd, build and audit prompts, which render only the heading. Both dispatch surfaces call `seedTaskContract` with the same persisted inputs, so crash-resume composes the same block.
  - The bound task ids are the lane pin when present, else the triage decision's `top_n`, minus the decision's deferrals. The markdown `## top_n` of `triage-report.md` is never the source: it is prose in triage's working-id namespace, where decomposition sub-ids are the documented norm.
  - A pair from `fleet_scope_paths` wins over the scope-path resolver. A malformed pair places nothing, and a member that neither source can place renders as an unresolved line and is never dropped. An unreadable record is a loud line, and sanitization is disclosed.
  - The predicate inventory is a `go test -list` run, so it is deterministic. A missing package, a compile failure or an empty package is reported in the note and never hidden. On a failed listing only the tail of the compiler output is carried, because the verdict lines are there.
  - Recall memory reuses the durable corpus on both dispatch paths and deliberately persists no second mutable summary. Provenance stays visible even when a lesson is shortened.
  - `seedDeliverableRoot` serves kind-declaring phases whose prompts carry no Task Contract block. Document cycles are judged by the authoritative kind from triage's report header.
- **Build budget.**
  - Size scaling is an upgrade on evidence and never a guess. A digest failure, an unknown size or a triage that has not completed yet gives 1.0.
  - It scales the Build correction ladder only. Audit and adversarial phases keep theirs.
  - The result rounds half-up, is clamped to the policy ceiling and never shrinks the ladder.
- **Stale marker auto-seal.** Without a lease naming the owner the owner cannot be proven dead, so the unfinished-cycle guard decides and the cycle is not torn down. Liveness is pid-based, not heartbeat-age based.
- **Workspace guard.** `lane-scope.json` is provisioned by the fleet supervisor before the cycle runs: pre-phase by design, not pollution. A genuinely polluted directory is archived whole, pin included, and the env-snapshot fallback re-materializes the pin for fleet lanes.
- **Worktree provisioning.**
  - Dirty paths of a reused worktree (tracked-modified and untracked, file-granular with `porcelain -uall`) are quarantined under their relative layout. Then `git reset --hard HEAD` runs.
  - That destroys nothing, because the dirty content is preserved first. Deleted-tracked paths and the old side of a rename have nothing on disk to preserve.
  - A name collision with an earlier attempt's quarantine gets a numeric suffix, so earlier salvage is never overwritten.
  - The alternative of cutting a fresh worktree instead is deliberately not implemented. Quarantine plus reset satisfies every acceptance criterion.
  - A knob that chooses between two equivalent outcomes is flag sprawl. The upgrade path is a `worktree.dirty` block in `policy.json`, if a second behavior is ever needed.
  - The salvage-snapshot subject is single-sourced and shared by the producer (`continuation_stamp.go`) and the base guard. A subject that drifted on one side silently disarms the other.
  - The ancestry walk is bounded. Beyond the bound the guard fails loudly and does not guess.
  - One git call classifies the walk with `%H<US>%s` lines. The unit separator cannot appear in a subject, so the split is unambiguous.
  - A remoteless repo bases on `HEAD`. A detached or feature checkout bases on the remote tip. A current or behind main is fast-forwarded at the boundary.
  - An absent `refs/remotes/origin/HEAD` falls back to `main`. A wrong guess fails loudly on the unknown ref at `worktree add`. It never silently bases on a stale local tip.
  - `CreateFrom` treats an existing directory at the cycle number as a stale collision: it is recreated and never reused. The add retry sleeps through a seam so tests count sleeps instead of paying them.
  - `WorktreePhase` (the write axis the role-gate, tree-diff guard and build-commit normalize key off) and `LeakRecoverablePhase` (an active cycle worktree exists) are distinct axes. The `Orchestrator` method forms consult the injected catalog, so a user phase counts as writing source iff its spec sets `writes_source`. The dispatch read-only fence is the complement of the same predicate, so the gate axis and the fence cannot disagree about who may write.
  - `WorktreeProvisioner.Create` is idempotent (an existing worktree for the cycle is reused) and `Cleanup` is best-effort: a missing worktree and an empty path are no-ops.
- **Pinned contracts that the tests carry.**
  - The transition oracle freezes the legality graph as a trust anchor that is independent of config (ADR-0058). It must never drift.
  - On an in-place root, the audit binding degrades to an empty worktree tree SHA, and ship then falls back to the auditor's value. This is a contract with ship.
  - A cycle never mutates its project root, so worktree fixtures hand the orchestrator a detached worktree and not the repository itself. This is a fixture convention. No single test pins it. `TestRecordAuditBinding_InPlaceRootDegradesToNoTreeSHA` pins the related in-place case.
  - An option that only integration-tagged tests execute needs an untagged test that names and runs it. Otherwise the apicover gate scores it false-green. This is a convention. No test pins it.
- **Spine replay harness.** `TestSpineReplay_LocalHistory` is an operator harness that is skipped in CI. It replays a project's real `.evolve/runs` history and checks if any historical cycle trips the spine floor at enforce. Run it before widening the floor (new anchors, new gates): `EVOLVE_SPINE_REPLAY_DIR=<project-root> go test -v -run TestSpineReplay_LocalHistory ./internal/core/`.

## Findings

- **cycle 1941, wave 126 (first seen on cycle 1628): a resumed ship met a moved main, and a passed audit ended FAIL.** Inbox item `resume-does-not-refresh-a-stale-worktree-base`. Spec: [retry-hooks-and-spine-telemetry](../retry-hooks-and-spine-telemetry.md) section 1.
  - Fact: the checkpoint of cycle 1941 resumed at `ship` with `worktree_base_sha` `df4ae9c1e`. Cycle 1942 had landed first. The ship returned `GIT_FF_MERGE_DIVERGED/precondition`, and the cycle sealed FAIL (`runs/cycle-1941/signals.ndjson`, seq 6, 2026-10-18). The console moved the audited commit onto main by hand (PR #961).
  - Cause 1: the resume loop had no ship recovery. `resumeDispatchRetryOpts` clears `shipRecovery` (`retry_opts.go:145`), and `retryPhaseRunner` never calls that hook. The fresh loop rebases and re-verifies this error. A resumed cycle ended on it.
  - Cause 2: the ship chooses its error code from the process environment. The landing returns the transient `GIT_FLEET_REBASE_NEEDED` only when `EVOLVE_FLEET=1` (`landing_intent.go:166`, `landing/integrate.go:48`). `evolve loop --resume` runs outside the fleet. It got the precondition code, which the router sends to the debugger.
  - Why no guard caught it: every checkpoint writer stores an empty `gitHead` (`checkpoint.go:159`, `:168`, `:183`). `LoadResumeState` skips the HEAD check for an empty value (`resume.go:185`).
  - Decision: the resume loop hands a ship error to the recovery ladder of the fresh loop. It then reroutes its cursor to the phase that the ladder picks (`recoverResumedShip`, `resume_ship_recovery.go:11`, called at `resume_execution.go:166`). This ladder already rebases a fleet lane at ship.
  - Decision: the landing code `GIT_FF_MERGE_DIVERGED` becomes `GIT_FLEET_REBASE_NEEDED` when the checkpoint base is strictly behind `main` (`staleBaseAsRebase`). The base must be an ancestor of `main`, and `main` must not equal it. A divergence at the tip of `main` keeps its code. A collider refusal keeps its code. A base that `main` does not hold keeps its code. A divergence from a branch other than `main` keeps its code.
  - Decision: the original code stays in the debug map as `original_code` (`shiperr.OriginalCodeKey`). The key is on the signal whitelist, so the `ship.error` line keeps the code that the landing returned.
  - Audit binding: after the rebase, the ladder routes to Ship only with a proof that the tree is the audited tree. The proof is the identity rebind or a composition verdict. Otherwise it routes to Audit or Build. Ship also refuses a tree that differs from the audited tree (`AUDIT_BINDING_TREE_MISMATCH`). No path ships a changed tree on the old audit.
  - Rejected, a refresh at resume time: a checkpoint before the ship holds pending files. The unwind, rebase and pend steps exist only for the shape that a failed ship leaves. A peer can land between the refresh and the ship, so the ship must recover in any case.
  - Rejected, a read of a resume key in the landing: no code read the key, and X18e deleted it. A reader adds a protocol key, and the ship must then know about resume. The core already holds the base and can test the ancestry.
  - Rejected, `GIT_FLEET_REBASE_NEEDED` for every non-fleet divergence: it changes the fresh non-fleet cycle, which this item does not cover.
  - Limits: the budget is `shipRecoveryBudget` for a contention code. It is the larger of `maxRecoveryDepth` (2) and the fleet width plus 1. `evolve loop --resume` does not run in the fleet, so its width is 1 and the budget is 2 for each resumed run.
  - Limits: a rebase conflict routes to the debugger. The resume loop has no debugger re-entry, so the cycle ends with the ship error and the worktree stays. A checkpoint from before Build still builds on the old base. It pays one re-audit after the rebase.
  - Decision (2026-10-20, the gap that the inbox triage found): a resumed cycle that ends on a stale base ends with the converted error. `recoverResumedShip` returns the error that `staleBaseAsRebase` made, and the resume loop records and returns it (`resume_execution.go:166`).
    - Before, the loop returned the raw error. A spent budget or a rebase conflict on a base behind `main` then aborted with `GIT_FF_MERGE_DIVERGED` in `abort_reason`.
    - The abort reason now names `GIT_FLEET_REBASE_NEEDED`, and `original_code` keeps `GIT_FF_MERGE_DIVERGED`. The cycle still ends FAIL. A divergence that is not a stale base keeps its raw code.
  - Pinned by `TestRunCycleFromPhase_AShipRefusedOnAStaleBaseRebasesAndReAuditsBeforeShipping`, `TestRunCycleFromPhase_AnExplanationCycleOnAStaleBaseRebindsAndReAuditsOnTheNewBase`, `TestRunCycleFromPhase_ASpentBudgetOnAStaleBaseAbortsAsARebaseNeedNotADivergence`, `TestRunCycleFromPhase_ARebaseConflictEndsTheCycleInsteadOfDispatchingTheDebugger`, `TestRunCycleFromPhase_ADivergenceThatIsNotAStaleBaseEndsTheCycle`, `TestRunCycleFromPhase_ABaseEqualToTheTipOfMainKeepsTheShipError`, `TestRunCycleFromPhase_AColliderRefusalIsNotRebased`, `TestRunCycleFromPhase_ARebaseThatProvesTheAuditedChangeShipsWithoutAReAudit`, `TestRunCycleFromPhase_TheShipErrorSignalNamesTheCodeTheLandingReturned`, `TestBaseBehindMain_OnlyAPinnedAncestorOfMainCounts` and `TestBaseBehindMain_WithNoWorktreeNeverRunsGit`. The landing keeps `cycle_branch` in its debug map (`TestCheckFastForward_DivergedNonFleet_PreconditionFFMergeDiverged`), and `staleBaseAsRebase` tells a collider refusal from a landing divergence by that key.
- **The resume identity check restated the activation belt** (inbox item `explanation-identity-belief-remaining-copies`, 2026-09-01). `requireResumeExplanationIdentity` compared the checkpoint to the host marker with its own table and its own leniency (`host.Worktree != ""`). The check now builds the typed binding from the cycle state, sets the authoritative cycle and workspace, and calls `explanationdocs.CrossCheckActivation`. One check stays here, because the belt cannot make it: with an active host, the cycle-state cycle must equal the authoritative cycle. Pinned by `TestRequireResumeExplanationIdentity_RejectsClearedSealedFields`, `TestRequireResumeExplanationIdentity_AcceptsAProvisionedWorktreeBeforeTheBuildSeal` and `TestRequireResumeExplanationIdentity_RejectsAZeroVersionAgainstAnActiveHost`.
  - The old check that the cycle-state workspace equals the host workspace is not restated. The caller guarantees it. `authoritativeResumeIdentity` takes `cs.WorkspacePath` as the declared workspace and returns it. In the fleet branch it returns the checkpoint directory, and only after it has proven that the two are equal (`resume_bootstrap.go`).
  - The leniency is not a fossil. `activateBuildExplanationContract` writes the marker without worktree and base at cycle start (`orchestrator.go`). `sealBuildExplanationContext` fills them only on the Build transition. A resume from a pre-Build checkpoint thus meets an unsealed marker, and the belt owns that rule.
  - Follow-up: `projectBuildExplanation` is a fourth copy of the host-marker comparison, outside the three that the item named. It reads `ActivationForCycle` and compares the run id, the contract version, the worktree and the base with its own table. This change does not route it, so that the diff stays within the item. A later item routes it through the belt and keeps its `NotYetBuilt` mapping.
- **cycles 1801, 1818, 1843: the identity proof never ran for a continuation lane** (research F2). The unwind declined for an item with `released_continuations`, the rebase replayed ship's commit, and `rebindPendingChange` returned with no proof. The log then said "not proven identical", and each cycle paid a Build and an Audit (102.8 min for the three). Pinned by `TestRecoverFromShipError_AContinuationLaneRunsTheIdentityProof`, `TestRecoverFromShipError_AContinuationLaneKeepsItsReleasedContinuation`, `TestRecoverFromShipError_AContinuationLaneWritesNoCarryShipCannotReProve`, `TestRecoverFromShipError_AContinuationLaneWhoseRebaseFailsKeepsShipsCommit` and `TestRouteRebasedExplanation_ACommittedChangeReportsTheProofSkipped`.
- **cycle 1822, wave 73: a stale citation killed a cycle whose code may have been correct** ([incident](../../incidents/2026-10-07-cycle-1822-explanation-refresh-abort.md)). The Builder's Changed Areas cited `go/internal/fleet/quota_test.go`. After an audit FAIL, the tdd repair round left that path out of the diff. Test files are non-material, so the refresh's material checks passed and the content check failed: "cited path … is not in the Build diff". `applyPostReviewGuards` aborted on any refresh error (infra-error class) even though the same block already routed a material change back to Build. The abort returned raw, not as `ErrCycleLevelFailure`, so `cycleRunErrorExit` skipped the inbox failure walk and the claim stranded in `processing/cycle-1822/`. The next boundary's `sync-main` then refused to run until `evolve inbox-mover recover-orphans`. An earlier cycle failed the same way citing `go/internal/phaseio/digest_test.go`. Both halves are now fixed: content failures take the re-author rung, and every abnormal exit walks the failure closeout at all three roots. The architecture review of the first fix found the rung still charged tdd's round (costing the audit its second repair) and still aborted, batch-fatally, once the budget was spent, which is the exact 1822 shape; a writer whose every successor leads to Build is now free and never aborts on content, and a spent budget ends only the cycle.
- Cycle 1708 (2026-09-26) met every CLI family walled on its build correction re-dispatch; the correction loop wrapped the runner's exit 85 as "correction 1 dispatch failed", recorded a FAIL outcome, wrote a failure digest and a lesson, dispatched a retrospective into the same wall and sealed the cycle FAIL, while cycle 1709 met the same wall on a first dispatch and was deferred. One seam now serves all four paths.

- Cycle 1708 (2026-09-26) met every CLI family walled on its build correction re-dispatch; the correction loop wrapped the runner's exit 85 as "correction 1 dispatch failed", recorded a FAIL outcome, wrote a failure digest and a lesson, dispatched a retrospective into the same wall and sealed the cycle FAIL, while cycle 1709 met the same wall on a first dispatch and was deferred. One seam now serves all four paths.

- **cycles 1787, 1791, 1792, 1798: a green package killed by the floor's deadline.** The floor's untagged self-check ran `go test -count=1 -timeout 120s <pkg>`, and `./cmd/evolve` takes 105–127 s when green. Cycle 1798 built correctly (local `go test ./cmd/evolve` ok at 105/109/115 s, ACS 178/178 PASS), and the floor still rejected it three times, each time with a timeout dump from an unrelated test (`runlease_hook.go:54`, `cmd_setup_latest_test.go:171`, `cmd_loop_unit13_seam_test.go:379`). The builder diagnosed the timeout in corrections 2 and 3 and edited nothing, which the floor could not accept, so the cycle failed. The floor's go-test budgets had been three scattered literals (120 s, 300 s, and the tagged path's 20 m since wave 43). The class fix is one budget, `addedtests.PackageTimeout`, a source scan that refuses any other `-timeout` value in pipeline code, and the floor's runner file and the budget on the protected surface.
- **cycles 1763, 1764, 1788: a green probe, a red floor** ([ADR-0117](../adr/0117-pre-handoff-probes-run-the-build-floor.md)). The floor was composed in three places (the mandatory explanation reviewer here, `productionBuildFloorChecks` and the document solution chain at the composition root), and each pre-handoff probe ran a subset with a subset of the inputs. `evolve selfcheck build` passed only a worktree, so it diffed against `HEAD`, skipped the added tag-gated packages (1764) and never ran the explanation check (1763). `evolve phase verify build` ran none of the floor (1788: a correction's one-Write rewrite dropped `## Explanation Documentation`, the probe printed OK, the floor rejected it on the last correction). The class fix is the named list with two halves, the one input projection, and the composition-root test that the cycle and the probes iterate the same list.
- **Retro on the retry path** ([ADR-0093](../adr/0093-retry-envelope-and-terminal-retro.md)). An audit FAIL always routed to retro, which analysed, classified and gated the retry in one step, so the retry depended on retro's prose and ADR-0092's repair was reachable on only 3 of 16 failures. Retro costs 20–47 minutes per attempt. The decision moved to the audit chokepoint, keyed on the audit's own declared class; cycles 1572/1574/1576/1577 all declare `code-audit-fail`. The adversarial review of the first cut found the global graph widening (hence `decisionOnlyEdge`), a prose-driven halt, and an inert resume surface.
- **Source-grep guards pass on dead code.** An earlier guard checked for `consumeAuditRepairGrant(` in each dispatch file and stayed green while the resume copy was dead and `resume.go` had no audit-FAIL branch. Another checked only that the repair seeder was called, and passed while `resume.go` passed the previous phase (`current`) instead of the dispatched one. Guards now drive the path (`TestResumePath_ReachesTheAuditFailDisposition`, `TestOrchestrator_AuditFailRetriesTheDevCycle`) or pin the argument. See [2026-09-03-repair-rounds-blind-and-flat](../../incidents/2026-09-03-repair-rounds-blind-and-flat.md).
- **cycle-1603** ([incident](../../incidents/2026-09-02-repair-round-stale-acs-verdict.md)). Round 1's agent-amended `ship_eligible=false` verdict survived into round 2 and forced every repaired PASS back to FAIL, so the repair loop could never succeed. That led to round-scoped retirement at the pre-dispatch seam. The first cut hung retirement on the repair grant; review blocked it as the wrong seam.
- **cycles 1679 and 1684** ([verification-wave findings](../../research/verification-wave-findings-2026-09-14.md) F16/F19, [incident addendum](../../incidents/2026-09-15-shipped-item-re-pinned-and-sealed-lease-blocks-refresh.md)). A recovery rebuild after a WARN audit, and a retro-routed retry after an out-of-vocabulary class, both rebuilt with none of the audit's findings. Both now carry the standing findings. The declined repair also left no line anywhere, which made the decision a coded signal.
- **Blocker breaker origins.** The operator directive of 2026-07-22 says a pipeline blocker is fixed directly, never passed to following cycles. Batch 5 burned six cycles on one recurring class with every signal on disk, and the 862–899 storm burned 37 on byte-identical defect strings. Batch 6 had three different failures share one empty fingerprint, which named the `unexplained-failures` rule. Batch 14 had three distinct auditor findings share one boilerplate fingerprint, which led to self-marked `Unexplained` digests ([fingerprint identity](../../chronicle/2026-07-fingerprint-identity.md)). The 2026-08-09 batch ran 10 failed cycles with 0 ships before the identity-keyed rule tripped, which added `consecutive-failures` ([incident](../../incidents/2026-08-09-zero-ship-batch.md)).
- **cycles 1329, 1332, 1334.** A diagnosed and consumed fingerprint re-tripped Rule B on every relaunch, because the breaker re-scans disk with no memory. That produced the ack ledger (ADR-0072 extension) and then automatic acks on inbox consumption, so an operator no longer retypes the fingerprint by hand.
- **Gate-forced FAILs had no class** ([incident](../../incidents/2026-10-07-cycle-1828-gate-forced-fail-earned-no-repair-and-halted.md)). Cycle 1828's auditor passed it (0.88, ACS 170/0), the skills-drift gate forced FAIL, and the envelope declined "audit declared no failure class" because only the auditor's report could declare one; the retro's prose `infra-systemic` claim then halted the loop. The gate RED was itself version skew (the in-process gate renders with the base binary's generator, and 1828 changed it), which the derived class cannot repair; that gate fix is carryover `skills-drift-gate-grades-with-worktree-generator`.
- **Bookkeeping-only FAILs** ([ADR-0086](../adr/0086-bookkeeping-regrade-microcycle.md)). In cycles 1390–1429, six cycles died to continuation re-drives (about 2M tokens each, 0/11 pass rate) only to author one JSON artifact.
- **cycles 506, 507, 514 (boot recovery).** Cycle 506 built the recovery functions but never wired them (audit F1, critical). Cycle 507 re-established the function contract in `boot_preflight_test.go` and the wiring in `cmd/evolve`. The 508–513 cascade showed that quarantining the rebuilt ship binary reopens the SHA mismatch, which led to the exclusion.
- **cycle-1008** ([lessons](../../research/lessons-and-resolutions-2026-07.md) F4). The builder recorded `./cmd/evolve` failing in `build-selfcheck.json` and handed off anyway, burning four downstream phases before the ACS toolchain gate refused ship. The advisory selfcheck became a rejecting floor.
- **Zero-package blind spots.** Cycle-660 was a build whose only claim was a deletion, and cycle-1101 a lane whose only change was a persona doc past its line budget. Both derive zero Go packages, so they hit the engine's early return, and the persona breach reached main's CI twice on 2026-07-23. Both checks now run outside that engine.
- **The apicover parity class.** Five live instances, including three main reds, a console PR red and cycle-1022's invisible audit override, came from enforced packages with unnamed exports. The naming floor moved to handoff, and cycle-1048 scoped hard failures to the files a change touched.
- **cycle-1145** gate-block. Cycles 1141/1144/1145 enrolled `./acs/cycle<N>` packages in `.apicover-enforce` under an invented "completeness invariant", routing them into the enforced coverage run, where the setup failure read as a test failure and every later lane rejected its own handoff. See the rationale block in `go/.apicover-enforce`.
- **cycle-1268.** The floor kept the head of a failing package's output: 400 bytes of a repeated `Deps.TokenResolver is nil` WARN and nothing about what failed. Keeping the tail costs the same bytes and carries the verdict lines.
- **cycle 1679 (added tag-gated package)** ([incident](../../incidents/2026-09-14-ship-gate-found-what-the-audit-could-not.md)). An added `//go:build acs` package was invisible to the default-context run and first ran red at ship, after the builder had handed the tree over.
- **cycle 1689 (F37)** ([incident](../../incidents/2026-09-26-lane-edited-the-control-plane-through-a-shell-tool.md)). The builder rewrote the protected `core/cyclerun.go` through a shell tool the role guard never sees, and the violation surfaced only at ship, after the audit. The architecture review (M1) added `--no-renames`.
- **The advisor menu was an inventory** ([runtime-reference.md](../../operations/runtime-reference.md), 2026-08-23). 65 cards competed for 12 enriched slots, 53 rendered degraded, and 47 had never been selected in 120 cycles. The fix hides a phase from the menu, never deletes it. Wiring the change took six layers, and a mutation survived at each layer until a test pinned it.
- **cycle-1551** ([incident](../../incidents/2026-08-24-personaless-menu-phase-lane-kill.md)). The advisor inserted an optional menu phase with no persona anywhere. The load failure killed the lane (rc=4) and the ADR-0072 halt stopped the batch. Later, cycles 1619/1620 spent 344 s and 311 s in a retrospective agent for the same deterministically known absence, which removed the agent dispatch for it.
- **cycle-660 and cycle-1591 (removal claims).** A build report asserted that orphaned RED scaffolds were "already removed by a concurrent actor" while the files sat in the worktree, and the prose-only claim passed review (660), which made removal claims machine-checked. Later an inbox record was "retired" more than once by a plain delete or a move into the ignored `processed/` directory with no `git rm`; the path stayed in the index, the next fresh checkout restored it, and it reopened as a live item that burned an empty lane (1591), which added the index check.
- **cycle 1679 (placeholder token)** ([verification-wave findings](../../research/verification-wave-findings-2026-09-14.md) F14). Round 5's build report carried `FULLSUITE_PLACEHOLDER` under a heading promising every number was executed, and the audit spent a round naming it.
- **Build-leak recovery** ([multicli validation run](../../operations/multicli-validation-run-2026-05-31.md)). Cycle-160's agy builder wrote `go/internal/phases/backfill/` into the main tree, which created recovery; cycle-162's leaked edit to a tracked source file added relocation of tracked edits. Relocating a rebuilt `go/evolve` would commit binary drift (cycle-153). A minimal fixture without `.evolve/` ignored showed recovery relocating `.evolve/ledger.tip` and choking on a nested worktree directory, which aborted the cycle; cycle-176 (issue #11) showed guard hooks writing nested `<subdir>/.evolve/guards.log`, whose relocation failed the gitignored `git add` and aborted the batch. The blanket `.evolve/` skip then made deliverables unrecoverable: cycle-262's tracked config edit and cycle-268's tdd, which died writing its own eval, led to relocating deliverable subpaths.
- **Cross-lane relocation** ([incident](../../incidents/2026-10-06-cycle-1811-cross-lane-eval-relocation.md)). In wave 68, lane 1812's scout copied its eval into the shared main tree; lane 1811's TDD recovery, comparing against a baseline taken at cycle start, moved it into 1811's worktree, the audit failed 1811 on the out-of-scope eval, and the repair deadlocked because the builder may not delete `.evolve/evals/*`. The same move had happened before: batch 2026-08-16a shipped a sibling's eval in the wrong lane's commit, and batch 2026-08-15b moved a sibling's minted `phase.json`. Three homes for one rule (the persona said the workspace, `isScoutEvalMaterialization` said the main tree, Gate A read both) made the scout's extra copy look sanctioned. The fix keyed main-tree paths to owners, made the workspace the eval's one home, and captured the recovery baseline per phase. Across runtime cycles with a lane pin, 49 of 138 workspace eval slugs differ from the pin's ids, so ownership reads the materialized eval home as well as the committed set.
- **cycle-962 and cycle-968 (carry-forward filter).** The scout's conflict oracle was a bare 1-arg `git merge-tree`, which is not a 3-way merge, reports clean on real conflicts and has no duplicate screen, so superseded orphan `cycle-*` branches were selected and burned rebase cycles; cycle-826's auditor found 6 conflict indicators and 36 markers on a "clean" candidate. Cycle-962 built the filter with no production caller; cycle-968 wired it into the fleet-rebase pre-screen after cycle-948's PASS-but-unlanded duplicate re-audits, and cycle-969 added `evolve branches`.
- **cycles 786–804 (composition carry-forward).** The RUNG 0 reader, verifier and writer and core's seam were built and unit-tested, but nothing in `cmd/evolve` bound the closures, so every clean fleet rebase still took a full re-audit. That produced the `…Wired` observability and its composition-root test.
- **Carryover growth and loss** ([03-carryover-lifecycle.md](../decomposition/03-carryover-lifecycle.md) holds the 2026-08-10 flood and the cycle-1538 resurrection). The array grew without bound (65 entries, 26.6 KB by cycle 506) because todos had no expiry, which led to the creation-time TTL stamp (cycles 507 and 518). A fixed 58-byte boilerplate prefix and uncapped defect text bloated every router prompt (cycle-488). The memo's `carryover-todos.json` had no reader, so PASS-branch learning never reached the next planner (cycle-667). Nothing retired a shipped todo (cycle-1440).
- **cycles 1078, 1104, 1146–1148, 1365 (continuation).** A review-gate rejection leaves `RunCycle` on the error path, which never reached `finalizeCycle`, so the preserved worktree had no manifest (1078); the abnormal epilogue now stamps. A wave-planner lane has no inbox claim, so its snapshot was orphaned (1078), which added the lane-scope binding (1104). The failure digest served as prior findings told the next builder nothing, and it repeated the identical protected-surface rejection while the fail reason held the remedy (1146→1148). A continuation adopted a base that predated a landed `.gitignore` carve-out (1365), which added the base advance. The absorbing FAIL of cycles 1412/1418 led to the decline release ([incident](../../incidents/2026-08-10-continuation-absorbing-fail.md)).
- **cycle-492** ([ADR-0044](../adr/0044-unified-phase-recovery-protocol.md) C1). A transition-table cycle exhausted the dispatch loop's bound without a recorded terminal outcome and paged the `FAILED_UNEXPLAINED` alarm.
- **Repeatedly re-dispatching the same CLI after a deliverable-contract block can silently demote the contract gate.** The CLI fallback chain fires only on infra exit codes, never a contract violation, so a CLI that systematically mis-formats a deliverable burns every correction until the breaker opens and enforce quietly becomes advisory for the rest of the run.
- **Comparing a contract block's whole rendered reason text is not a safe identity check.** A partially repaired defect set changes the rendered string, which can suppress escalation exactly where an incapable CLI's signature is strongest; violation-code-set intersection is the safe identity.
- **A contract-block escalation trigger with no available target still needs a remedy.** Without one, an incapable CLI keeps receiving the same plain directive until the breaker opens purely for lack of anywhere to escalate to.
- **A CLI family can be capable of ordinary dispatches yet incapable of a whole class of contract-mandated repairs** (e.g. creating a missing artifact), while a sibling family never fails that same class — which is why a remediation-carrying rejection also escalates, not only a bare contract block.
- **A remediation-carrying, `Blocks==0` rejection (evalgate's shape) escalates exactly like a bare contract block**, on its second identical occurrence, and the escalated dispatch still carries the remediation text; a `Blocks==0` rejection with no remediation (topngate/triagecap/build-floor) never escalates, and two genuinely different rejections never do either. Pinned by `TestEvalGateEscalation_SecondIdenticalRejectionEscalatesAndRecovers`, `TestEvalGateEscalation_DifferentReasonsDoNotEscalate`, `TestEvalGateEscalation_RemediationlessRejectionNeverEscalates` and `TestEvalGateEscalation_EscalatedDirectiveKeepsTheRemediation`.
- **A ledger entry recording only Kind/Role with no evidence blinds breaker diagnostics.** A demotion entry's Action field must carry the decision verb and the evidence, not just its category.
- **D1, cycle-274.** A dead-shell continuation prompt anchored directly below agent diff content lost its distinguishing leading newline when the stripper deleted the diff line outright instead of blanking it in place, making a genuinely wedged shell misread as novel and re-learned at LLM cost; blanking in place keeps the anchor and the pane classified.
- **cycle-802 (retro-bridge-timeout-width10).** Both the main dispatch loop and the resume loop wrote `result.FinalVerdict` unconditionally at the end of every phase, so a post-verdict, non-floor phase (retrospective, memo, a scan phase) failing under quota or an artifact timeout clobbered an already-PASS audit verdict back to FAIL and zeroed a whole resume batch's waves. The same incident had separately mislabeled a phase that ran as one that never ran (dossier-retro-skipped-mislabel): those records used to land in `SkippedPhases`, so a FAIL cycle's dossier read a phase as skipped while its report sat on disk.
- **cycle-516 (carryover-todo-expiry-never-set).** `recordFailureLearning` created both the initial per-cycle-failure `CarryoverTodo` and its sibling `FailedRecord` without ever stamping `ExpiresAt`, so the already-wired `PruneExpiredCarryoverTodos` pass was a permanent no-op in production — none of 71 live `cycle-N-failed-*` entries carried an `expiresAt` key. The creation site now stamps both, proven end-to-end: a todo created this second survives an immediate real prune run because of a real, not-yet-elapsed TTL stamp, not because of prune's own legacy no-stamp survival rule.
- **The skills-drift storm (cycles 836/838/841/843/849).** A floor phase (audit) returning FAIL with no dispatch error — its in-process CI-parity gates overriding a narrative PASS to FAIL — was never fed to failure-learning, so `state.FailedAt` stayed empty and the failure-adapter and Scout could not learn the recurrence; the storm re-derived the same root cause repeatedly because nothing recorded audit's err==nil gate-FAIL.
- **cycle-1046.** The S1 failure-digest assembler was wired only at `recordFailureLearning` (the phase-error path); a verdict-path FAIL reaching retro via `cyclerun` dispatch produced no digest and no disposition, blinding both the disposition contract and the blocker breaker for the most common FAIL class. `ensureFailureDigest` became the one wiring both paths share.
- **cycles 1054/1060 and c1329.** A constant verdict-path fallback reason collided two different tasks' audit FAILs; `verdictFailDistinguisher` added per-failure content. Before that, the ship-time repo-contract-gate rejection's real message vocabulary matched no pre-class needle and fell into "unknown," indistinguishable from every other unclassified failure, until three recurrences tripped the identical-fingerprint breaker and false-halted the batch.
- **A hand-rolled sentinel parser once dropped the placeholder-echo guard**, letting the Deliverable Contract's own printed example ("<one line per defect>") echo into a report and mint a constant cross-task defect identity — the exact false-trip `verdictFailDistinguisher` exists to prevent. It now reads through `phasecontract.ReadFailureBlock`, the same authority the verdict path, dossier and failure-learning consult.
- **cycle-1440 (fingerprint-normalizer-path-variance).** `normalizeReasonForFingerprint` stripped only two identity-noise tokens (`narrative=<verdict>` and go-test durations); every other per-cycle-varying token — cycle-numbered artifact paths, bare "cycle N" prose, and a retry loop's attempt/retry denominator — still split one recurring defect into N fingerprints, so the identical-fingerprint breaker never reached its ceiling.
- **batch-19, cycle-1208.** Cycles 1197/1199/1207 aborted in phase build for three distinct reasons, but the abnormal epilogue wrote only its constant per-phase template, so all three digests were `Unexplained` with one fingerprint and the diagnosability breaker had to halt the batch to get an operator. The abort cause became the missing distinguisher, truncated from the tail (`causeHead`) since an error chain's distinguishing content lives at the end, not the shared prefix.
- **cycle-7 (worktree teardown).** A wrong prune destroyed an entire PASS cycle's work, which is why `teardownCycleWorktree`'s decision stays asymmetric: a missed prune only costs disk, so both no-prune conditions (preserve, or the cycle did not complete normally) err toward keeping the tree.
- **The resume path used to leak worktrees.** `teardownCycleWorktree` was originally an anonymous closure inside `RunCycle`'s cleanup stack. `RunCycleFromPhase` registered the other exit actions (lock release, run-ID clear, lease stop) by hand but had no counterpart for worktree disposal, so every resumed cycle leaked its worktree regardless of verdict; extracting the one shared rule closed the gap.
- **cycle-1048 (abnormal exit blind spot).** The loop's abort path returned immediately on failure, skipping `finalizeCycle`, so the seal, dossier and coherence floors never ran and left a two-hour stale `phase=retro` record with no dossier — invisible to every monitor, since detectors read artifacts the failure path itself never produced. `abnormalEpilogue` now runs as a deferred, construction-guaranteed epilogue on every exit path. Pinned by `TestAbnormalEpilogue_WritesDossierDigestAndCoherentState` and `TestAbnormalEpilogue_NoopAfterNormalCloseout`.
- **Verdict-coherence false halts and blind spots.** A verdict-coherence floor that could not tell a genuine forged verdict from a benign clean-exit late write halted good batches on green artifacts, which added the self-heal reconciliation. A cycle whose ship hit landing-queue contention and correctly routed to the debugger still closed out PASS with nothing shipped, which added the lost-landing floor. A genuine PASS that was mis-graded FAIL and routed audit→retro→end silently discarded its worktree, which added the silent-no-ship warning.
- **Workspace pollution across cycle-number reuse.** A killed attempt's leftover `scout-report.md`/`build-report.md` made Scout short-circuit on stale artifacts and steer downstream phases via the old task selection; the workspace is now archived before any phase runs when a prior attempt polluted the same cycle number.
- **A committing builder's work must become pending again.** Recording the worktree's HEAD at creation as the cycle base lets the post-build soft-reset (`normalizeWorktreeToBase`) restore a builder's committed work to the working tree instead of leaving it buried in a commit the audit never sees as a diff.
- **`cs.ActiveWorktree` was write-only for a while.** Teardown deleted the worktree directory but left the record naming it, so the next reader (retro's dispatch, resume, checkpoint) handed a deleted path to the bridge, whose IsDir guard refused the launch.
- **A bare scoped id can resolve to a stale duplicate.** With many inbox records once sharing one id, a bare name resolved to a two-week-old consumed namesake and every phase worked the cured ghost, which is why `fleet_scope_paths` discloses each id's live path beside it.
- **The challenge token was agent-invented before the orchestrator minted one,** producing a token that did not match the one written to `challenge-token.txt`. The orchestrator now mints it once per cycle in the same 8-byte-hex shape the bridge already used, so ledger entries stay indistinguishable across the change.
- **The correction ladder's salvage rung.** A valid deliverable left at the wrong path once burned two full re-dispatches before it relocated automatically; a correction attempt that carried only the violation text, with no evidence of what the previous attempt had actually touched, motivated the rung-3 kernel-verified evidence digest.
- **`evolve campaign run --simulate` left closeout commits on the operator's own branch** ([incident](../../incidents/2026-09-14-simulate-runs-against-the-checkout.md)), because the dossier producer always committed. `cycleDossierParams.FilesOnly` and the `WithDossierCommit` root knob separated "write the record" from "commit it".
- **A fixed, shared `/tmp` project root is cross-process mutable state.** Concurrent fleet lanes running the test suite against the same hardcoded path sampled each other's writes, accumulating tens of thousands of stray run entries whose pollution signature (an archived-polluted-workspace message, `fatal: not a git repository`) read as false FAILs in the suites-stay-green meta-predicates on an otherwise idle, green host. `TestCoreTests_NeverPinSharedTmpProjectRoot` guards the whole `go/internal` tree recursively (not just this package), since a package-local scan would let a re-pin in any sibling or nested package ship undetected.
- **Two rung-2 hunk-intersection implementations once compared different coordinate spaces and could disagree on the same diff pair.** The production-wired `intersectingHunks`/`RunScopedMergeReview` (`mergerung2.go`) compares each hunk's old-side (pre-image) line range; an unwired duplicate compared the new-side (post-image) range instead, so a hunk pair whose old-side ranges intersected could have disjoint new-side ranges and be silently skipped as "no conflict" on the other path. Both call sites now agree on the old-side semantic. Pinned by `TestScopedReviewImplementations_MustAgreeOnOverlap`.
- **`WithCatalogPublisher` closes a stale-catalog-snapshot class for a mid-cycle mint.** The bridge's deliverable-contract resolver is bound once at cycle start over a snapshot of the phase catalog (a method value over that catalog's own map); `registerMintedPhases` replaces `o.catalog` with a new `Catalog` (a new map) when the advisor mints a phase mid-cycle, so a resolver still bound to the pre-mint snapshot misses the freshly-minted phase for the rest of the cycle and every contract-dependent consumer falls back to the unresolved-agent path. The publisher sink, called after the catalog splice with the merged catalog, carries the new catalog to a resolver bound over it instead. Publishing is scoped tightly: only a successfully-registered mint publishes (a registrar rejection — out-of-envelope, driverless profile, bad name — publishes nothing, since a routable ghost with no runner must never reach the live resolver), an unrelated unknown agent name still misses after a mint (no over-broad catalog widening), and an orchestrator built without the option is an unaffected no-op. Pinned by `TestRegisterMintedPhases_PublishesCatalogToLiveResolver`, `TestRegisterMintedPhases_PublishedCatalogStillMissesUnknownAgent`, `TestRegisterMintedPhases_RejectedMintPublishesNothing` and `TestRegisterMintedPhases_NoPublisherIsNoop`.
- **`profileForModelRouting` is the production DI seam feeding the model-tier-envelope guard real per-phase profiles; a nil-returning stub here silently disabled both the ceiling and the universal floor for every live cycle**, even though the router-level guard (`router.ClampPlanModelRouting`) was itself correct — the defect was purely in what profile the guard was handed. A phase whose own profile declares an explicit `model_tier_envelope` clamps an above-ceiling advisor tier down to the envelope's max; a phase whose profile declares no envelope still clamps a below-floor tier up to the compiled `universalTierFloor.Min`; a phase with no profile file on disk at all degrades nil-safe (no clamp, no error, matching `ValidatePin`'s nil-profile pass-through) — genuinely absent is distinct from present-but-envelope-less, and only the latter gets the universal floor. A within-envelope tier passes through unclamped (the guard must never degenerate into always-clamp), and a fired ceiling clamp is persisted into `phase-plan.json`, the operator-visible artifact, not silently applied. Pinned by `TestModelTierEnvelope_CeilingClampsThroughRealProfileLookup`, `TestModelTierEnvelope_UniversalFloorClampsThroughRealDispatch`, `TestModelTierEnvelope_WithinEnvelopeTierPassesThrough`, `TestModelTierEnvelope_AbsentProfileDegradesNilSafe` and `TestModelTierEnvelope_ClampRecordedInPhasePlan`. Since L1b it reads the profile through `phaseProfile`, the one lookup contract escalation also uses: `phaseAgentName` first, then the `<phase>.json` convention every minted or user phase follows. Before, it consulted the built-in table alone, so a phase outside the table (a minted phase with a real `.evolve/profiles/<phase>.json`) got a nil profile and no clamp at all (2026-10-06, L1b of the CLI routing table, ADR-0119; `TestProfileForModelRouting_AMintedPhaseReadsItsOwnProfile`).
- **`TestOrchestrator_Enforce_RunsUserPhaseBetweenBuildAndAudit` is the single end-to-end proof that catalog merge, routing-order splicing, signal triggers, and orchestrator dispatch work together as one pipeline for user-defined phases.**
- **`backfillArtifactPath`'s phase→filename mapping is load-bearing** — a wrong filename means a backfilled artifact lands where the downstream phase never reads it. `retro`→`retrospective-report.md` and `build-planner`→`build-plan.md` are deliberate overrides of the naive default-branch guess. Pinned by `TestBackfillArtifactPath_AllPhases`.
- **`optionalSkipDetails` splits the skip's ledger entry by error class** (`optional_infra_skip` vs `optional_missing_persona_skip`); merging a missing-persona skip into the infra bucket previously hid a distinct failure class. Pinned by `TestOptionalSkipDetails_SplitsByErrorClass`.
- **`HasRunner` exists because dispatch has a missing-runner escape hatch**: a phase the router nominates without a registered runner is silently skipped rather than erroring; `HasRunner` is the seam tests use to prove a routing→dispatch handoff is actually wired. Pinned by `TestOrchestrator_HasRunner`.
- `PlanJudge`/`NewPlanJudge` (`phase_judge.go`) have no production caller yet; the routing-quality judge is wired only in tests, pending a live policy gate.
- `LedgerEntry.TaskID` exists because inbox-lifecycle records once bypassed the chained append entirely (raw O_APPEND, no `prev_hash`), which could break the hash chain under fleet concurrency; routing them through the chain via this field closed that gap.
- `chainReviewer.Review` must let a `Demoted` flag survive both exits: production mounts the contract gate mid-chain (buildFloor → evalgate → contract → triagecap → topngate), so collapsing a later rejection to a bare `Approve:true` would swallow the gate's own "stopped enforcing" report, and returning a later reviewer's rejection verbatim would swallow it too — the case that hits triagecap, which sits directly after the contract gate. `carryDemotion` is what keeps it visible on the short-circuit path.
- A fresh explanation-document rebase deliberately supersedes the legacy composition rungs. A new base makes the rationale of the Build not valid. Thus the recovery rebuilds and re-audits, even when the patch-id of the lane is stable. Pinned by `TestFreshExplanationRebase_BypassesTheLegacyCompositionRungs`.
- `MintRunID` implements ULID generation inline (Crockford base32, 48-bit ms timestamp + 80-bit crypto/rand) rather than pulling in a third-party ULID library, since the repo carries no third-party deps and the needed spec subset is small.
- Ship recovery's budget scales with fleet width for contention-class errors (every `AUDIT_BINDING_*` code and `GIT_FLEET_REBASE_NEEDED`): with N fleet lanes racing one main, the odds a sibling moves HEAD during this lane's audit→ship window grow with N, so a fixed recovery budget shows a steady abort rate that looks like loop failure while being pure landing-queue contention. The budget becomes `max(maxRecoveryDepth, fleetWidth+1)` so every sibling can bump HEAD once plus one clean pass, while every other error code keeps the constant budget so width can never inflate retries for genuine failures.
- `retryOpts` is a single Strategy-value registry of phase-retry recovery hooks. The sequential, resume and evaluate-batch loops all consult it, so a hook cannot silently diverge between them. A nil field means that the path deliberately does not run that hook. `retry_opts_parity_test.go` pins the full field set by reflection. The resume loop uses the reference set minus backfill and ship recovery. A resumed cycle that already shipped therefore degrades a failed best-effort observer and does not abort.
- A phase that returns `VerdictSKIPPED` produces no deliverable to review, so the reviewer is never consulted for it — this avoids noise and false rejections of skipped-by-policy phases. Pinned by `TestOrchestrator_ReviewerSkippedPhasesNotConsulted`.
- `safety_invariants_test.go` loads the real phase configuration via the `kerneltest` fixture and references phases only through STRUCTURAL accessors (`FirstAnchor`/`ShipTerminal`/`Evaluator`), never by hardcoded name, so renaming a phase in the registry never requires rewriting a safety-invariant test; adversarial cases mutate the loaded config with fixture-derived names instead of literals.
- `decideAfterDebugger` maps the debugger phase's recovery decision (surfaced on `PhaseResponse.Signals`) to the next phase, mirroring `decideAfterRetro`: RESHIP→ship; RERUN_PHASE→the named phase (defaulting to audit); BLOCK/empty/unknown→end. A malformed decision already safe-defaults to BLOCK in the debugger's Classify, so this conservatively ends on anything not explicitly RESHIP/RERUN_PHASE.
- `IsShippingVerdict`'s allowlist shape is load-bearing: `CycleOutcomeSkippedUnknown` once fell through a denylist-shaped breaker and was misread as shipped, so an unrecognised label must classify as non-shipping by default rather than defaulting to "shipped". `TestIsShippingVerdict_WholeOutcomeVocabulary` walks the whole ADR-0079 outcome vocabulary to keep the allowlist honest.
- The 862→899 batch (37 cycles) forged a FAIL verdict over green audit/ACS artifacts with byte-identical defect strings, which the deterministic verdict-incoherence floor now catches by design. Cycles 930/931/932 later false-halted the whole batch on the opposite failure mode: a runner-side CI-parity gate (the integration tier) legitimately downgraded a green-sentinel audit to FAIL, and because the floor did not yet read `AuditFailReasons`, every diagnosed gate-downgrade read as forgery — fixed by `hasSubstantiveFailReasons`. Cycle-1329 (`ship|unknown|76d0f4fca190`, 3x identical-fingerprint recurrence) was the ship-phase twin: audit and ACS both recorded PASS (161/161 green) but ship legitimately rejected the cycle post-audit (`REPO_CONTRACT_GATE`), a shape indistinguishable from genuine forgery until `ShipFailReasons` joined `AuditFailReasons` as a diagnosed-downgrade carrier.
- **A worktree provisioning failure's git error used to reach only the orchestrator's stderr.** Continuing with an empty `ActiveWorktree` after a provisioning error is correct — the role gate then denies every source phase — but the git error itself never reached the cycle's failure-reason or failure-digest surfaces, so the recorded identity was only the downstream phase refusal (`scout|infra-error|…`), and a real, recurring provisioning failure stayed invisible to the identical-fingerprint breaker. The cause is now persisted where the failure-digest assembler reads it, keyed on the cause text so two different provisioning causes fingerprint distinctly and the same cause fingerprints identically across runs; a successful provision adds no failure reason. Pinned by `TestWorktreeProvisionFailure_PersistsCause`, `TestWorktreeProvisionFailure_DigestUsesProvisioningCause`, `TestWorktreeProvisionFailure_SuccessAddsNoFailureReason` and `TestWorktreeProvisionFailure_EmptyStderrCauseStillIdentifies`.
- **Cycle 1806 (wave 65, 2026-10-06) was a fleet lane lost to another process's fetch.** `gh pr update-branch` moved a train branch while the lane's `git fetch origin` was updating every remote-tracking ref in the shared store, so the fetch failed with `cannot lock ref 'refs/remotes/origin/train/wave64-boundary': is at … but expected …`, provisioning failed, the scout launch was refused for lack of a worktree, and the cycle sealed FAIL with a P0 failure-learning todo about the task. The narrowed, retried fetch and the lane deferral above are the fix ([incident](../../incidents/2026-10-06-cycle-1806-fetch-ref-lock.md)).
- A width-3 batch (2026-07-13) emitted 76 stderr-only `spine not satisfied` fail-open WARNs with no counter, dossier field or threshold — an epidemic with no dashboard, and the WARN named only the phase being entered, not which predecessor's artifact was missing. `UnsatisfiedSpineAnchor` + `CycleResult.SpineFailOpens` (cycle-1166) made the signal countable and attributable before deciding whether the fix is artifact-retry, driver-bounce recovery, or an enforce flip.
- The R8.5 spine-floor flip to `enforce` (2026-07-16) was validated by replaying 536 real `.evolve/runs` directories through `router.Digest` + `StateMachine.SpineSatisfiedUpTo`: zero would-block transitions on any cycle shape since ~cycle-480, after the scout/audit digest fallbacks landed; the only misses were pre-convention dirs (cycles 361-479). `TestSpineReplay_LocalHistory` (env-gated on `EVOLVE_SPINE_REPLAY_DIR`) is the re-runnable harness behind that evidence.

## Core file group 5 rationale

The reasons that the comments of the phase-I/O, ports, reset, resume, repair, retry and reviewer files carried. The code and the tests state the what.

- **Phase-I/O stays byte-identical below its stage.**
  - The build-plan envelope is served only at `EVOLVE_PHASE_IO>=advisory`. It also needs the planner enabled and a readable file.
  - Every other case returns an empty body, and the build phase reads disk as before. A missing file gives an empty body, never an error.
  - `PhaseInput` is assembled only at `>=enforce`, from one `router.Digest`; below it the zero value keeps dispatch identical to the pre-cutover loop. A digest failure skips the shadow comparison and, at enforce, still assembles the input with an empty upstream view.
  - `NewPhaseInput` deep-copies the env map so a later loop iteration cannot mutate a sealed input. `PhaseInput` is excluded from JSON because its channels are unexported; the subprocess path keeps using `Context`.
  - The shadow comparator re-derives expected values from the signal directly, not through `HandoffsFromSignals`, so a projection bug surfaces as a mismatch. It compares against the keys the phases really read (`challengeToken` camelCase, `carryover_summary`), so key drift surfaces. The shadow ledger append is best-effort and must never affect the cycle.
- **Judge parsing.** `parseJudgeVerdict` takes the LAST balanced JSON object.
  - A brace inside the rationale must not be miscounted.
  - The echoed example in the prompt must not be taken as the answer.
  - A judge error fails open to score -1.
  - Phases are ordered deterministically, so the prompt prefix stays cache-friendly.
- **Phase request fields.**
  - `ProjectRoot` is the runtime-data root and `Worktree` the shipped-tree root; collapsing them lets a predicate run against the wrong tree. An empty `Worktree` is the degraded provisioning-failure mode.
  - `WorktreeBaseSHA` is the host-owned cycle base. Integrity checks diff against it, never against a mutable manifest field.
  - `Fence` comes from the orchestrator's write-permission rule, and the runner enforces it. A phase never sets it. `WorktreeVerified` never crosses the subprocess envelope.
  - `ModelRoutingCLI/Tier` are set only in `auto` mode and applied as a soft overlay (promote to chain primary, keep the fallback chain). `BudgetScale` of 0 or 1 is unscaled; below 1 never shrinks.
  - `PersonaProber`: only a KNOWN persona absence (an error wrapping `ErrAgentDocMissing`) is deterministic enough to exclude a phase from the menu.
- **Ports.**
  - `LedgerEntry.cycle` accepts an int or a legacy string. On-disk bytes are never rewritten, because that can cascade hash-chain breaks through every later entry.
  - Ledger `Source` on `phase_skipped` entries is `router`, `psmas` or `content`. `Action` carries the self-heal verb (`extend`, `pause`) or the inbox verb (`claim`, `promote`). `WorktreeTreeSHA` binds ship's tree-drift check to the audited changes, not the auditor's unchanged base.
  - `GuardDecision.Alarm` marks an integrity violation (an agent touching the control plane that grades it) and additionally emits a CRITICAL record to `.evolve/integrity-alarm.jsonl`.
  - `PhaseRequest.ModelRoutingEffort` and `BridgeRequest.Effort` carry the ladder effort of a retry (ADR-0142 L3). Empty means the tier default. No core path sets it until L5.
  - `BridgeRequest.ProjectRoot` zero disables sandbox confinement for the call; `RequireSandbox` fails the launch closed. `ChainAttempt` makes a chain-walking handle pass the request through instead of walking again. `SessionName` pins a tmux session, which the caller then owns for teardown. `SecondaryArtifacts` are existence-only waits; the artifact-timeout final poll completes without them and the phase gate reports the absence.
  - One function derives the bridge PID file name from the stdout-log path. The bridge writer and the observer reader share it, so a rename moves both.
- **Post-build repin.** It is never operator-authorized and fails open (a refusal warns and returns a zero result); the ship gate stays the backstop. An empty build commit is unverifiable, so a stripped binary cannot self-authorize a repin. The provenance function is a package seam so the decision stays git-free under test.
- **Orphan pruning.**
  - A superseded branch is kept before any PR check when a worktree has it checked out or a continuation binding names it.
  - A delete runs only when `hasOpenPR` reports false.
  - A refused delete is recorded and the walk continues.
  - An error from listing, the registry, git or `hasOpenPR` aborts before a further delete.
- **All-families quota exhaustion.**
  - It is terminal only for two or more tries that all exited 85. The bridge alternates families, so an all-85 sequence means every family at every allowed tier is drained. A single try proves nothing.
  - `cyclehealth.ClassifyOutcome` matches the abort-reason prefix to classify DEFERRED. The loop then stops resumable and does not burn the next cycle.
- **Repair rounds.**
  - The audit-repair and decline reason prefixes are single constants shared by the emitter and the consumer. A drifted literal silently disables the bound.
  - The context keys are exported and single-sourced because the setter lives in core and the readers in `internal/phases/*`.
